# Design Document: MCP Servers for the Portal and the Edge LocalServer

## Overview

This feature adds two Model Context Protocol (MCP) servers so that Kiro and other MCP clients can operate the product through tools instead of hand-written REST calls, using only the credentials the product already has:

1. **Portal_MCP_Server** — one new CDK stack (`EdgeCVPortalMcpStack`) deploying one Lambda behind its own small API Gateway **HTTP API**, speaking Streamable HTTP with the official `mcp` Python SDK 2.3.0. The gateway attaches **no authorizer** to the MCP routes; the SDK's bearer middleware plus a `CognitoAccessTokenVerifier` inside the Lambda is the single validator. Authorization-server discovery uses the MCP specification's **well-known-URI mechanism**: the Lambda serves RFC 9728 Protected Resource Metadata at the root (`/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource`) and at the six path-suffixed locations, and an **RFC 8414 authorization-server metadata facade** at `/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server` whose `issuer` is the MCP API origin and whose endpoints are the existing Cognito hosted UI (Decision point 2). The SDK still emits the RFC 9728 `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer … resource_metadata="…"` challenge on 401, but API Gateway remaps that response header to `X-Amzn-Remapped-WWW-Authenticate`, so the design does **not** rely on it (documented deviation from Requirement 2.4's header wording, owner sign-off item; review-2 H1). Every tool is dispatched to the **existing portal handler Lambda** that serves the equivalent REST route, with the caller's verified Cognito access-token claims forwarded verbatim, so RBAC (`rbac_middleware` / `shared_utils.RBACManager`), the Portal_Identity_Registry, input validation, and the handlers' own audit writes run unchanged. Authentication is OAuth 2.1 against the **existing Cognito user pool**: a dedicated MCP app client and resource server are added to the pool (configuration, not a new identity store); Kiro obtains an access token with the hosted-UI authorization-code + PKCE flow it already implements. One deployable exposes an aggregate endpoint (`/mcp`) and five domain views (`/mcp/data`, `/mcp/models`, `/mcp/devices`, `/mcp/workflows`, `/mcp/admin`) over one tool registry. Because Lambda is request/response and the SDK's session manager may be entered only once per instance, the Starlette application is **composed per invocation** from module-level server objects (Decision point 2).
2. **Edge_MCP_Server** — a child process inside the existing `flask-app` container, running the same `mcp` SDK from an **isolated virtualenv baked into the image** (so none of the LocalServer's pinned dependencies move), bound to loopback only (which under the container's `network_mode: host` is the device's shared loopback — reachable by any local process, so the child is designed to be safe to reach directly and carries explicit transport-security settings), and reached through a new `/mcp` route on the existing FastAPI Backend_API listener (port 5000 or 5443). The route carries the existing `authorize_request` decision matrix, so the Local_Session_Token / Existing_Token_Auth rules decide access exactly as for every other route; the child never acts on its own authority — it forwards each tool call (and the identity lookup) to the Backend_API over loopback with the caller's own credential, where the same authorizer decides again — and enforces MCP-scoped role gating from the role the validated Local_Session_Token's synced account carries. Everything is off by default behind two new Greengrass component-configuration keys (`McpServerEnabled`, `McpMutatingToolsEnabled`), read through the same shared IPC reader `LocalLoginEnabled` uses today.

Both servers share one naming, classification, confirmation, and error vocabulary (Requirement 14), ship read-only tools first (Requirement 17), and are covered by protocol-level, authentication-negative, RBAC-denial, audit, CDK, and on-hardware tests (Requirement 16).

This is the **twelfth revision**. Revisions 2–11 addressed every finding of the first nine reviews (dispositions in the response tables at the end): revision 5 introduced the SSM dispatch-map carrier (D30), the stack-owned shared layer (D31), the single-attempt `invoke` with `status_tool` (D32) and the two-step opt-out (D33); revision 6 reclassified `GET /snapshot` as a Mutating_Tool, added the D34 result hygiene (path scrub, `image_field`, `drop_fields`; Property 19), derived the portal invoke budget from the remaining invocation time (D32) and specified rendered descriptions and listed aliases (D35); revision 7 corrected the edge catalog's description of what its routes accept and return (per-spec `limit` bounds, dotted-path `drop_fields` and the oversized-image guard, `body_constants`, `timeout_s`, the mechanical Property 2 argument ↔ route clause) and deferred two operations with no MCP-compatible route (Backlog 17, 19); revision 8 reclassified `portal_workflows_validate` as a Phase-2 Mutating_Tool, added the portal classification and permission tests, `stream_url`, `body_fields`, `tail_chars` and sign-off item (h), and scanned the concrete-argument portal rows against their handlers (Backlog 20); revision 9 addressed every finding of the eighth review (H1, M1, M2, N1–N4) and applied a row-by-row check of all 164 catalog rows of both servers against their routes on five axes — route and dispatch, classification, permission, arguments, shaping (dispositions in "Response to the design review (revision 9)" at the end): the portal classification rule walks the route function's whole call graph, drops the `'success'`-audit clause, names the shared-layer writers and accepts ten status-sync GETs through a declared `ToolSpec.accepted_writes` list (H1); permission declarations that admitted roles the handler rejects now equal the handler's set (M1, and five more rows the sweep found); tools whose routes do nothing or need an excluded upload are removed with Backlog items (`POST /components`, `POST /components/discover`, the component configure that deploys nothing, the portal static pins, the bucket-creation stub), the components row is split (M2); argument lists, path parameters and destructive classifications are corrected row by row, a path-parameter identifier can no longer address a different route (no `/`, not `.`/`..`; percent-encoded on the edge), and the edge text tail is cut by encoded size (N4). Revision 10 applied the nine findings of the ninth review (M1, M2, N1–N7), each to its whole defect class rather than only to the rows it names (dispositions and per-class scan counts in "Response to the design review (revision 10)" at the end): `usecase_id` and `limit`/`offset` forwarding is explicit on every row whose route reads them (*corrected in revision 11: except the audit-log `usecase_id` dead filter, deliberately not offered — Backlog 25 — and now recorded as `usecase_not_forwarded='dead_filter'`*), and every dispatcher-sliced collection offers `offset`; the argument test folds constants, follows `getattr` delegation and checks path arguments against the route template; the edge classification walk is transitive and declares its accepted effects (`accepted_effects`); `accepted_writes` no longer key on line numbers and `SHARED_LAYER_WRITERS` is derived from the layer; id-addressed reads are bound to the named Use_Case (`bind_usecase_path`) or named in sign-off item (j); `portal_data_labeling_manifest_get` leaves `CATALOG` (portal 184 tools, 87 in Phase 1); and `portal_devices_deployment_create` confirms its target. Revision 11 applied the eight findings of the tenth review (M1, N1–N7), each to its whole defect class (dispositions and class-scan counts in "Response to the design review (revision 11)" at the end): `edge_image_source_preview` no longer takes the route's settings override, so no read-only tool forwards a free-form object or a pipeline fragment (Backlog 28); the edge effect list sees Pillow, `tempfile`, `os.replace`/`os.rename` and every non-read `shutil` call, and declares the JP6 PNG staging; `usecase_not_forwarded` makes the `usecase_id` exemptions machine-readable; the bare-identifier path rule raises on ambiguity; a D36 refusal is indistinguishable from a missing object and is counted in Property 6; `target_devices` has `minItems: 1`; and the `/`-joined tool cells follow one stated shorthand rule. The tool counts are unchanged (portal 184, edge 73). **This revision** applies the six NITs of the eleventh review (verdict `APPROVED`; N1–N6, dispositions in "Response to the design review (revision 12)" at the end): both D36 outcomes return the same whole result, `status: 404` included; `model_name` writes its `(→ path …)` entry on the three edge rows it fills; a Pillow image value is defined once, `accepted_effects` entries are keyed by the effect-list name, and the effect list gains the `os` mutators and the `pathlib` twins (two new entries on `_test_connection`/`_preview`); the `_x` label rule matches the columns; every `RO` edge string argument has a `pattern` or `enum` or is listed in `RO_FREE_TEXT_ARGS`; and three line citations are corrected. The tool counts are again unchanged (portal 184, edge 73). Where a finding label is cited inline, "review-N" marks the Nth review's numbering (review-2 … review-11); unqualified labels refer to the first review.

### Research baseline (primary sources, verified 2026-10-18)

- **MCP protocol revision.** The current revision is **2026-07-28** (modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/versioning, "The current protocol version is 2026-07-28", fetched 2026-10-18). Its changelog (modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/changelog) removes protocol-level sessions and the `Mcp-Session-Id` header (SEP-2567), removes the `initialize` handshake so every request carries `io.modelcontextprotocol/protocolVersion` and `clientCapabilities` in `_meta` (SEP-2575), adds the mandatory `server/discover` RPC, requires `ttlMs`/`cacheScope` on list results and recommends deterministic `tools/list` order (SEP-2549), and reclassifies the 2024-11-05 HTTP+SSE transport as Deprecated (SEP-2596). Standard transports are **stdio** and **Streamable HTTP** (modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/streamable-http): a single endpoint accepting POST, `MCP-Protocol-Version` required on every POST, `Mcp-Method`/`Mcp-Name` routing headers, servers MUST validate `Origin` (403 if invalid), SHOULD bind local servers to 127.0.0.1, SHOULD authenticate all connections, MAY answer each POST with a single JSON object instead of an SSE stream, and MUST answer `GET` with 405 when they offer no server-initiated stream. Backward compatibility with handshake-era revisions (2025-11-25 and earlier) is defined in the same document.
- **MCP authorization** (modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/basic/authorization and its subpages, verified 2026-10-18): optional; HTTP transports SHOULD conform; stdio SHOULD NOT and takes credentials from the environment. A conforming server is an OAuth 2.1 resource server that publishes RFC 9728 Protected Resource Metadata and **MUST implement one of two discovery mechanisms** (…/basic/authorization/authorization-server-discovery, "Protected Resource Metadata Discovery Requirements", re-read 2026-10-18): (i) the `WWW-Authenticate` header carrying `resource_metadata="…"` on 401 responses (RFC 9728 §5.1), or (ii) serving the metadata at a well-known URI — either path-suffixed (`https://example.com/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource/public/mcp` for the endpoint `https://example.com/public/mcp`) or at the root (`https://example.com/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource`). Clients MUST support both mechanisms, use the header's URL when present, and otherwise "MUST fall back to constructing and requesting the well-known URIs in the order listed above" (path-suffixed first, then root). The server accepts tokens only in `Authorization: Bearer` (never in the query string), MUST validate that tokens were issued for it (RFC 8707 audience), MUST NOT accept or transit tokens issued for other resources ("token passthrough" is forbidden by the Security Best Practices page), returns 401 for invalid/expired tokens and 403 for insufficient scope. Authorization-server discovery (same subpage): for an issuer **without** a path, clients MUST try `<issuer>/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server` then `<issuer>/.well-known/openid-configuration`; for a path-bearing issuer, `/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server/<path>`, `/.well-known/openid-configuration/<path>`, then `<issuer>/.well-known/openid-configuration`; the document's `issuer` MUST be identical to the issuer the PRM advertised. Clients MUST use PKCE S256, MUST send the RFC 8707 `resource` parameter, and (…/basic/authorization/security-considerations, "PKCE", re-read 2026-10-18) "If `code_challenge_methods_supported` is absent, the authorization server does not support PKCE and MCP clients **MUST** refuse to proceed" — for both RFC 8414 and OpenID Connect Discovery documents. Client registration: Client ID Metadata Documents, pre-registration, or (deprecated) Dynamic Client Registration.
- **API Gateway header handling (review-2 H1).** The API Gateway developer guide's "important notes" page (docs.aws.amazon.com/apigateway/latest/developerguide/api-gateway-known-issues.html, fetched 2026-10-18) lists the response header `WWW-Authenticate` as **Remapped** for Lambda and HTTP integrations ("a 401 response resulted from remapping of the `WWW-Authenticate` header to `X-Amzn-Remapped-WWW-Authenticate`"). The HTTP API parameter-mapping page (…/http-api-parameter-mapping.html, fetched 2026-10-18) lists `x-amzn-*` among the reserved headers for which "You can't configure request or response mappings", so the remapped header cannot be copied back by an integration response mapping, and HTTP APIs cannot customize gateway responses. A field report reproducing the remapping on an HTTP API v2 Lambda integration and on Lambda Function URLs (anthropics/claude-ai-mcp issue #748, 2026-08-02) found no proxy-integration override; its workaround was a CloudFront Function. Consequence for this design: the Lambda-emitted 401 challenge is not expected to reach clients as `WWW-Authenticate`; discovery relies on mechanism (ii) above (see Decision point 2 and Security "Token handling"). Deployed-stack observation (2026-10-25, the smoke check and the owner's Kiro IDE session): the HTTP API passed the Lambda's `WWW-Authenticate` challenge through unchanged and no `X-Amzn-Remapped-WWW-Authenticate` header appeared, so on this hosting the remapping is REST-API behaviour; the well-known-URI discovery stays the design, and the reasoning above is kept as the record of why it was chosen.
- **Tool semantics** (modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/server/tools): names SHOULD be 1–128 characters of `A-Za-z0-9_.-`, unique and case-sensitive; `annotations` (`readOnlyHint`, `destructiveHint`, `idempotentHint`, `openWorldHint`) MUST be treated as untrusted by clients; tool execution errors are returned as results with `isError: true`.
- **Python SDK — package facts.** PyPI package `mcp`, latest stable **2.3.0** (released 2026-10-02), `Requires-Python >=3.10` (pypi.org/pypi/mcp/json, fetched 2026-10-18). Runtime dependencies: `pydantic>=2.12.0`, `httpx2>=2.10.0`, `jsonschema>=4.20.0`, `uvicorn>=0.31.1` (not on emscripten), `starlette>=0.27` (`>=0.48.0` on Python ≥3.14), `sse-starlette>=3.0.0`, `pyjwt[crypto]>=2.10.1`, `anyio>=4.9`, `mcp-types==2.3.0`, `opentelemetry-api>=1.28.0`, `python-multipart>=0.0.9`, `typing-extensions>=4.13.0`, `typing-inspection>=0.4.1`. The maintained 1.x line is 1.30.0 (2026-09-07).
- **Python SDK — API surface used by this design (read from the installed 2.3.0 wheel, 2026-10-18).** `mcp.server.lowlevel.server.Server(name, *, version, instructions, cache_hints, lifespan, on_list_tools, on_call_tool, …)`; `mcp.server.caching.CacheHint(ttl_ms: int = 0, scope: Literal["public","private"] = "private")` (the field is `scope`, not `cache_scope`); `mcp.server.streamable_http_manager.StreamableHTTPSessionManager(app, *, json_response, stateless, security_settings, …)` whose `run()` async context manager **raises `RuntimeError` when entered a second time on the same instance** ("can only be called once per instance … Create a new instance if you need to run again") and whose `handle_request` raises "Task group is not initialized" when `run()` is not active; `StreamableHTTPASGIApp(session_manager)` is the ASGI endpoint; `mcp.server.auth.middleware.bearer_auth.BearerAuthBackend(token_verifier, *, resource_server_url=None)` (Starlette `AuthenticationBackend`; when `resource_server_url` is given it additionally requires `AccessToken.resource` to equal that URL) and `RequireAuthMiddleware(app, required_scopes, resource_metadata_url)` (401 with `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer error="invalid_token", error_description="Authentication required", resource_metadata="…"` when no authenticated user is on the scope — `_send_auth_error` emits exactly `error`, `error_description` and `resource_metadata`, never the spec's optional `scope=` hint; 403 `insufficient_scope` when a required scope is missing); `mcp.server.auth.middleware.auth_context.AuthContextMiddleware` and `get_access_token()`; `mcp.server.auth.routes.build_resource_metadata_url(url)` → `https://host/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource<path>` (used for the challenge URL) and `create_protected_resource_routes(resource_url, authorization_servers, scopes_supported, …)` → one Starlette `Route` at that well-known path (GET/OPTIONS, CORS-wrapped) built on `mcp.shared.auth.ProtectedResourceMetadata` (`model_config = ConfigDict(url_preserve_empty_path=True)`, `resource: AnyHttpUrl`, `authorization_servers: list[AnyHttpUrl]`) — **not used by this design** (D27): passing a pre-built `AnyHttpUrl("https://host")` yields `"https://host/"` in the document because the standalone `AnyHttpUrl` adds the slash before the model's `url_preserve_empty_path` can act (verified with pydantic 2.14.1 on 2026-10-18: a `str` input is preserved, an `AnyHttpUrl` input is not), and the function's type hint invites exactly that call; `Server.streamable_http_app(...)` composes exactly these pieces into **one Starlette whose PRM route lives inside that app** — so mounting it under a path prefix moves the PRM route away from the RFC 9728 location (the reason this design composes the root app itself, H2); `mcp.server.transport_security.TransportSecuritySettings(enable_dns_rebinding_protection, allowed_hosts, allowed_origins)` — an absent `Origin` is accepted (`TransportSecurityMiddleware._validate_origin` returns True for an empty header), a disallowed one is 403, a disallowed `Host` 421; **`Server.streamable_http_app(...)` applies loopback defaults when `transport_security is None` and `host in ("127.0.0.1", "localhost", "::1")`: `allowed_hosts=["127.0.0.1:*", "localhost:*", "[::1]:*"]` **and** `allowed_origins=["http://127.0.0.1:*", "http://localhost:*", "http://[::1]:*"]`** (`mcp/server/lowlevel/server.py:753–757` — the `if transport_security is None and host in (…)` test at 753 through the `allowed_origins` default at 757; re-verified 2026-10-18 in both installed 2.3.0 copies, review-4 N6) — i.e. a loopback child that relies on the defaults *accepts* loopback `Origin` values (review-3 M3); the same method takes `max_request_body_size: int = DEFAULT_MAX_REQUEST_BODY_SIZE` (4 MiB, `transport_security.py:15`), enforced by `RequestBodyLimitMiddleware` inside `StreamableHTTPSessionManager` (413 on oversize); an absent `MCP-Protocol-Version` header is treated as **`DEFAULT_NEGOTIATED_VERSION = "2025-03-26"`** (`mcp_types/_types.py:26`, read by `streamable_http.py:639/950`), and a header value outside `HANDSHAKE_PROTOCOL_VERSIONS` is routed to the 2026 entry point, which validates it and returns a structured rejection (`streamable_http_manager.py:187–198`); `mcp.server.auth.middleware.auth_context.auth_context_var` is the `contextvars.ContextVar[AuthenticatedUser | None]` that `AuthContextMiddleware` sets and `get_access_token()` reads (`auth_context.py:10–43`; `AuthenticatedUser(access_token: AccessToken)` in `bearer_auth.py:16–22`); `mcp.server.auth.provider.AccessToken(token, client_id, scopes, expires_at, resource, subject, claims)`, `TokenVerifier.verify_token(token) -> AccessToken | None`; client side: `mcp.client.Client(server | url | transport, *, mode="auto"|"legacy"|<revision>, raise_exceptions, client_info, …)` connects in process to a `Server` object, and `mcp.client.streamable_http.streamable_http_client(url, http_client=httpx2.AsyncClient(...))` accepts a caller-supplied `httpx2.AsyncClient`, so an in-process `httpx2.ASGITransport(app)` drives the real HTTP transport in tests.
- **ASGI on Lambda.** `mangum` **0.22.0** (2026-08-22, `Requires-Python >=3.10`, dependency `typing-extensions`). Read from the wheel: `Mangum.__call__(event, context)` constructs a **new `LifespanCycle` per invocation** when `lifespan` is `auto`/`on` (startup and shutdown around every request), exposes the raw event as `scope["aws.event"]`, and supports HTTP API payload format 2.0 (`requestContext.http.sourceIp`/`userAgent`). Consequence (H3): a module-level app whose lifespan enters `StreamableHTTPSessionManager.run()` fails on the second (warm) invocation; the session managers must be created per invocation.
- **Starlette.** 1.9.0 (installed with the SDK under Python 3.11): `Mount` compiles `path + "/{path:path}"`, so a request to exactly the mount path does not match and `redirect_slashes=True` answers 307 to the trailing-slash URL — a second reason not to mount per-view apps (H2). **Middleware order (review-4 M1):** `Starlette.build_middleware_stack` wraps the app with `for cls, args, kwargs in reversed(middleware): app = cls(app, *args, **kwargs)` (`starlette/applications.py:81` in 1.9.0; the same loop is at `applications.py:92` in 0.52.1 and `:98` in 0.49.3 — re-read 2026-10-18), so the **first** entry of the `middleware=` list is the **outermost** wrapper and the last entry is the innermost, adjacent to the router. Starlette **1.8.0 (2026-10-13) and later require Python ≥3.11; 1.7.0 (2026-09-23) is the last release supporting Python 3.10** (pypi.org/pypi/starlette/1.7.0/json, `Requires-Python >=3.10`, fetched 2026-10-18). `sse-starlette` 3.5.0 requires `starlette>=0.49.1`, satisfied by 1.7.0.
- **botocore retries (review-4 M2; read from the installed botocore 1.42.74, 2026-10-18).** Both retry modes re-send a request after a client-side read timeout: the legacy handler's `GENERAL_CONNECTION_ERROR` list (`botocore/retryhandler.py:34–39`) contains `ReadTimeoutError`, and `data/_retry.json` gives every service without an override `max_attempts: 5`; the standard mode's `TransientRetryableChecker._TRANSIENT_EXCEPTION_CLS` (`botocore/retries/standard.py:305–308`) contains `HTTPClientError`, of which `ReadTimeoutError` is a subclass (`exceptions.py:94/140`). Neither mode knows whether the first attempt's side effect happened. `botocore.config.Config(retries={'total_max_attempts': 1})` is the documented way to make exactly one attempt (`config.py:141–146`, `valid_options = ('max_attempts', 'mode', 'total_max_attempts')` at 432; values below 1 are rejected at 446). `httpx` (0.24.1, the backend's) and `httpx2` (2.14.0, the venv's) perform **no** transport-level retries unless `HTTPTransport(retries=N)` is set — the default is 0.
- **AWS service quotas the stack is sized against (fetched 2026-10-18).** Lambda: "Function environment variables — 4 KB, for all environment variables associated with the function, in aggregate" (docs.aws.amazon.com/lambda/latest/dg/gettingstarted-limits.html; not adjustable). Systems Manager Parameter Store: "Maximum value size — 4 KB (standard) / 8 KB (advanced)" (…/systems-manager/latest/userguide/parameter-store-advanced-parameters.html; advanced parameters are billed per parameter-month). **Prices (review-5 N8; aws.amazon.com/systems-manager/pricing/ and aws.amazon.com/dynamodb/pricing/on-demand/, both fetched 2026-10-19):** Parameter Store "Advanced — $0.05 per advanced parameter per month (prorated hourly …)" and "$0.05 per 10,000 Parameter Store API interactions" for advanced parameters (standard parameters and standard-throughput interactions: no additional charge); DynamoDB on-demand `PayPerRequest Write Request Units` in US East (N. Virginia) = **$0.000000625 per WRU, i.e. US$0.625 per million writes** (read from the page's price feed `b0.p.awsstatic.com/pricing/2.0/meteredUnitMaps/dynamodb/USD/current/dynamodb.json`, `regions["US East (N. Virginia)"]["PayPerRequest Write Request Units"].price`, 2026-10-19 — the page itself renders from that feed; the revision-5 figure of US$1.25 was the pre-November-2024 list price). IAM: "Role policy size can't exceed 10,240 characters" for the aggregate of a role's inline policies, white space not counted (…/IAM/latest/UserGuide/reference_iam-quotas.html). CloudFormation cross-stack references: "You must remove all the imports that reference exported output values before you can delete or modify the exported output values" (…/AWSCloudFormation/latest/UserGuide/using-cfn-stack-exports.html) — a producer stack's update fails while a consumer stack still imports a value whose content would change. `AWS::Lambda::LayerVersion` has no mutable properties: every content change creates a new version (new ARN) and deletes the old resource, so its ARN is exactly such a value; function, table, user-pool and API ARNs are stable across updates.
- **Edge lock resolution (performed 2026-10-18 with `uv pip compile mcp==2.3.0`).** Under `--python-version 3.10 --python-platform aarch64-manylinux_2_35` (JP6, Ubuntu 22.04) the resolver picks: `annotated-types==0.8.0 anyio==4.15.1 attrs==26.1.0 cffi==2.1.1 click==8.5.0 cryptography==50.0.2 exceptiongroup==1.3.1 h11==0.16.0 httpcore2==2.14.0 httpx2==2.14.0 idna==3.20 jsonschema==4.26.0 jsonschema-specifications==2025.9.1 mcp==2.3.0 mcp-types==2.3.0 opentelemetry-api==1.45.1 pycparser==3.11 pydantic==2.14.1 pydantic-core==2.50.1 pyjwt==2.15.1 python-multipart==0.0.32 referencing==0.37.0 rpds-py==0.30.0 sse-starlette==3.5.0 starlette==1.7.0 truststore==0.10.5 typing-extensions==4.16.0 typing-inspection==0.4.4 uvicorn==0.54.0`. Under `3.11 / aarch64-manylinux_2_31` (JP5, Ubuntu 20.04, glibc 2.31) the only differences are `rpds-py==2026.9.1`, `starlette==1.9.0`, and no `exceptiongroup`. Wheel availability (pypi.org JSON, 2026-10-18): `rpds-py 0.30.0` ships `cp310`/`cp311` `manylinux_2_17` aarch64 and x86_64 wheels (`Requires-Python >=3.10`; later releases require ≥3.11); `pydantic-core 2.50.1` ships `cp310`/`cp311` `manylinux_2_17` aarch64 and x86_64; `cryptography 50.0.2` ships `cp39-abi3` and `cp311-abi3` `manylinux_2_17` aarch64 and x86_64 wheels; `cffi 2.1.1` ships `cp310`/`cp311` `manylinux_2_17` aarch64 and x86_64; `starlette 1.7.0`, `exceptiongroup 1.3.1` and every other package are pure Python. `manylinux_2_17` wheels install on glibc 2.31 (JP5) and newer.
- **Kiro MCP configuration** (kiro.dev/docs/mcp/configuration/, fetched 2026-10-18): `mcp.json` at `.kiro/settings/mcp.json` (workspace) and `~/.kiro/settings/mcp.json` (user), merged with workspace precedence. Local servers: `command`, `args`, `env` (with `${VAR}` expansion of **explicitly approved** variables — "Mcp Approved Env Vars" in Kiro settings; unapproved variables trigger a security warning), `disabled`, `autoApprove`, `disabledTools`. Remote servers: `url`, `headers` (documented example `"Authorization": "Bearer ${API_TOKEN}"`), `oauth { clientId, clientSecret (CLI only — "client_secret won't work from the IDE"), redirectUri (loopback, e.g. `http://127.0.0.1:8080/oauth/callback`), clientMetadataUrl, oauthScopes }`, `oauthScopes` (fallback; "If you encounter OAuth scope errors, use an empty array"), `disabled`, `autoApprove`, `disabledTools`. The page names Cognito, Auth0 and Okta as supported pre-registered-client authorization servers and states the **exact issuer matching rule** ("Every character must match exactly: scheme, host, port, path … Kiro does not normalize either value, and a mismatch aborts discovery"). It also states (re-read 2026-10-18, review-2 M1): "If you bring your own identity provider, your MCP server must serve authorization-server metadata at the **RFC 8414** well-known URL and validate Bearer tokens against the provider's JWKS. For an issuer without a path such as `https://auth.example.com`, metadata lives at `https://auth.example.com/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server`" — OpenID Connect Discovery is not mentioned anywhere on the page, nor is `WWW-Authenticate`/`resource_metadata`. Its troubleshooting entry "OAuth issuer mismatch" instructs: "Fetch `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource` from your MCP server. Note the URL in `authorization_servers[0]`. Fetch the authorization-server metadata for that issuer URL" — i.e. the **root** PRM form. When no refresh token is available at expiry, "Kiro automatically triggers a new browser-based authentication flow"; when neither `oauthScopes` is set, Kiro requests `openid email profile offline_access`. **Tool-call timeouts (review-7 N7):** the configuration page and the MCP overview, usage and servers pages (kiro.dev/docs/mcp/, …/usage/, …/servers/; all re-fetched 2026-10-19) document **no per-server tool-call timeout key** — the only `TIMEOUT` on any of them is an example `env` value (`"TIMEOUT": "30000"`) for a local server's own environment — so a 130 s `edge_textgen_generate` call is expected to be awaited; the design records this as an observation, not a guarantee.
- **Cognito** (docs.aws.amazon.com/cognito/latest/developerguide/authorization-endpoint.html, fetched 2026-10-18): the authorize endpoint's `resource` parameter is "the identifier of a resource that you want to bind to the access token in the `aud` claim … Amazon Cognito validates that the value is a URL and sets the audience of the resulting access token to the requested resource. **You can request a user pool resource server with an identifier in a URL format, or a URL of your choosing.** Values … must begin with `https://`, `http://localhost`, or a custom URL scheme". So the five view URLs need not be registered resource-server identifiers (resolves N9's second item by documentation). Access tokens carry `sub, iss, client_id, token_use: "access", scope, username, exp, iat, jti, origin_jti` and `aud` only when resource binding was requested (docs …/amazon-cognito-user-pools-using-the-access-token.html); they never carry `email` or custom attributes. Live probe of the portal's pool (`https://cognito-idp.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/<user-pool-id>/.well-known/openid-configuration`, re-run 2026-10-18): `issuer` is exactly `https://cognito-idp.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/<user-pool-id>` (no trailing slash); `authorization_endpoint`, `token_endpoint` and `revocation_endpoint` are `https://dda-portal-<accountId>.auth.us-east-1.amazoncognito.com/oauth2/authorize|token|revoke` (the hosted-UI domain `auth-stack.ts:62–66` creates; `AuthStack` already outputs that origin as `UserPoolDomainUrl`, `auth-stack.ts:164–167`); `jwks_uri` is `<issuer>/.well-known/jwks.json`; `token_endpoint_auth_methods_supported`, `response_types_supported`, `scopes_supported` are present; `code_challenge_methods_supported`, `registration_endpoint` and `client_id_metadata_document_supported` are absent; both RFC 8414 locations (`/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server/<poolId>` under the host and `<issuer>/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server`) return HTTP 400. Cognito's hosted UI does support PKCE — the authorize-endpoint page documents `code_challenge_method` ("Amazon Cognito authentication server supports only S256") and `code_challenge` — so the facade in Decision point 2 advertises (`code_challenge_methods_supported: ["S256"]`) a capability Cognito has but omits from its discovery document. **App-client authentication flows (review-3 H1):** the `CreateUserPoolClient` API reference (docs.aws.amazon.com/cognito-user-identity-pools/latest/APIReference/API_CreateUserPoolClient.html, fetched 2026-10-18) states "If you don't specify a value for `ExplicitAuthFlows`, your app client supports `ALLOW_REFRESH_TOKEN_AUTH`, `ALLOW_USER_SRP_AUTH`, and `ALLOW_CUSTOM_AUTH`" — so an app client whose template omits the property accepts `InitiateAuth`/`USER_SRP_AUTH` with a username and password, a sign-in that goes through neither the hosted UI nor PKCE. The installed `aws-cdk-lib` 2.270.0 `UserPoolClient.configureAuthFlows` (`aws-cognito/lib/user-pool-client.js`) returns `undefined` — omitting the property — when `authFlows` is absent **or an empty object** (`Object.keys(props.authFlows).length === 0`), and emits `["ALLOW_REFRESH_TOKEN_AUTH"]` alone when any flag is present but false (e.g. `{ userSrp: false }`), because the refresh flow is appended unless `refreshTokenRotationGracePeriod` is set. **Callback URLs (review-3 N9):** the app-client settings page (docs.aws.amazon.com/cognito/latest/developerguide/user-pool-settings-client-apps.html, fetched 2026-10-18) states "Amazon Cognito requires HTTPS over HTTP except for `http://localhost` for testing purposes only" and that callback URLs are matched as pre-registered absolute URIs — so a plain-HTTP loopback redirect must use the literal host `localhost` (Kiro's documentation example `http://127.0.0.1:8080/oauth/callback` cannot be registered on a Cognito client), and the client's `redirectUri` must equal the registered string character for character.
- **API Gateway.** REST APIs with a `COGNITO_USER_POOLS` authorizer treat the token as an ID token when no OAuth scopes are configured on the method (docs.aws.amazon.com/apigateway/latest/developerguide/apigateway-enable-cognito-user-pool.html) — so the existing portal methods accept only ID tokens and an MCP access token can never be replayed against them. HTTP APIs have a `$default` stage with no path prefix; **HTTP APIs cannot customize gateway responses**, so a gateway-level JWT authorizer could never emit an RFC 9728 challenge of its own (one of the reasons H4 removed it; with the remapping fact above, no gateway path emits the header to clients either way). Throttling: stage-level `DefaultRouteSettings` and per-route `RouteSettings` on `AWS::ApiGatewayV2::Stage`.
- **Lambda runtime.** `_X_AMZN_TRACE_ID` is a reserved environment variable that "changes with each invocation" (docs.aws.amazon.com/lambda/latest/dg/configuration-envvars.html, "Reserved environment variables", fetched 2026-10-18). This design uses it to bind the shared layer's request audit context to exactly one invocation (D25, H6).

### Key research findings (codebase, read on branch `remediation`; line numbers as of HEAD `c3bf88c1`, 2026-10-19)

*Implementation verified against `ed7f78ab` (2026-10-22); line numbers remain as of `c3bf88c1` unless a row says otherwise.*

*HEAD moved from `a1615269` (the commit the fifth review checked) to `c3bf88c1` while this revision was being written (the `integration/all-specs` merge of 2026-10-19 01:31 UTC). The merge touched eight files this design cites by line — `src/backend/app.py`, `endpoints/workflow.py`, `endpoints/download_file.py`, `workflow_engine/api.py`, `gstreamer/pipeline_builder.py`, `Dockerfile.x86_64_nvidia`, `edge-cv-portal/backend/functions/deployments.py`, `build-custom.sh` — and every citation into them below was re-read at the new HEAD; `utils/auth.py`, `local_auth/config.py`, `shared_utils.py` (all cited members unmoved), the four other backend Dockerfiles, `docker-compose.yaml`, `requirements.txt`, the portal `lib/*.ts`, `bin/app.ts`, the deploy scripts and the cited test files did not change. The recipes gained a `ModelLoadSelfHealEnabled` default key, which does not affect the two keys this design adds. The historical response tables (revisions 2–5) keep the line numbers that were true when they were written.*

- **Portal token types.** `edge-cv-portal/frontend/src/services/api.ts` sends the Cognito **ID token** (`localStorage 'idToken'`, refreshed with Amplify `fetchAuthSession({forceRefresh: true})`); every API method in `edge-cv-portal/infrastructure/lib/api-gateway-stack.ts` (`EdgeCVPortalAPI`, stage `v1`, a `cdk.NestedStack` created inside `ComputeStack` at `compute-stack.ts:3807`) and in the nested route stacks (`user-admin-api-stack.ts`, `quick-setup-api-stack.ts`, `camera-registry-api-stack.ts`, `node-designer-api-stack.ts`, `dda-labeling-api-stack.ts`, `workflow-manager-gaps-api-stack.ts`, `workflow-tuning-api-stack.ts`, `build-fleet-stack.ts`) uses `apigateway.CognitoUserPoolsAuthorizer` with no `authorizationScopes`. `api-core-stack.ts`, `api-model-stack.ts` and `api-data-stack.ts` exist under `lib/` but are **instantiated by no stack in `bin/app.ts`**; `api-model-stack.ts` is exercised only by `infrastructure/test/detector-export-infra.test.ts` (imports `ApiModelStack` at line 28 and synthesizes it at 103/330 as a "duplicate route table that no stack instantiates"), and the other two are referenced by no `.ts` source (review-2 N3). This design ignores all three. The custom Lambda authorizer `backend/functions/jwt_authorizer.py` (RS256/JWKS, `ALLOWED_AUDIENCES`, `ISSUER_WHITELIST`, `COGNITO_ISSUER`, `token_use == 'id'`) is defined in `compute-stack.ts` (`JwtAuthorizerHandler`) but attached to no method; this design leaves it untouched (F7).
- **User pool** (`infrastructure/lib/auth-stack.ts`): pool `dda-portal-users`, hosted-UI domain `dda-portal-${ACCOUNT_ID}`, client `dda-portal-client` with `authFlows: { userSrp: true }`, `oAuth.flows.authorizationCodeGrant`, scopes `email openid profile`, `generateSecret: false`, callback `http://localhost:3000`, custom attributes `custom:role`/`custom:groups`; token validities are not overridden (Cognito defaults 1 h / 1 h / 30 d). `bin/app.ts` instantiates `AuthStack`, `StorageStack`, `TestRunnerStack`, `ComputeStack` (line 149), `NodeDesignerStack` (193), `BuildFleetStack` (219), `SyntheticDataStack` (236), `FrontendStack` (254) and passes `userPool` into `ComputeStack` and `NodeDesignerStack`; `deploy-infrastructure.sh` runs `npx cdk deploy --all` with `-c portalRegistryEnforced=...`. **CDK caveat:** `userPool.addResourceServer(...)` creates the `UserPoolResourceServer` as a child of the *pool* construct, i.e. inside `AuthStack`; using it from the MCP stack with an identifier derived from the MCP API would create an AuthStack→McpStack reference and a cross-stack cycle. The MCP stack therefore instantiates `new cognito.UserPoolResourceServer(this, …, { userPool })` and `new cognito.UserPoolClient(this, …, { userPool })` in its own scope (`aws-cognito/lib/user-pool-resource-server.d.ts` in the installed `aws-cdk-lib`). `AuthStack` declares `public readonly userPoolDomain?: cognito.UserPoolDomain` — **optional in the type** (`auth-stack.ts:15`) although the constructor always assigns it at 63–66 (prefix `dda-portal-${ACCOUNT_ID}`; review-3 N1); the MCP stack's prop is the non-optional `userPoolDomain: cognito.UserPoolDomain`, and `bin/app.ts` passes `authStack.userPoolDomain!` with a comment citing the unconditional assignment, so the one call site is where the non-null assertion lives; the stack derives the hosted-UI origin exactly as `auth-stack.ts:164` does (`https://${domain.domainName}.auth.${REGION}.amazoncognito.com`) for the RFC 8414 facade. The existing web client `dda-portal-client` sets `authFlows: { userSrp: true }`; the MCP client must **not** copy `authFlows: {}` from a "no flows" intuition — CDK omits `ExplicitAuthFlows` for an empty object and Cognito then defaults to SRP + custom + refresh (Research baseline; review-3 H1).
- **RBAC and identity.** `shared_utils.get_user_from_event` reads `requestContext.authorizer.claims` (`sub`, `cognito:username` or `username`, `email`, `custom:role` as Claimed_Role; `_display_identity` substitutes the username when there is no email). `RBACManager.get_user_role` resolves from the Portal_Identity_Registry (`USER_ROLES_TABLE`, `_lookup_identity` — GetItem) when `PORTAL_REGISTRY_ENFORCED` is on and raises `RegistryUnavailable` on lookup failure; with it off `_legacy_role` (`shared_utils.py:1093–1142`) resolves in this order: (1) `user_info['role'] == 'PortalAdmin'` → PortalAdmin; (2) a `global` registry row **only if its role is `PortalAdmin`**; (3) the Use_Case row **only when `usecase_id != 'global'`**; (4) the JWT `custom:role`; else Viewer. **Consequence for MCP (review-3 M2):** access tokens carry no `custom:role`, so the MCP user dict always says `'Viewer'`; at the `'global'` scope used by every `G`-scoped tool, steps 2–4 therefore give PortalAdmin (global row) or Viewer — a `global` DataLabeler/DataScientist/UseCaseAdmin row is ignored by step 2. The web UI does not have this gap because its ID token carries `custom:role` (step 4), which is exactly how the `dda_labeling.py` labeler routes (`@rbac_check([LABELING_TASKS_SELF], allow_global=True)`, module comment at 2165–2171: "through the Cognito custom:role claim (shared_utils role resolution step 4)") and the build routes (`rbac_middleware.require_builds_read/submit/cancel` = `rbac_check(..., allow_global=True)`, 511–530) admit non-PortalAdmins today. Under enforcement `_lookup_identity` (740–800) requires an **enabled `global` row** for every principal and lets an enabled Use_Case row override it for that Use_Case; the User Manager writes the global row at account creation (`user_admin.py:_put_registry_identity`, 339–356) and the backfill populates older accounts. The full tool-level consequence and the owner decision are in Decision point 5. `RBACManager.get_accessible_usecases(user_id, user_info)` performs a **`query`** on `USER_ROLES_TABLE` (non-admins) or a **`scan`** on `USECASES_TABLE` (PortalAdmin) and returns `[]` on any exception (`shared_utils.py:1280–1313`). `rbac_middleware.rbac_check`/`super_user_only` write `unauthorized_access` (denied) and `authorization_unavailable` (failure) audit entries with `identity=attribution_from(event, user, usecase_id=...)` (`rbac_middleware.py:132–286`); many older handlers call `shared_utils.check_user_access(user_id, usecase_id, required_role)` / `is_super_user(user_id)` and answer a bare 403 without an audit entry (`devices.py`, `models.py`, `deployments.py`, `training.py`, `data_management.py`). `models.py:441` checks `'DataScientist'`; `models.py:522` checks `'Admin'`, which is not a `Role` value, so `check_user_access` returns False for every caller (Backlog 1).
- **Audit schema and attribution.** `shared_utils.log_audit_event(user_id, action, resource_type, resource_id, result, details, identity=None, event=None)` writes `event_id = f"{user_id}_{timestamp}"`, `timestamp`, `user_id`, `action`, `resource_type`, `resource_id`, `result`, `details`, `ttl` (90 days) and `item.update(_attribution_attributes(identity, event))`; `record_audit_event_strict` (raising; `event_id` carries a uuid suffix) and `finalize_audit_event` (Query + UpdateItem) implement audit-before-effect; `sanitize_audit_details` drops `password`/`verifier`/`hash`/`temp*` keys. `ATTRIBUTION_FIELDS = ('username','email','source_ip','user_agent','identity_source')`; `attribution_from(event, user, usecase_id, identity_source)` is the one reader of `requestContext.identity.sourceIp/userAgent` and returns **exactly the five fields** — `tests/test_portal_registry_units.py::TestAttributionFromClaims` asserts dict equality with exactly those five keys for ordinary events, so the marker fields must be added **only** for MCP-originated events. `_attribution_attributes(identity, event, usecase_id)` records `'unknown'` for all five when neither `identity` nor `event` is supplied. **Audit call-site survey (re-counted 2026-10-18 with an AST walk over `backend/functions/*.py`, counting `log_audit_event`/`record_audit_event_strict` calls that pass an `identity=` or `event=` keyword; review-2 N2):** of the ~45 handler modules in the catalogs, only `user_admin.py` (11/11 calls), `build_jobs.py` (5/5), parts of `devices.py` (3/11), `usecases.py` (5/24) and `model_converter.py` (1/4) pass `identity=`/`event=`; `data_management.py` (0/4), `training.py` (0/3), `pre_labeled_datasets.py` (0/1), `deployments.py` (6 calls), `workflows.py` (6), `models.py` (2), `dda_labeling.py` (16), `plugin_records.py` (10), `plugin_importer.py` (8), `custom_node_types.py` (6), `packaging.py` (6), `device_logs.py` (4), `shared_components.py` (4), `device_registrations.py` (4), `synthetic_data.py` (4), `workflow_testing.py` (4), `camera_registry.py` (3), `model_import.py` (3), and others pass **neither** — including read-only success paths such as `devices.py:list_devices`/`get_device`. Unmarked handler entries are therefore the rule, not the exception (H6), and every one of these modules calls `get_user_from_event(event)` (at the top of `handler()` in `devices.py:93`, `deployments.py:884`, `workflows.py:846`, `plugin_records.py:1824`, `components.py:201`, `device_logs.py:57`, `training.py:1692`; per route function in `models.py`, `dda_labeling.py`, `training.py`) before writing audit entries — the hook point D25 relies on. `functions/audit_logs.py:get_audit_logs` serves `GET /audit-logs` with a DynamoDB `scan`, `limit` (default 100, max 500), `next_token` (base64 `ExclusiveStartKey`), filters `usecase_id`/`action`/`user_id`/`start_time`/`end_time`, and a static `get_available_actions()` list consumed by `frontend/src/pages/AuditLogs.tsx`.
- **CDK layout limits.** The latest synthesized `EdgeCVPortalComputeStack.template.json` (`infrastructure/cdk.out.bak-20260929T125137Z`, measured 2026-10-18) is **652,017 bytes with 248 resources and 10 outputs** — about 350 KB of headroom under CloudFormation's 1 MB template limit; the September 2026 synths peaked at 901,692 bytes / 274 resources (`cdk.out.bak-20260907T135706Z`), which is when `suppressTemplateIndentation: true` was added to `bin/app.ts:154`, so the pressure is historical rather than current (review-4 N7). The cross-stack exports this design adds (one `Output`/`Export` per handler ARN the MCP stack imports: 32 on `ComputeStack` — 31 direct plus the nested `ApiGatewayStack`'s component-configuration handler surfaced through it — and 11 across `NodeDesignerStack`, `BuildFleetStack` and `SyntheticDataStack`; 43 in all) cost roughly 10 KB in total and stay far inside both the size limit and the 200-outputs-per-template limit. Its `ApiGatewayStack` nested stack **sat at the 500-resource limit before the ARN-imported integrations removed the per-method `AWS::Lambda::Permission` resources** (`api-gateway-stack.ts:155–160` records the history; the latest synth, `EdgeCVPortalComputeStackApiGatewayD6C385F5.nested.template.json` in `cdk.out.bak-20260929T125137Z`, has **345** resources — review-5 N2); new route groups still import the REST API by id and attach their own nested stack by convention (`UserAdminApiStack` pattern). **Shared-layer pattern across top-level stacks (review-4 H2):** `NodeDesignerStack` (`node-designer-stack.ts:713–714`, `NodeDesignerSharedLayer`), `BuildFleetStack` (`build-fleet-stack.ts:399–400`, `BuildFleetSharedLayer`) and `SyntheticDataStack` (`synthetic-data-stack.ts:175–177`, `SyntheticSharedLayer`, commented "layer matches the ComputeStack's SharedLayer") each build their **own** `lambda.LayerVersion` from `path.join(__dirname, '../../backend/layers/shared')`; `bin/app.ts` passes only `computeStack.api.restApiId`/`restApiRootResourceId` (lines 206–207, 226–227, 247–248) and `computeStack.apiUrl` (257) to other stacks — never a layer — because a `LayerVersion` is replaced (new ARN) on every content change and an imported export cannot change while a consumer stack holds it (Research baseline, "AWS service quotas"). `ComputeStack` exposes publicly only `api`, `apiUrl`, and `ddaLabelingHandler` (`compute-stack.ts:122–130`); its handler constructs are `DeviceRegistrationsHandler, QuickSetupHandler, UseCasesHandler, DevicesHandler, DeviceLogsHandler, DeviceLogsAnalyzerHandler, DeploymentsHandler, AuthHandler, UserManagementHandler, JwtAuthorizerHandler, DatasetsHandler, CapturesHandler, PreLabeledDatasetsHandler, LabelingHandler, LabelingMonitorHandler, TrainingHandler, CompilationHandler, PackagingHandler, GreengrassPublishHandler, ComponentsHandler, DataManagementHandler, SharedComponentsHandler, DataAccountsHandler, ModelImportHandler, ModelConverterHandler, ModelsHandler, AuditLogsHandler, CameraSyncHandler, CameraRegistryHandler, AccountSyncHandler, UserAdminHandler, CameraVideoPinHandler, DdaLabelingHandler, WorkflowsHandler, WorkflowValidationHandler, WorkflowPackagingHandler, WorkflowGeneratorHandler, WorkflowTestingHandler, WorkflowTuningHandler, TrainingEventsHandler, CompilationEventsHandler`. **`ComponentConfigurationHandler` is not among them: it is created inside the `ApiGatewayStack` nested stack at `api-gateway-stack.ts:711`** (`component_configuration.handler`, 60 s timeout) and `ApiGatewayStack` exposes only `api`, `apiUrl` and four resource ids (M1). `NodeDesignerStack` exposes its handlers (`pluginRecordsHandler`, `customNodeTypesHandler`, …), `BuildFleetStack` exposes `buildJobsHandler`, `buildFleetHandler`, `buildConfigHandler`, `SyntheticDataStack` exposes `syntheticDataHandler`. The Lambda role factory `createLambdaRole` grants broad rights; `quick_setup.py` is the precedent for a "minimal, dedicated role — NOT createLambdaRole". `layers/jwt/requirements.txt` and `functions/requirements.txt` are **sha256-pinned by the Security_Preservation_Gate** (`test/backend-test/security/baselines/dependency_baseline_out_of_scope.json`) and must not change. **Admin route registration (review-3 M1):** `user-admin-api-stack.ts:103–129` registers exactly `GET /admin/users`, `POST /admin/users/{username}/password`, `POST …/forgot-password`, `PUT …/role`, `GET /admin/edge-sync/devices`, `POST /admin/edge-sync/devices/{deviceId}`; there is **no** `POST /admin/users`, `POST …/disable`, `POST …/enable` or `DELETE /admin/users/{username}` method anywhere under `lib/`, although `user_admin.py` implements all four (`create_account` 691; `handler()` 581–586) and `frontend/src/services/api.ts` already calls them (`createAdminUser` 1555–1564, disable 1620, enable 1635) — a pre-existing portal defect outside this spec's scope; the catalog therefore wraps none of them (Backlog 2) and a mechanical test pins every catalog route to a synthesized method (Testing Strategy). `infrastructure/test/camera-registry-infra.test.ts:283–305` is the precedent for enumerating `METHOD /path` strings from a synthesized template (`findResources('AWS::ApiGateway::Method')` joined to `AWS::ApiGateway::Resource` `PathPart`s, imported parents resolved by a known prefix); the nested API stacks import the REST root (`RestApi.fromRestApiAttributes(..., rootResourceId)`) and, where they attach below it, `/devices/{id}` (`camera-registry-api-stack.ts:66–70`), `/labeling/{id}` (`dda-labeling-api-stack.ts:77–85`), `/workflows/generate` and `/workflows/{id}` (`workflow-manager-gaps-api-stack.ts:54–70`). CDK API checked in the installed `node_modules/aws-cdk-lib` **2.270.0** (`package.json` declares `^2.273.0`; `npm install` at implementation time will resolve a newer 2.x — the APIs below are stable L2/L1 surface present in both, review-2 N7): `aws-apigatewayv2.HttpApi` (`apiEndpoint`, `createDefaultStage`, `defaultStage`), `HttpStage` (`StageOptions.throttle`, `stageName: '$default'`), `HttpNoneAuthorizer`, `aws-apigatewayv2-integrations.HttpLambdaIntegration` (`payloadFormatVersion: PayloadFormatVersion.VERSION_2_0`), `CfnStage.RouteSettings` (per-route settings are not on the L2 — N6).
- **Edge backend.** `src/backend/app.py` is FastAPI served by uvicorn 0.23.2 on `0.0.0.0:5000` (plaintext) or `0.0.0.0:5443` with `constants.DDA_LOCAL_SERVER_SSL_CERT/KEY` when `utils.is_authorization_enabled_on_station()` is true (`app.py:385–397`, `# nosec B104` comments), with `CorrelationIdMiddleware` (142) and `CORSMiddleware` (144); routers are included at lines 167–197 (`download_file.unauthenticated_router` at 181, `local_auth.router` at 187, `health.router` at 192, `text_generation.router` only `if VLLM_AVAILABLE`, 196). `__main__` (410) runs `start_vllm_runtime()`/`health.set_vllm_server()` (444–445) and stops `vllm_server` in the `finally:` block that starts at line 468 (`vllm_server.stop()` at 473; re-read 2026-10-19 at HEAD `c3bf88c1`). Routers from `endpoints/route/access_log_router.py:get_api_router()` (line 111) attach `Depends(authorize_request)` and the `AccessLogRoute` (structured `api.access` log with `request_id` from `asgi_correlation_id`). `utils/auth.py:authorize_credential(credential) -> None` implements the decision matrix; `_validate_local_session_token(credential) -> bool` discards the `Account` that `local_auth.session_tokens.validate_token(secret, token, now, cache)` returns; `validate_token(token)` (Existing_Token_Auth) returns `None` and discards the introspection response (M3). `authorize_request` is `Depends(optional_oauth2_scheme)` — header only. **`endpoints/download_file.py` is different:** `unauthenticated_router = APIRouter()` (line 56; no `AccessLogRoute`, no `authorize_request`), every route takes `token: str = None` and calls `validate_token_in_query_param(token)` → `authorize_credential(token)` — the credential is read from the `token` **query parameter**, an `Authorization` header is ignored, and no `api.access` record is produced (H5). Its routes: `/snapshotfile/{fileName}`, `/workflows/{workflowId}/capture-details/{captureId}/input-image|output-image`, `/workflows/executions/{execution_id}/output-image|overlay-image|node-image`, `GET /workflows/{workflow_id}/results/export`. Uvicorn access logging is disabled (`log_config="dda_logging/uvicorn_disable_logging.json"`), so a loopback URL carrying `?token=` is not logged by the server.
- **Edge route effects and return shapes (bodies read 2026-10-19; review-5 H1, M1, M2, N3, N7).** `GET /snapshot` (`endpoints/system.py:288–291`) returns `{"archivePath": Snapshotter.take_snapshot(stationName)}`; `snapshot/Snapshotter.py:45–76` builds `/aws_dda/system/snapshot-<station>-<ts>.tar` and runs `subprocess.check_output(["sh", "/snapshot/snapshot.sh", path])` — the script (`snapshot/snapshot.sh`) first `rm`s every `/aws_dda/system/snapshot-*.tar*`, then tars Greengrass logs, work directories, config and the LocalServer database into a new `.tar.gz` — and returns the string `"snapshotfile/<file>.tar.gz"` (the download-route path, validated by `SNAPSHOT_FILE_PATTERN`, `utils/constants.py:110`): a GET that **changes device state** (one archive replaced per call, a shell script run) and whose body names a file. `workflow.py:list_images` (423–477) returns `query.get_inference_results(output_path)` whose items are built by `utils/inference_results_utils.py:save_image_object` (69–162) with `imageDataFilePath`, `inputImageFilePath`, `inferenceFilePath` (device paths), **`image` = the base64 of the output image** (`utils.get_image_bytes_from_file`, `utils/utils.py:208–210`) and no `captureId` (`read_image_from_jsonl` is called without one at 225; the capture id is the file name of `inferenceFilePath` minus `.jsonl`, which `list_images` itself derives at 470–471). `image_source.py:preview_image` (198–244) returns `GetPreviewImageResponse(image: Optional[str] /* base64 */, imageFileName: Optional[str])` (97–108; folder sources return the file name only, 211; camera sources return `gst_pipeline_executor.execute_image_source_pipeline(...)`'s `{"image": <base64>, "captureLocation": <path>}` — `gst_pipeline_executor.py:65–93` — which FastAPI filters through the declared return model, so `captureLocation` never leaves the route); a preview writes one fixed-name scratch frame `/aws_dda/image-capture/preview/default_file_prefix-<imageSourceId>.jpg` that every call overwrites (`pipeline_builder.py:380–382`, `constants.py:33/37/38`) — a cache slot, not device state. `test_stream_connection` (163–176) returns `StreamConnectionTestResponse.image` as base64 (152–158). `workflow_engine/api.py:get_workflow_execution_overlay` (465–481) returns `run_artifacts.read_mask_overlay(...)` = `{"maskImage": <base64 | None>, "maskBackground": …}`; `registration_to_dict` (109–122) carries `artifactPath`; `execution_to_dict` (125–138) carries `outputDir`; `inference_result.py:save_capture_ids_for_inference_results_download` (166–188) writes `capture-id-path-<ts>` under `/aws_dda/system` and returns it as `captureIdPath`. The five `download_file.py` image routes return `FileResponse(..., media_type="image/jpg" | "image/jpeg")` (148/169/204/242/290; `image/jpg` is a non-registered type the shaping normalizes to `image/jpeg`). `api.py:trigger_workflow(registration_id, db)` (253) and `workflow.py:create_workflow(db)` (513–520) read **no body** — the latter creates an empty workflow with a random id, and the definition is applied by `PATCH /workflows/{workflowId}` → `ModifyWorkflowRequest(name, description, inputConfigurations, imageSources, featureConfigurations, outputConfigurations)` (531–541). `AddImageSourceRequest.location` is "the folder path, or the Stream_URL of an RTSP/RTMP camera" (`image_source.py:311–317`). `endpoints/system.py:get_local_server_component_version()` (146–175) is the existing reader of the running LocalServer component's version (from `LOCAL_SERVER_COMPONENT_DECOMPRESSED_PATH`, IPC fallback), already used by the system-health route (220).
- **Edge route arguments, bounds and further base64 carriers (bodies re-read 2026-10-19 at HEAD `c3bf88c1`; review-6 M1–M5, N1, N3, N4, N6).** *Bounds:* `workflow.py:list_images` (423–432) runs `MaxNumberOfResultsValidator` (409–418), which raises HTTP 400 for any `maxResults` ≤ 0 **or > 2** — the route serves at most two items per call (route default 2) and pages with `startingPoint`. `inference_result.py:list_inference_results` (81–117) paginates with `PageParams` (`resources/pagination/base_paginator.py:32–35`: `page ≥ 1`, `size ∈ [1, 100]`, default 12) and returns `PagedResponseSchema {total, page, size, results}` (39–57); it also filters on `prediction` (`Normal`/`Anomaly`, `constants.py:48–49`), `downloaded`, `textNoteFilter` (≤ `DB_TEXT_NOTE_MAX_LENGTH` = 50, `constants.py:65`), `humanClassificationProvided`, `humanReviewRequired`, `captureType` (`Inference`/`Capture`, 55–56). `api.py:list_registration_executions` (207–230) clamps `limit` to 1..50 (default 10) and takes `notable: bool`. *Required/defaulted bodies:* `inference_result.py:reset_inference_result_summary_start_time` (75–78) takes `ResetInferenceResultSummaryTimeRequest(resetTime: Optional[int] = None)` (72–73) and writes it straight into `summaryStartTime` (`dao/sqlite_db/workflow_metadata_dao.py:45–50`); the summary route then filters `inferenceCreationTime >= summaryStartTime` (`inference_result_dao.py:99–118`), so a `None` reset makes the summary read zero until the next reset — the HMI always sends `resetTime = now` in epoch seconds (`src/frontend/src/api/InferenceResultAPI.ts:101–106`). `workflow.py:run_inference_for_stream` (244–352) takes `RunWorkflowRequest` (141–147: `captureImageCount` 1–100, `captureTimeInterval` 1–120 s, `capturePrefix`, `returnImageString` default **True**, `returnPartialResultsEarly`), deletes the base64 `image` only when `returnImageString` is false (303–304), returns the capture-task id as `{"captureTaskId"}` for model-less workflows (345) and `captureId` with the inference result otherwise (293/295). `image_source.py:capture` (254–280) takes `CaptureImageRequest(filePrefix: str = None)` (246–247) and declares `CaptureImageResponse(image: str)` (250–251), so FastAPI's response filtering returns the base64 frame **only** — `execute_image_source_pipeline`'s `captureLocation` (`gst_pipeline_executor.py:94`) never leaves the route and no identifier of the written file (`<imageCapturePath>/<prefix>-<unix ms>.jpg`, `pipeline_builder.py:384–390`) exists in the response. *Pin routes:* `static_image_camera.py:pin_static_image` (175–223) and `static_video_camera.py:pin_static_video` (101–) accept exactly two shapes — multipart `file` (read through `_read_body_capped`, caps `MAX_PIN_FILE_BYTES` = 50 MiB `utils/static_image_camera.py:66` / `MAX_PIN_VIDEO_BYTES` = 100 MiB `utils/video_loop.py:66`, file name from the part's `filename`, 152) or JSON `{"capturedImagePath": <device path under a captures root>}` (202–220); `GET /static-image-camera/pin` returns `{pinned, cameraId, metadata}` (`utils/static_image_camera.py:184–192`). *More base64 carriers:* `GET /system/station` (`system.py:305–309`) sets `logoImage = utils.get_station_logo()` = `f"data:image/{type};base64,…"` of the first file under `/aws_dda/assets/logo` (`utils/utils.py:212–219`, `constants.py:31`; no size bound); `list_images` items carry `inferenceResult.mask_image` for segmentation models (`inference_results_utils.py:105/123`; `InferenceResultModel.mask_image`, `data_models/common.py:137`); `GET /workflows/{id}/results` items (`InferenceResultHistoryModel.maskImage`, `common.py:143`, stored at `inference_results_utils.py:346`, column `dao/sqlite_db/models.py:135`) and `GET …/results/{capture_id}` (the full ORM row, `inference_result_accessor.py:139–140` → `db.get(InferenceResult, …)`) carry `maskImage`. *Unbounded embed:* `api.py:get_workflow_registration` (175–194) appends **every** `WorkflowExecution` of the registration (`.all()`, 181–187); only continuous runs are ever evicted (`workflow_engine/run_retention.py:40–41`: "Every other run … is never deleted"). *Accepted read-only side effects (review-6 N1):* `stream_ingest/connection_test.py:run_connection_test` (55–91) acquires a lease on the camera's ingest session (62) and may `session.restart(...)` a backing-off session (72–75), released in `finally` (91); `feature_config.py:list_feature_configs` (89–124) and `get_gpu_status` (127–152) call `__get_triton_instance()` (192 → `TritonEdgeClient.get_instance`, `dda_triton/triton_edge_client.py:58–70`, which constructs the client and `create_server` on first use — only when `triton_repo_has_models()`) and publish `model_status_shadow.report(...)` (121, 150) — all the HMI's own polling effects. *Export:* `POST /workflows/{id}/results/export` (`inference_result.py:164–188`) writes `/aws_dda/system/capture-id-path-<ts>` and returns it; its only consumer is `download_file.py:get_inference_result_data_for_retraining` (311–345), the query-token zip route, which requires the **full** path (`CAPTURED_IDS_PATH_PATTERN`, `constants.py:109`, realpath under `DDA_SYSTEM_FOLDER`). *Timeouts:* `text_generation.py:DEFAULT_TEXT_GEN_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 120.0` (290–292) bounds one non-streaming generate call, retries included; `generate_text` (559–564) takes a free `Dict` body — `body: Dict[str, Any] = Body(default={})`, no request model — whose fields are read by module helpers, not by the signature (review-7 M2): `normalize_generation_request` reads `body.get("prompt")` (179), `max_tokens`/`temperature`/`top_p` over `GENERATION_DEFAULTS` (71; `max_tokens` must be 1..the model's `max_model_len`, 193–203) and `system_prompt` (256), and `_validate_image_field(body, "image", findings)` (236; the helper is 85–132) and `_validate_image_field(body, "reference_image", …)` (237–238) read the two image fields by **string name**. *Stream sources (review-7 M1):* `AddImageSourceRequest.location` (`image_source.py:317`, under the comment "the folder path, or the Stream_URL of an RTSP/RTMP camera", 316) and `EditImageSourceRequest.location` (344) are the only request fields that carry a Stream_URL. *Result edits (review-7 N4):* `inference_result.py:EditInferenceResult(captureId: str, flagForReview?, downloaded?, humanClassification? ∈ {Normal, Anomaly}, textNote? ≤ DB_TEXT_NOTE_MAX_LENGTH)` (143–148), `UpdateInferenceResultsRequest(inferenceResults: List[EditInferenceResult])` (150–151), `update_inference_results` (153–155). *Run log size (review-7 N6):* `api.py:get_workflow_execution_log` (484–501) returns a `PlainTextResponse` of `run_artifacts.read_run_log` (366–389) — the rolled backup then the live file, each capped at `run_log.DEFAULT_MAX_BYTES` = 512 KiB with `DEFAULT_BACKUP_COUNT` = 1 (`run_log.py:62–63`), so a Run_Log body can reach ≈ 1 MiB. *Rescan:* `aravis_functions.rescan_cameras()` (142–) calls `Aravis.shutdown()` to tear down the cached USB/GenICam context and re-enumerates — idempotent in result, not in effect. *Portal:* `captures.py:list_captures` (101–) requires `usecase_id` **and** `prefix` (119–128, 400 otherwise), caps `limit` at 100 (123), and Backlog 18 adds its Use_Case membership check; `datasets.py` (same Backlog 18 fix) routes by path (44–51): `GET /datasets` → `list_datasets` (`prefix` optional, 112–116), `GET /datasets/preview` → `get_image_preview` (`prefix` required, 367–377; `offset ≥ 0`, `limit` 1–50 default 8, `extensions`), `POST /datasets/count` → `count_images` (`prefix` required, 260–268).
- **Portal route effects and arguments (bodies read 2026-10-19 at HEAD `c3bf88c1`; review-7 H1, M3).** *Validation writes state:* `workflow_validation.py:validate_workflow` (358–489, reached from `handler()` at 518–520 on `resource == '/workflows/{id}/validate'`) requires **`Permission.WORKFLOW_EDIT`** through `authorize_workflow_access` (373; a principal with `WORKFLOW_READ` only receives the 403 of `forbidden_response`, 137–156, which writes an `unauthorized_access` audit entry — `WORKFLOW_EDIT` is held by DataScientist, UseCaseAdmin and PortalAdmin, `shared_utils.py:898/949`, while `WORKFLOW_READ` is also held by Viewer and Operator, 839/865), reads **only `body.get('version')`** (381; absent = `latest_version`) and validates the **stored** definition of that version, then **`s3.put_object`** stores the findings document (456–466) and **`dynamodb.Table(WORKFLOW_VERSIONS_TABLE).update_item(… SET validation_status = :vs …)`** records `{status, findings_key, validated_at}` on the version item (469–479) — the value the packaging/deployment guard reads (code comment at 454–455) and that `workflows.py:list_versions` (784–825) and `get_workflow` (446–489) return as `validation_status` (819 / 487). A GET-shaped "validate" that persists state is mutating under Decision point 4's rule (review-7 H1). The other read-only POST routes were re-read and write nothing: `model_import.validate_model` (1133–1206), `model_converter.inspect_model_endpoint` (1379–1485; its helper `_refused_model_source` writes a `'denied'` entry only), `datasets.count_images` (249–316), `pre_labeled_datasets.validate_manifest` (218–261; `_insufficient_permissions` writes `'denied'` only), `device_logs_analyzer.analyze_device_logs` (336–393). *Arguments the handlers actually read (an AST scan of every portal route function a concrete-argument row names, one level of same-module helpers — the review-7 M3 follow-up):* `device_logs_analyzer.py:handler` (306–333) dispatches `POST` with a path `id` to `analyze_device_logs(device_id, user, query_parameters)`, which reads **`usecase_id`** (341, required at 344–345) and **`hours`** (`int(query_params.get('hours', '1'))`, 342) from the query string, parses no body, and checks `is_super_user(...) or check_user_access(user_id, usecase_id)` itself (348); `device_logs.py:62–67` routes the same path the same way. `training.py:transform_manifest` (1667–1872) requires `usecase_id` and `source_manifest_uri` (1699–1700) and reads `output_manifest_uri?`, `task_type?`. `compilation.py:start_compilation_job` (398–744) reads `targets` (list, default `['x86_64-cpu']`, each validated against `COMPILATION_TARGETS`, 421–428) and **`auto_triggered`** (436), which the MCP schema does not offer (Backlog 20). `greengrass_publish.py:publish_component` (691–1369) requires `component_name` and `component_version` (718–724) and reads `friendly_name?`, `targets?`, `skip_fit_check?` (725–726). `data_management.py:create_folder` (449–516) requires `folder_path` (473; an S3 key prefix, `/`-terminated by the handler); `bucket` (472) is defaulted to the Use_Case data bucket (479–480) before the 400 at 482–483. `synthetic_data.py:get_prompt_template` (338–372) requires `object_type` and `defect_type` (341–351); `put_prompt_template` (375–) requires `usecase_id`, `object_type`, `defect_type`, `template_text`. `workflow_packaging.py:package_workflow` (2539–; `WORKFLOW_PACKAGE` at 2550) requires `architectures` (2558–2560) and reads `version?` (2570). `build_jobs.py:list_builds` (501–530) and `get_build_logs` (678–748) read `limit` (clamped to `MAX_PAGE_SIZE` = 200 / `MAX_LOG_EVENTS_LIMIT` = 1000, 116–121) and **`nextToken`** (camel case; `training.py:get_training_logs` reads `nextForwardToken`/`nextBackwardToken` instead, 1341–1342); `list_build_branches` (767–795) requires `repository` (`build_source.normalize_repository_url` rejects empty input, 780–784). `dda_labeling.py:get_admin_review` (3573–3636) reads `limit` (clamped to `REVIEW_PAGE_SIZE_MAX` = 100, 3597–3604) and `next_token`. `models.py:update_model_stage` (396–490), `workflow_generator.py:get_generation_job` (1266–1360) and the node-designer reads take the Use_Case from the stored item, not from the request — `usecase_id` is consumed by the pre-dispatch RBAC check and never forwarded to them (the server-consumed-argument rule, Property 2). Revision 9 re-checked **every** catalog row of both servers against its route on five axes (route and dispatch, classification, permission, arguments, shaping); the revision-8 claim that the remaining concrete-argument rows matched their handlers did not hold, and the rows now carry the corrected cells ("Response to the design review (revision 9)" lists each).
- **Edge configuration reader.** `local_auth/config.py` defines `parse_local_login_enabled` (strict true-value parser, Property 23 of portal-user-manager), `_read_local_login_enabled_via_ipc()` — which fetches the **whole** component configuration through the module-private `_IPC_CONFIG_READER` (one `DefectDetectionConfig(get_ipc_client())` instance, created once per process because an earlier fresh-connection-per-poll pattern leaked connections and tripped the fatal awscrt "Continuation ref count has gone negative" abort) and returns only `CONFIG_KEY = "LocalLoginEnabled"` — and `LocalLoginConfig` (30 s daemon poller, transitions logged once, read failures ⇒ disabled). There is no reusable "read all keys" function today (M4). `utils/server_setup.py:339` (`get_local_login_config().start()`) starts the poller inside `_start_local_login_config_isolated()` (lines 331–345); `_start_user_accounts_sync_isolated()` is the separate function at 317–328 (review-2 N1).
- **Edge dependency reality.** `src/backend/requirements.txt` pins `fastapi==0.109.2`, `uvicorn==0.23.2`, `pydantic==2.6.4`, `httpx==0.24.1`, `jsonschema==4.17.3`, `structlog==22.1.0`, `asgi-correlation-id==4.2.0`. The JP5 build log (`.gdk_build_jp5.log.prev-20260927T214022Z`) confirms those versions in the JP5 image; the JP6 build log (`.gdk_build_jp6.log.prev3`, step #61, the vLLM layer) shows the vLLM install upgrading the JP6 image to `fastapi-0.141.1`, `starlette-1.3.1`, `pydantic-2.13.4`, `httpx-0.28.1`, `jsonschema-4.26.0`, `rpds-py-0.30.0` while `uvicorn` stays 0.23.2. `uvicorn>=0.31.1` is a hard dependency of every `mcp` release and the one floor no image satisfies. `dda_triton/triton_setup.py:create_virtual_env` is the precedent for dependencies baked at image-build time and merely verified at runtime.
- **Edge Dockerfiles (read 2026-10-18).** None of `src/backend/Dockerfile`, `Dockerfile.jp5`, `Dockerfile.jp6`, `Dockerfile.jp7`, `Dockerfile.x86_64_nvidia` has a wholesale `COPY . .`; each copies packages one by one (`COPY local_auth ./local_auth`, `COPY user_accounts_sync ./user_accounts_sync`, `COPY vllm_runtime ./vllm_runtime`, … at `Dockerfile:81–127`, `Dockerfile.jp5:194–235`, `Dockerfile.jp6:406–447`, `Dockerfile.jp7:552–591`, `Dockerfile.x86_64_nvidia:107–149`), so a new package needs its own `COPY` line (H1). Interpreters: the generic `Dockerfile` and `Dockerfile.x86_64_nvidia` **build CPython 3.11.9 from source** (`Python-3.11.9.tgz`, `make install`, `update-alternatives` → `/usr/local/bin/python3.11`); `Dockerfile.jp5` builds `python${PYTHON_VERSION}` (3.11) from source with `--with-ensurepip=install` on focal (glibc 2.31) and bootstraps pip with `get-pip.py`; `Dockerfile.jp6` installs distro `python3.10` + `python3.10-venv` (and a stub `python3.11`); `Dockerfile.jp7` installs distro `python${PYTHON_VERSION}` (3.11) + `-venv` on Ubuntu 24.04. `python3 -m venv` works on every variant: from-source CPython ships the `ensurepip` stdlib module with bundled wheels, and the distro images install the `-venv` package (N3).
- **Edge packaging and gate.** Images are `docker save`d as `flask-app.tar` and `react-webapp.tar` (`build-custom.sh:save_image_tar`), loaded and started by the seven recipe variants (`recipe.yaml`, `recipe-arm64.yaml`, `recipe-arm64-jp5.yaml`, `recipe-arm64-jp6.yaml`, `recipe-arm64-jp7.yaml`, `recipe-amd64.yaml`, `recipe-amd64-nvidia.yaml`), each carrying `LocalLoginEnabled: "false"` in `DefaultConfiguration`; `src/docker-compose.yaml` runs the backend with `network_mode: host` (lines 4/152/224), `privileged: true`, `expose: 5000, 5443`. **Consequence for a loopback child (review-3 M3):** under `network_mode: host` the container shares the host's network namespace, so `127.0.0.1:5790` is the *host's* loopback — reachable by every process on the device and by every other host-network container, not only by the Backend_API process; a loopback bind limits exposure to the device itself, nothing finer. `build-custom.sh` runs an explicit pytest file list in the flask-app container and the preservation suite. The Security_Preservation_Gate tracks: `src/backend/Dockerfile.jp5/.jp6/.jp7` (masked baselines `docker_baseline_backend_Dockerfile.jp{5,6,7}_masked.txt`, `_docker_preservation_support.py:75–80`), `src/backend/Dockerfile` + `src/docker-compose.yaml` + the edgemlsdk/frontend Dockerfiles (`docker_baseline_out_of_scope.json`), `src/backend/requirements.txt` and `station_install/setup_station.sh` (`_dependency_preservation_support.py`), the two portal requirement files (`dependency_baseline_out_of_scope.json`), and `cdk.out` drift (`cdk_out_baseline.json`, `secrets_cdk_out_baseline.json`). **Not tracked:** `Dockerfile.x86_64_nvidia` and the seven recipe variants (no `recipe` reference exists under `test/backend-test/security/preservation/` or `baselines/`, N2) — the steering text mentions recipes, but no baseline exists today.
- **Tests.** Portal: `edge-cv-portal/backend/tests/conftest.py` builds a moto session stack with the real `shared_utils` layer on `sys.path`; `test_jwt_authorizer_verification.py` mints RS256 tokens with `cryptography` and replaces `get_jwks_keys` with an in-memory JWKS; `test_property_audit_attribution.py` is the Hypothesis pattern (`@settings(max_examples=100, deadline=None)`, `Feature: … Property N` tags); `test_portal_registry_units.py` pins `attribution_from`'s exact five-key output. CDK: `infrastructure/test/jwt-authorizer-audience.test.ts` synthesizes `ComputeStack` with `Template.fromStack`; `context-helpers.property.test.ts` is the fast-check pattern (jest 29.7.0, fast-check 3.23.2). Edge: `test/backend-test/local_auth/test_local_auth_endpoints.py` drives routers with FastAPI `TestClient` and monkeypatched `local_login_config`; **two existing test files pin the return values this design changes (review-2 H2):** `test/backend-test/utils/test_auth.py` covers `validate_token`/`validate_remotely` (not the decision matrix) and at lines 60–62 asserts `self.assertIsNone(result)` for `result = auth.validate_token(token="good-token")` against a `_introspection_response` mock whose `.json()` returns `{"active": True}`; `test/backend-test/api-endpoints/test_authorize_request.py` is the **decision-matrix regression suite** (`TestRowOpenAccess`, `TestRowExistingTokenAuthOnly`, `TestRowLocalLoginOnly`, `TestRowBothMechanisms`, `TestExemptPaths`, `TestRouterWiring`, `TestDownloadFileQueryParamTokenPath`, driven through the real `get_api_router()` with `FakeIntrospection`) and at lines 420/424/430 asserts `authorize_credential(None) is None`, `authorize_credential(make_local_token()) is None`, `authorize_credential(VALID_BEARER) is None`. `build-custom.sh:262–267` runs `test/backend-test/utils/test_auth.py` **inside the flask-app container as a build gate** ("A failure fails the build here, before packaging"), and `test/backend-test/security/preservation/test_preservation_secrets_auth_suite.py` (also in that gate, `build-custom.sh:296–298`) pins `test_auth.py`'s **source text** — the six `validate_token(token="…")` fixture literals in order, the six method names, and the literal `self.assertIsNone(result)`. Consequence (§6 `utils/auth.py` row): `validate_token` keeps returning `None` (the introspection dict is surfaced through a new private `_introspect_token`), so `test_auth.py` is not edited at all, and `test_authorize_request.py:424/430` are updated in the same commit as `utils/auth.py`. The build host's default `python3` is **3.9.5** (`/usr/local/bin/python3`), `python3.11` exists only at `~/.local/bin/python3.11`, and `uv`/`uvx` are at `~/.local/bin` (N4). The on-hardware harness (`test/on-hardware/harness/`, stages `test_00_health.py` … `test_40_coexistence.py`) logs in through `harnesslib/client.py:login(username, password)` (line 305) from a credential *reference* (`DDA_HARNESS_CREDENTIALS`, `env:`/`file:`) and redacts `Authorization` everywhere.
- **Docs.** `docs/connect-to-device.md` documents AWS IoT Secure Tunneling to the device; `.kiro/settings/mcp.json` holds three third-party stdio servers (`awslabs.aws-documentation-mcp-server`, `awslabs.cdk-mcp-server`, `aws-diagrams`, launched with `uvx`); `edge-cv-portal/ADMIN_GUIDE.md` has no MCP or `LocalLoginEnabled` material; no `.md` documents the LocalServer configuration keys.

### Design decisions

| # | Decision | Choice | Rationale |
|---|----------|--------|-----------|
| D1 | Portal topology (Decision point 1) | **One deployable** (one Lambda, one HTTP API, one Cognito app client + resource server, one tool registry) exposing the aggregate endpoint `/mcp` and five domain views `/mcp/data`, `/mcp/models`, `/mcp/devices`, `/mcp/workflows`, `/mcp/admin`; each view is a `Server` instance over a domain-filtered subset of the same tool objects, served by its own `StreamableHTTPSessionManager` on an **exact-path `Route`** in one root Starlette app | Separate deployables would multiply auth configuration, audit plumbing, and client logins; six exact routes cost ~40 lines and give users and `disabledTools` a small context footprint. Identical tool objects keep Requirement 14 trivially true. Exact routes (not `Mount`) keep `/mcp` and `/mcp/<view>` canonical without trailing-slash redirects (H2) |
| D2 | Portal hosting and transport (Decision point 2) | **Remote Streamable HTTP only**, Lambda (`PYTHON_3_11`, 512 MB, 29 s — D32) behind a **dedicated API Gateway HTTP API** `dda-portal-mcp` (`$default` stage, no path prefix) in a new top-level stack `EdgeCVPortalMcpStack`; **no gateway authorizer** (`HttpNoneAuthorizer` on every route) — the SDK's `BearerAuthBackend`/`RequireAuthMiddleware` with `CognitoAccessTokenVerifier` inside the Lambda is the single validator; no stdio package in this spec | HTTP API's prefix-free URL makes `https://<id>.execute-api.<region>.amazonaws.com/mcp` the canonical resource and lets RFC 9728 metadata sit at the standard root and path-suffixed `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource[/mcp[/<view>]]` locations, and the RFC 8414 facade at `/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server` — the well-known-URI discovery mechanism the MCP specification allows as an alternative to the `WWW-Authenticate` header, which API Gateway remaps (review-2 H1).
