*Process model.* `mcp_edge/supervisor.py:McpSupervisor` (daemon thread started from `app.py`'s `__main__`, after `health.set_vllm_server(vllm_server)`, inside its own try/except like `_start_user_accounts_sync_isolated`) spawns `/opt/dda/mcp-venv/bin/python -m mcp_edge.child --port 5790 --backend-url http://127.0.0.1:5000|https://127.0.0.1:5443 [--features vllm] [--mutating-enabled] --server-version <v>` with `cwd=BACKEND_ROOT`, `PYTHONPATH=BACKEND_ROOT`, stdout piped to the log relay, stderr to the application log. `BACKEND_ROOT = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(mcp_edge.__file__)))` — the parent of the `mcp_edge` package: `src/backend` on the host and **`/` in the image**, because no backend Dockerfile sets `WORKDIR` and every package is copied to `./<pkg>` relative to `/` (review-2 N6). `PYTHONPATH=/` makes every top-level directory of the image (`/utils`, `/local_auth`, `/dao`, …) importable as a namespace package, so the path alone does not isolate the child; the **import-boundary test** (`test_child_import_boundary.py`, which stubs `utils`, `local_auth` and `dao` to raise and imports every `mcp_edge/child/*` module) is the guard that the child never imports them. `app.__file__` is deliberately not used to derive the root, to avoid a circular import between `app.py` and the supervisor. It restarts a crashed child with exponential backoff (1, 2, 4 … 60 s) and records restart counts for `/mcp/status`; it stops the child on `McpServerEnabled` → false and restarts it when `McpMutatingToolsEnabled` or the feature set changes (the child is stateless). A supervisor failure is logged and never propagates (12.3).

*Transport and exposure.* The child binds `127.0.0.1:5790` with **explicit** `TransportSecuritySettings(enable_dns_rebinding_protection=True, allowed_hosts=['127.0.0.1:5790', 'localhost:5790'], allowed_origins=[])` and `max_request_body_size=MCP_PROXY_MAX_BODY` (1 MiB) passed to `Server.streamable_http_app` — not the SDK's loopback defaults, which would accept `http://127.0.0.1:*`/`http://localhost:*` Origins (review-3 M3). The loopback bind limits exposure to the device: under `network_mode: host` the socket is the host's loopback and any local process can connect (D28), so the child is designed to be safe to reach directly — it validates no credential itself and executes nothing without the Backend_API accepting the caller's credential on the forwarded call. The Backend_API gains `endpoints/mcp_proxy.py` on `get_api_router()` (so `authorize_request` and `AccessLogRoute` apply): `api_route("/mcp", methods=["POST"])` (POST only — FastAPI answers `GET`/`DELETE` with 405 itself, so a legacy client's standalone-SSE `GET` never reaches the child or holds a proxy connection open) rejects **any request carrying an `Origin` header with 403 `invalid_origin`** (no browser may drive the device's MCP endpoint; the header is also absent from the forwarded allowlist, so the child never sees one — M6), applies the exposure rule of D14 (non-loopback peer on a non-TLS listener → 403 `mcp_loopback_only`), returns 503 `mcp_disabled`/`mcp_unavailable` when the child is not running, and otherwise forwards method, body, and the headers `Authorization, Content-Type, Accept, MCP-Protocol-Version, Mcp-Method, Mcp-Name, Mcp-Param-*, Mcp-Session-Id` with `X-Request-ID = asgi_correlation_id.correlation_id.get()` to `http://127.0.0.1:5790/mcp`, relaying status and `Content-Type`. The route is a plain `def` (FastAPI runs it in the threadpool) — and because a synchronous endpoint cannot `await request.body()`, the request body arrives through a tiny **async dependency** `async def _mcp_body(request: Request) -> bytes: return await request.body()` declared as `body: bytes = Depends(_mcp_body)`, which FastAPI awaits on the event loop before calling the sync endpoint (review-2 N5; bodies above `MCP_PROXY_MAX_BODY = 1 MiB` → 413 `payload_too_large`). The endpoint then builds `req = client.build_request('POST', 'http://127.0.0.1:5790/mcp', content=body, headers=forwarded)` and calls `resp = client.send(req, stream=True)` with the backend's existing `httpx` 0.24.1 — so the upstream **status and `Content-Type` are known before the response object is constructed**. For every non-`text/event-stream` upstream response (the only kind the child emits under `json_response=True`) the body is then **buffered** (`resp.read()`, ≤ 4 MiB) and returned as a plain `Response`, so an upstream read timeout anywhere in the body still becomes `504 mcp_timeout` (review-3 N8); only a `text/event-stream` upstream (defensive branch, unreachable with the child's configuration) is relayed with `StreamingResponse(_relay(resp), …)` where `_relay` is the synchronous generator `try: yield from resp.iter_raw() finally: resp.close()`. Starlette runs both the sync endpoint and a sync generator in its threadpool — no sync I/O on the event loop (N11). TLS: when `authorization_settings.json` exists the only listener is 5443 with the device certificate, so MCP over the LAN is TLS with that certificate (12.4). **Known limitation, stated plainly (review-4 N4):** on a device with local login enabled but Existing_Token_Auth **not** configured — the deployment shape `portal-user-manager` produces (`LocalLoginEnabled=true`, no `authorization_settings.json`) — the Backend_API listens on plaintext `0.0.0.0:5000` only, so D14 answers every non-loopback `/mcp` request with 403 `mcp_loopback_only` and MCP is **tunnel-only** there (SSH or AWS IoT Secure Tunneling port-forward to 5000, `docs/connect-to-device.md`); LAN access requires the TLS listener, i.e. authorization settings present. This is the correct reading of Requirements 12.4 (no plaintext beyond loopback) and 12.7 (no new listener), and it is presented to the owner as a known limitation, not a sign-off item. The child reaches the backend over loopback with `verify=False` on the TLS listener (nosec, `healthcheck.py` precedent).

*Device authentication.* A user obtains a Local_Session_Token with `POST /local-auth/login` (username/password of a synced account; uniform 401; 5-failure/15-minute lockout; 12 h lifetime; revoked on account disable through the per-request cache re-check) — no change (9.3, 9.4, 9.5). The token is given to the MCP client as `Authorization: Bearer ${DDA_EDGE_TOKEN}` from an environment variable (Kiro `headers` + approved `${VAR}` expansion); a helper script `scripts/dda-edge-mcp-login.sh` is shipped and its **documented usage is `eval "$(scripts/dda-edge-mcp-login.sh https://<device>:5443)"`** (review-4 N5): the script prompts for the username, reads the password with `read -s` (never from an argument or the environment), POSTs to `/local-auth/login`, prints exactly one line `export DDA_EDGE_TOKEN=<token>` to stdout and everything else to stderr, and never writes a file — under `eval "$(…)"` the token never appears on a typed command line, so it is not recorded in `~/.bash_history`. The script's stderr output ends with a one-line reminder that pasting the `export` line manually **does** record the 12-hour token in shell history (`HISTCONTROL=ignorespace` with a leading space, or `history -d <n>`, are the documented remedies). Existing_Token_Auth bearers work identically. On expiry the Backend_API answers 401 at the `/mcp` proxy and the client must log in again; the server never extends or caches tokens (9.5).

### Decision point 4 — Tool surface and safety

**Recommendation: wrap only existing routes, classify by effect (not HTTP verb), gate mutating tools by the same roles as the UI, require `confirm` for destructive tools, mark every action with the MCP_Audit_Marker (D5, D6, D9, D13, D21–D23, D25).** The complete initial catalogs — name, description, input schema sketch, backing route and handler file/function, required permission/role, classification, phase — are in "Tool catalogs" below. Classification rules: a tool is **read-only** iff the backing route changes no Portal/device state — classified by **what the route does, not what it is called or which verb it uses**: POST routes such as `/models/validate`, `/models/inspect`, `/datasets/count`, `/datasets/validate-manifest`, `/devices/{id}/logs/analyze` and text generation are read-only (each re-read: no write call — Key research findings); **`POST /workflows/{id}/validate` is the counter-example** — it "only validates", yet it persists the findings document to S3 and `validation_status` onto the WorkflowVersions item, which the packaging/deployment guard reads, and it requires `WORKFLOW_EDIT`, so it is **mutating** (`portal_workflows_validate`, `M`, Phase 2 — review-7 H1); GET routes that act — `GET /cameras/{id}/connect`, `GET …/models/{m}/start`, `GET /workflows/{id}/retry` and `GET /snapshot`, which runs `/snapshot/snapshot.sh`, deleting every earlier archive under `/aws_dda/system/` before writing a new one — are **not** read-only (review-5 H1); **mutating** otherwise; **destructive** when it deletes (a resource, a Portal_Identity row, a team membership, stored source files in replace mode, an earlier snapshot archive, or a device's synced accounts by full-set replacement), rolls back, replaces a running deployment, restarts/reboots (including a workflow's digital-input process), resets (including a wholesale definition replacement that resets every field the call omits), stops, pauses/resumes a running thing, disconnects, unpins, disables/deletes an account, or promotes/demotes a plugin lifecycle state (5.2, 8.2, 10.1). On **both** servers the rule is enforced mechanically as well as by reading: on the edge an AST test walks every `RO` spec's route function **transitively** over `src/backend` (revision 10, review-9 N1; resolution rules in Testing Strategy, `test_child_catalog_classification.py`) and fails when a function on that call graph invokes `subprocess`/`os.system`/`os.remove`/`os.unlink`/`os.replace`/`os.rename`, a `shutil` function other than the reads `disk_usage`, `which` and `get_terminal_size` (`shutil.disk_usage` in `system.get_system_health`, 213, is a read), a `tempfile` function that creates a file or directory (`mkstemp`, `mkdtemp`, `NamedTemporaryFile`, `TemporaryFile`, `SpooledTemporaryFile`, `TemporaryDirectory` — `tempfile.gettempdir`, reached on the eight sweep tools at `stream_ingest/protocol.py:82`, is a path lookup), Pillow's `Image.save` on a **Pillow image value** — a call of `Image.open`, `Image.new`, `Image.fromarray` or an `ImageOps` function, a method call on such a value, or a name bound from one (assignment or `with` target); `.save(...)` on such a value is the effect `Image.save` (this is the one definition, cited by Property 2, the Invariants and the Testing Strategy; revision 11, review-10 N7; revision 12, review-11 N3) — `os.makedirs`/`os.mkdir`/`os.rmdir`/`os.chmod`, a `pathlib` twin of a listed call on a `Path` value (the same value rule applied to `Path(...)`: `Path.unlink`/`rename`/`replace`/`touch`/`mkdir`/`rmdir`/`chmod`, and `Path.open` with a write mode; revision 12, review-11 N3), or opens a path for writing or writes through `Path.write_*` (review-5 H1) — unless the spec lists that `'<module>.<function>:<call>'` in **`accepted_effects`**, the twin of `accepted_writes`, allowed only with the fixed `ACCEPTED_EFFECT_NOTE` "process-level housekeeping: the device runtime's own effect, the same one the HMI's use of the route has; nothing the caller supplied is persisted". The housekeeping it accepts is described once here: the first construction of the process-wide stream-ingest manager unlinks the stale `/dev/shm` frame segments a previous backend's workers left behind (`stream_ingest/manager.py:384–398` → `protocol.sweep_segments`, `protocol.py:230`), whichever route constructs it first; the capability probe runs once per process and is cached (`capabilities.py:298–353`); and the preview pipeline fixes the permissions of the frame it just wrote, deletes it when it is empty, regenerates the CSI acquisition config from the source's **stored** settings, creating its directory and setting its mode (`gst_pipeline_executor.py:85–90`, `csi_capture.py:78`, `os.makedirs` 69 and `os.chmod` 80 — entries since revision 12, review-11 N3; no `RO` tool takes the route's settings override, revision 11, review-10 M1) and, on JP6 only, decodes the CSI frame through Pillow and stages it beside the frame as `<frame>.dda_decoded.png` (`pipeline_builder.py:112–127`, 172–190, `im.save` 187 — fixed case of `test_child_catalog_classification.py`; revision 11, review-10 N7); on the portal, `test_mcp_catalog_classification.py` walks an `RO` spec's `handler_function` and **every** function it reaches in its own module and in the other `functions/*.py` modules (a call through an imported module object, such as `camera_registry.create_response`, is followed, never counted as a write) and fails on any **write call** — an attribute call named `put_item`, `update_item`, `delete_item`, `batch_write_item`, `transact_write_items`, `batch_writer`, `put_object`, `delete_object`, `delete_objects`, `copy_object`, `upload_file`, `upload_fileobj`, `start_execution`, `invoke`, `send_message`, `publish`, `put_parameter`, or matching `^(create|update|delete|put|cancel|start|stop|terminate|reboot|reset)_`, or a call to one of the shared-layer writers the walk does not enter — `SHARED_LAYER_WRITERS`, **derived by the test** (revision 10, review-9 N2): every first-party function of `layers/shared/python/*.py` (the modules without a `*.dist-info` distribution; `six.py` is vendored) whose body, transitively within the layer, contains a write call, minus `log_audit_event`, which is neither a member nor traversed; at `c3bf88c1` that is fourteen functions — `shared_utils.record_audit_event_strict`, `finalize_audit_event`, `RBACManager.assign_user_role`, `remove_user_role`; `detector_conversion.reconcile_conversion`, `apply_conversion_transition`, `invoke_finalize`; `s3_cors.ensure_bucket_cors`; `user_roles_dao.UserRolesDAO.assign_user_to_usecase`, `bulk_assign_users`, `update_user_role`, `remove_user_from_usecase`, `remove_all_user_assignments`, `remove_all_usecase_assignments` — and the test asserts the derived set contains the seven revision 9 named; a call counts when it does not resolve to a `functions/` function and its callee name (the `Name`, or the attribute on a layer module or layer object such as `rbac_manager`) is a derived writer's — unless the spec lists that write in **`accepted_writes`**, matched on `(module.function, call, receiver source text)` plus an occurrence count (review-8 H1; revision 10, review-9 N2: a line-number key broke on any unrelated edit above the call, so the line stays only in the row note). There is **no** audit-result clause: audited reads are the norm here (`devices.list_devices` 349, `get_device` 474, `device_logs.list_log_groups` 246, `get_component_logs` 376, `deployments.get_deployment` 1308, `data_management.list_buckets` 293, `usecases.list_usecases` 1346/1367, `get_usecase` 1882 write `'success'` entries and are `RO`; `handler_audits` records that), and audit-before-effect is caught through the named strict-audit writers. **Accepted reading — status sync (review-8 H1):** a GET that refreshes its own stored record from the authoritative external state stays `RO` when every write on its path is listed in `accepted_writes` and the row carries the fixed note "status sync: the GET refreshes its own stored record from the authoritative external state — the same write the web UI performs when it shows the record; nothing the caller supplied is persisted" (the catalog tables abbreviate it as "status sync (fixed note)"; `catalog.py` holds it once as `STATUS_SYNC_NOTE`). Ten Phase-1 tools carry it (`portal_models_get`, `_training_get`, `_compilation_get`; `portal_data_labeling_jobs_list`, `_labeling_job_get`; `portal_workflows_generation_job_get`, `_test_run_get`, `_simulation_get`, `_plugin_modules_list`, `_build_servers_list`), and three are **owner-confirmed edge cases** because they are transitions rather than refreshes: `get_generation_job`'s lazy reap moves a pending/running job past `deadline_at` to failed (`record_job_failure`, 593–632, called at 1309–1312); `get_compilation_status` persists its own poll-fault count and latches an entry to `FAILED` after five failed polls (841–855, 867; `compilation_status.py:53`); and `get_training_job` reconciles a detector Conversion_Record, whose winning claim async-invokes the packaging finalize that publishes the converted component (`sync_conversion_record`, 1130–1148 → `detector_conversion.py:722/749`). Each is what the web UI's own status poll does; the owner confirms them at Phase-1 sign-off item (i) or reclassifies them as `M`. Run over all 89 revision-9 portal `RO` tools with a backing route at `c3bf88c1` (88 once `portal_data_labeling_manifest_get` left `CATALOG`), the rule finds exactly those twelve accepted call sites and nothing else, both with revision 9's hand-listed writers and with the derived `SHARED_LAYER_WRITERS` — eleven `accepted_writes` entries, because `get_training_job`'s two `table.update_item` calls (1249, 1291) are one entry with count 2 (revision-9 and revision-10 response tables). The review-5 H1 class of error survived six reviews on the portal side because only the edge had the test (review-7 H1; Testing Strategy). Annotations: read-only → `read_only_hint=True`; mutating → `read_only_hint=False, destructive_hint=<destructive>, idempotent_hint=<per tool>`; all → `open_world_hint=False`. Descriptions (Requirements 1.2, 5.1; review-5 M4): rendered by `to_mcp_tool` from the spec's own fields as `"<description sentence> Touches: <resource_type><scope>. <Read-only.|MUTATING.|MUTATING, DESTRUCTIVE — requires confirm=…>"` (D35), so the text can never disagree with the annotations; deprecated aliases are listed with a deprecation note (14.4). Excluded permanently (5.5, 10.8, Out of Scope): `/auth/refresh`, `/admin/users/{u}/password`, `/admin/users/{u}/forgot-password`, `/quick-setup/*`, `POST /device-registrations` and `/device-registrations/{id}/command` (Setup_Token), `/devices/{id}/ssh-tunnel*` (tunnel tokens), `/git-connections*` (stored credentials), `/data-accounts*` (cross-account role/external-id configuration), `/code-assist`, `/local-auth/login|status`, `/authorization-configurations`, every route addressed by a **filesystem path or file name** (`/snapshotfile/{fileName}`, `GET|DELETE /captured-images` with their `path`/`filePath` query parameters — M5), `/streams/*/subscribe|heartbeat|frame|settings` (HMI viewer session mechanics). **Deferred, not excluded (review-6 M4, N3):** two operations inside covered areas have no MCP-compatible contract today and are wrapped by no tool until a route exists — `POST /static-video-camera/pin` (multipart up to 100 MiB or a device-path JSON body; the proxy's 1 MiB cap and the no-path rule leave nothing to wrap — Backlog 17) and `POST /workflows/{id}/results/export` (its only output is a device path consumed solely by the excluded query-token zip route — Backlog 19); their areas keep every other tool (video pin get/unpin; results summary/list/get/update/reset/delete). On the portal, six registered routes are wrapped by no tool for the same reason (revision 9; the sixth, revision 10): `POST /components` and `POST /components/discover` have no handler branch (`components.lambda_handler` answers 404, 216–228 — Backlog 21), the portal static image/video pins accept only a `stagingKey` issued by the excluded presigned upload route (`camera_registry.py:2087–2092`, 2454–2459 — Backlog 22), `POST /components/configure` records a `PENDING` item and deploys nothing (`component_configuration.py:299–322` — Backlog 23), and `POST /usecases/{id}/data/buckets` is a disabled stub that answers 400 (`data_management.py:322–341` — Backlog 24); and `GET /labeling/{id}/manifest` answers with a body that carries no Use_Case (`labeling.py:1125–1149`), so the shaper (D36) cannot bind it to the named Use_Case — `portal_data_labeling_manifest_get` returns with Backlog 18's handler-side binding (review-9 N5); their areas keep every other tool. **Every argument a row lists is a parameter the route reads, and every parameter the route requires is a required argument** — checked mechanically by Property 2's argument clause (review-6 M5), because the sixth review found five rows whose default call the route would have answered with 400 and the seventh found two more rows naming arguments no handler reads (`portal_workflows_validate.definition`, `portal_devices_logs_analyze.component`/`question` — review-7 H1/M3); revision 8 ran the clause by hand over the concrete-argument portal rows and corrected seven, and revision 9 ran it, with the route, classification, permission and shaping checks, over every row of both catalogs (the response table lists each corrected cell). The clause has one explicit exemption, the **server-consumed arguments** — `confirm` (consumed by the validator), `usecase_id` where the route does not read it (consumed by the pre-dispatch RBAC check; Requirement 5.6 still requires it on every `U` tool, and it is forwarded **only** through an explicit `param_map` entry), `limit`/`offset` on collection specs whose route does not paginate (consumed by the shaper), and the edge `text_tail_arg` (`tail_chars`, consumed by the shaper) — enumerated in the catalog constant `SERVER_CONSUMED_ARGS` that the tests read, so nothing is exempt by convention.

### Decision point 5 — Authorization mapping, rate limiting, expiry

**Recommendation (D3–D5, D10, D12–D14).** *Portal:* access-token claims → `get_user_from_event` yields `user_id = sub`, `username` (access tokens carry `username`), `email` falls back to the username (`_display_identity`), Claimed_Role `'Viewer'` (no `custom:role` in access tokens). With `PORTAL_REGISTRY_ENFORCED=true` this is identical to the web path (the registry decides). With it **off**, `_legacy_role` cannot see a `custom:role` claim, so an MCP principal resolves to PortalAdmin only via the `global` registry row, to a Use_Case role via its Use_Case row, and otherwise to Viewer — never more than the web UI grants the same user (C4). `RegistryUnavailable` → `authorization_unavailable` (3.4).

**Enforcement-off consequence for `global`-scoped tools (review-3 M2; D29).** Every `U`-scoped tool behaves as the web UI does in both modes (the Use_Case row decides). Every `G`-scoped tool, and every `usecase_or_global` tool called **without** `usecase_id`, is evaluated at the `'global'` scope, where enforcement-off resolution gives **PortalAdmin (with a `global` PortalAdmin row) or Viewer — nothing else**, because `_legacy_role` ignores a `global` row whose role is not PortalAdmin and the MCP user dict carries no `custom:role`. The affected tools are exactly those whose declared permission some non-PortalAdmin role holds but Viewer does not: **Phase 1** — `portal_data_labeler_jobs_list`, `portal_data_labeler_pool_get`, `portal_data_labeler_task_image_url_get`, `portal_data_labeler_task_next` (`labeling:tasks-self`: DataLabeler, DataScientist, UseCaseAdmin), `portal_workflows_builds_list`, `portal_workflows_build_get`, `portal_workflows_build_logs_get`, `portal_workflows_build_branches_list`, `portal_workflows_build_servers_list`, `portal_workflows_build_config_get` (`builds:read`: DataScientist, UseCaseAdmin); **Phase 2** — `portal_data_labeler_task_steal`, `_submit`, `_presentation_failure` (`labeling:tasks-self`), `portal_workflows_build_submit`, `_build_retry` (`builds:submit`); **Phase 3** — `portal_workflows_build_cancel` (`builds:cancel`). Unaffected: `G` tools whose permission Viewer holds (`view_usecases`, `view_deployments`, `view_models`), `G` tools whose permission only PortalAdmin holds anyway (`manage_users`, `system_admin`, `create_usecases`), and the whoami tools. In the web UI these same routes admit DataLabelers/DataScientists/UseCaseAdmins through the ID token's `custom:role` (`_legacy_role` step 4), so **until `PORTAL_REGISTRY_ENFORCED=true` and the principal holds a `global` registry row naming the role, the labeler self-service and build tools are PortalAdmin-only through MCP, and Requirement 3.6 is met only under enforcement**. A PortalAdmin without a `global` row (an account created directly in Cognito before the User Manager) is Viewer through MCP in both modes and must be backfilled. This is stated in the ADMIN_GUIDE (with the tool list above) and is Phase 1 **owner sign-off item (e)**: accept the documented limitation (the design's recommendation), or require `portalRegistryEnforced=true` for deployments that enable `deployMcpServer`, or authorize the `AdminGetUser`-sourced `custom:role` augmentation (D29 option (C), Backlog 12). The RBAC matrix test encodes whichever choice is made (Testing Strategy). The MCP Lambda looks up the principal's email with `cognito-idp:AdminGetUser` (cached per `sub` for the container lifetime; failure → `'unknown'`) and passes it in `event['mcp'].principal_email` so audit entries keep an email attribution (4.1). *Edge:* `GET /local-auth/whoami` yields `Principal(username, role, source)`; the role is the live `Account.role` returned by `session_tokens.validate_token` (so a role changed by the Account_Sync_Service applies on the next request), never a header or argument (10.4). *Role-less principals* (10.5): read-only tools only. *Self-identity exception (N5):* `portal_admin_whoami` and `edge_whoami` carry no permission and are callable by every authenticated principal including DataLabeler, because they reveal only the caller's own identity and role; Requirement 3.6 is read as applying to tools that touch Portal resources. *Rate limiting:* D10. *Expiry:* portal 401 + challenge → client refresh/re-auth; edge 401 → re-login; neither server extends lifetime; mid-session disablement: Cognito refuses refresh / `validate_token` re-checks `enabled` per request (9.4).

### Decision point 6 — Packaging, distribution, client configuration

**Recommendation (D19, D20, "Client configuration", "Documentation").** Portal: distributed as part of the portal deploy (`deploy-infrastructure.sh` → `cdk deploy --all` includes `EdgeCVPortalMcpStack`). **Opt-out is a two-step procedure (D33; review-4 M3)** because `cdk deploy --all` does not destroy a stack that merely left the app and `ComputeStack` cannot drop exports the deployed MCP stack still imports: (1) in `edge-cv-portal/infrastructure/`, `npx cdk destroy EdgeCVPortalMcpStack --force` **with the stack still present in the app** (no `deployMcpServer=false`; `--force` answers `cdk destroy`'s interactive confirmation prompt, which the operator otherwise answers by hand — review-5 N4; `cdk destroy` synthesizes the app to find the stack, so it runs with the same environment a deploy uses — `AWS_REGION` exported and the trusted-account list resolvable from context, `TRUSTED_USECASE_ACCOUNT_IDS` or the SSM parameter (`bin/app.ts:86–137`), because `ComputeStack` throws at synth when the list is empty (`compute-stack.ts:151–152`) and the whole app must synthesize for `cdk destroy` to locate the stack; at the branch base `bin/app.ts` also refuses to synthesize without `-c portalRegistryEnforced=<value>` (or `-c allowLegacyRoles=true` on a fresh install; added in `3f181d42`, after the pin), so step (1) passes the deployed enforcement value, which only satisfies that check because `cdk destroy` deploys no template, and the guard prints step (1) with the region and the `CDK_CONTEXT_ARGS` the deploy has already resolved; see the revision-12 "Implementation drift" row) — this removes the HTTP API, Lambda, both layers, the IAM role, the SSM parameter, and the MCP app client and resource server from the pool; (2) `DEPLOY_MCP_SERVER=false ./deploy-infrastructure.sh`, so `ComputeStack`'s now-unreferenced exports are dropped cleanly. **The variable follows one rule (D33; review-5 N4):** `portal_mcp_optout_guard` treats `DEPLOY_MCP_SERVER` as **off** iff its value, trimmed and lower-cased, is exactly `false` — the same test `mcpServerEnabled` applies to the CDK context (`groundedSamWorkerEnabled` default-ON semantics) — and only then is `-c deployMcpServer=false` appended to `CDK_CONTEXT_ARGS`, in the same place the registry-enforcement block appends `-c portalRegistryEnforced=…` today (`deploy-infrastructure.sh:70–76`); every other value, including `true`, unset, empty, `0` and `no`, appends nothing and the CDK default (on) applies. Running step (2) first is caught by the **guard** sourced from a new `scripts/portal-mcp-stack.sh` (the `scripts/portal-registry-enforcement.sh` pattern): `portal_mcp_optout_guard "$AWS_REGION" "$DEPLOY_MCP_SERVER"` aborts the deploy (exit 1, `❌` message naming step (1)) when the value is off **and** `aws cloudformation describe-stacks --stack-name EdgeCVPortalMcpStack` succeeds; a `describe-stacks` failure other than "does not exist" (credentials, throttling) also aborts rather than guessing, like `portal_registry_enforced_for_deploy`. Invoking `npx cdk deploy --all -c deployMcpServer=false` directly in `infrastructure/` does not run the guard and is documented as unsupported for that reason. Re-enabling is one step (`DEPLOY_MCP_SERVER=true` or unset → default on → `cdk deploy --all` recreates the stack; Kiro users re-authenticate because the app client id changes). The `computeStack.exportValue(...)` retention technique is recorded only as the fallback for an operator who already ran step (2) first and is looking at the "Export … in use" error: add `exportValue(fn.functionArn)` calls in each producing stack for the ARNs the MCP stack imports, deploy, destroy the MCP stack, remove them. CDK outputs `McpServerUrl`, `McpViewUrls`, `McpUserPoolClientId`, `McpOAuthScope`, `McpRedirectUri`, `McpProtectedResourceMetadataUrl`, `McpAuthorizationServerMetadataUrl`; server version `mcp_portal.__version__` (semver, bumped on catalog changes) plus `MCP_BUILD_SHA` from the deploy; delivered phases are the constant `mcp_portal.DELIVERED_PHASES` (N7). Edge: files inside the LocalServer image + venv; delivered phases `mcp_edge.DELIVERED_PHASES`; **the server identity's version is aligned to the LocalServer component version as Requirement 15.3 asks (review-5 N7):** `app.py` passes `component_version=system.get_local_server_component_version()` (`endpoints/system.py:146`, the reader the system-health route already uses; `"NOT_FOUND"` when it cannot be determined) to the supervisor, which spawns the child with `--server-version f"{mcp_edge.__version__}+{component_version}"` — semver build metadata, e.g. `1.0.0+1.0.130` — so `serverInfo.version` carries both the catalog version and the component version; `/mcp/status` reports the two parts separately (`serverVersion`, `componentVersion`) and the `mcp.access` record's `mcp_server` is `dda-edge-mcp@<identity version>`. Kiro configuration examples (secret-free) are added to the docs and, as `disabled: true` entries, to `.kiro/settings/mcp.json` (15.2).

### Decision point 7 — Testing strategy

**Recommendation: see "Testing Strategy"** — protocol-level tests with the official SDK `Client` in memory and over the real Streamable HTTP client transport bound to the ASGI app in process (`httpx2.ASGITransport`), RS256 test keys with an in-memory JWKS for authentication negatives, moto-backed registry/audit for RBAC and audit assertions across every tool × role with enforcement on and off, Hypothesis properties for the pure cores, jest/`Template` assertions for the CDK stack, host-runnable edge unit tests under `test/backend-test/mcp_edge/` (run with `uv run --python 3.11`), a new on-hardware stage `test_50_mcp.py`, a legacy-era client interaction per server, a two-invocations-in-one-process test for the Lambda handler, an SDK-challenge test over ASGI (proving the SDK's 401 header upstream of API Gateway) paired with a deployed-stack smoke check for the header as clients receive it, discovery-document unit tests (root/suffixed PRM, RFC 8414 facade, identical issuer strings), an AST-based `handler_audits` honesty test that classifies audit calls by their `result` argument, a process-level "credential never logged" test for the edge child, a request-audit-context binding test, a two-sided catalog-route-registration check (pytest pins `catalog_routes.json` to the catalog, jest pins it to the synthesized REST route tables — review-3 M1), an RBAC matrix whose expectations come from the real resolver under both enforcement modes with the D29 denials tagged (review-3 M2), a CDK assertion on the MCP client's `ExplicitAuthFlows` (review-3 H1), a child transport-security test with a control case against the SDK's loopback defaults (review-3 M3), a middleware-order assertion on `build_asgi_app().user_middleware` (review-4 M1), a dispatch-client test proving one `Invoke` attempt and the lazily cached SSM dispatch map (review-4 H1/M2), CDK assertions that the environment is under 3,000 bytes with no `MCP_BACKEND_FUNCTIONS` variable, that the role has six exact statements and that no `LayerVersion` is imported (review-4 H1/H2), a bats file for the opt-out guard under the existing offline deploy-script harness (review-4 M3), AST-based **classification tests on both catalogs** — edge: no `RO` route runs a subprocess or writes a device path (review-5 H1); portal: no write call on an `RO` handler's call graph outside its declared `accepted_writes`, with `workflow_validation.validate_workflow` as the fixed not-`RO` case and the ten status-sync GETs and eight audited reads as fixed `RO` cases (review-7 H1, review-8 H1) — shaping properties for the path scrub, image fields, dropped fields and the oversized-image guard (review-5 M1/M2, review-6 M2/N2), and an AST-based **argument ↔ route-parameter consistency test** on both catalogs — every `param_map` target names a parameter the route reads (for a free-`Dict` edge route, a name in `ToolSpec.body_fields` that also appears as a string literal in the route module — review-7 M2), every parameter the route requires is a required argument, and the only exemptions are the enumerated `SERVER_CONSUMED_ARGS` — with the five rows the sixth review found and the two the seventh found as fixed cases (review-6 M5, review-7 M3).

### Decision point 8 — Phasing

**Recommendation: three phases per server, read-only first** — see "Phasing". Phase 1 (MVP) ships every read-only tool of every capability area in Requirements 1 and 8 with full auth/audit/test coverage, except `portal_devices_logs_analyze` (long fan-out) and `portal_workflows_plugin_generate_status_get` (polls a Phase-2 generation session), which are Phase 2 (revision 10, review-9 N6); Phase 2 adds lower-consequence mutating tools; Phase 3 adds high-consequence tools. Entry criteria, including the owner sign-off items (Requirement 3.9 interpretation, no gateway authorizer, the Requirement 2.4 header deviation and "naming the User_Pool" reading, the enforcement-off limitation of D29, the `max(k, 1)` reading of Requirement 16.4 and the `aud`-less-token rule — items (f) and (g), review-4 N3 — the Requirement 17.4 deviation for the operation-level deferrals — item (h), review-7 N8 — the status-sync reading (i), the account-scoped id-addressed Greengrass reads (j, revision 10), and the destructive list), are listed per phase.

## Components and Interfaces — Portal

### 1. `EdgeCVPortalMcpStack` (new, `edge-cv-portal/infrastructure/lib/mcp-stack.ts`)

A top-level `cdk.Stack` instantiated in `bin/app.ts` after `computeStack`, `nodeDesignerStack`, `buildFleetStack` and `syntheticDataStack`, guarded by the new context flag `deployMcpServer` (default **on**, resolved by a new `mcpServerEnabled(contextValue)` helper in `context-helpers.ts` with the `groundedSamWorkerEnabled` default-ON semantics; only an explicit `false` omits the stack). `deploy-infrastructure.sh` derives the context from the `DEPLOY_MCP_SERVER` environment variable under one rule (D33; review-5 N4): `portal_mcp_optout_guard` treats the variable as **off** iff its value, trimmed and lower-cased, is exactly `false` — the `mcpServerEnabled` test — and only an off value appends `-c deployMcpServer=false` to `CDK_CONTEXT_ARGS` (next to the registry-enforcement block, `deploy-infrastructure.sh:70–76`); any other value, including `true` and unset, appends nothing, so the CDK default (on) applies; and the guard refuses an off value while `EdgeCVPortalMcpStack` is still deployed — omitting a stack from the app does not destroy it, and `ComputeStack` cannot drop exports the deployed MCP stack imports (D33; review-4 M3). Props: `userPool: cognito.IUserPool`, `userPoolDomain: cognito.UserPoolDomain` (required on the prop; `bin/app.ts` passes `authStack.userPoolDomain!` because `AuthStack` declares the member optional at `auth-stack.ts:15` but assigns it unconditionally at 63–66 — the non-null assertion sits at that one call site with a comment, review-3 N1; the hosted-UI origin for the RFC 8414 facade is derived exactly as `auth-stack.ts:164` does: `` `https://${props.userPoolDomain.domainName}.auth.${cdk.Aws.REGION}.amazoncognito.com` ``), `userRolesTable`, `auditLogTable`, `usecasesTable`, `backendHandlers: Record<McpBackendKey, lambda.IFunction>`, `ssoIdentityProviderName?: string`, `mcpMutatingToolsEnabled: string`, `mcpRedirectUris: string[]`, `mcpAllowedOrigins: string[]` (default `[]`; validate-first item (viii), review-5 N5), `mcpBuildSha: string`. There is deliberately **no `sharedLayer` prop** (D31; review-4 H2): the stack builds its own `McpSharedLayer` from `../../backend/layers/shared`, so the only values imported from other stacks are update-stable ARNs (functions, tables, the user pool) — never a `LayerVersion`, whose ARN changes on every content change and would block `ComputeStack`'s updates for as long as the MCP stack imported it.

**Additive members in existing stacks (M1; narrowed by review-4 H2):** `ComputeStack` (`compute-stack.ts`) gains `public readonly mcpBackendHandlers: Record<string, lambda.IFunction>` holding the existing handler functions by backend key: `devices`, `deviceLogs`, **`deviceLogsAnalyzer`**, `deployments`, `models`, `modelImport`, `modelConverter`, `training`, `compilation`, `packaging`, `greengrassPublish`, `components`, **`componentConfiguration`**, `sharedComponents`, `datasets`, `preLabeledDatasets`, `dataManagement`, `captures`, `labeling`, `ddaLabeling`, `workflows`, `workflowValidation`, `workflowPackaging`, `workflowTesting`, `workflowGenerator`, `useCases`, `userManagement`, `userAdmin`, `auditLogs`, `cameraRegistry`, `cameraVideoPin`, `deviceRegistrations`. Because `ComponentConfigurationHandler` is created inside the `ApiGatewayStack` nested stack (`api-gateway-stack.ts:711`), `ApiGatewayStack` gains `public readonly componentConfigurationHandler: lambda.Function` (assigned from the existing construct — no new resource, so the 500-resource limit is untouched) and `ComputeStack` surfaces it as `mcpBackendHandlers['componentConfiguration']`. `NodeDesignerStack` (`pluginRecordsHandler`, `pluginImporterHandler`, `nodeGeneratorHandler`, `pluginBuildsHandler`, `pluginComponentsHandler`, `pluginSimulatorHandler`, `customNodeTypesHandler`), `BuildFleetStack` (`buildJobsHandler`, `buildFleetHandler`, `buildConfigHandler`), and `SyntheticDataStack` (`syntheticDataHandler`) already expose theirs. These **two** members (`ComputeStack.mcpBackendHandlers`, `ApiGatewayStack.componentConfigurationHandler`) are the complete list of changes to existing stacks — `ComputeStack`'s `SharedLayer` construct (line 165) is **not** exposed (D31) — and a snapshot test pins them. Every exported value is a function ARN, which is stable across updates, so none of the 32 resulting `ComputeStack` exports (nor the 11 from `NodeDesignerStack`/`BuildFleetStack`/`SyntheticDataStack`) can block a later update of its producing stack.

Resources created:

| Construct | Definition |
|---|---|
| `McpHttpApi` | `new apigwv2.HttpApi(this, 'McpHttpApi', { apiName: 'dda-portal-mcp', createDefaultStage: false })`; `corsPreflight` **omitted** (browser clients are out of scope; CORS would weaken the Origin rule); no custom domain in this spec. `canonicalUrl = \`${httpApi.apiEndpoint}/mcp\``, `viewUrls = views.map(v => \`${canonicalUrl}/${v}\`)`, `apiHost = cdk.Fn.select(2, cdk.Fn.split('/', httpApi.apiEndpoint))` |
| `McpDefaultStage` | `new apigwv2.HttpStage(this, 'McpDefaultStage', { httpApi, stageName: '$default', autoDeploy: true, throttle: { rateLimit: 20, burstLimit: 40 } })`; per-route limits via the escape hatch (N6): `(stage.node.defaultChild as apigwv2.CfnStage).addPropertyOverride('RouteSettings', Object.fromEntries(['POST /mcp','GET /mcp','DELETE /mcp','POST /mcp/{view}','GET /mcp/{view}','DELETE /mcp/{view}'].map(k => [k, { ThrottlingRateLimit: 10, ThrottlingBurstLimit: 20 }])))` |
| `McpResourceServer` | `new cognito.UserPoolResourceServer(this, 'McpResourceServer', { userPool: props.userPool, identifier: canonicalUrl, userPoolResourceServerName: 'dda-portal-mcp', scopes: [new cognito.ResourceServerScope({ scopeName: 'portal.tools', scopeDescription: 'Invoke DDA portal MCP tools as the signed-in user' })] })` — created in **this** stack's scope (not `userPool.addResourceServer`, which would place it in `AuthStack` and create a cross-stack cycle through the API id) |
| `McpUserPoolClient` | `new cognito.UserPoolClient(this, 'McpUserPoolClient', { userPool: props.userPool, userPoolClientName: 'dda-portal-mcp-client', generateSecret: false, authFlows: { userSrp: false } /* emits ExplicitAuthFlows: ['ALLOW_REFRESH_TOKEN_AUTH'] only — an explicit false key is REQUIRED: CDK omits the property for {} and Cognito then defaults to refresh + USER_SRP + CUSTOM (review-3 H1) */, oAuth: { flows: { authorizationCodeGrant: true }, scopes: [cognito.OAuthScope.resourceServer(resourceServer, portalToolsScope)], callbackUrls: ['http://localhost:7899/oauth/callback', ...props.mcpRedirectUris] }, preventUserExistenceErrors: true, accessTokenValidity: Duration.hours(1), refreshTokenValidity: Duration.days(30), supportedIdentityProviders: [COGNITO (+ SAML when ssoIdentityProviderName)] })`. Synthesized `ExplicitAuthFlows` = `['ALLOW_REFRESH_TOKEN_AUTH']` (no SRP, password, custom or `USER_AUTH` flow: the hosted-UI code grant with PKCE and the refresh grant are the only token paths); no `openid` scope → Cognito issues no ID token to MCP clients. `props.mcpRedirectUris` are validated at synth: each must parse as `http://localhost[:port]/path` or `https://…` (Cognito's plain-HTTP-for-`localhost`-only rule, Research baseline; review-3 N9) |
| `McpSharedLayer` (D31; review-4 H2) | `new lambda.LayerVersion(this, 'McpSharedLayer', { code: lambda.Code.fromAsset(path.join(__dirname, '../../backend/layers/shared')), compatibleRuntimes: [lambda.Runtime.PYTHON_3_11], description: 'shared_utils for the MCP Lambda — same asset as ComputeStack SharedLayer (NodeDesignerStack/BuildFleetStack/SyntheticDataStack pattern)' })` — the MCP stack's **own** copy of the shared layer, built from the same working-tree asset as `compute-stack.ts:165–166`, `node-designer-stack.ts:713–714`, `build-fleet-stack.ts:399–400` and `synthetic-data-stack.ts:176–177`. Not imported from `ComputeStack`: a `LayerVersion` ARN changes on every content change, and CloudFormation refuses to change an exported value another stack imports, so importing it would fail every later `ComputeStack` deploy that touched `layers/shared` until the MCP stack was destroyed |
| `McpLayer` | `new lambda.LayerVersion(this, 'McpLayer', { code: fromAsset('../../backend/layers/mcp', { bundling }), compatibleRuntimes: [PYTHON_3_11] })`, bundled at CDK synth (sign-off item (m) = (b), 2026-10-25); `backend/layers/mcp/requirements.txt` = `mcp==2.3.0`, `mangum==0.22.0` (the direct pins), `requirements.lock.txt` = every transitive pin with hashes, resolved for CPython 3.11 on x86_64 manylinux2014; `build.sh <output-dir>` installs the lock with `pip install --platform manylinux2014_x86_64 --only-binary=:all: --python-version 3.11 --implementation cp --no-deps -r requirements.lock.txt -t <output-dir>/python/` as the local bundling step (the python3.11 bundling image runs the same script as the Docker fallback) and fails the synth when a wheel is missing |
| `McpBackendFunctionsParameter` (D30; review-4 H1) | `new ssm.StringParameter(this, 'McpBackendFunctions', { parameterName: '/dda-portal/mcp/backend-functions', tier: ssm.ParameterTier.ADVANCED /* 8 KB value limit; the standard tier's 4 KB is too small for 43 ARNs */, description: 'MCP backend key -> handler function ARN (read once per Lambda container)', stringValue: cdk.Stack.of(this).toJsonString(Object.fromEntries(Object.entries(props.backendHandlers).map(([key, fn]) => [key, fn.functionArn]))) })` — `Stack.toJsonString` (`core/lib/stack.d.ts:420`) renders a token-bearing object as a `Fn::Join` of literal JSON and the imported ARNs. **Sizing (review-5 N1):** 40 of the 43 backing functions have CloudFormation-generated names `<stack>-<logicalId+hash>-<12 random>` (≤ 64 chars), so their ARNs are 107–111 characters; the three fixed-name handlers (`cameraVideoPin` = `dda-portal-camera-video-pin`, `compute-stack.ts:2624`; `workflowGenerator` = `dda-portal-workflow-generator`, `:3162`; `syntheticData` = `dda-synthetic-data-handler`, `synthetic-data-stack.ts:397`) have ≈ 75-character ARNs; 40 × (quoted key ≈ 15 + quoted ARN ≈ 113 + separators) + 3 × ≈ 95 ≈ **5.5 KB** — more than the whole 4 KB Lambda environment quota on its own, which is why this is a parameter and not a variable. The advanced tier costs US$0.05 per advanced parameter per month plus US$0.05 per 10,000 API interactions (one parameter, one `GetParameter` per cold start — aws.amazon.com/systems-manager/pricing/, fetched 2026-10-19; review-5 N8). The parameter is a resource **of the MCP stack**, so `cdk destroy EdgeCVPortalMcpStack` removes it (D33) |
| `McpPortalRole` | Dedicated `iam.Role` (NOT `createLambdaRole`; `quick_setup.py` precedent) with the managed policy `AWSLambdaBasicExecutionRole` and **exactly six inline statements** (M2; sixth added by review-4 H1): (1) `lambda:InvokeFunction` on the exact ARNs in `backendHandlers`; (2) `dynamodb:GetItem`, `dynamodb:Query` on `userRolesTable.tableArn` (GetItem for `_lookup_identity`/`_legacy_role`, Query for `get_accessible_usecases`); (3) `dynamodb:Scan` on `usecasesTable.tableArn` (read by `portal_admin_whoami` only, via `get_accessible_usecases` for PortalAdmins); (4) `dynamodb:PutItem` on `auditLogTable.tableArn`; (5) `cognito-idp:AdminGetUser` on `userPool.userPoolArn`; (6) `ssm:GetParameter` on `backendFunctionsParameter.parameterArn` — written as an explicit `iam.PolicyStatement`, **not** `parameter.grantRead(role)`, because `StringParameter.grantRead` (`aws-ssm/lib/parameter.js`) adds `ssm:DescribeParameters` on `*` plus `GetParameters`/`GetParameterHistory`, and this role carries no wildcard resource. No wildcard actions or resources (6.5, C8). **Policy size:** statement (1) lists 43 ARNs ≈ 6.5 KB of the role's inline-policy budget; IAM's limit is 10,240 characters per role (white space excluded), so the synthesized policy stays under it — `mcp-stack.test.ts` measures the serialized inline policy of the synthesized template and asserts it is below 9,000 characters so growth is noticed before IAM rejects it |
| `McpPortalHandler` | `lambda.Function` `PYTHON_3_11`, `handler: 'mcp_portal.handler.handler'`, `code: fromAsset('../../backend/functions')` (repo convention), `layers: [mcpSharedLayer, mcpLayer]` (both layers are resources of this stack; the template's `Layers` property contains no `Fn::ImportValue`), `memorySize: 512`, `timeout: Duration.seconds(29)` (D32: 29 s under the HTTP API's 30 s integration limit; the dispatcher's 24 s remaining-time guard and 2 s + 20 s invoke budget are sized against it — review-5 M3), `logGroup: logGroup` (the explicit `McpPortalHandlerLogGroup`, next row), `environment` below |
| `McpPortalHandlerLogGroup`, `McpTimeoutMetricFilter`, `McpTimeoutAlarm` (review-5 M3) | `const logGroup = new logs.LogGroup(this, 'McpPortalHandlerLogGroup', { logGroupName: '/dda-portal/mcp-server', retention: logs.RetentionDays.ONE_MONTH, removalPolicy: cdk.RemovalPolicy.DESTROY })` passed to the function as `logGroup` (the `node-designer-stack.ts:1145–1150`/`test-runner-stack.ts:133` pattern — an explicit group, **not** `fn.logGroup`/`logRetention`, which in the installed `aws-cdk-lib` add a `LogRetention` custom-resource Lambda and its role to the stack); `new logs.MetricFilter(this, 'McpTimeoutMetricFilter', { logGroup, filterPattern: logs.FilterPattern.literal('"Task timed out"'), metricNamespace: 'DdaPortalMcp', metricName: 'LambdaTimeouts', metricValue: '1' })` and `new cloudwatch.Alarm(this, 'McpTimeoutAlarm', { metric: filter.metric({ statistic: 'sum', period: Duration.minutes(5) }), threshold: 1, evaluationPeriods: 1, treatMissingData: NOT_BREACHING })` — the only detector of a tool call the function could not answer (Error Handling, "MCP Lambda terminated by its own timeout"); no alarm action is wired (the operator attaches the portal's existing notification topic, ADMIN_GUIDE) |
| Routes | `POST\|GET\|DELETE /mcp`, `POST\|GET\|DELETE /mcp/{view}`, `GET /.well-known/oauth-protected-resource` (root PRM, review-2 H1), `GET /.well-known/oauth-protected-resource/mcp`, `GET /.well-known/oauth-protected-resource/mcp/{view}`, `GET /.well-known/oauth-authorization-server` (RFC 8414 facade, review-2 M1) → one `HttpLambdaIntegration('McpIntegration', handler, { payloadFormatVersion: PayloadFormatVersion.VERSION_2_0 })`, **all with `authorizer: new apigwv2.HttpNoneAuthorizer()`** (H4). No `$default` route. Authentication happens in the Lambda (SDK bearer middleware); the four `.well-known` GET route shapes are unauthenticated by RFC 9728/RFC 8414. The reason is recorded in a code comment and in the CDK test |
| Outputs | `McpServerUrl` (canonical), `McpViewUrls`, `McpUserPoolClientId`, `McpOAuthScope`, `McpRedirectUri`, `McpProtectedResourceMetadataUrl` (root form), `McpAuthorizationServerMetadataUrl` (`${apiEndpoint}/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server`) |

Lambda environment: `USECASES_TABLE`, `USER_ROLES_TABLE`, `AUDIT_LOG_TABLE`, `PORTAL_REGISTRY_ENFORCED` (same `portalRegistryEnforced` context resolution as `ComputeStack`), `COGNITO_USER_POOL_ID`, `COGNITO_REGION`, `COGNITO_ISSUER` (`https://cognito-idp.${REGION}.amazonaws.com/${poolId}`; the token verifier's `iss`), `COGNITO_HOSTED_UI_ORIGIN` (`https://${domain.domainName}.auth.${REGION}.amazoncognito.com`; the facade's `authorization_endpoint`/`token_endpoint`/`revocation_endpoint` are `${origin}/oauth2/authorize|token|revoke`), `MCP_AS_ISSUER` (`httpApi.apiEndpoint` — the `AWS::ApiGatewayV2::Api` `ApiEndpoint` attribute, `resource.attrApiEndpoint` in `aws-apigatewayv2/lib/http/api.js`, i.e. `https://<id>.execute-api.<region>.amazonaws.com` with **no trailing slash**; the single string that both the PRMs' `authorization_servers[0]` and the facade's `issuer` emit — D27; `discovery.py` validates the format at import so a surprise in the attribute fails loudly rather than serving a mismatched issuer), `MCP_CANONICAL_URL`, `MCP_API_HOST`, `MCP_ALLOWED_AUDIENCES` (CSV of the six endpoint URLs), `MCP_CLIENT_ID`, `MCP_REQUIRED_SCOPE`, `MCP_MUTATING_TOOLS_ENABLED` (`'true'|'false'` from context `mcpMutatingToolsEnabled`, default `'false'`, normalized by a new `mcpMutatingToolsEnabled(contextValue)` helper mirroring `portalRegistryEnforced`), `MCP_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` (CSV from context `mcpAllowedOrigins`, **empty by default** → `TransportSecuritySettings.allowed_origins=[]`; the validate-first (viii) fallback, review-5 N5), **`MCP_BACKEND_FUNCTIONS_PARAMETER`** (the SSM parameter *name* `/dda-portal/mcp/backend-functions` — D30, review-4 H1; the map itself is **never** an environment variable), `MCP_BUILD_SHA`, `MCP_SERVER_VERSION`. There is **no** `MCP_BACKEND_FUNCTIONS` variable and **no** `MCP_DELIVERED_PHASES` variable: the delivered phases are the code constant `mcp_portal.DELIVERED_PHASES` bumped by the release PR (N7). **Environment sizing (review-4 H1):** the variables above total ≈ 1.3 KB — six endpoint URLs in `MCP_ALLOWED_AUDIENCES` (≈ 400 bytes), the issuer/origin strings, three table names, ids and flags — against Lambda's 4 KB aggregate quota; `mcp-stack.test.ts` serializes the synthesized `Environment.Variables` with the token placeholders expanded to worst-case lengths (execute-api hostname 50 characters, table names 64) and asserts the total stays below 3,000 bytes so future additions are caught at test time, not at `UpdateFunctionConfiguration`.

### 2. `mcp_portal` package (new, `edge-cv-portal/backend/functions/mcp_portal/`)

| Module | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| `handler.py` | Module level: `SERVERS = {view: build_server(view) for view in VIEWS}`, `VERIFIER = CognitoAccessTokenVerifier()`, boto3 clients. `build_asgi_app() -> Starlette` composes the root app exactly as described in Decision point 2 ("Root-app composition"): fresh `StreamableHTTPSessionManager`s, six exact `Route`s under `RequireAuthMiddleware`, the eight `discovery.routes()` (root PRM, six path-suffixed PRMs, RFC 8414 facade), the root middleware list **`[AuthFailureAuditMiddleware, AuthenticationMiddleware(BearerAuthBackend(VERIFIER)), AuthContextMiddleware]` in that order — rule: Starlette's first `middleware=` entry is the outermost wrapper (`applications.py:81` wraps in `reversed()` order), and the audit wrapper must enclose the verifier whose failure it records (review-4 M1)** — and the `AsyncExitStack` lifespan. `def handler(event, context): dispatch.LAMBDA_CONTEXT = context; return Mangum(build_asgi_app(), lifespan='auto')(event, context)` — a new app (and new managers) per invocation (D26); the module-level `LAMBDA_CONTEXT` is sound because a Lambda container processes one invocation at a time (the same argument D25 rests on) and gives the dispatcher `get_remaining_time_in_millis()` for the budget guard (D32; review-5 M3). `Mount` and `Server.streamable_http_app()` are not used anywhere |
| `discovery.py` (new in revision 3; D27) | Pure functions + Starlette routes, no I/O: `prm_document(resource_url: str) -> dict` returns `{"resource": resource_url, "authorization_servers": [MCP_AS_ISSUER], "scopes_supported": [MCP_REQUIRED_SCOPE], "bearer_methods_supported": ["header"]}`; `authorization_server_document() -> dict` returns the RFC 8414 facade (`issuer: MCP_AS_ISSUER`, `authorization_endpoint: f"{COGNITO_HOSTED_UI_ORIGIN}/oauth2/authorize"`, `token_endpoint: …/oauth2/token`, `revocation_endpoint: …/oauth2/revoke`, `jwks_uri: f"{COGNITO_ISSUER}/.well-known/jwks.json"`, `response_types_supported: ["code"]`, `grant_types_supported: ["authorization_code", "refresh_token"]`, `code_challenge_methods_supported: ["S256"]`, `token_endpoint_auth_methods_supported: ["none"]`, `scopes_supported: [MCP_REQUIRED_SCOPE]`); `routes() -> list[Route]`: `GET /.well-known/oauth-protected-resource` → `prm_document(MCP_CANONICAL_URL)`, `GET /.well-known/oauth-protected-resource/mcp[/<view>]` ×6 → `prm_document(<that URL>)`, `GET /.well-known/oauth-authorization-server` → the facade; every response is `JSONResponse(..., headers={"Cache-Control": "public, max-age=3600"})`, `methods=["GET"]` only. Module-import-time assertions: `MCP_AS_ISSUER` starts with `https://`, has no path, query, fragment or trailing slash, and equals the origin of `MCP_CANONICAL_URL`; the facade's `issuer` is the very same object. Strings are used end to end — no `AnyHttpUrl` construction — so the documents carry the environment values byte-for-byte (the trailing-slash trap of D27) |
| `token_verifier.py` | `CognitoAccessTokenVerifier(TokenVerifier)`: `jwt.PyJWKClient(f"{COGNITO_ISSUER}/.well-known/jwks.json", cache_keys=True)` → `jwt.decode(token, key, algorithms=['RS256'], issuer=COGNITO_ISSUER, options={'verify_aud': False, 'require': ['exp','iat','iss','sub','token_use','client_id','scope','jti']})`; then `token_use == 'access'`, `client_id == MCP_CLIENT_ID`, `MCP_REQUIRED_SCOPE in scope.split()`, and `aud` (if present; string or list) ∩ `MCP_ALLOWED_AUDIENCES` ≠ ∅. Returns `AccessToken(token=token, client_id=client_id, scopes=[...], resource=aud_or_None, expires_at=exp, subject=sub, claims=<verified payload>)`; any failure → `None` (the SDK answers 401 + challenge). Failure classification for M10: when the **signature and issuer verified** but a claim check failed (`exp`, `token_use`, `client_id`, scope, `aud`), it stores `AuthFailure(sub, reason, client_id)` in the request-scoped `contextvars.ContextVar` `CURRENT_AUTH_FAILURE`; unsigned/unparseable/wrong-issuer tokens store nothing (no parseable Principal). Never logs the token; logs `sub` and the failure class only |
| `auth_audit.py` | `AuthFailureAuditMiddleware` (pure ASGI, **outermost — i.e. the first entry of `build_asgi_app()`'s `middleware=` list**, since Starlette wraps the list in reverse; listed last it would run *inside* `AuthenticationMiddleware`, reset the variable after the verifier had set it, and never see the failure — review-4 M1): resets `CURRENT_AUTH_FAILURE` at request start, lets the request run, and when the response status is 401 **and** a failure with a `sub` was recorded, writes one marked audit entry (`action='authentication_failed'`, see Data Models) using `sourceIp`/`userAgent` from `scope['aws.event']['requestContext']['http']` and `requestId` from `scope['aws.event']['requestContext']`. Audit write failures are swallowed (the 401 is still returned) and logged at ERROR. Requirement 4.7 — reachable because the gateway has no authorizer (H4) |
| `catalog.py` | Declarative `ToolSpec` dataclass: `name, domain, title, description (one sentence ending in '.', ≤ 300 chars — the base of the rendered description, D35), resource_type (str: 'device', 'deployment', 'model', 'workflow', 'collection', … — recorded by audit.py and named in the rendered description), input_schema (JSON Schema 2020-12 dict; a top-level `oneOf`/`anyOf` is allowed only when its branches differ in `required` alone — the exactly-one / at-least-one argument groups a route enforces, revision 9), backend (McpBackendKey), handler_module (e.g. 'devices'), handler_function (the function that implements the route, e.g. 'list_devices' — never a module entry point that dispatches to route functions, never a cross-module shim: `camera_video_pin.handler` forwards to `camera_registry.handler`, 45, so the static-video specs name `camera_registry` functions with backend `cameraVideoPin`; 'handler' only when the route is an inline branch of it; `portal_admin_whoami`, the one local spec, names `server.whoami`), method, resource, path_template, param_map (tool arg → path/query/body name, e.g. next_token → nextForwardToken for `/training/{id}/logs`, next_token → nextToken for `/builds`), permissions (list[Permission]), scope ('usecase' \| 'global' \| 'usecase_or_global' — the third mirrors `rbac_check(allow_global=True)`: the RBAC scope is `args['usecase_id']` when supplied, else `'global'`; review-3 N3; no spec uses it since revision 9 — the two that did have no global form their handlers honour), usecase_arg ('usecase_id' \| None), usecase_not_forwarded (`Literal['stored_record', 'injected', 'dead_filter'] \| None = None` — revision 11, review-10 N1: why a `usecase_id` that the handler or its decorator reads is not forwarded; `'injected'` on the ten `setdefault`-scoped specs, whose record's Use_Case is injected as the RBAC scope (Backlog 27), `'stored_record'` on `portal_data_synthetic_session_retrain`, whose session Use_Case overwrites the body value (`synthetic_data.py:1248`), `'dead_filter'` on `portal_admin_audit_logs_query`, which offers no `usecase_id` because the filter matches no entry (`audit_logs.py:84`, Backlog 25); set only where `usecase_id` has no `param_map` entry **and** the read collector finds such a read, so an exemption cannot go stale), classification ('read_only' \| 'mutating'), destructive (bool), idempotent (bool), confirm_target (arg name \| tuple of per-branch targets \| None — the tuple names one target per branch of the top-level `oneOf`, `target_devices[0]` meaning that array argument's first item; revision 10, review-9 N7), confirm_literal (str \| None — exactly one of `confirm_target`/`confirm_literal` is set when `destructive`; the validator requires `confirm == args[confirm_target]` for a name, `confirm ==` the target of the branch present for a tuple, else `confirm == confirm_literal` — review-3 N2), handler_audits (bool: the backing route's success path writes ≥1 audit entry itself — an audit call whose `result` is literal `'success'`/`'pending'`, absent, or non-literal, found on the call graph the classification walk covers; `'denied'`/`'failure'`/`'failed'`/`'rejected'` calls do not count — review-2 M2; a spec whose success path audits on some branches only is pinned `handler_audits=False` in the test's `PINNED_AUDIT_FLAGS` table with its reason, so the envelope always records the call — `portal_devices_shared_components_provision` (only its daemon thread writes the success entry, `shared_components.py:839–849`), `portal_data_labeling_job_create` (only the DDA backend audits), `portal_data_synthetic_previews_approve` (only `approved` audits, `synthetic_data.py:975–986`), `portal_workflows_plugin_generate_message` (only an accept turn audits); on the auditing branch such a call carries the handler's entry plus the envelope, the documented exception to the `max(k, 1)` count), handler_audits_denials (bool: a 403 from the route is already audited — the route is decorated with rbac_check/require_permission/super_user_only/require_portal_admin/require_builds_*, or a function on its call graph writes a `result='denied'` entry **and builds the 403 itself** — `model_converter._refused_model_source`'s `'denied'` entry belongs to a 400, 108–125, and does not count), audit_args (identifier args recorded in details), accepted_writes (tuple[AcceptedWrite, ...] = () — `AcceptedWrite(function='<module>.<function>', call='<verb>', receiver='<receiver source text>', count=<n>)`, the write calls the classification test tolerates on an `RO` spec, matched on `(function, call, receiver)` with exactly `count` occurrences — the line number is documentation in the row note only (revision 10, review-9 N2); allowed only together with `accepted_writes_note = STATUS_SYNC_NOTE`, the fixed status-sync note of Decision point 4; review-8 H1), bind_usecase_path (str \| None — a dotted path into the 2xx body naming the object's own Use_Case, D36; `'job.usecase_id'` on `portal_data_labeling_job_get`; revision 10, review-9 N5), phase (1\|2\|3), result_shape ('object' \| 'collection'), collection_key, deprecated_alias (str \| None), alias_removed_in (str \| None — semver of the release that drops the alias; set iff `deprecated_alias` is set, rendered into the alias's description — D35, review-5 M4), status_tool (str \| None — **required for every `M`/`M!` spec, `None` for `RO`**: the name of the `RO` tool in the same catalog that observes the affected resource, used in the "operation may have been applied; call `<status_tool>` before retrying" message after a `timeout` or post-dispatch `backend_failure` — D32, review-4 M2; Property 2 checks it names an existing `RO` tool), limit_max (int \| None) and limit_default (int \| None — review-6 M1: when set, the schema generator renders the `limit` property as `{"type": "integer", "minimum": 1, "maximum": limit_max or 200, "default": limit_default or 50}` instead of the catalog-wide 200/50, so a spec never offers a page size its route rejects or a result the bound would cut — `portal_data_datasets_preview_get` 50, `portal_data_captures_list` 30 against the handler's 100 because each capture carries presigned URLs, revision 9)`, resource_id_path (str \| None — a dotted path to a nested created id, §2 `shaping.py`; revision 9). **Argument ↔ route rule (review-6 M5; review-7 M3):** every `param_map` target, and every schema argument without a `param_map` entry (passed under its own name), must name a parameter the backing route reads — for the portal, a `params.get(k)` / `body.get(k)` / `body[k]` / `k in body` read in `handler_function` or a function on its call graph, where the read collector **folds** `k` (revision 10, review-9 N3): a string literal; a module-level string constant, same-module or imported (`Name` or `module.CONST`); and each element of a tuple/list/set — module-level or function-local — that a loop or comprehension iterates, a tuple target over pairs included (`for field, coerce in ((SAMPLE_EXPORT_FIELD, …), …)`, `tuning_settings.py:151–154`); the call graph also follows a `getattr(<imported module>, '<literal>')` bound to a name and then called (`labeling._delegate_dda_job_creation` → `dda_labeling.create_dda_job`, 264–271); and a **path** parameter is not looked for among the reads: a `param_map` target that fills one — written in the row or derived by the bare-identifier rule of the Tool-catalogs conventions, which `catalog.py` applies at import and which raises when two bare identifiers would claim one path parameter (revision 11, review-10 N2) — must be a `{name}` segment of the spec's `resource` template, which the route test pins to the registered method and walks from the entry point to `handler_function`, because entry points read `pathParameters` and pass the value positionally (`usecases.handler` → `get_usecase(path_parameters['id'], user)`, 1303–1304; 67 route functions at `c3bf88c1` receive their path identifier that way) — and every parameter the handler **requires** (a `400 … required` branch over a `queryStringParameters`/body read whose variable is not reassigned between the read and the check, e.g. `captures.py:125–128` — `create_folder`'s `bucket` is reassigned at 479–480 and so is optional, review-8 N1 — or a `validate_required_fields(body, [...])` list, e.g. `training.py:1699`) must be in `input_schema.required`, directly or through every branch of a `oneOf`/`anyOf` group; Property 2 checks both by AST. The only arguments exempt from the first half are the **`SERVER_CONSUMED_ARGS = ('confirm', 'usecase_id', 'limit', 'offset')`** — consumed by the validator (`confirm`), the pre-dispatch RBAC check (`usecase_id`) and the shaper (`limit`/`offset` on specs whose route does not paginate). The dispatcher's rule for them is mechanical: **a `SERVER_CONSUMED_ARGS` member is forwarded iff `param_map` names it** — `usecase_id → usecase_id` (query) or `usecase_id → id` (path) where the handler reads it, `limit → limit`/`next_token → nextToken` where the route paginates — so forwarded pagination always carries an explicit `param_map` entry even when the names coincide, and nothing in the set is ever passed implicitly; the test checks the mapped targets like any other and skips only the unmapped members, and it checks the converse (revision 10, review-9 M1/M2/N4): when the handler reads `usecase_id` — itself or through its `rbac_check`/`require_permission` decorator — the spec maps it, unless it sets `usecase_not_forwarded` or is a `G` spec whose decorator falls back to `'global'` (`rbac_check(allow_global=True)`, `require_builds_*` — exempt by scope; revision 11, review-10 N1 — the row prose alone was not machine-readable); a handler that reads none needs no exemption, because the clause cannot fire and the collector's finding is the check; when the handler reads `limit` or `offset`, or the spec maps a cursor (`next_token`), `limit` — and `offset` when the handler reads it — has a `param_map` entry; and a spec whose `limit` is dispatcher-sliced maps no cursor and offers `offset`. **Classification rule (review-7 H1; review-8 H1):** a spec is `read_only` only if the walk of Decision point 4 — every function `handler_function` reaches in its own module and in the other `functions/*.py` modules — finds no write call (the named write verbs and the `create_`/`update_`/`delete_`/`put_`/`cancel_`/`start_`/`stop_`/`terminate_`/`reboot_`/`reset_` prefixes on any receiver that is not an imported module, and the derived `SHARED_LAYER_WRITERS`) outside `accepted_writes`, each entry matched on function, call and receiver with its count (`test_mcp_catalog_classification.py`); there is no audit-result clause. The declared `permissions` admit a role set that is a subset of the set the handler admits, the handler's set being recorded per row (`test_mcp_catalog_permissions.py`). `CATALOG: tuple[ToolSpec, ...]` is the single source of truth (tables below); `tools_for_view(view, delivered_phases, mutating_enabled)` returns the deterministic `(domain, name)`-sorted list of `Tool` objects, in which every included spec that has a `deprecated_alias` contributes a **second** entry under the alias name (same schema and annotations, description prefixed `Deprecated alias of <name>; removed in <alias_removed_in>.`, sorted by its own name — D35); `catalog_routes() -> list[dict]` returns the sorted, de-duplicated `{"method", "resource", "backend"}` triples of every spec that has a backing route (`portal_admin_whoami` is local and contributes none; the `resource` is the REST template string the handler routes on, e.g. `/admin/users/{username}/role`), and `python -m mcp_portal.catalog --export-routes` writes them to the checked-in `mcp_portal/catalog_routes.json`, which the Python side pins to the catalog and the CDK side pins to the synthesized route tables (review-3 M1; Testing Strategy); `to_mcp_tool(spec)` renders `mcp.types.Tool(name, title, description=render_description(spec), input_schema, annotations=ToolAnnotations(read_only_hint=..., destructive_hint=..., idempotent_hint=..., open_world_hint=False))`, where **`render_description(spec)` = `f"{spec.description} Touches: {spec.resource_type}{' in the Use_Case named by usecase_id' if spec.usecase_arg else ''}. {classification_text(spec)}"`** and `classification_text` is exactly `"Read-only."` for `RO`, `"MUTATING."` for `M`, and `f"MUTATING, DESTRUCTIVE — requires confirm={confirm_text(spec)}."` for `M!`, where `confirm_text` is the target name, `'<' + ' \| '.join(targets) + '>'` for a per-branch tuple (`requires confirm=<target_thing_group \| target_devices[0]>`, review-9 N7), else `repr(spec.confirm_literal)` (D35; Requirements 1.2, 5.1; Property 2) |
| `server.py` | `build_server(view) -> Server`: `Server(name=f"dda-portal-mcp/{view}", version=MCP_SERVER_VERSION, instructions=<view-specific usage text>, cache_hints={'tools/list': CacheHint(ttl_ms=300_000, scope='private')}, on_list_tools=..., on_call_tool=...)` (N1: the field is `scope`); `on_call_tool` runs the pipeline `authenticate → lookup → validate → gate → authorize → dispatch → audit → log → shape` |
| `principal.py` | `principal_from_access_token(AccessToken) -> Principal(sub, username, email, claims)`; `email` from a per-container LRU over `cognito-idp:AdminGetUser(UserPoolId, Username=username)`; failure → `'unknown'`; builds the `user` dict exactly as `get_user_from_event` would (`user_id=sub, username, email, role='Viewer'`). The `AdminGetUser` response also carries the account's `custom:role` attribute; this module **deliberately reads only `email`** (D29 option (A)) — reading `custom:role` into `user_info['role']`/the forwarded claims is option (C), Backlog 12, and is not implemented unless the owner selects it at sign-off item (e) |
| `authz.py` | `authorize(spec, principal, usecase_id) -> None \| Denied \| Unavailable` using `shared_utils.rbac_manager.has_permission(user_id, scope, permission, user_info=user)` for each declared permission (any-of, like `rbac_check`), where `scope` is `args['usecase_id']` for `'usecase'` tools, `'global'` for `'global'` tools, and `args.get('usecase_id') or 'global'` for `'usecase_or_global'` tools (review-3 N3) — the real resolver (`get_user_role` → registry or `_legacy_role`) is the oracle, so the enforcement-mode behaviour of D29 is inherited, not re-implemented; also returns the `identity_source` (`attribution_from(mcp_event, user, usecase_id)['identity_source']`, one GetItem) that `dispatch` places in `event['mcp']`; writes the `unauthorized_access` / `authorization_unavailable` entries with `log_audit_event(..., identity=attribution_from(event=mcp_event, user, usecase_id))` where `mcp_event` is the synthesized event (so `source_ip`, `user_agent`, `identity_source`, and the marker are all populated). Details mirror `rbac_check`'s (`required_permissions`, `usecase_id`, `user_role`, `claimed_role`, plus `mcp_tool`) |
| `dispatch.py` | `build_backend_event(spec, args, principal, mcp_ctx) -> dict` (REST proxy v1 shape, see Data Models; `claims` is the verified payload object itself, never merged with anything) and `invoke(spec, event) -> BackendResponse(status, body_json, headers)` via `LAMBDA.invoke(FunctionName=backend_functions()[spec.backend], InvocationType='RequestResponse', Payload=json.dumps(event))`. **Client (D32; review-4 M2, review-5 M3):** module-level `LAMBDA = boto3.client('lambda', config=botocore.config.Config(connect_timeout=2, read_timeout=20, retries={'total_max_attempts': 1}))` — exactly one attempt; botocore would otherwise re-send `Invoke` after a client-side `ReadTimeoutError` (legacy `GENERAL_CONNECTION_ERROR` list / standard `HTTPClientError` class, Research baseline) and run a mutating handler twice. **Budget guard:** `LAMBDA_CONTEXT = None` at module level, set by `handler()` per invocation; `invoke()` first evaluates `remaining = LAMBDA_CONTEXT.get_remaining_time_in_millis() if LAMBDA_CONTEXT else None` and returns `ToolFailure('timeout', 'insufficient time budget to start the backing operation; retry')` — the plain message, `effect` absent, because nothing ran — without calling `LAMBDA.invoke` when `remaining is not None and remaining < MIN_DISPATCH_BUDGET_MS` (`24_000`; the function timeout is 29 s, so up to 5 s of cold start and pre-dispatch calls are tolerated, and 2 s remain after a maxed-out 22 s invoke for the envelope, telemetry and response). A post-dispatch `ReadTimeoutError` → `timeout`; `FunctionError` in the response, a transport error or a non-JSON payload → `backend_failure`; for `M`/`M!` specs both post-dispatch messages name `spec.status_tool` (see `errors.py`). **Dispatch map (D30; review-4 H1):** `backend_functions() -> dict[str, str]` loads `json.loads(SSM.get_parameter(Name=os.environ['MCP_BACKEND_FUNCTIONS_PARAMETER'])['Parameter']['Value'])` **once per container, lazily on the first tool call** (a module-level cache populated under a lock; `SSM = boto3.client('ssm', config=Config(connect_timeout=2, read_timeout=5, retries={'total_max_attempts': 3}))` — the read is idempotent, so retries are safe here), so cold-start cost is one `GetParameter` and unit tests patch the module-level clients without touching the environment; a backend key absent from the map, or a `GetParameter` failure, → `backend_failure` with the key / `'dispatch map unavailable'` in the message and an ERROR log; the cached map is refreshed inside a container in exactly one situation: when `invoke` raises `ResourceNotFoundException` (the backing function's ARN changed because CloudFormation replaced that function — the one `Invoke` error that proves **nothing ran**), `backend_functions(refresh=True)` reloads the parameter once and the invoke is issued once more with the fresh ARN; any other failure is never retried, so the D32 at-most-one-execution guarantee holds (a new catalog key always ships with new Lambda code, which replaces the containers anyway) |
| `audit.py` | `write_envelope(spec, principal, mcp_ctx, outcome, duration_ms, backend_status, resource_id)`: `log_audit_event(user_id=sub, action='mcp_tool_call', resource_type=spec.resource_type, resource_id=<id arg or 'collection'>, result='success'\|'failure'\|'denied', details=sanitize_audit_details({'tool': spec.name, 'usecase_id': ..., 'status': backend_status, 'error_category': ..., 'duration_ms': ..., <audit_args>}), identity=attribution_from(event=mcp_event, user, usecase_id))`. Envelope rules (D6, N8): written for every **pre-dispatch** outcome other than a denial/unavailability (those write their own marked entries); after dispatch, written when `status < 300 and not spec.handler_audits`, or when `status >= 300` **except** `status == 403 and spec.handler_audits_denials` (the decorated handler already wrote a marked `unauthorized_access`), and always when the D36 binding check refused a 2xx body — `result='denied'`, `details.reason='usecase_binding'`, `details.status` = the backend's 2xx status — even when `handler_audits` is True, because the handler's own entry records a read that MCP did not deliver (revision 10, review-9 N5). On a handler-side failure the handler may also have written its own marked failure entry; that duplicate is marked, never unmarked, and is documented. **Count contract (review-3 N4):** when the backing handler writes `k` entries on success, the total for that completion is exactly `max(k, 1)` marked entries — the handler's `k` when `k ≥ 1` (no envelope), the one envelope when `k = 0`; "exactly one *MCP-originated* entry" in Requirement 16.4 is read as "exactly one entry **added by the MCP layer** when the handler writes none, and none when it does"; the one exception besides `PINNED_AUDIT_FLAGS` is the D36 refusal, whose total is `k + 1` — the handler's `k` entries plus the `denied` envelope (Property 6; revision 11, review-10 N4) |
| `telemetry.py` | `log_tool_completion(...)` emits exactly one JSON line per tool completion to CloudWatch through the Lambda logger (Requirement 6.3, M9): `{"event": "mcp_tool", "sub", "username", "tool", "view", "outcome", "category", "status", "duration_ms", "request_id", "client", "server_version"}` — no arguments, no token, no email. Lambda's standard invocation/error/duration metrics come for free |
| `errors.py` | `ToolFailure(category, message, status=None)` (`status`: the backend's HTTP status where there is one; both D36 outcomes pass `status=404` — revision 12, review-11 N2) → `CallToolResult(is_error=True, content=[TextContent(text=f"{category}: {message}")], structured_content={'category', 'message', 'request_id', 'status', 'effect'?, 'status_tool'?})`; **`effect_unknown_message(spec, category)`** (D32; review-4 M2): for a Mutating_Tool whose dispatch ended in `timeout` or a post-dispatch `backend_failure` (transport error, `FunctionError`, malformed payload — i.e. the backing handler may have run), the text is `f"{category}: the operation may have been applied; call {spec.status_tool} before retrying"` and `structured_content` carries `effect: "unknown"` and `status_tool: spec.status_tool`; a pre-dispatch failure or an `RO` tool uses the plain message (the effect is known: none). `map_backend_status(status, body) → category` (400/422 → `invalid_input`, 401 → `not_authenticated`, 403 → `not_authorized`, 404 → `not_found`, 409 → `conflict`, 429 → `throttled`, 5xx → `backend_failure`) with the handler's `error` string passed through after a denylist scrub (anything matching `Traceback\|File "` is replaced by the category text; bearer-like strings removed) |
| `shaping.py` | **Use_Case binding first (D36; revision 10, review-9 N5):** on a 2xx of a spec with `bind_usecase_path`, `check_binding(spec, body, usecase_id)` resolves the dotted path in the parsed body (the `drop_fields` path syntax); when the value is absent or is not the string the pre-dispatch check authorized, the body is dropped and the call returns `ToolFailure('not_found', 'no such object in the named Use_Case', status=404)` (`outcome.binding_refused = True` for `audit.py`); a backend 404 on a `bind_usecase_path` spec returns that same failure, `status=404` included, instead of `map_backend_status`'s scrubbed handler text, so the two results are identical except `request_id` (revision 11, review-10 N3;
