revision 12, review-11 N2: the real backend status goes only to the `mcp_tool` line and the envelope's `details.status`); then pagination/limit application for `result_shape == 'collection'` (`limit`, `offset`, `next_token` forwarded through `param_map` when the route supports them: `GET /audit-logs` uses `limit`/`next_token`, `GET /training/{id}/logs` uses `limit`/`nextForwardToken` — `training.py:1341`, N10 as corrected in revision 9; `GET /builds` and `/builds/{id}/logs` use `limit`/`nextToken`, `GET /datasets/preview` uses `offset`/`limit` and `GET /captures` uses `limit` — revision 10, review-9 M2; otherwise sliced in the dispatcher, which then honours `offset` too — review-9 N4), `MAX_RESULT_BYTES` truncation with `{truncated: true, returned, total_hint}` — **except** on a spec whose pagination is forwarded (`next_token` in `param_map`): a page that would exceed the bound is not cut, because the route's token already points past the whole page; the call returns `result_too_large` naming a smaller `limit` (revision 9) — and extraction of `created_id` / `job_id` from well-known response keys (`deployment_id`, `training_id`, `job_id`, `model_id`, `workflow_id`, `compilation_job_name`, `build_id`, `run_id`, `package_id`) or, when the spec sets `resource_id_path` (a dotted path, revision 9), from that nested key (`dataset.dataset_id`, `team.team_id`, `session.session_id`, `test_run.test_run_id`, `simulation_run.run_id`, `server.server_id`, `jobs[0].build_job_id`, `job.build_job_id`) into `structured_content.resource_id` (1.4) |
| `__init__.py` | `__version__` (semver string, also `MCP_SERVER_VERSION`'s default), `DELIVERED_PHASES: tuple[int, ...] = (1,)` (bumped to `(1, 2)`, `(1, 2, 3)` by the release PR that ships each phase — N7), `PROTOCOL_REVISIONS = ('2026-07-28', '2025-11-25', '2025-06-18', '2025-03-26')`, `VIEWS = ('data', 'models', 'devices', 'workflows', 'admin')` |

### 3. Shared-layer and audit-log changes (small, additive; `layers/shared/python/shared_utils.py`)

- **Marker fields.** New constant `MCP_MARKER_FIELDS = ('channel', 'mcp_tool', 'mcp_server', 'mcp_client', 'mcp_request_id')`. `ATTRIBUTION_FIELDS` is **unchanged** (five fields): `tests/test_portal_registry_units.py` asserts that `attribution_from` returns exactly those five keys for ordinary events, and that must keep holding.
- **`attribution_from(event, user, usecase_id, identity_source)`.** When `event.get('mcp')` is a dict, the returned mapping additionally contains the five marker fields (`channel='mcp'`, the other four from the block, `'unknown'` when absent) and `email` falls back to `event['mcp']['principal_email']` when the claims carry none; when `identity_source` is not passed and the block carries one, it is used (no registry read). For events without an `mcp` block the return value is byte-identical to today.
- **Request audit context (D25).** Module-level `_REQUEST_AUDIT_CONTEXT: Optional[dict] = None`. `get_user_from_event(event)` calls `_remember_request_event(event)` first, storing `{'event': event, 'invocation': os.environ.get('_X_AMZN_TRACE_ID')}` on every call (browser invocations overwrite it with a marker-less event), **except** when the stored event carries an `mcp` block, the new one does not, and the trace id is unchanged — then the stored event is kept (D25's in-process-synthesis rule). `_current_request_mcp_event() -> Optional[dict]` returns the remembered event only if its `invocation` equals the current `_X_AMZN_TRACE_ID` value **and** it carries a dict `mcp` block; otherwise `None`. `_attribution_attributes(identity, event, usecase_id)`: when both `identity` and `event` are `None`, it substitutes `event = _current_request_mcp_event()`; it then produces the five Attribution_Fields as today, plus `channel` (`identity.get('channel', 'api')`) on **every** entry and the four `mcp_*` attributes only when `channel == 'mcp'`. `log_audit_event`, `record_audit_event_strict`, and `finalize_audit_event` need no signature change — they already call `_attribution_attributes(identity, event)`. Net effect: every audit entry written by a backing handler during an MCP-originated invocation is marked, whether or not the handler passes `identity`/`event`; entries written during browser invocations are unchanged apart from the new `channel='api'` attribute. In pytest (no Lambda runtime) `_X_AMZN_TRACE_ID` is absent on both sides, so the context is honoured; a test sets and changes the variable to prove the binding.
- **`functions/audit_logs.py:get_audit_logs`** accepts `channel=api|mcp` and `mcp_tool=<name>` query filters, appended to the existing `filter_expressions` list that the handler joins with `' AND '` (`audit_logs.py:122–170`). Because entries written before this change carry **no** `channel` attribute (up to 90 days of them under the TTL), the `api` filter is the parenthesized clause `(attribute_not_exists(#channel) OR #channel = :channel)` with `:channel = 'api'`, so pre-upgrade history stays visible under "Web & API"; the `mcp` filter is `#channel = :channel` with `:channel = 'mcp'`; `mcp_tool=<name>` adds `#mcp_tool = :mcp_tool` (`ExpressionAttributeNames` gain `#channel`/`#mcp_tool`; review-3 N5). `get_available_actions()` gains `'mcp_tool_call'`, `'authentication_failed'` and `'authorization_unavailable'`; `frontend/src/pages/AuditLogs.tsx` gets a channel `Select` (All / Web & API / MCP) and shows `mcp_tool` in the details column (4.6). No table or GSI change (F5).

### 4. Request pipeline (portal `on_call_tool`)

1. **Authenticate.** `get_access_token()` (SDK context var) must be non-`None`; the SDK already answered 401 for the HTTP path, so `None` here means an in-memory test client → `not_authenticated`.
2. **Lookup.** Unknown name → SDK protocol error (`-32602`), unless it matches a `deprecated_alias` (then the current spec is used and the result carries `deprecation` text, 14.4).
3. **Validate.** `jsonschema.Draft202012Validator(spec.input_schema).validate(args)` → `invalid_input` naming the failing property; `usecase_id` is required by schema for every `scope == 'usecase'` tool (5.6) and optional for `usecase_or_global` tools; for destructive tools `confirm` must equal `args[spec.confirm_target]` for a name, the target of the branch present for a per-branch tuple (`portal_devices_deployment_create`: `args['target_thing_group']`, or `args['target_devices'][0]` — the schema runs first and requires `minItems: 1` and a non-empty `THING_NAME_PATTERN` on both targets, so the index always exists and `[]`/`""` are `invalid_input`; revision 11, review-10 N5), else `spec.confirm_literal` (D9; review-3 N2, review-9 N7) → `confirmation_required`. Every schema has `additionalProperties: false`, so no argument can be named like a claim.
4. **Gate.** `spec.phase ∉ DELIVERED_PHASES` → `-32602` unknown tool (17.3); `classification == 'mutating' and not MCP_MUTATING_TOOLS_ENABLED` → `mutating_tools_disabled` (5.4; the tool is also absent from `tools/list`).
5. **Authorize.** `authz.authorize` (D5). On `Denied` → audit + `not_authorized`; on `Unavailable` → audit + `authorization_unavailable`.
6. **Dispatch.** `dispatch.invoke` — which first applies the remaining-time guard (`< 24_000 ms` → `timeout`, nothing invoked; D32, review-5 M3) — with `mcp_ctx = {request_id: ctx.request_id, client: client_label(ctx), server: f"dda-portal-mcp/{view}@{version}", tool: spec.name, source_ip, user_agent, principal_email, identity_source}`, where `ctx` is the SDK's `ServerRequestContext` handed to `on_call_tool` (`mcp/server/context.py`: fields `session`, `request_id`, `meta`, `protocol_version`, …) and **`client_label(ctx)`** (shared helper, identical in `mcp_edge`; review-2 N4) resolves the MCP client identity in this order: (a) `ctx.meta.get(mcp_types.CLIENT_INFO_META_KEY)` — the 2026-07-28 per-request `_meta["io.modelcontextprotocol/clientInfo"]` object (`mcp_types/_types.py:59`), rendered `f"{info['name']}/{info.get('version', '?')}"`; (b) else `ctx.session.client_params.client_info` (`ServerSession.client_params`, `mcp/server/session.py:65`, the handshake-era `initialize` params — populated for legacy clients only while a session exists, so `None` under `stateless=True`), rendered the same way; (c) else `'unknown'`. The result is capped at 128 characters and restricted to printable ASCII before it enters any audit or log record.
7. **Audit.** For a spec with `bind_usecase_path`, `shaping.check_binding` runs on the 2xx body first (D36), so a refused body is audited as `denied` and step 9 returns `not_found` without it; then the envelope per the rules in `audit.py`.
8. **Log.** `telemetry.log_tool_completion(...)` (6.3).
9. **Shape.** `BackendResponse.status < 300` → `CallToolResult(content=[TextContent(json)], structured_content={result: <shaped body>, resource_id?, truncated?})`, unless step 7 refused the body, which returns `not_found` "no such object in the named Use_Case" (D36; revision 11, review-10 N4); otherwise `map_backend_status` → error result (on a `bind_usecase_path` spec a 404 returns the same D36 failure, `status=404` on both — review-10 N3, review-11 N2). 2xx bodies are parsed with `json.loads`; non-JSON 2xx bodies become `{"text": body}`.

`on_list_tools` returns `ListToolsResult(tools=[to_mcp_tool(s) for s in tools_for_view(...)])`; the SDK stamps `ttlMs`/`cacheScope` from `cache_hints` and `serverInfo` into `_meta` (1.7, 15.3).

## Components and Interfaces — Edge

### 5. `src/backend/mcp_edge/` package (new; runs in **both** interpreters)

The package is importable by the Backend_API process (supervisor, config, proxy helpers: stdlib + the backend's existing `httpx`/`fastapi`) and by the child process (`__main__`, server, catalog, role gate: the venv's `mcp`). The split is enforced by module: nothing under `mcp_edge/child/` is imported by `app.py`, and nothing under `mcp_edge/child/` imports `utils`, `local_auth`, or `dao` (a unit test asserts both, so the child can never read the credential cache, session secret, or `authorization_settings.json` — Requirement 10.8).

| Module | Runs in | Responsibility |
|---|---|---|
| `config.py` | backend | `McpConfig` built on the `local_auth.config` pattern: keys `McpServerEnabled` and `McpMutatingToolsEnabled` selected from the dict returned by the new shared function `local_auth.config.read_component_config_via_ipc()` (see §6), parsed with `local_auth.config.parse_local_login_enabled` (reused as the strict true-value parser, 12.1), plus the **optional** `McpAllowedOrigins` (CSV of `scheme://host[:port]` values; absent or empty ⇒ `[]`; not in any recipe's `DefaultConfiguration` — the validate-first (viii) fallback, review-5 N5), own 30 s daemon poller (`LocalLoginConfig`'s structure: `refresh()`, `start()`, `stop()`, transitions logged once, read failure ⇒ both disabled, 12.2). Exposes `server_enabled`, `mutating_enabled`, `allowed_origins`, and `on_change(callback)`; a change of `allowed_origins` restarts the child like a change of `mutating_enabled` |
| `supervisor.py` | backend | `McpSupervisor(config, backend_base_url, features, version, component_version)`: daemon thread; `desired_state` from `McpConfig`; spawns `[VENV_PYTHON, '-m', 'mcp_edge.child', '--port', '5790', '--backend-url', backend_base_url, '--server-version', f"{version}+{component_version}" /* Requirement 15.3: serverInfo.version aligned to the LocalServer component version, e.g. 1.0.0+1.0.130 — review-5 N7 */, *(['--allowed-origins', ','.join(config.allowed_origins)] if config.allowed_origins else []), *(['--features', 'vllm'] if vllm else []), *(['--mutating-enabled'] if mutating else [])]` with `cwd=BACKEND_ROOT`, `env={'PYTHONPATH': BACKEND_ROOT, 'PATH': ..., 'DDA_MCP_LOG_FORMAT': 'jsonl'}` (no AWS/Greengrass variables are inherited; `BACKEND_ROOT = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(mcp_edge.__file__)))`, `/` in the image — Decision point 3 "Process model", review-2 N6), `stdout=PIPE`, `stderr=PIPE`, `start_new_session=True`; readiness = `GET http://127.0.0.1:5790/healthz` within 20 s; restart with backoff 1→60 s, `restart_count`, `last_exit_code`, `last_error` for `/mcp/status`; `stop()` sends SIGTERM, waits 10 s, then SIGKILL. All exceptions are caught and logged; the thread never dies (12.3) |
| `log_relay.py` | backend | Reads child stdout lines; each JSON line → `structlog.stdlib.get_logger('mcp.access').info(...)` with the fields in Data Models; malformed lines → `mcp.child` logger at INFO; stderr lines → `mcp.child` at WARNING. Because stderr is where the child's `logging` output lands, the relay applies the same scrub rule as a last line of defence: a stderr line containing `token=` (case-insensitive) is replaced by `"<redacted: credential-bearing line dropped>"` before it is logged (review-2 M4). Any relay exception is swallowed (11.4). The backend's own `httpx` 0.24.1 logs the same `HTTP Request:` line at INFO for the proxy's upstream call, but that URL (`http://127.0.0.1:5790/mcp`) carries no credential — no action beyond this note |
| `proxy.py` | backend | `forward(body: bytes, headers, correlation_id) -> Response`: header allowlist (`Origin` is never forwarded), `X-Request-ID` injection, then `req = CLIENT.build_request('POST', 'http://127.0.0.1:5790/mcp', content=body, headers=...)`; `resp = CLIENT.send(req, stream=True)` with `timeout=httpx.Timeout(connect=2.0, write=10.0, read=MCP_PROXY_UPSTREAM_READ_S, pool=2.0)`, so the upstream status and `Content-Type` are known before any response object exists (**`MCP_PROXY_UPSTREAM_READ_S = 150.0`** — above the largest per-spec backend timeout the child applies, `edge_textgen_generate.timeout_s = 130`, itself above the route's own 120 s wall clock, so the child, not the proxy, is always the first to give up and the client receives an `isError` `timeout` rather than a `504` — review-6 N4). `CLIENT = httpx.Client(transport=httpx.HTTPTransport(retries=0), timeout=...)` — the backend's `httpx` 0.24.1 default, written out so a proxied `tools/call` is forwarded to the child **exactly once** (D32; review-4 M2; a retry here would re-run a mutating tool). **Two relay modes (review-3 N8):** (a) when the upstream `Content-Type` is **not** `text/event-stream` — the only case the child produces under `json_response=True` — the body is **buffered** (`resp.read()`, capped at `MCP_PROXY_MAX_UPSTREAM_BODY = 4 MiB` → `502 {"error":"mcp_bad_upstream"}`) and returned as a plain `Response(content, status_code=resp.status_code, media_type=...)`, so a read timeout anywhere in the body is caught before headers are sent and maps to `504 mcp_timeout`; (b) when it **is** `text/event-stream` (defensive only; unreachable with the child's configuration), `StreamingResponse(_relay(resp), ...)` with `_relay` = `try: yield from resp.iter_raw() finally: resp.close()` and read timeout `None` — here a mid-stream timeout can only truncate the stream, never change the already-sent status (documented, Error Handling). Hop-by-hop headers dropped; connection refused → `503 {"error":"mcp_unavailable"}`. The request body is read upstream of this function by the route's async dependency (review-2 N5; §6). Called from a **synchronous** route function, so `httpx` 0.24.1's blocking I/O runs in FastAPI's threadpool, never on the event loop (N11) |
| `status.py` | backend | Pure `build_status(config, supervisor, version, component_version) -> dict` for `GET /mcp/status` (12.8) |
| `child/__main__.py` | child | argparse → **`configure_logging()`** → `build_server(...)`; `uvicorn.run(app, host='127.0.0.1', port=args.port, log_config=None, access_log=False)` (the venv's uvicorn); `--help`/`--version` print and exit. **`configure_logging()` (review-2 M4, Requirement 9.7):** the venv's `httpx2` 2.14.0 logs `'HTTP Request: %s %s "%s %d %s"'` with the **full `request.url` including the query string** at `INFO` to logger `httpx2` on every response (`httpx2/_client.py:1084–1091` sync, `1923–1930` async), which for the five `credential_in_query` tools would be the `?token=<credential>` loopback URL; so the child (1) never calls `logging.basicConfig(level=INFO)` or otherwise lowers the root below `WARNING` — `logging.getLogger().setLevel(logging.WARNING)` is set explicitly; (2) sets `logging.getLogger('httpx2').setLevel(logging.WARNING)` and `logging.getLogger('httpcore2').setLevel(logging.WARNING)` (the latter covers `httpcore2.connection\|http11\|http2\|proxy\|socks`), plus `uvicorn`, `uvicorn.access`, `uvicorn.error`, `mcp` at `WARNING`; (3) installs a root-level `logging.Filter` (`CredentialScrubFilter`) on every root handler that **drops** any record whose formatted message contains `token=`, `Authorization`, or `Bearer ` (case-insensitive) — belt and braces against any library or future code path; (4) routes the root handler to **stderr** (the supervisor relays stderr to the application log at WARNING) and reserves **stdout** exclusively for `child/logging.emit` JSON lines. The child's own structured records are therefore the only INFO-level output, and they are built by the whitelisting record builder |
| `child/server.py` | child | `Server(name='dda-edge-mcp', version=args.server_version /* "<catalog>+<component>", N7 */, instructions=..., cache_hints={'tools/list': CacheHint(ttl_ms=60_000, scope='private')}, on_list_tools, on_call_tool)`; `app = server.streamable_http_app(streamable_http_path='/mcp', json_response=True, stateless_http=True, host='127.0.0.1', transport_security=TransportSecuritySettings(enable_dns_rebinding_protection=True, allowed_hosts=['127.0.0.1:5790', 'localhost:5790'], allowed_origins=args.allowed_origins /* [] unless --allowed-origins is given — review-5 N5 */), max_request_body_size=MCP_PROXY_MAX_BODY, custom_starlette_routes=[Route('/healthz', healthz, methods=['GET'])])` — the SDK helper **is** appropriate here (one server, long-lived uvicorn process, no auth, no PRM). The `transport_security` argument is **explicit** (review-3 M3): left `None`, the SDK's loopback defaults would accept `Origin: http://127.0.0.1:*`/`http://localhost:*`, contradicting the proxy's "any `Origin` → 403" rule; with `allowed_origins=[]` any `Origin` is 403 at the child too, `allowed_hosts` admits the proxy's `Host: 127.0.0.1:5790` (and `localhost:5790` for local tooling) and nothing else (421), and `max_request_body_size` mirrors the proxy's 1 MiB cap (413) so a direct caller cannot exceed what the proxy allows. No `auth=`/`token_verifier=`: the child enforces role gating, not authentication. **Trust boundary (D28):** bound to loopback, which under `network_mode: host` is reachable by any local process — the proxy is a convenience and audit point, not the boundary; the child never acts on its own authority, because every `whoami` and route call carries the caller's credential to the Backend_API, where `authorize_request`/`authorize_credential` decide (D12, Property 16), so a direct caller gains nothing it could not get from the Backend_API itself, but its `/mcp` request gets no proxy `api.access` record (its tool completions are still logged by the child's `mcp.access` line) |
| `child/catalog.py` | child | `ToolSpec` with the same fields as the portal's (including `description`/`resource_type`/`deprecated_alias`/`alias_removed_in` rendered by the same `render_description` rule with the scope text `" on this device"` — D35; `confirm_target`/`confirm_literal` under the one D9 rule — review-3 N2; `status_tool` required for every `M`/`M!` spec — D32, review-4 M2; no `scope`/`permissions`, the edge gate is `role_policy`) plus `feature` (`None` or `'vllm'`), `role_policy` (`'read' \| 'mutate'`), **`accepted_effects: tuple[str, ...] = ()`** (revision 10, review-9 N1 — `'<module>.<function>:<call>'` effect calls the transitive classification walk tolerates on an `RO` spec, set only together with `accepted_effects_note = ACCEPTED_EFFECT_NOTE`, Decision point 4; seeded in the edge table with exactly what the walk finds at `c3bf88c1`), **`credential_in_query: bool`** (H5) — `True` only for the five `download_file.py` image tools — the D34 shaping fields (review-5 H1/M1/M2; review-6 M2/M3): **`scrub_paths: bool = True`**; **`image_field: str \| None = None`** (`'image'` on `edge_image_source_preview`, `edge_image_source_test_connection` and `edge_image_source_capture`, `'maskImage'` on `edge_execution_overlay_get` and `edge_result_get`, `None` elsewhere); **`result_constant: dict \| None = None`** (`{"captured": True}` on `edge_image_source_capture` only); **`drop_fields: tuple[str, ...] = ()`** — root-relative dotted paths, `[]` for array elements — (`('logoImage',)` on `edge_system_station_get`; `('images[].image', 'images[].inferenceResult.mask_image')` on `edge_workflow_images_list`; `('inferenceResult.mask_image',)` on `edge_workflow_run`; `('results[].maskImage',)` on `edge_results_list`; `('executions',)` on `edge_registration_get`); and three request-side fields (review-6 M1/M2/M5/N4): **`limit_max`/`limit_default`** as the portal's (`2`/`2` on `edge_workflow_images_list`, `50`/`None` on `edge_registration_executions_list`, `100`/`None` on `edge_results_list`); **`body_constants: dict = {}`** — JSON merged into the request body **beneath** the caller's mapped arguments (the caller's value wins), where the sentinel `dispatch.NOW_EPOCH_S` is replaced by `int(time.time())` at dispatch (`{"returnImageString": False}` on `edge_workflow_run`; `{"resetTime": NOW_EPOCH_S}` on `edge_results_summary_reset`); **`timeout_s: float = 60.0`** — the read timeout of this spec's backend call (`130` on `edge_textgen_generate`, whose route allows 120 s; everything else the default); **`body_fields: tuple[str, ...] \| None = None`** (review-7 M2) — set **only** when the route's body parameter is an untyped `Dict`/`Any` (no request model), naming the body keys the route's helpers read (`('prompt', 'image', 'max_tokens')` on `edge_textgen_generate`; `None` everywhere else); and **`text_tail_arg: str \| None = None`** (review-7 N6) — the name of the integer argument whose value is the number of trailing characters of a `text/plain` body to keep (`'tail_chars'` on `edge_execution_log_get`; `None` elsewhere). The same argument ↔ route rule as the portal applies (review-6 M5): every `param_map` target and every pass-through argument names a parameter of the route's FastAPI signature (path/query parameters, `Depends()`-injected models such as `PageParams`) or a field of its request model — **or, when `body_fields` is set, a name in `body_fields`**, each of which must also appear as a string literal in the route module (`"prompt"`, `"image"`, `"max_tokens"` in `text_generation.py`), so a renamed body key fails the test — and every non-defaulted route parameter is a required argument; the edge `SERVER_CONSUMED_ARGS` are `('confirm', 'limit', 'offset', 'tail_chars')` (the edge has no `usecase_id`); the module constant **`RO_FREE_TEXT_ARGS: frozenset[tuple[str, str]]`** (revision 12, review-11 N5) holds the `(tool, argument)` pairs of `RO` string properties that carry neither a `pattern` nor an `enum`, each with a one-line reason in a comment — `('edge_textgen_generate', 'prompt')`, `('edge_textgen_generate', 'image')`, `('edge_results_list', 'text_note_filter')`, `('edge_execution_node_image_get', 'port')` (generation input; generation input; a result filter of ≤ 50 chars; selects a node frame the run reported), the complete list from a scan of the 47 `RO` edge specs' string properties; `CATALOG` per the edge table below |
| `child/shaping.py` | child | Pure functions implementing D34 and the size bound, in this order on a 2xx JSON body (the **same** order §7 step 5 states — review-7 N1): `drop_fields` (dotted paths, `[]` = every array element; `resolve_path(body, path)` is the one helper both `drop_fields` and `image_field` use) → `extract_image(spec.image_field)` (→ `ImageContent` or `result_too_large`/`backend_failure`) → **`result_constant` merge** (immediately after extraction, so the constant passes through the guard and the scrub like any other field) → **`drop_oversized_images(body)`** (D34(d): any remaining string ≥ 16 KiB whose first 16 base64 characters decode to an image magic number → `"<dropped: <n> bytes image>"`, path appended to `dropped_fields`) → `scrub_paths(body)` (→ scrubbed body + `scrubbed_fields`; value rule `^(file://)?/(aws_dda\|…)/\S*$`, single token; the `{"text": …}` wrapper of a non-JSON body is not walked — review-6 N2) → collection `limit`/`offset` slicing (only for specs whose route does not paginate itself) → `MAX_RESULT_BYTES` truncation; `image/*` bodies are not walked and become `ImageContent` directly (`image/jpg` → `image/jpeg`); a `text/plain` body is first cut, when the spec sets `text_tail_arg`, to the longest suffix of at most `args[spec.text_tail_arg]` characters whose `json.dumps({"text": …})` fits `MAX_RESULT_BYTES − 1 000` bytes (`truncated: true`, `total_chars` recorded when anything was cut — D34(e), review-8 N4) and then wrapped as `{"text": …}`. `resource_id` is a named body key (`captureId`, `captureTaskId`, `executionId`) or, on a spec with `resource_id_from_body=True` (`edge_workflow_create` only: the 2xx body is the new id as a bare JSON string, `workflow_accessor.py:94`, `workflow.py:520`), the scalar body itself. Returns `(content: list[TextContent \| ImageContent], structured_content: {result, scrubbed_fields?, dropped_fields?, truncated?, total_chars?, resource_id?})`. Property-tested (Properties 9 and 19) |
| `child/principal.py` | child | `resolve_principal(authorization_header) -> Principal(username, role, source)` by calling `GET {backend}/local-auth/whoami` with the caller's `Authorization` header and `X-Request-ID`; 401 → `not_authenticated`; 200 → fields from the body |
| `child/authz.py` | child | `decide(spec, principal, mutating_enabled) -> Allowed \| Denied(reason)`: read-only → allowed for every principal (incl. `anonymous`); mutating → requires `mutating_enabled` (else `mutating_tools_disabled`), `principal.source == 'local_session'` and `principal.role in {'Operator','UseCaseAdmin','PortalAdmin'}` (else `not_authorized`); destructive → additionally `confirm` (else `confirmation_required`). Pure function (Property-tested) |
| `child/dispatch.py` | child | `call_backend(spec, args, credential, request_id) -> BackendResponse` via one module-level `httpx2.Client(verify=False /* https://127.0.0.1:5443 only, nosec — healthcheck.py precedent */, timeout=httpx2.Timeout(connect=2.0, read=60.0, write=10.0, pool=2.0), transport=httpx2.HTTPTransport(retries=0, verify=False))`, each request sent with `timeout=httpx2.Timeout(connect=2.0, read=spec.timeout_s, write=10.0, pool=2.0)` so a spec whose route is slower than the 60 s default (`edge_textgen_generate`, 130 s) is not cut off by the child (review-6 N4) — `retries=0` is the library default and is written out so the single-attempt guarantee of D32 is explicit and asserted (the client comes from a `make_client(backend_url)` factory; `test_child_protocol.py` patches `httpx2.HTTPTransport` and asserts the factory passes `retries=0`); a read timeout or transport error on an `M`/`M!` spec yields the same "operation may have been applied; call `<status_tool>` before retrying" message as the portal (review-4 M2). **Request building:** path parameters from `param_map` (every path parameter has an entry: `catalog.py` derives it for a bare identifier ending in `_id`, and any other filler is written `(→ path …)` in its row — `model_name` on `edge_model_start`/`_stop` and `edge_textgen_generate`; revision 12, review-11 N1), each value percent-encoded as one segment (`urllib.parse.quote(value, safe='')`) after the schema has already refused `/` and the values `.`/`..` (Input validation, path-parameter identifiers — `httpx2` 2.14.0 removes dot-segments when it builds a URL, so `capture_id="../../../cameras/X/disconnect"` on `edge_result_get` would otherwise reach `GET /cameras/X/disconnect`; revision 9), query parameters for GET routes, and for body routes `json = {**resolve_constants(spec.body_constants), **mapped_args}` — the caller's arguments override the constants, and `resolve_constants` replaces the `NOW_EPOCH_S` sentinel with `int(time.time())` (`edge_results_summary_reset` therefore always sends a `resetTime`, as the HMI does — review-6 M5); `edge_static_image_pin` is the one spec whose body is `multipart/form-data` (`files={'file': (file_name, image_bytes, 'application/octet-stream')}`, the route sniffs the format itself — review-6 M4), where `image_bytes = base64.b64decode(image_base64, validate=True)` is computed **before** any request is built and a `binascii.Error` (bad padding, a misplaced `=` — strings the schema `pattern` admits) is returned as `invalid_input` "image_base64 is not valid base64" with nothing dispatched (review-7 N3). Argument renames are plain `param_map` entries: `edge_image_source_add/_update` send `stream_url` as the request model's `location` field (review-7 M1). For `credential_in_query=False` specs it sends `Authorization: Bearer <credential>` and `X-Request-ID`; for `credential_in_query=True` specs it sends the credential as the `token` **query parameter** (`?token=<credential>`) because those `download_file.py` routes read only that parameter (`validate_token_in_query_param` → `authorize_credential`); the request stays on loopback. **Why the URL is not logged (review-2 M4 — three independent controls, not an assumption):** on the Backend_API side, uvicorn access logging is disabled (`dda_logging/uvicorn_disable_logging.json`) and those routes carry no `AccessLogRoute`; on the child side, `httpx2`'s own INFO line (which would contain the full URL) is silenced by `child/__main__.configure_logging()` (`httpx2`/`httpcore2` loggers at WARNING, root at WARNING, `CredentialScrubFilter` dropping any record containing `token=`); and `dispatch.py` never formats the URL into an exception message or record — on `httpx2` errors it logs `spec.name`, the exception class and the status only (`_safe_error(exc)` strips any `token=` substring before the message reaches a record). `test_child_protocol.py` exercises a `credential_in_query` call under captured logging and stderr and asserts the credential appears nowhere. In the open-access row no credential exists and the parameter is omitted (the route's `authorize_credential(None)` allows) |
| `child/logging.py` | child | `emit(record: dict)` → one JSON line on stdout (`sys.stdout.write(json.dumps(record) + "\n"); sys.stdout.flush()`); never includes `Authorization`, tokens, query strings, or passwords (the record builder whitelists fields — Property 13 covers records it builds). It does **not** cover arbitrary `logging` output from libraries; that is `configure_logging()`'s job (`child/__main__.py` row), and the two are tested separately (`test_child_logging.py` for the builder, `test_child_protocol.py::test_credential_in_query_never_logged` for the process-level output) |
| `__init__.py` | both | `__version__`; `DELIVERED_PHASES = (1,)` (N7); `VENV_PYTHON = '/opt/dda/mcp-venv/bin/python'`; `CHILD_PORT = 5790` |

### 7. Edge request pipeline (child `on_call_tool`)

1. **Authenticate/identify.** `resolve_principal(headers['Authorization'])` → on 401 from `/local-auth/whoami`, `not_authenticated`. Through the proxy this happens only if the token expired between the proxy's check and the tool call (9.5); for a **direct loopback caller** (possible under `network_mode: host`, D28) it is the normal outcome of presenting no or an invalid credential — the Backend_API, not the child, makes the decision in both cases.
2. **Lookup / validate / gate / authorize.** As the portal pipeline, with `child/authz.decide` (D13) and `feature` availability (`not_available_on_device` when `spec.feature` ∉ enabled features, 8.4 — such tools are also absent from `tools/list`).
3. **Dispatch.** `call_backend(...)` with the same credential and `X-Request-ID`. For routes on `get_api_router()` the Backend_API runs `authorize_request` again (defense in depth; also covers tokens revoked mid-call) and logs its own `api.access` entry with the same `request_id` (11.3). For the five `credential_in_query` image routes the Backend_API runs `authorize_credential(token)` from the query parameter and writes **no** `api.access` record (they are on the plain `unauthenticated_router`); correlation for those tools rests on the child's `mcp.access` record alone (H5, documented in `docs/edge-mcp-server.md`).
4. **Log.** `emit({...})` one record per completion with outcome and duration (11.1); denials and auth failures at `level: 'warning'` with username only (11.2). `mcp_client` comes from the same `client_label(ctx)` rule as the portal (review-2 N4): `ctx.meta.get(mcp_types.CLIENT_INFO_META_KEY)` → `ctx.session.client_params.client_info` → `'unknown'`.
5. **Shape** (`child/shaping.py`, D34; review-5 H1/M1/M2). `image/*` bodies (the five `download_file.py` routes; `image/jpg` normalized to `image/jpeg`) ≤ 2 MiB → `ImageContent(data=b64, mime_type=...)`, larger → `result_too_large` with `{bytes, limit}` (8.5). A `text/plain` body (`edge_execution_log_get`) is cut to the longest suffix of at most `tail_chars` characters (default and maximum 150 000) whose JSON encoding fits `MAX_RESULT_BYTES − 1 000` bytes (`truncated: true` and `total_chars` recorded when anything was cut — D34(e), review-8 N4) and wrapped as `{"text": …}`. 2xx JSON bodies pass, in order (the order §5 `child/shaping.py` implements — review-7 N1): **(a)** the `spec.drop_fields` paths (root-relative dotted paths, `[]` = every array element — `images[].image`, `results[].maskImage`, `executions`, `logoImage`) removed (`dropped_fields` recorded); **(b)** when `spec.image_field` names a non-empty string, it is base64-decoded and sniffed — decoded size ≤ 2 MiB → one `ImageContent(data=<original base64>, mime_type=<sniffed, default image/jpeg>)` appended to `content` and the field deleted from the body, larger → `result_too_large {bytes, limit}`, undecodable → `backend_failure`; then `spec.result_constant`, if any, is merged into the body **immediately, before (c) and (d)**; **(c)** the **oversized-image guard** (D34(d), review-6 M2): any other string ≥ 16 KiB whose first decoded bytes are an image magic number → `"<dropped: <n> bytes image>"`, recorded in `dropped_fields`; **(d)** the **path scrub** (`scrub_paths=True` on every spec): every key matching `PATH_KEY_RE` whose value is path-like, and every string value that is a single device-root token (`^(file://)?/(aws_dda|tmp|opt|home|root|var|etc|usr|snapshot)/\S*$` — review-6 N2), is replaced by its **file name only** and the key path is appended to `structured_content.scrubbed_fields` (Property 19); the `{"text": …}` wrapper of a non-JSON body is not walked; **(e)** collection slicing (dispatcher-paginated specs only) and the `MAX_RESULT_BYTES` bound on what remains → `structured_content.result`; errors → categories as the portal. **Never accepts a filesystem path as an argument** — `/snapshotfile/{fileName}`, `GET|DELETE /captured-images` (`path`/`filePath` query parameters) and the JSON `capturedImagePath` form of the pin routes are excluded, `capture-details` / execution images are addressed only by `workflow_id`+`capture_id` / `execution_id` (+ `node_id`, `port`), `edge_image_source_add/_update` take **`stream_url`** (schema `pattern ^rtsps?://|^rtmps?://`, mapped by `param_map` to the request model's `location` field — the field is named `location` on the route, where for folder sources it is a device directory, so the tool never exposes that name or that use: folder sources stay HMI-only — review-7 M1), `edge_static_image_pin` takes the image **bytes** (`image_base64`) and a bare `file_name`, and Property 2 rejects any edge `input_schema` property whose name matches `PATH_KEY_RE` (review-6 M4) while allowing a `param_map` **target** to match it only when the mapped property's schema carries a URL-scheme-anchored `pattern` — **and returns none under the scrubbed keys** (Requirement 8.5; Property 19). Nor does an `RO` spec forward a free-form object, or a string the backend splices into a pipeline, a command or a persisted configuration: `edge_image_source_preview` takes `image_source_id` alone (revision 11, review-10 M1 — the route's settings override is not offered; Backlog 28). Every `RO` string argument carries a `pattern` or an `enum`, or is listed with its reason in `RO_FREE_TEXT_ARGS` (§5 `child/catalog.py`; Property 2): the `prompt` and `image` of `edge_textgen_generate` are generation input for the model, `edge_results_list`'s `text_note_filter` (≤ 50 chars) is a result filter, and `edge_execution_node_image_get`'s `port` selects a node frame the run reported; none of them reaches a pipeline, a command or a persisted configuration (revision 12, review-11 N5).

## Tool catalogs

Conventions used in the tables: **Tool** names separated by `/` share one route family, and a name written `_x` is the first name's stem — the first name without its final `_`-segment — plus `_x` (`portal_models_training_list` / `_get` / `_logs_get` → `portal_models_training_get`, `portal_models_training_logs_get`); every other name is written in full, and in the other columns a `_x` label names the row's tool whose name ends in `_x`, the shortest if several (revision 11, review-10 N6); a backticked `_x` that matches no tool of the row is either a code identifier of the handler module (`_authorize`, `_is_jp6`) or, in descriptive text only, a tool of another row, and in prose outside the tables a `_x` abbreviates a prefix of the preceding full name plus `_x`; a label that carries a class, phase, permission or argument always names a tool of its row (revision 12, review-11 N4); **Class** `RO` = Read_Only_Tool (`readOnlyHint` true), `M` = Mutating_Tool, `M!` = Mutating_Tool with `destructiveHint` true (requires `confirm`); **Scope** `U` = Use_Case-scoped (`usecase_id` argument required; the dispatcher maps `usecase_id` to `{id}` only through an explicit `(→ path id)` entry — revision 10, review-9 M1), `G` = `'global'`, or `U|G` = `'usecase_or_global'` (`usecase_id` optional; the RBAC scope is `usecase_id` when supplied, else `'global'` — the `rbac_check(allow_global=True)` rule, review-3 N3); **Perm** is the `shared_utils.Permission` value declared in the catalog and evaluated pre-dispatch (D5) — chosen so the role set it admits equals, or is a strict subset of, the set the backing handler admits (handlers using `check_user_access(..., 'DataScientist')` map to a DataScientist-held permission such as `promote_models`/`manage_datasets`/`create_training_jobs`/`workflow:create`; `'UseCaseAdmin'` — or a handler-side UseCaseAdmin/PortalAdmin role test such as `dda_labeling._require_review_admin` (3451–3473) — maps to `update_usecases`; `@super_user_only`/`require_portal_admin` map to `system_admin`/`manage_users`, which only PortalAdmin holds). **Phase** per "Phasing". Every tool takes the shared argument names of D22; `limit`/`offset` on every `RO` list tool, dispatcher-sliced unless the row maps them, so a row that lists an unmapped `limit` lists `offset` too (revision 10, review-9 N4) — or `limit`/`page` where the route paginates by page number (`edge_results_list`), or a forwarded `limit` alone where the route paginates itself without an offset (a cursor such as `next_token`, or a bounded newest-first window: `edge_registration_executions_list`, `portal_data_captures_list`); whenever the route reads `limit` or `offset` the row writes the forwarding, `(→ query limit)`/`(→ query offset)` (review-9 M2) — a spec never offers a pagination argument its route cannot honour (D23; review-6 M5), and `limit` is bounded by the spec's `limit_max`/`limit_default` where the route's own bound is narrower than 200/50 (review-6 M1 — written in the Key-arguments column as `limit ≤ N`); `confirm` on every `M!` tool, written in the Key-arguments column as `confirm`=`<arg>` (→ `confirm_target=<arg>`), `confirm`=`<a | b>` (→ a per-branch `confirm_target` tuple over a `oneOf`, review-9 N7), `confirm`=`"literal"` (→ `confirm_literal`), or a bare `confirm`, which means `confirm_target` is the identifier argument listed immediately before it in that column (review-3 N2). Every other argument a row lists is forwarded to the route under its own name or its `param_map` target and is read by the route (Property 2's argument clause) — an identifier argument (a name ending in `_id`) other than `usecase_id` written without `(→ …)` fills the path parameter of its own name or camelCase form (`user_id` → `{user_id}`; edge `workflow_id` → `{workflowId}`), else, when exactly one such bare identifier remains, the one path parameter the row leaves unmapped (`device_id` → `{id}` on every `/devices/{id}/…` row, likewise `model_id`, `dataset_id`, `workflow_id` and `build_id` → `{id}`: 26 portal tools whose route function receives the value positionally from the entry point — revision 10, the review-9 N3 scan); `catalog.py` writes that entry into `param_map` and **raises at import when two bare identifiers would claim one path parameter** (revision 11, review-10 N2 — which is why `portal_devices_camera_add` writes `camera_source_id? (→ body camera_source_id)`), and a bare identifier left once every path parameter is filled travels under its own name; any other argument that fills a path parameter is written with its `(→ path …)` entry (`model_name` (→ path `modelName`) on `edge_model_start`/`edge_model_stop`, `model_name` (→ path `model_name`) on `edge_textgen_generate` — revision 12, review-11 N1), so every path parameter of every row has a `param_map` entry; the only arguments the server consumes itself and never forwards are `confirm`, `usecase_id` where the route does not read it (the RBAC scope), `limit`/`offset` on dispatcher-sliced collections and the edge `tail_chars` (`SERVER_CONSUMED_ARGS`; Decision point 4, review-7 M3). Every row also records `handler_module`/`handler_function` in the catalog (named in the table where it disambiguates). **`status_tool`** (D32; review-4 M2) is set on every `M`/`M!` row of both catalogs and is not repeated in the tables; it follows one convention: the object's `_get` tool for create/update/configure/pin/assign tools (`portal_models_training_create` → `portal_models_training_get`, `edge_workflow_update` → `edge_workflow_get`) — except where the new id is server-generated and a timed-out caller never receives it, which name a read addressed by what the caller supplied (`portal_devices_deployment_create` → `portal_devices_deployment_for_target_get`, `portal_admin_usecase_create` → `portal_admin_usecases_list`; revision 9) — the collection's `_list` tool for delete/cancel/unpin/remove tools (`portal_devices_delete` → `portal_devices_list`, `edge_static_image_unpin` → `edge_static_image_pin_get`), and the area's status/health tool for process-level tools (`edge_system_restart_dda` and `edge_system_snapshot_create` → `edge_system_health_get`, `edge_model_start`/`edge_model_stop` → `edge_feature_configurations_list`); Property 2 requires the value to name an `RO` tool in the same catalog, and the catalog export (`catalog_routes.json`'s sibling `catalog_tools.json`, written by the same `--export-routes` command) records every `(tool, status_tool)` pair so reviewers can read the mapping without opening `catalog.py`. **`handler_audits`** is True for every tool whose backing route function (or a function on its call graph — the classification walk's, revision 9; one level was too shallow for `build_fleet.execute_fleet_action` → `audit_fleet_action` and the cross-module `plugin_records.authorize_record_access` chains) contains **at least one `log_audit_event`/`record_audit_event_strict` call whose `result` argument (5th positional or `result=` keyword) is the literal `'success'`, the literal `'pending'`, absent (`record_audit_event_strict`'s default `AUDIT_RESULT_PENDING` — the audit-before-effect entry later completed by `finalize_audit_event`), or a non-literal expression** (a variable or conditional, as in `devices.py`, `shared_components.py`, `plugin_importer.py`, `build_fleet.py`); calls whose `result` is the literal `'denied'`, `'failure'`, `'failed'` or `'rejected'` are **not** counted, because they run only on denial/failure paths (review-2 M2). Per the call-site survey this is still the **majority** of tools (for example `devices.py:list_devices`/`get_device` write `list_devices`/`get_device` success entries). It is False for routes that write nothing on success — for example the `audit_logs.py`, `components.py`, `datasets.py`, `captures.py`, `user_management.py` routes, the static-video GET (`camera_registry.get_static_video_status`; the video unpin, `remove_static_video_pin`, writes a success entry, 2743 → 2024) and `models.py` reads, `deployments.py:list_deployments` (lines 922–993, no audit call at all), and the **`camera_registry.py` reads `get_cameras` (621) / `get_conflicts` (666), whose only audit call is the `'denied'` entry inside the `authorize()` helper they call (lines 402–428)** — so a successful `portal_devices_cameras_list` gets the `mcp_tool_call` envelope. Same shape, also False: `deployments.py:get_camera_binding_context` (4177; `'denied'` only), routes whose only helper audit is `packaging.py:_fail_conversion` (975; `'failure'` only) or `workflows.py`/`workflow_packaging.py:forbidden_response` (129 / 507; `'denied'` only). The AST-based enumeration test (Testing Strategy) evaluates the `result` argument and keeps the flag honest. **`handler_audits_denials`** is True when a 403 from the route is already audited: the route is decorated with `@rbac_check`/`@require_permission`/`@super_user_only`/`@require_portal_admin`/`@require_builds_*`, **or** a function on its call graph contains an audit call whose `result` is the literal `'denied'` and itself builds the 403 (`camera_registry.authorize`, `dda_labeling._require_review_admin`, `synthetic_data._authorize`, `workflows.forbidden_response`, `workflow_packaging.forbidden_response`, `deployments.get_camera_binding_context`'s inline call) — in both cases the envelope is suppressed on 403 (N8) and the handler's own marked `unauthorized_access` entry is the record. **`usecase_id` forwarding** is written in the Key-arguments column as `(→ query usecase_id)`, `(→ body usecase_id)` or `(→ path id)` — its `param_map` entry; a `usecase_id` without one is RBAC-only and never forwarded (§2), so on the routes that take the scope from the stored record (`labeling.py:991`/`dda_labeling.py:504/524/5764`; Backlog 27) the record's Use_Case stays the scope. Revision 10 checked every row against its handler and decorator (review-9 M1): a route that reads `usecase_id` has the entry, or the row says why it stays RBAC-only (the handler authorizes the stored record's Use_Case, or the injection); since revision 11 (review-10 N1) a row whose handler or decorator **reads** the value without forwarding it also writes the machine-readable reason `usecase_not_forwarded=…` (§2) — `'injected'` on the ten `setdefault` specs, `'stored_record'` on `portal_data_synthetic_session_retrain`, `'dead_filter'` on `portal_admin_audit_logs_query` — while a row whose handler reads none carries no field (the collector's finding is its check); a `G` spec forwards none, and on the decorated `G` routes (`rbac_check(..., allow_global=True)`, `require_builds_*`) the decorator then evaluates `'global'`, the spec's own scope. **`accepted_writes`** are written as `(function, call, receiver, count)` tuples with the line in the note (review-9 N2), the edge's **`accepted_effects`** as `'<module>.<function>:<call>'` with the housekeeping of Decision point 4 (review-9 N1), where `<call>` is the effect-list name the walk reports (`os.unlink`, `subprocess.run`, `open`, `Image.save`, `os.makedirs`), not the receiver text (the call site `im.save`, `pipeline_builder.py:187`, is the entry `…:Image.save`; revision 12, review-11 N3), and **`bind_usecase_path`** (D36) where set. **Path-parameter identifiers** (revision 9): every argument that fills a path parameter carries `^(?!\.{1,2}$)[A-Za-z0-9._:@+=-]{1,256}$` — no `/`, not `.` or `..` — so a crafted value can neither add a segment nor climb one (a `camera_id` of `static-image/pin` would turn `portal_devices_camera_delete` into an unpin, `camera_registry.py:2840–2842`; on the edge, see §5 `child/dispatch.py`). **Presigned GET URLs** inside `RO` results (dataset previews, captures, labeler and review items, synthetic sessions) are returned as the route returns them to the same principal (1.3): each is a time-limited grant to read one object the caller may already read, signed by the handler's role session — not a credential of the Principal (2.7, 5.5) — and telemetry never logs results.

Excluded from this view: `POST /admin/users/{u}/password`, `POST /admin/users/{u}/forgot-password` (credential material, 5.5); the **account lifecycle operations — create, disable, enable, delete** — implemented in `user_admin.py` (`create_account` 691; `handler()` 581–586 dispatching `disable_account`, `enable_account`, `delete_account` via `_set_account_enabled` 1449) but **not registered in `user-admin-api-stack.ts`** (103–129 registers `GET /admin/users` and the password / forgot-password / role / edge-sync routes only; the frontend's `createAdminUser`, disable and enable calls target these unregistered routes — a pre-existing portal defect, review-3 M1), so exposing any of them through Lambda dispatch would create a path the Portal_API does not provide (1.3); they join Phase 3 only once the routes are registered (Backlog 2); `POST /usecases/verify-role` (handles cross-account role ARNs/external ids); `/auth/*`. The route-registration test (Testing Strategy) makes this exclusion mechanical: every catalog `(method, resource)` must exist in a synthesized REST API template.

Capability-area coverage check against Requirement 1.1: use cases ✓ (admin), devices and registrations ✓, deployments and preflight ✓, device logs ✓, models (registry/import/conversion) ✓, compilation ✓, training ✓, datasets and data management ✓, labeling (Ground Truth + DDA) ✓, workflows (definitions/test runs ✓ Phase 1; **validation Phase 2** — `portal_workflows_validate` persists `validation_status`, so it is a Mutating_Tool, review-7 H1; packaging Phase 3) ✓, components and configuration ✓ (list, get, configuration schema, delete, shared components; the configure route is deferred because it deploys nothing — Backlog 23), build jobs and fleet ✓, custom node types and plugin records ✓, synthetic data ✓ (incl. preview approval, revision 9), camera registry ✓ (static pin upload deferred — Backlog 22), quick setup status ✓ (`portal_devices_registrations_list` reads registration status), audit logs ✓.

### Edge catalog (Backend_API routes in `src/backend/endpoints/*.py` and `src/backend/workflow_engine/api.py`)

Role policy per D13: `RO` tools for every principal (including `anonymous` in the open-access row, loopback only); `M`/`M!` tools only for a `local_session` principal whose synced role is Operator, UseCaseAdmin, or PortalAdmin, and only while `McpMutatingToolsEnabled` is true. `feature: vllm` tools exist only when the child was started with `--features vllm`. **`credential_in_query`** marks the five tools whose backing routes live on `download_file.py`'s plain router and read the credential from the `token` query parameter (H5).

| Tool | Backing route → handler | Class | Phase | Key arguments / notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `edge_whoami` | `GET /local-auth/whoami` → `endpoints/local_auth.py:whoami` (new) | RO | 1 | returns `username`, `role`, `source`, `expiresAt`; the self-identity exception (N5) |
| `edge_system_health_get` | `GET /system-health` → `endpoints/system.py:get_system_health` | RO | 1 | — |
| `edge_system_component_status_get` | `GET /dda-component-status` → `system.py:get_dda_component_status` | RO | 1 | — |
| `edge_system_greengrass_components_list` | `GET /greengrass-components` → `system.py:list_greengrass_components` | RO | 1 | `limit`, `offset` (dispatcher-sliced; `collection_key='components'` — the body is `{components: […]}`, `system.py:273–274`) |
| `edge_system_snapshot_create` | `GET /snapshot` → `system.py:get_snapshot` (**GET but state-changing → mutating**, review-5 H1: `Snapshotter.take_snapshot` runs `sh /snapshot/snapshot.sh`, which first deletes **every** existing `/aws_dda/system/snapshot-*.tar*` — `snapshot.sh:20`, so an archive made in the HMI and not yet downloaded is lost — and writes a new `.tar.gz` there) | **M!** | 2 | `confirm`=`"snapshot"` (`confirm_literal`; revision 9 — deleting the earlier archives is destructive under Decision point 4, which the review-6 H1 disposition's `destructive=False` contradicted); `idempotent=False`, `role_policy='mutate'`, `status_tool='edge_system_health_get'`; the body `{"archivePath": "snapshotfile/snapshot-<station>-<ts>.tar.gz"}` is scrubbed to the file name `snapshot-<station>-<ts>.tar.gz` (`scrubbed_fields: ["archivePath"]`, D34) — the archive itself is **not** retrievable through MCP (the `/snapshotfile/{fileName}` download route is excluded, 8.5); the name was never released, so no `deprecated_alias` is kept for `edge_system_snapshot_get` (14.4 applies to released names) |
| `edge_system_station_get` | `GET /system/station` → `system.py:get_station` (305–309) | RO | 1 | no arguments; **`drop_fields=('logoImage',)`** (review-6 M2 — the body carries the station logo as a `data:image/…;base64,` URL of unbounded size, `utils/utils.py:212–219`; a branding asset with no operational value, so it is dropped rather than returned as `ImageContent`, keeping this poll-style Phase-1 tool small; `dropped_fields: ["logoImage"]` tells the caller a logo exists) |
| `edge_system_restart_dda` | `POST /restart-dda` → `system.py:restart_dda` | M! | 3 | `confirm`=`"restart-dda"` (`confirm_literal`; no target identifier) |
| `edge_mcp_status_get` | `GET /mcp/status` → `endpoints/mcp_proxy.py:get_mcp_status` (new) | RO | 1 | enabled, listening, version, component version, restarts |
| `edge_workflows_list` / `edge_workflow_get` | `GET /workflows`, `GET /workflows/{workflowId}` → `endpoints/workflow.py:list_workflows`, `get_workflow` | RO | 1 | `workflow_id` (`_get`); both: `accepted_effects=('stream_ingest.protocol.sweep_segments:os.unlink',)` — a workflow's stream sources get their health from the ingest manager (`workflow.py:499/508` → `image_source_accessor.py:526–530`), whose first construction sweeps stale segments (Decision point 4; revision 10, review-9 N1) |

| `edge_static_image_unpin` / `edge_static_video_unpin` | `DELETE …/pin` → same | M! | 2 | `confirm`=`"static-image"` / `"static-video"` (`confirm_literal`; the pin is a singleton with no identifier) |
| `edge_feature_configurations_list` / `edge_gpu_status_get` / `edge_input_configurations_list` / `edge_output_configurations_list` | `GET /feature-configurations`, `/feature-configurations/gpu-status`, `/input-configurations`, `/output-configurations` → `endpoints/feature_config.py:list_feature_configs` (89–124), `get_gpu_status` (127–152), … | RO | 1 | no arguments. **Accepted reading for `_list`/`_gpu_status_get` (review-6 N1):** when Triton is the runtime and its model repository is non-empty, both routes call `__get_triton_instance()` (192 → `TritonEdgeClient.get_instance`, `dda_triton/triton_edge_client.py:58–70`), which constructs the client — and with it `create_server` — on first use, and both publish a model-status shadow report (`model_status_shadow.report`, 121/150); these are **the HMI's own polling effects** (its feature list and GPU badge hit the same routes), persist nothing the tool could be said to have changed, and so stay `RO`; named as fixed `RO` cases in `test_child_catalog_classification.py`, not `accepted_effects` entries (revision 10): neither path calls a function of the classification list — the client creation goes through the native `mlops` SDK and the shadow write runs on a daemon thread through the IPC accessor (`model_status_shadow.py:123–170`) — so the transitive walk finds nothing to accept |
| `edge_model_start` | `GET /feature-configurations/models/{modelName}/start` → `feature_config.py:start_feature_config` (GET but state-changing → mutating) | M | 3 | `model_name` (→ path `modelName`; revision 12, review-11 N1) |
| `edge_model_stop` | `GET /feature-configurations/models/{modelName}/stop` → `feature_config.py:stop_feature_configs` | M! | 3 | `model_name` (→ path `modelName`; revision 12, review-11 N1), `confirm`=model_name |
| `edge_textgen_models_list` (`feature: vllm`) | `GET /text-generation/models` → `endpoints/text_generation.py:list_text_generation_models` | RO | 1 | — |
| `edge_textgen_generate` (`feature: vllm`) | `POST /text-generation/{model_name}/generate` → `text_generation.py:generate_text` (559–564; `body: Dict[str, Any] = Body(default={})` — a free `Dict`, no request model; no persisted state) | RO | 1 | `model_name` (→ path `model_name`; revision 12, review-11 N1), `prompt`, `image?` (**base64**, schema `maxLength: 960_000` — ≈ 700 KiB decoded under the proxy's 1 MiB request cap; the route's own `DEFAULT_MAX_IMAGE_BYTES` is 16 MiB, 294–296, so the proxy cap is the binding one — review-6 N4), `max_tokens?` (integer ≥ 1; the route rejects values above the model's `max_model_len` with 422 → `invalid_input`, 193–203); **`body_fields=('prompt', 'image', 'max_tokens')`** (review-7 M2 — the route's signature carries no field names: `prompt` is read by `normalize_generation_request` as `body.get("prompt")`, 179, `image` by `_validate_image_field(body, "image", findings)`, 236, `max_tokens` at 193–203; the argument ↔ route test checks the three names against `body_fields` and asserts each appears as a string literal in `text_generation.py`); the body's other keys (`temperature`, `top_p`, `system_prompt`, `reference_image`) are not offered and would join `body_fields` if they were; **`timeout_s=130`** (the route's `DEFAULT_TEXT_GEN_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 120.0`, 290–292, bounds a whole non-streaming generation incl. retries, so the child's 60 s default would have cut legitimate generations short; the proxy's upstream read is 150 s — review-6 N4). **Client-side note (review-7 N7):** a generation may legitimately take up to 130 s; Kiro's MCP configuration documents no per-server tool-call timeout key (kiro.dev/docs/mcp/configuration/ and the overview, usage and servers pages, re-fetched 2026-10-19 — the only `TIMEOUT` on them is an example `env` value for a local server), so Kiro is expected to wait; a client that applies its own per-call timeout must raise it, or bound the generation with `max_tokens` — stated in `docs/edge-mcp-server.md` and beside the edge `mcp.json` example |

Excluded: `/local-auth/login`, `/local-auth/status` (the client authenticates outside MCP, 10.8), `/authorization-configurations` (settings fields), `/snapshotfile/{fileName}` (file-name addressed download), **`GET /captured-images` and `DELETE /captured-images`** (`image_source.py:286–308`: both are addressed by filesystem paths — `path` matching `CAPTURED_IMAGE_FOLDER_PATTHERN` and `filePath` matching `CAPTURED_IMAGE_FILE_PATH_PATTHERN` — which the "never accept a filesystem path" rule of pipeline step 5 forbids; M5. Captured images remain reachable through `edge_workflow_images_list` — whose items are scrubbed to file names, `inferenceFilePath` → `<capture_id>.jsonl` (D34) — and the capture image tools, which take that capture id), `/streams/{camera_id}/subscribe|frame|heartbeat|unsubscribe|settings` (HMI viewer session mechanics), **`POST` and `GET /workflows/{workflow_id}/results/export`** (review-6 N3: the POST, `inference_result.py:164–188`, writes `/aws_dda/system/capture-id-path-<ts>` and returns that path; its **only** consumer is the GET on the query-token router, `download_file.py:311–345`, which needs the full path under `DDA_SYSTEM_FOLDER` — through MCP the scrubbed basename could be fed back nowhere, so the tool's sole effect would have been one file write per call with no `RO` tool able to observe it; export stays HMI-only until an id-addressed export route exists, Backlog 19), **`POST /static-video-camera/pin`** (review-6 M4: multipart up to 100 MiB or a device-path JSON body — Backlog 17), the JSON `capturedImagePath` form of `POST /static-image-camera/pin` (a device path; the multipart form is wrapped by `edge_static_image_pin`), `/text-generation/{model_name}/generate-stream` (streaming), `/health` (unauthenticated probe; `edge_system_health_get` covers health).

Capability-area coverage check against Requirement 8.1 (and 17.4 — no area is dropped; the two operation-level deferrals below are listed with their Backlog items because no phase can deliver them without a new route): system health and component status ✓, station ✓ (`edge_system_station_get`, Phase 1), snapshot — **creation** is the Phase-2 Mutating_Tool `edge_system_snapshot_create` (there is no read-only snapshot route: `GET /snapshot` creates one — review-5 H1) and snapshot **download** is excluded (file-name-addressed `/snapshotfile/{fileName}`, 8.5; the archive stays on the device for the HMI or the operator), workflows and registrations (definitions, graphs, executions, results, overlays, logs, node status) ✓, inference results: summary ✓, listing ✓ (`edge_results_list`, page-paginated), single result ✓, update/reset/delete ✓, **export — HMI-only until an id-addressed export route exists (Backlog 19; review-6 N3)**, cameras and image sources (listing, connection state, stream health, preview, capture — the captured frame as `ImageContent`, review-6 M3) ✓, streams capabilities ✓, static image pin: get/pin/unpin ✓; static video pin: get/unpin ✓, **pin deferred (Backlog 17; review-6 M4)**, feature configurations and model start/stop ✓, GPU status ✓, input/output configurations ✓, text generation where available ✓.

## Data Models

### Synthesized backend event (portal dispatch, REST proxy v1 shape)

```jsonc
{
  "httpMethod": "GET",
  "resource": "/devices",                       // the CDK route template the handler routes on
  "path": "/devices",                           // concrete path (path parameters substituted)
  "pathParameters": null,                       // or {"id": "..."} etc.
  "queryStringParameters": {"usecase_id": "uc-123"},
  "headers": {"Content-Type": "application/json", "User-Agent": "kiro/1.2.3 mcp"},
  "body": null,                                 // JSON string for POST/PUT/PATCH
  "isBase64Encoded": false,
  "requestContext": {
    "requestId": "<mcp request id>",
    "authorizer": {"claims": {                  // the VERIFIED access-token payload, verbatim (jwt.decode output), nothing added
      "sub": "…", "iss": "https://cognito-idp.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/us-east-1_…",
      "client_id": "<mcp client id>", "token_use": "access", "scope": "<canonical>/portal.tools",
      "username": "jdoe", "aud": "<endpoint URL>", "exp": 1760000000, "iat": 1759996400, "jti": "…"
    }},
    "identity": {"sourceIp": "203.0.113.7", "userAgent": "kiro/1.2.3 mcp"}
  },
  "mcp": {                                      // the MCP_Audit_Marker source; never present on API Gateway events
    "channel": "mcp", "tool": "portal_devices_list", "server": "dda-portal-mcp/devices@1.0.0",
    "client": "kiro/1.2.3", "request_id": "<mcp request id>",
    "principal_email": "jdoe@example.com", "identity_source": "registry"
  }
}
```

`get_user_from_event` yields `user_id = sub`, `username = 'jdoe'`, `email = 'jdoe'` (no `email` claim → username fallback), `role = 'Viewer'` (no `custom:role`) and remembers the event as the request audit context (D25); `attribution_from` yields `email` from `event['mcp']['principal_email']` when the claims carry none, `source_ip`, `user_agent` as today, `identity_source` from the block (no registry read), plus the marker fields.

### Audit log entry (existing table, additive attributes)

Existing: `event_id`, `timestamp`, `user_id`, `action`, `resource_type`, `resource_id`, `result`, `details`, `ttl`, `username`, `email`, `source_ip`, `user_agent`, `identity_source`. **New top-level attributes:** `channel` (`'api'` | `'mcp'`, on every entry written after this change), and on MCP entries only `mcp_tool`, `mcp_server`, `mcp_client`, `mcp_request_id`. New `action` values: `mcp_tool_call` (envelope; `resource_type` = the catalog's resource type, e.g. `device`, `model`, `workflow`, `collection` for lists; `result` ∈ `success | failure | denied`; `details` = `{tool, usecase_id?, status, error_category?, duration_ms, reason?, <identifier args>}` after `sanitize_audit_details`, `reason` being `'usecase_binding'` with `result = 'denied'` on a D36 refusal — revision 10, review-9 N5) and `authentication_failed` (written by `AuthFailureAuditMiddleware`: `user_id = sub`, `resource_type = 'mcp_server'`, `resource_id = <endpoint URL>`, `result = 'denied'`, `details = {reason: 'expired'|'token_use'|'client_id'|'scope'|'audience', client_id, token_use}`, `mcp_tool = '-'`, `mcp_client` from `User-Agent`, `mcp_request_id` = the API Gateway request id; Requirement 4.7). Existing `unauthorized_access` and `authorization_unavailable` entries written by the MCP Lambda carry the same marker attributes and `details.mcp_tool`. Entries written by backing handlers during an MCP invocation carry the marker through `attribution_from` (when they pass `identity`/`event`) or through the request audit context (when they pass neither).

### Portal per-invocation log line (`mcp_tool`, CloudWatch; Requirement 6.3)

```json
{"event": "mcp_tool", "sub": "…", "username": "jdoe", "tool": "portal_devices_list", "view": "devices",
 "outcome": "success", "category": null, "status": 200, "duration_ms": 412, "request_id": "<mcp request id>",
 "client": "kiro/1.2.3", "server_version": "1.0.0"}
```

Exactly one line per tool completion (success, error result, denial, availability failure; a D36 refusal is an error result with `category: "not_found"`, logged with the backend's real 2xx `status` while the tool result carries `404` — revision 11, review-10 N4; revision 12, review-11 N2); never arguments, tokens, or email.

### Edge access-log record (`mcp.access` structlog event)

```json
{"event": "mcp tool", "channel": "mcp", "tool": "edge_registrations_list", "mcp_server": "dda-edge-mcp@1.0.0+1.0.130",
 "mcp_client": "kiro/1.2.3", "request_id": "<X-Request-ID>", "principal": "operator1", "role": "Operator",
 "source": "local_session", "outcome": "success", "category": null, "status": 200, "duration_ms": 41, "level": "info"}
```

`principal` is `"anonymous"` and `role` null in the open-access row; for Existing_Token_Auth principals `principal` is the introspection response's `username`/`sub` or `"unknown"`; denials and authentication failures use `level: "warning"`, `outcome: "denied"`/`"not_authenticated"`. The underlying route's `api.access` record shares `request_id` (11.3) for every route on `get_api_router()`; the five `credential_in_query` image routes produce no `api.access` record.

### Edge component configuration (recipe `DefaultConfiguration` additions, all seven recipe variants)

```yaml
LocalLoginEnabled: "false"          # existing
McpServerEnabled: "false"           # NEW — start the Edge_MCP_Server child and serve /mcp
McpMutatingToolsEnabled: "false"    # NEW — list and accept Mutating_Tools (requires McpServerEnabled)
```

### `AuthDecision` and `GET /local-auth/whoami` (edge, authenticated)

`utils/auth.py:AuthDecision(source, username, role, expires_at)` is the new return value of `authorize_credential` (`None` in the open-access row; previously always `None`); `authorize_request` stores `request.state.auth_decision`. Responses: Local_Session_Token → `{"username": "operator1", "role": "Operator", "source": "local_session", "expiresAt": 1760043200}` (`expiresAt` decoded from the validated token's payload by `_session_token_expiry`); Existing_Token_Auth → `{"username": "<introspection username or sub, else 'unknown'>", "role": null, "source": "existing_token_auth", "expiresAt": <introspection exp or null>}`; open-access row → `{"username": "anonymous", "role": null, "source": "anonymous"}`.

### `GET /mcp/status` (edge, authenticated)

`{"enabled": true, "mutatingToolsEnabled": false, "listening": true, "endpoint": "/mcp", "transport": "streamable-http", "protocolRevisions": ["2026-07-28","2025-11-25","2025-06-18","2025-03-26"], "serverVersion": "1.0.0", "deliveredPhases": [1], "componentVersion": "1.0.130", "features": ["vllm"], "restartCount": 0, "lastExitCode": null, "lastError": null}`.

### Tool error result (both servers)

`CallToolResult(is_error=True, content=[TextContent(text="not_authorized: Operator role or higher required to invoke edge_registration_trigger")], structured_content={"category": "not_authorized", "message": "...", "request_id": "...", "status": 403})`.

### Shaped edge result with an image field and scrubbed paths (D34; review-5 M1/M2)

`edge_image_source_preview` on a camera source: `CallToolResult(content=[TextContent(text='{"result": {}}'), ImageContent(data="<base64 JPEG, ≤ 2 MiB decoded>", mime_type="image/jpeg")], structured_content={"result": {}, "request_id": "..."})` — the `image` field left `result` and became `ImageContent`. `edge_workflow_images_list`: `structured_content = {"result": {"images": [{"imageDataFilePath": "1718000000000.jpg", "inputImageFilePath": "1718000000000.jpg", "inferenceFilePath": "0f3c…-4d2a.jsonl", "creationTime": "…", "inferenceResult": {…}, "humanReviewRequired": false}]}, "dropped_fields": ["images[].image"], "scrubbed_fields": ["images[].imageDataFilePath", "images[].inputImageFilePath", "images[].inferenceFilePath"]}` — every path reduced to its file name, the base64 frame dropped (fetch it with `edge_capture_output_image_get(workflow_id, capture_id="0f3c…-4d2a")`); a segmentation item additionally lists `images[].inferenceResult.mask_image` under `dropped_fields` (review-6 M2). `edge_system_snapshot_create`: `{"result": {"archivePath": "snapshot-line1-2026-10-19-08-15-00.tar.gz"}, "scrubbed_fields": ["archivePath"]}`. `edge_image_source_capture` (review-6 M3): `content=[TextContent('{"result": {"captured": true}}'), ImageContent(data="<base64 JPEG>", mime_type="image/jpeg")]`, `structured_content={"result": {"captured": true}}` — the body's only field left `result` as `ImageContent` and `result_constant` supplied the text part. `edge_registration_get` (review-6 M2): `{"result": {"registrationId": "…", "status": "registered", "artifactPath": "<file name only>", "triggerHealth": […]}, "dropped_fields": ["executions"], "scrubbed_fields": ["artifactPath"]}`. `edge_system_station_get`: `{"result": {"name": "Line 1", …}, "dropped_fields": ["logoImage"]}`. A body in which the oversized-image guard fired (D34(d)): `{"result": {"…": "<dropped: 412340 bytes image>"}, "dropped_fields": ["<path of that field>"]}`. `edge_execution_log_get` on a 921 600-character Run_Log with the default `tail_chars` (D34(e), review-7 N6): `{"result": {"text": "<the last 150 000 characters, or fewer when their JSON encoding would exceed MAX_RESULT_BYTES − 1 000 bytes>"}, "truncated": true, "total_chars": 921600}`; with `tail_chars=2000`, the last 2 000 characters.

### Protected Resource Metadata (portal, served by `mcp_portal/discovery.py`; D27)

`GET https://<id>.execute-api.<region>.amazonaws.com/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource/mcp` → `{"resource": "https://<id>.execute-api.<region>.amazonaws.com/mcp", "authorization_servers": ["https://<id>.execute-api.<region>.amazonaws.com"], "scopes_supported": ["https://<id>.execute-api.<region>.amazonaws.com/mcp/portal.tools"], "bearer_methods_supported": ["header"]}`; one document per endpoint URL at `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource/mcp/<view>` with `resource` = that view's URL (RFC 9728 §3.3: "The resource value returned MUST be identical to the protected resource's resource identifier value into which the well-known URI path suffix was inserted" — rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9728.txt, re-read 2026-10-18); and the **root** form `GET /.well-known/oauth-protected-resource` → the canonical `/mcp` document above, byte-identical (review-2 H1; the form Kiro's troubleshooting guide fetches and the spec's second fallback). **RFC 9728 §3.3 tension at the root location (review-4 N1):** for the root well-known URL the identifier the suffix was inserted into is the **origin** `https://<id>.execute-api.<region>.amazonaws.com`, so a strictly conforming client could reject a root document whose `resource` is `<origin>/mcp`. The design keeps the canonical URL there deliberately: the MCP specification lists the root location as a legitimate place for the *MCP endpoint's* metadata (authorization-server-discovery, "Well-Known URI … At the root"), the official SDK client accepts it — `_validate_resource_match` (`mcp/client/auth/oauth2.py:580–592`) calls `check_resource_allowed(requested_resource=<server URL>, configured_resource=<PRM resource>)` (`mcp/shared/auth_utils.py:31–65`), which passes when the PRM `resource` has the same origin and its path is a prefix of the server URL's path (trailing slashes normalized), and the client then sends the PRM's `resource` as the RFC 8707 `resource` parameter — and `/mcp` is already in the accepted audience set. Kiro's check is **undocumented** (its configuration page describes fetching the root PRM and reading `authorization_servers[0]` only), so "Kiro accepts the root PRM whose `resource` is `<canonical>/mcp`" is validate-first item (vii) on the deployed stack. **Fallback if Kiro rejects it:** the root document's `resource` becomes `MCP_AS_ISSUER` (the origin, exactly §3.3-conformant) and the origin is appended to `MCP_ALLOWED_AUDIENCES` as a seventh accepted value, so a token Cognito binds to the origin is accepted at every endpoint; the six path-suffixed documents are unaffected either way. `authorization_servers[0]` is the `MCP_AS_ISSUER` string with no trailing slash. The 401 challenge of each endpoint names its own path-suffixed metadata URL (reaching the client only if the gateway stops remapping the header).

### Authorization Server Metadata facade (portal, RFC 8414; D27, review-2 M1)

`GET https://<id>.execute-api.<region>.amazonaws.com/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server` →

```json
{"issuer": "https://<id>.execute-api.<region>.amazonaws.com",
 "authorization_endpoint": "https://dda-portal-<accountId>.auth.<region>.amazoncognito.com/oauth2/authorize",
 "token_endpoint": "https://dda-portal-<accountId>.auth.<region>.amazoncognito.com/oauth2/token",
 "revocation_endpoint": "https://dda-portal-<accountId>.auth.<region>.amazoncognito.com/oauth2/revoke",
 "jwks_uri": "https://cognito-idp.<region>.amazonaws.com/<poolId>/.well-known/jwks.json",
 "response_types_supported": ["code"],
 "grant_types_supported": ["authorization_code", "refresh_token"],
 "code_challenge_methods_supported": ["S256"],
 "token_endpoint_auth_methods_supported": ["none"],
 "scopes_supported": ["https://<id>.execute-api.<region>.amazonaws.com/mcp/portal.tools"]}
```

`issuer` is the same `MCP_AS_ISSUER` string the PRMs advertise (Requirement 7.5, Kiro exact match). The three Cognito endpoints are the live values of the pool's own OpenID Connect document (Research baseline), reproduced from `COGNITO_HOSTED_UI_ORIGIN`; `jwks_uri` is the key set the token verifier already uses. The facade issues nothing: codes and tokens are minted and verified by Cognito. `Cache-Control: public, max-age=3600`. No `openid-configuration` alias is served (an OpenID Provider document has mandatory fields — `userinfo_endpoint`, `subject_types_supported`, `id_token_signing_alg_values_supported` — that would misdescribe this facade; the MCP spec requires clients to try the RFC 8414 location for a path-less issuer).

## Client configuration

### Portal — Kiro IDE and CLI (remote, OAuth, public client)

```jsonc
// .kiro/settings/mcp.json or ~/.kiro/settings/mcp.json — values come from the EdgeCVPortalMcpStack outputs; no secrets
{
  "mcpServers": {
    "dda-portal": {
      "url": "https://abc123.execute-api.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/mcp",          // McpServerUrl (or a view: .../mcp/devices)
      "oauth": {
        "clientId": "1example23456789abcdefghijk",                                // McpUserPoolClientId (public client, PKCE)
        "redirectUri": "http://localhost:7899/oauth/callback",                   // McpRedirectUri — must equal the registered value character for character (host `localhost`, not 127.0.0.1: Cognito registers plain-HTTP callbacks for localhost only)
        "oauthScopes": ["https://abc123.execute-api.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/mcp/portal.tools"]  // McpOAuthScope
      },
      "disabled": true,                                                          // repository default (15.2); set false to opt in
      "autoApprove": ["portal_admin_whoami", "portal_devices_list", "portal_devices_get"],
      "disabledTools": []
    }
  }
}
```

The same entry works for Kiro CLI (confidential-client fields are not needed: the MCP app client has no secret). Both Kiro IDE and CLI bind the loopback listener to the configured `redirectUri` literally and Cognito matches callback URLs exactly, so the value must be copied from the `McpRedirectUri` output unchanged — Kiro's documentation example `http://127.0.0.1:8080/oauth/callback` cannot be used because Cognito accepts plain HTTP only for the host `localhost`; a different port is registered through the `mcpRedirectUris` CDK context (review-3 N9). The MCP app client allows **only** the hosted-UI authorization-code grant with PKCE and the refresh grant (`ExplicitAuthFlows: ALLOW_REFRESH_TOKEN_AUTH`), so there is no username/password sign-in path against it from any script (review-3 H1). `oauthScopes` is set explicitly so Kiro requests only the resource-server scope; Cognito rejects unknown scopes (`offline_access`) with `invalid_scope`, and if a Kiro build still appends defaults the documented fallback `"oauthScopes": []` makes Cognito issue every scope the client allows — exactly `portal.tools` (validate-first item ii). Kiro discovers the authorization server from the PRM's `authorization_servers[0]` (the MCP API origin) at `/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server` — the RFC 8414 facade (D27) — not from Cognito's own discovery document. A repository entry is added to `.kiro/settings/mcp.json` with `disabled: true` and placeholder URL/client id; the three existing third-party servers are untouched.

### Edge — Kiro (remote, bearer header from the environment)

```jsonc
{
  "mcpServers": {
    "dda-edge-line1": {
      "url": "https://<device-lan-address>:5443/mcp",     // TLS listener (authorization settings present); or
      // "url": "http://127.0.0.1:5000/mcp",              // plaintext listener through an SSH / IoT Secure Tunnel port-forward
      "headers": { "Authorization": "Bearer ${DDA_EDGE_TOKEN}" },   // Local_Session_Token from scripts/dda-edge-mcp-login.sh
      "disabled": true,
      "autoApprove": ["edge_whoami", "edge_system_health_get", "edge_registrations_list"]
    }
  }
}
```

`DDA_EDGE_TOKEN` must be added to Kiro's approved environment variables ("Mcp Approved Env Vars"; Kiro expands only approved `${VAR}` references) and is set in the shell that launches Kiro with `eval "$(scripts/dda-edge-mcp-login.sh https://<device>:5443)"` — the helper prompts for the password (`read -s`) and prints the `export` line for `eval`, so the token never appears on a typed command line or in `~/.bash_history` (review-4 N5).
