The token lives 12 h; when it expires the client sees 401 and the user re-runs the login helper. **Which URL form applies (review-4 N4):** the LAN form (`https://<device>:5443/mcp`) exists only on a device whose Existing_Token_Auth is configured (`authorization_settings.json` present — the TLS listener); on a device with **local login enabled but no authorization settings** (the shape `portal-user-manager` deploys) the Backend_API listens on plaintext 5000 only and `/mcp` answers non-loopback peers with 403 `mcp_loopback_only`, so such devices are reachable **only** through the tunnel form (`http://127.0.0.1:<forwarded>/mcp`) — a known limitation documented in `docs/edge-mcp-server.md`. The device certificate on 5443 is self-issued (`device.pem.crt`); the docs describe trusting it for the client machine (or using the tunnel form). No password, token, or client secret is ever written to `mcp.json` (C3). **Tool-call duration (review-7 N7):** `edge_textgen_generate` may take up to 130 s (`timeout_s`; the route's own wall clock is 120 s). Kiro's MCP configuration documents no per-server tool-call timeout key (kiro.dev/docs/mcp/configuration/ plus the MCP overview, usage and servers pages, re-fetched 2026-10-19 — the only `TIMEOUT` on them is an example `env` value for a local server), so Kiro is expected to wait for the server's answer; a client that does apply a per-call timeout must raise it to at least 130 s for this server, or pass `max_tokens` to bound the generation — the sentence appears verbatim in `docs/edge-mcp-server.md` beside this example.

## Security Considerations

### Non-negotiable constraints (how each is satisfied)

- **C1 Reuse existing credential systems only.** Portal: Cognito user-pool access tokens validated inside the Lambda by `CognitoAccessTokenVerifier` (same pool, same JWKS, same issuer string as `jwt_authorizer.py`'s `COGNITO_ISSUER`) behind the SDK's bearer middleware; authorization through `shared_utils.rbac_manager` and the Portal_Identity_Registry. Edge: the `/mcp` route sits behind `authorize_request`, so the Local_Session_Token and Existing_Token_Auth rules decide access unchanged; the child validates nothing itself and only forwards the caller's own credential over loopback (header, or `?token=` for the five routes that read it there). The MCP app client and resource server are **configuration of the existing pool**, not a new identity system (F2).
- **C2 No new identity stores.** No table, file, or token issuer is added. The MCP Lambda caches `AdminGetUser` results in process memory only (email attribution).
- **C3 No API keys or plaintext secrets in configuration.** `mcp.json` examples contain a public client id, URLs, and `${DDA_EDGE_TOKEN}` references; the MCP app client has no secret; no API Gateway usage plan / API key is created; the edge child receives no credentials in its environment or arguments.
- **C4 RBAC on every call.** Portal: pre-dispatch `has_permission` plus the handler's own check; claims are forwarded verbatim (no synthesized `custom:role`, no service identity; the MCP Lambda holds no `cognito-idp:Admin*` beyond `AdminGetUser` and cannot mint tokens). Edge: `authorize_request`/`authorize_credential` at the proxy and again on every underlying route; the role comes only from `validate_token`'s live account record.
- **C5 Audit on every action.** Portal: every completion yields at least one marked entry and never an unmarked one — the handler's own entries are marked through `attribution_from` or the request audit context (D25), and the `mcp_tool_call` envelope covers routes that write nothing; on success exactly `max(k, 1)` marked entries where `k` is the number the handler writes itself (the MCP layer adds exactly one when `k = 0` and none otherwise — review-3 N4; a D36 refusal adds its `denied` envelope to the handler's `k` — revision 11, review-10 N4), and exactly one on every pre-dispatch outcome; denials, availability failures and claim-level authentication failures are audited (3.3, 3.4, 4.7); audit-before-effect paths run inside the handlers unchanged, and `record_audit_event_strict` failures still abort the effect and surface as `backend_failure` (4.4). Edge: one `mcp.access` record per completion, correlated by `request_id` where the route logs.
- **C6 Steering compliance.** See "Design notes for the implementation phase".
- **C7 Additive and reversible.** Portal: a separate stack, one additive public member on `ComputeStack` (`mcpBackendHandlers`) and one on `ApiGatewayStack` (`componentConfigurationHandler`), no exported `LayerVersion` (D31); **reversal is the two-step procedure of D33 (review-4 M3)** — `npx cdk destroy EdgeCVPortalMcpStack --force` (removes the MCP API, Lambda, both layers, role, SSM parameter, app client and resource server) **then** `DEPLOY_MCP_SERVER=false ./deploy-infrastructure.sh` (drops `ComputeStack`'s now-unreferenced exports) — because `cdk deploy --all` does not destroy a stack that merely left the app, and a `ComputeStack` update that dropped exports still imported by the deployed MCP stack would fail; the deploy script's guard refuses the flag while the stack exists. Nothing in the pool, tables or existing functions changes on reversal; the audit `channel` attribute and the request audit context are additive (browser entries differ only by `channel='api'`). Edge: with `McpServerEnabled=false` no child runs, `/mcp` answers 503, and `requirements.txt`, `docker-compose.yaml`, listener ports, TLS selection, CORS, and the decision matrix are byte-identical/unchanged; the only always-on differences are the venv directory and `mcp_edge/` package in the image, two new routes, the `read_component_config_via_ipc` refactor and the `validate_token` → `_introspect_token` split (both behaviour-preserving; `validate_token` still returns `None`), `AuthDecision` as a return value nobody consumed before, and one startup log line.
- **C8 Security posture.** No secrets in logs or shell strings (token verifier logs `sub` and failure class; child log records are whitelisted; `Authorization` and query strings are never relayed to `api.access`/`mcp.access`; the edge login helper is used as `eval "$(scripts/dda-edge-mcp-login.sh …)"` with a `read -s` password prompt, so neither the password nor the session token is typed on a command line or recorded in shell history — review-4 N5); least-privilege IAM with exact ARNs and no wildcards (six statements incl. `ssm:GetParameter` on the one dispatch-map parameter, D30); the only non-loopback bind is the existing one (5000/5443), and the `/mcp` exposure rule is documented in code with the same `nosec`-style comment convention used in `app.py`.

### Requirement 3.9 interpretation ("no synthesized authorizer context"; M8)

Requirement 3.9 and C4 forbid invoking a handler "with a synthesized authorizer context". D4 does construct the `requestContext.authorizer` block of the dispatched event, so the design states its interpretation explicitly: the prohibited thing is a context that asserts an identity, role or claim the credential did not carry. In this design `requestContext.authorizer.claims` **is the verified access-token payload object itself** — the return value of `jwt.decode` after signature, issuer, expiry, `token_use`, `client_id`, scope and audience checks — copied without additions, removals or substitutions; `custom:role` is absent because the token has none, so `get_user_from_event` yields the Claimed_Role `Viewer`; no caller-supplied value can enter the block because every tool schema has `additionalProperties: false` and `build_backend_event` never reads tool arguments into `requestContext`; the only other request-context fields are `requestId` and `identity.sourceIp/userAgent`, taken from the MCP request. This is the same information API Gateway's Cognito authorizer would place there had the handler been called over HTTP with that token — minus nothing, plus nothing. The owner is asked to confirm this interpretation as a Phase 1 entry criterion (see Phasing); Property 5 enforces it mechanically.

### Token handling and documented deviations

1. **Audience rule (RFC 8707).** Accepted `aud`: any of the six endpoint URLs. Deviation from "exact resource match": a token bound to `/mcp/devices` is accepted at `/mcp` and vice versa — all six URLs are the same deployable, the same catalog, and the same authorization path, so the risk RFC 8707 addresses, a token issued for one resource being accepted by another, does not arise between them. A token **without** `aud` is accepted only when `client_id` is the MCP client and the scope is present; such a token cannot be used anywhere else in the portal (the REST API accepts ID tokens only), so passthrough is impossible even in this case. This is a re-reading of Requirement 2.4's "validate that each token was issued for the Portal_MCP_Server as the intended audience" (and of the MCP specification's RFC 8707 MUST) in which `client_id` + scope stand in for `aud` when the client did not send `resource`; it is **Phase 1 owner sign-off item (g)** (review-4 N3). Both rules are implemented in `token_verifier.py` and covered by tests; `BearerAuthBackend` is constructed without `resource_server_url` as the consequence, noted in code.
2. **Discovery (D27; review-2 H1/M1).** Protected Resource Metadata is served at the root and the six path-suffixed well-known URIs (the MCP spec's mechanism (ii)); the SDK's `WWW-Authenticate` challenge is emitted but API Gateway delivers it as `X-Amzn-Remapped-WWW-Authenticate`, so **Requirement 2.4's header clause is a documented deviation** (owner sign-off; CloudFront is the designed remedy if declined). The PRMs name the MCP API origin as the authorization server, and that origin serves an RFC 8414 facade pointing at the Cognito hosted UI with `code_challenge_methods_supported: ["S256"]`, because Cognito publishes OpenID Connect Discovery only (no RFC 8414 document; both locations 400) and its document omits the PKCE field the MCP client rules require. **Requirement 2.4's "naming the User_Pool as the authorization server" is read as satisfied in substance** (the facade's endpoints and JWKS are the User_Pool's; the owner confirms this reading at Phase 1 sign-off). Security properties of the facade: it is a static document (no state, no secrets, no code or token ever passes through it); a tampered facade could at worst redirect a browser to a non-Cognito authorize endpoint — mitigated because the document is rendered from Lambda environment variables set by CDK (not configurable at runtime), and the token verifier accepts only Cognito-issued tokens (`iss == COGNITO_ISSUER`, Cognito JWKS), so a token from any other issuer is rejected regardless of what the facade says.
3. **Scopes.** The required scope is advertised in every PRM document's `scopes_supported` and configured in `mcp.json` (`oauthScopes`); the SDK's 401 challenge does not carry the spec's optional `scope=` hint (a SHOULD, not implemented by `RequireAuthMiddleware` 2.3.0 — accepted; adding a header-rewriting middleware for a hint Kiro does not need is not worth the complexity). `insufficient_scope` 403s are produced by `RequireAuthMiddleware` when an authenticated token lacks the scope.
4. **Token lifetime.** Access 1 h, refresh 30 d (mirroring the web client's Cognito defaults); the design adds no lifetime (2.8). Revocation: account disable → refresh refused immediately; outstanding access tokens expire ≤ 1 h (same as the web UI).
5. **Origin / DNS rebinding.** Portal: `allowed_origins=[]` (from `MCP_ALLOWED_ORIGINS`, empty unless the owner sets the validate-first (viii) fallback — review-5 N5) and `allowed_hosts=[MCP_API_HOST]` → any `Origin` header is 403, a foreign `Host` 421. Edge: the proxy rejects any `Origin` header with 403 and never forwards it; the child enforces the **same** rule itself with explicit `TransportSecuritySettings(allowed_hosts=['127.0.0.1:5790', 'localhost:5790'], allowed_origins=[])` (any `Origin` → 403, foreign `Host` → 421) and a 1 MiB body cap, because under `network_mode: host` the child's loopback socket is reachable by any local process and the SDK's loopback defaults would otherwise accept `http://127.0.0.1:*`/`http://localhost:*` Origins (review-3 M3; D28).
6. **Token passthrough.** The portal Lambda never forwards the access token anywhere (claims only); the edge child forwards the caller's credential **only** to the device's own Backend_API over loopback — the same server that received it, so it is not a different resource's token. For the five image routes the credential travels in the loopback URL's `token` parameter because that is the only place those routes read it. The URL is kept out of every log by three controls (review-2 M4): server side, uvicorn access logging is disabled and the routes carry no `AccessLogRoute`; child side, `child/__main__.configure_logging()` holds the root, `httpx2` and `httpcore2` loggers at WARNING (the venv's `httpx2` 2.14.0 would otherwise log the full URL at INFO) and installs a root `CredentialScrubFilter` dropping any record containing `token=`; and `log_relay.py` redacts any stderr line containing `token=` before relaying it. The process-level test (`test_child_protocol.py::test_credential_in_query_never_logged`) asserts the credential appears in neither captured logging nor stderr.
7. **Unauthenticated traffic reaches the Lambda (H4 consequence).** Bounded by the stage/route throttles (D10); a request without a bearer token costs one Lambda invocation with no network I/O (the SDK short-circuits before the verifier runs; the verifier itself short-circuits before any JWKS fetch for unparseable tokens).

### Flagged items (F1–F8) disposition

- **F1 open-access devices.** Mitigations adopted: off by default (12.1); `/mcp` served to non-loopback peers only on the TLS listener (12.4, D14); in the open-access row the principal is `anonymous`, which D13 limits to Read_Only_Tools regardless of `McpMutatingToolsEnabled`. **Residual risk, stated precisely (review-3 M3):** the child is bound to loopback, which under `network_mode: host` is the device's shared loopback and therefore reachable by any local process, not only the Backend_API; the child never acts on its own authority — every `whoami` and route call carries the caller's credential to the Backend_API, where `authorize_request`/`authorize_credential` decide — so a direct loopback caller gains nothing it could not get from the Backend_API itself (in the open-access row that is read access to device state, exactly as the Backend_API grants today), but its `/mcp` request gets no proxy `api.access` record (the child's `mcp.access` line and the Backend_API's own `api.access` line for the forwarded route remain). Documented in `docs/edge-mcp-server.md` with this wording.
- **F2 ID vs access token.** Resolved by D3; `auth-stack.ts` is untouched — the new client/resource server are created in the MCP stack on the imported pool.
- **F3 edge pins.** Resolved by D11/D20: no pin in `requirements.txt` moves; Dockerfiles and recipes change and the tracked Dockerfiles are rebaselined (Design notes).
- **F4 edge role enforcement.** New, MCP-scoped (`mcp_edge/child/authz.py`); Backend_API routes and HMI unchanged.
- **F5 audit volume.** D7: individual entries, `channel` attribute, filter added to `GET /audit-logs`; owner to revisit if cost matters.
- **F6 Lambda + Streamable HTTP.** D17/D26/D32: JSON responses, stateless, no streams, fresh session managers per invocation; 29 s Lambda / 30 s gateway bound, with the dispatcher's invoke budget derived from the remaining invocation time so a backing call never outlives the function (review-5 M3).
- **F7 `jwt_authorizer.py`.** Left alone; the MCP token verifier is a separate module with its own (stricter) rules.
- **F8 edge frontend/HMI.** `authorize_credential`'s return value changes from `None` to `Optional[AuthDecision]`; `validate_token` keeps returning `None` (the introspection JSON is surfaced through the new private `_introspect_token`, review-2 H2). Raise/allow behaviour is unchanged and no production caller used the return value (`authorize_request` discarded it; `download_file.validate_token_in_query_param` still does); `test_authorize_request.py:424/430` are the only tests pinning it and are updated in the same commit (§6). The login/status routes and token format are untouched.

## Error Handling

### Portal

| Operation | Failure | Recoverable? | Caller receives | Logged |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Gateway | throttle exceeded | yes (retry) | HTTP 429 from API Gateway, no Lambda run | API Gateway metrics only |
| Bearer middleware | no `Authorization` header / not `Bearer` | yes (authenticate) | HTTP 401; the SDK sets `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer error="invalid_token", error_description="Authentication required", resource_metadata="…"` (no `scope=` hint), which API Gateway delivers as `X-Amzn-Remapped-WWW-Authenticate` — clients fall back to `GET /.well-known/oauth-protected-resource[/mcp[/<view>]]` (D27) | none (no principal) |
| Discovery routes | malformed path / unsupported method | no | 404 (unknown path) / 405 `Allow: GET` | none |
| `CognitoAccessTokenVerifier` | unparseable token, unknown `kid`, bad signature, wrong `iss` | yes | 401 + challenge | INFO failure class; **no audit** (no parseable Principal) |
| | JWKS fetch failure | transient | 401 + challenge | ERROR with `kid`, no token |
| | signature OK but `exp` passed, `token_use != access`, wrong `client_id`, scope absent, `aud` outside the allowed set | yes | 401 + challenge | INFO with `sub` and reason class; **audit `authentication_failed`** (marked) via `AuthFailureAuditMiddleware` (4.7) |
| `RequireAuthMiddleware` | authenticated but required scope missing | no | 403 `insufficient_scope` | INFO |
| Transport security | `Origin` present / foreign `Host` | no | 403 / 421 (SDK) | WARNING |
| `tools/call` lookup | unknown tool / undelivered phase | no | JSON-RPC `-32602` | INFO |
| Argument validation | schema violation; missing `usecase_id`; bad `confirm` | yes | `isError` `invalid_input` / `confirmation_required` naming the property; envelope `mcp_tool_call` failure | INFO (property name only); `mcp_tool` line |
| Mutating switch | tool is mutating and `MCP_MUTATING_TOOLS_ENABLED=false` | no | `isError` `mutating_tools_disabled`; envelope | INFO; `mcp_tool` line |
| Pre-dispatch RBAC | permission not held | no | `isError` `not_authorized`; audit `unauthorized_access` (denied, marked) | INFO; `mcp_tool` line |
| | `RegistryUnavailable` | transient | `isError` `authorization_unavailable`; audit `authorization_unavailable` (failure, marked) | ERROR with exception; `mcp_tool` line |
| Email lookup | `AdminGetUser` fails | yes (degraded) | none — attribution `email='unknown'` | WARNING |
| `ssm:GetParameter` (dispatch map, first tool call per container — D30) | parameter missing / access denied / throttled after the client's 3 attempts / value not a JSON object / backend key absent from the map | transient (next container or next call retries the load; a missing key is a deploy defect) | `isError` `backend_failure` with `'dispatch map unavailable'` or the missing key; nothing invoked; envelope failure | ERROR with the parameter name (never the value) |
| Pre-dispatch time budget (D32; review-5 M3) | `LAMBDA_CONTEXT.get_remaining_time_in_millis() < 24_000` when `invoke()` is entered (slow cold start, slow JWKS/SSM/`AdminGetUser`/registry calls) | yes (retry — nothing ran) | `isError` `timeout` with the **plain** message "insufficient time budget to start the backing operation; retry" (no `effect: unknown`, no `status_tool`); nothing invoked; envelope failure | WARNING with the remaining milliseconds; `mcp_tool` line |
| `lambda:InvokeFunction` | throttled (`TooManyRequestsException`) / `FunctionError` / client read timeout (20 s) / malformed response | transient; **no SDK retry — exactly one attempt** (`retries={'total_max_attempts': 1}`, D32); **on timeout the effect is unknown** (the backing Lambda keeps running up to its own timeout) | `isError` `throttled` (429) / `backend_failure` / `timeout`; for an `M`/`M!` tool the `timeout` and post-dispatch `backend_failure` messages read "the operation may have been applied; call `<status_tool>` before retrying" with `structured_content.effect = "unknown"`; envelope failure | ERROR with function ARN and request id (review-4 M2) |
| MCP Lambda terminated by its own 29 s timeout **after** dispatch (review-5 M3) | the function's remaining time is exhausted while `Invoke` is outstanding or during the envelope/telemetry writes | — (effect **unknown**; the client's retry may repeat a mutation) | API Gateway bare 5xx (502/504): no `isError`, no `effect: unknown` steering, no `mcp_tool_call` envelope, no `mcp_tool` line. **Prevented** by the remaining-time guard (≥ 24 s before `Invoke`) and the 2 s + 20 s client timeouts, which leave ≥ 2 s for the writes; **detected** by the Lambda runtime's `Task timed out after 29.00 seconds` log line → a CloudWatch Logs metric filter and alarm on the MCP function's log group (created by `mcp-stack.ts`, alarm action left to the operator's existing topic), so a residual occurrence is visible even though no audit entry exists | Lambda runtime line only (the function cannot log) |
| Backend status | 400/422 → `invalid_input`; 401 → `not_authenticated`; 403 → `not_authorized`; 404 → `not_found`; 409 → `conflict`; 429 → `throttled`; 5xx → `backend_failure` | per category | `isError` with the handler's `error` string scrubbed — except a 404 on a `bind_usecase_path` spec, which returns the D36 result `ToolFailure('not_found', 'no such object in the named Use_Case', status=404)` (revision 11, review-10 N3; revision 12, review-11 N2); envelope failure except 403 from a `handler_audits_denials` route | INFO (status, tool) |
| Use_Case binding (D36; revision 10, review-9 N5) | a 2xx body of a `bind_usecase_path` spec whose value at that path is absent or is not the authorized `usecase_id` | no (the object belongs to another Use_Case, or names none) | `isError` `not_found` "no such object in the named Use_Case" — the body is dropped, so nothing of the object is returned, and its existence is not confirmed because a missing id answers the identical result — `structured_content.status` is `404` on both and only `request_id` differs (backend 404 row; revision 11, review-10 N3; revision 12, review-11 N2); envelope `mcp_tool_call` with `result='denied'`, `details.reason='usecase_binding'` (written even when `handler_audits`) | INFO with the tool and the path, never the body; `mcp_tool` line with category `not_found` and the backend's real status |
| Audit envelope write | `log_audit_event` swallows DynamoDB errors | degraded | tool result unaffected; the Lambda logs ERROR `audit_envelope_failed` — strictness is the handler's job (4.4) | ERROR |
| Auth-failure audit write | DynamoDB error | degraded | 401 unaffected | ERROR |
| Result shaping | result > `MAX_RESULT_BYTES` | yes | truncated collection with `truncated: true`, or `isError` `result_too_large` for single objects | INFO |
| App composition / lifespan | exception while building the app or entering a session manager | no | 500 from Mangum | ERROR with traceback — covered by the two-invocations test |
| Lambda crash outside a tool | unexpected exception | — | SDK sanitizes to "Internal server error" JSON-RPC error | ERROR with traceback (CloudWatch only) |

### Edge

| Operation | Failure | Recoverable? | Caller receives | Logged |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `/mcp` proxy auth | decision matrix rejects | yes (login) | 401 `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer` (existing behaviour) | `api.access` 401 |
| `/mcp` proxy policy | any `Origin` header; non-loopback peer on plaintext listener | no | 403 `{"error":"invalid_origin"}` / `{"error":"mcp_loopback_only"}` | WARNING with peer ip |
| `/mcp` proxy body | body larger than `MCP_PROXY_MAX_BODY` (1 MiB) read by the async dependency | no | 413 `{"error":"payload_too_large"}` | `api.access` 413 |
| `/mcp` proxy availability | `McpServerEnabled=false`; child not ready; connection refused | transient | 503 `{"error":"mcp_disabled"}` / `{"error":"mcp_unavailable"}` | INFO / WARNING |
| `/mcp` proxy upstream | child read timeout (`MCP_PROXY_UPSTREAM_READ_S` = 150 s — above every spec's `timeout_s`, so the child always answers first; review-6 N4) while waiting for the status line **or** while buffering a non-`text/event-stream` body (the only kind the child emits under `json_response=True`) | transient | 504 `{"error":"mcp_timeout"}` — reachable because the body is buffered before any header is sent (review-3 N8); in practice reached only if the child itself hangs | WARNING |
| `/mcp` proxy upstream | buffered upstream body larger than `MCP_PROXY_MAX_UPSTREAM_BODY` (4 MiB) | no | 502 `{"error":"mcp_bad_upstream"}` | WARNING with size |
| `/mcp` proxy upstream | read timeout **inside** a `text/event-stream` relay after the status line (defensive branch; unreachable with the child's `json_response=True`) | — | the stream is truncated; the already-sent status cannot change (documented limitation of streaming relays) | WARNING |
| Child transport security | `Origin` header present / `Host` not `127.0.0.1:5790`\|`localhost:5790` / body > 1 MiB on a direct loopback call | no | 403 / 421 / 413 from the SDK's `TransportSecurityMiddleware` / `RequestBodyLimitMiddleware` (review-3 M3) | SDK WARNING ("Invalid Origin header") |
| `McpConfig` read | IPC error / unparseable value | degraded | treated as disabled (12.1) | WARNING once per failure streak |
| `read_component_config_via_ipc` lock | contention between the two pollers | — | the second poller waits (sub-second) | none |
| Supervisor | spawn failure; child exit; readiness timeout | yes (backoff restart) | `/mcp` → 503 until ready; `/mcp/status` shows `lastExitCode`/`lastError` | ERROR per failure, INFO per restart |
| Supervisor | venv missing (`VENV_PYTHON` absent) or `mcp_edge` not importable in the venv | no | `/mcp/status.lastError`; 503 | ERROR once |
| Child `resolve_principal` | `/local-auth/whoami` 401 (token expired mid-call) | yes | `isError` `not_authenticated` | `mcp.access` warning |
| Child role gate | mutating tool and (switch off / role-less / role below Operator / no `confirm`) | no | `isError` `mutating_tools_disabled` / `not_authorized` / `confirmation_required` | `mcp.access` warning (username only) |
| Child feature gate | `feature: vllm` tool without vLLM | no | `isError` `not_available_on_device` | info |
| Child backend call | connection error / read timeout after `spec.timeout_s` (60 s default; 130 s for `edge_textgen_generate`, whose route allows 120 s — review-6 N4) | transient; exactly one attempt (`httpx2.HTTPTransport(retries=0)`, D32), so a mutating route runs at most once; on timeout the effect is unknown | `isError` `backend_failure` / `timeout`; for an `M`/`M!` tool the message reads "the operation may have been applied; call `<status_tool>` before retrying" (review-4 M2) | warning |
| Child argument schema | `limit` above the spec's `limit_max` (e.g. `edge_workflow_images_list` with `limit=3`), `page` < 1, `tail_chars` outside 1–150 000, a base64 argument above `maxLength: 960_000`, a `file_name`/`file_prefix` outside its pattern, a `stream_url` not matching `^rtsps?://\|^rtmps?://`, an `inference_results` item without `capture_id` | yes | `isError` `invalid_input` naming the property — **before dispatch**, so the route's own 400 (`MaxNumberOfResultsValidator`) is never reached through MCP (review-6 M1/M4/N4; review-7 M1/N4/N6) | info |
| Child argument decoding (review-7 N3) | `image_base64` passes the schema `pattern ^[A-Za-z0-9+/=]+$` but `base64.b64decode(..., validate=True)` raises `binascii.Error` (bad padding, misplaced `=`) | yes | `isError` `invalid_input` "image_base64 is not valid base64" — decoded **before** the multipart request is built, so nothing is dispatched | info |
| Child text result (D34(e); review-7 N6) | a `text/plain` body longer than `tail_chars` (default 150 000) — a Run_Log can reach ≈ 1 MiB | — (not a failure) | the longest suffix of at most `tail_chars` characters whose JSON encoding fits `MAX_RESULT_BYTES − 1 000` bytes (review-8 N4), as `{"text": …}` with `truncated: true` and `total_chars` in `structured_content`; the caller lowers `tail_chars` or reads the full log in the HMI | none |
| Child backend status | same mapping as portal; 401 → `not_authenticated` (incl. `credential_in_query` routes rejecting the token) | per category | `isError` with the route's `detail` scrubbed | info |
| Child image result | `image/*` body > 2 MiB, or a spec's `image_field` decodes to > 2 MiB (D34) | yes | `isError` `result_too_large {bytes, limit}` | info |
| Child image field (D34; review-5 M2) | `image_field` present but not valid base64 | no | `isError` `backend_failure` ("image field is not valid base64"); the remaining JSON is not returned | WARNING with tool name and field |
| Child result scrub (D34; review-5 M1, review-6 N2) | a 2xx body carries path-like values under `PATH_KEY_RE` keys or single-token device-root strings (a free-text value that merely begins with `/aws_dda/…` and contains whitespace, and the `{"text": …}` wrapper of a non-JSON body, are left alone) | — (not a failure) | the values are reduced to file names; `structured_content.scrubbed_fields` lists them so the omission is visible | none |
| Child oversized-image guard (D34(d); review-6 M2) | a 2xx body carries a base64 image ≥ 16 KiB under a field no spec names (`image_field`/`drop_fields` missed it) | — (degraded, not failed) | the string is replaced by `"<dropped: <n> bytes image>"` and the path listed in `dropped_fields`; the rest of the result is returned | WARNING with tool name and field path (so the catalog can be corrected) |
| Log relay | malformed line / logging exception | degraded | tool result unaffected (11.4) | best effort |
| Log relay | child stderr line containing `token=` (any library logging the loopback URL) | — | line replaced by `<redacted: credential-bearing line dropped>` | `mcp.child` WARNING (redacted text only) |
| Child `httpx2` request logging | a future `basicConfig(INFO)` or library raising log levels | prevented | `configure_logging()` pins `httpx2`/`httpcore2`/root at WARNING and `CredentialScrubFilter` drops `token=` records; `test_credential_in_query_never_logged` fails otherwise | none |
| Backend `app.py` startup | supervisor construction raises | degraded | LocalServer starts without MCP; `/mcp` 503 | ERROR `[MCP SUPERVISOR STARTUP FAILED]` |

## Input validation

| Input | Rules | On failure |
|---|---|---|
| Tool arguments (both) | `jsonschema` Draft 2020-12 against `spec.input_schema` (a top-level `oneOf`/`anyOf` only over `required` sets); `additionalProperties: false`; identifiers `^[A-Za-z0-9._:/-]{1,256}$`, and **an identifier that fills a path parameter** `^(?!\.{1,2}$)[A-Za-z0-9._:@+=-]{1,256}$` — no `/`, not `.` or `..` (revision 9: a `/` or a dot-segment would let a value address a different route — `camera_registry.py:2840–2842` on the portal; `httpx2` dot-segment removal on the edge, where the child also percent-encodes each segment); `limit` integer 1–`limit_max` with default `limit_default` (200/50 unless the spec narrows them to the route's own bound or to what fits the result bound — `edge_workflow_images_list` 2/2, `edge_registration_executions_list` 50, `edge_results_list` 100, `portal_data_captures_list` 30/20, `portal_data_datasets_preview_get` 50/8, `portal_models_training_list` 200/20 — review-6 M1/M5, revision 9); `offset` ≥ 0; `page` ≥ 1 (default 1; only on specs whose route paginates by page); `next_token` ≤ 2048 chars; free text ≤ 20 000 chars; **base64 image arguments** (`edge_textgen_generate.image`, `edge_static_image_pin.image_base64`) `maxLength: 960_000` and `pattern: ^[A-Za-z0-9+/=]+$` — ≈ 700 KiB decoded, sized so the JSON-RPC request stays under the proxy's 1 MiB body cap (review-6 M4/N4); bare file names and prefixes (`file_name` `^[A-Za-z0-9._-]{1,128}$`, `file_prefix`/`capture_prefix` `^[A-Za-z0-9._-]{1,64}$` — no `/`, so none can carry a path); nested bodies (`definition`, `declaration`, `annotation`, `engine_configuration`, `config`, `inference_results`) ≤ 256 KiB after serialization (`inference_results`: 1–100 items, each with a required `capture_id` — review-7 N4); `tail_chars` integer 1–150 000, default 150 000 (`edge_execution_log_get` only; review-7 N6); `hours` integer 1–168, default 1 (`portal_devices_logs_analyze`; review-7 M3); `max_tokens` integer ≥ 1 (`edge_textgen_generate`; the route enforces the model-specific upper bound); `version` integer ≥ 1 (`portal_workflows_validate`, `portal_workflows_package`); no argument may be named `sub`, `iss`, `aud`, `scope`, `client_id`, `token_use`, `username`, `email` or `custom:*` (schema-level, M8), and no edge argument name matches `PATH_KEY_RE` — the Stream_URL argument is `stream_url`, whose `param_map` target `location` is permitted because the property's schema carries a URL-scheme-anchored `pattern` (Property 2; review-6 M4, review-7 M1) | `invalid_input` naming the property |
| `usecase_id` | required on every `U`-scoped tool; non-empty identifier | `invalid_input` |
| `confirm` | required on `M!` tools; must equal `args[spec.confirm_target]` for a name, the target of the `oneOf` branch present for a per-branch tuple (`portal_devices_deployment_create`: the thing-group name or `target_devices[0]` — review-9 N7; `target_devices` is `minItems == maxItems == 1` and both targets carry `THING_NAME_PATTERN`, so `[]` and `""` fail the schema before the comparison — revision 11, review-10 N5), else `spec.confirm_literal` (exactly one is set per `M!` spec — D9, review-3 N2) | `confirmation_required` |
| `MCP-Protocol-Version` header | handled by the SDK: a value outside the supported set → structured protocol-version rejection; **absent → the request is handled as revision 2025-03-26** (`DEFAULT_NEGOTIATED_VERSION`), the specification's backward-compatibility rule, declared in D17 as the Requirement 7.2 legacy path rather than a 7.3 deviation (review-3 N6) | SDK error / legacy path |
| `Origin` header | portal: valid only when listed in `MCP_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` (empty by default → never valid); edge proxy: valid only when listed in `McpAllowedOrigins` (empty by default → never valid), never forwarded; **edge child: same list via `--allowed-origins`** (explicit `TransportSecuritySettings`, never the SDK's loopback defaults — review-3 M3); each list entry must be `scheme://host[:port]` with no path (validated at CDK synth / by the child's argparse) — validate-first (viii), review-5 N5 | 403 |
| `Host` header (portal) | must equal `MCP_API_HOST` | 421 (SDK) |
| Access token (portal) | see Token handling | 401 + challenge |
| `Authorization` (edge) | existing matrix | 401 |
| `/mcp` request body (edge) | read by the async dependency `_mcp_body`; ≤ `MCP_PROXY_MAX_BODY` = 1 MiB; forwarded as opaque bytes (the child's SDK parses the JSON-RPC) | 413 `payload_too_large` |
| `.well-known` discovery routes (portal) | `GET` only; no query parameters read; static documents from environment strings validated at import (`MCP_AS_ISSUER` is `https://`, no path/query/fragment/trailing slash, equals the origin of `MCP_CANONICAL_URL`) | import-time `AssertionError` fails the cold start (CloudWatch ERROR) rather than serving an inconsistent issuer |
| Component configuration values (edge) | `parse_local_login_enabled` semantics: only `true`/`"true"` (case-insensitive, trimmed) enable | treated as disabled |
| CDK context `mcpMutatingToolsEnabled`, `deployMcpServer`, `mcpRedirectUris` | `portalRegistryEnforced`-style truthy set; default-ON helper for `deployMcpServer`; each redirect URI must be `http://localhost[:port]/<path>` or an `https://` URL — plain HTTP is accepted for the host `localhost` only, because Cognito registers no other plain-HTTP callback (Research baseline; review-3 N9); `http://127.0.0.1:…` fails synth with a message naming this rule | synth error / default |
| Child CLI arguments | `--port` 1024–65535, `--backend-url` must be `http://127.0.0.1:…` or `https://127.0.0.1:…`, `--features` ⊆ `{vllm}`, `--server-version` matches `^\d+\.\d+\.\d+(\+[A-Za-z0-9.\-]+)?$` (catalog semver plus the component version as build metadata, N7), `--allowed-origins` CSV of `scheme://host[:port]` | process exits 2 (supervisor logs, no restart loop: non-retryable) |
| Edge 2xx result bodies (D34) | `drop_fields` paths removed; `image_field` must decode as base64 and to ≤ 2 MiB; any other ≥ 16 KiB string that sniffs as an image is replaced (D34(d)); path scrub over `PATH_KEY_RE` keys and single-token device-root strings (`{"text": …}` wrappers exempt); then the `MAX_RESULT_BYTES` bound | `dropped_fields`; `result_too_large` / `backend_failure`; `"<dropped: n bytes image>"` + `dropped_fields`; file names only + `scrubbed_fields`; truncation |
| `edge_image_source_add/_update` `type`/`stream_url` | `type` ∈ `{Camera, ICam, NvidiaCSI, RTSP, RTMP}` (no `Folder`); `stream_url` matches `^rtsps?://\|^rtmps?://`, ≤ 2048 chars, and is forwarded as the request model's `location` field (`param_map`) — the route's `location` is a device directory for folder sources, which the tool never offers (§7 step 5; review-7 M1) | `invalid_input` |
| `edge_static_image_pin` `image_base64` decoding | after the schema check, `base64.b64decode(value, validate=True)` must succeed (review-7 N3) | `invalid_input` "image_base64 is not valid base64", nothing dispatched |

## Invariants and enforcement ownership

| Invariant | Owner | Why there |
|---|---|---|
| A tool runs only for a principal the existing credential system authenticated | Portal: SDK bearer middleware + `CognitoAccessTokenVerifier` (the gateway has no authorizer); Edge: `utils/auth.py:authorize_request`/`authorize_credential` **on every forwarded route** (the proxy's check is additional, not load-bearing: the child's loopback socket is reachable by any local process under `network_mode: host`, so the invariant rests on the credential the child forwards, never on who reached the child — D28, review-3 M3) | the only validators; the child and the dispatcher never validate credentials themselves |
| Every tool completion produces exactly `max(k, 1)` marked audit entries on success (`k` = entries the handler writes itself) | Portal: `mcp_portal/audit.py` envelope rules + `handler_audits` flags kept honest by the AST test | the MCP layer adds exactly one entry when the handler adds none and never duplicates a handler's entry (review-3 N4) |
| Only Cognito's hosted-UI code grant (PKCE) and refresh grant can mint an MCP access token | `mcp-stack.ts` `McpUserPoolClient` (`ExplicitAuthFlows = ['ALLOW_REFRESH_TOKEN_AUTH']`) + the `mcp-stack.test.ts` assertion + the deployed `describe-user-pool-client` check | CDK silently omits the property for `authFlows: {}` and Cognito's default would admit `USER_SRP_AUTH` (review-3 H1); the invariant must be pinned in the template, not assumed |
| Every catalog `(method, resource)` is a registered Portal_API method **that its backend's handler dispatches to `handler_function`** | `catalog_routes.json` pinned to `CATALOG` (pytest) and to the synthesized REST route tables (jest), plus the handler-dispatch half of `test_mcp_catalog_routes_export.py` (revision 9) | Requirement 1.3: Lambda dispatch must never reach a handler branch the Portal_API does not expose (review-3 M1) — nor a registered route whose handler has no branch for it (`POST /components`, review-8 M2) |
| Effective role comes from the Portal_Identity_Registry / synced account record, never from an MCP input | Portal: `shared_utils.RBACManager`; Edge: `local_auth.session_tokens.validate_token` via `/local-auth/whoami` | the existing single sources of truth |
| Every audit entry written during an MCP invocation carries the marker | Portal: `shared_utils.attribution_from` + the request audit context (D25) in the shared layer, bound to `_X_AMZN_TRACE_ID` | the layer sees every audit write; the handler process is the only place the marker can be attached to handler-written entries |
| Every tool completion produces at least one marked audit record (portal) / one `mcp.access` record (edge) | Portal: `mcp_portal/audit.py` envelope rules; Edge: `mcp_edge/child/logging.py` + `log_relay.py` | the marker needs request context only the MCP layer has |
| Destructive tools cannot run without `confirm` | catalog + `on_call_tool` validation step (both servers) | annotations are untrusted; must be server-side |
| Mutating tools are absent and refused when the switch is off | `tools_for_view` and the gate step (both) | one switch, two effects, same module |
| The edge child cannot read device secrets | module boundary (`mcp_edge/child/` imports neither `utils`, `local_auth` nor `dao`) + import-boundary unit test | structural, not procedural |
| With MCP disabled the LocalServer behaves as today | `McpSupervisor` desired-state logic; `/mcp` route 503; unchanged `requirements.txt`/compose; behaviour-preserving `read_component_config_via_ipc` refactor | C7 |
| MCP never widens a REST route's permissions | catalog permission ≤ handler permission (documented per row) + handler's own check still runs | defense in depth; the "no more permissive" test enumerates both |
| An id-addressed portal read returns an object only when it belongs to the Use_Case the principal was authorized in (Requirement 3.3; revision 10, review-9 N5) — except the sign-off (j) reads while the owner accepts REST parity (revision 11, review-10 N4) | the backing handler where it authorizes the stored record's Use_Case (models, training, workflows, plugins, synthetic sessions, the `setdefault`-injected labeling routes); otherwise `mcp_portal/shaping.py`'s D36 check on `bind_usecase_path` specs (`portal_data_labeling_job_get`); a read neither can bind is kept out of `CATALOG` (`portal_data_labeling_manifest_get`) or named in sign-off item (j) (the account-scoped Greengrass reads) | the pre-dispatch check sees only the **named** Use_Case; the object's own Use_Case is known only to the handler or to the response body, so the check must sit where the object is |
| The MCP Lambda works on warm invocations | per-invocation `build_asgi_app()` (D26) + the two-invocations test | `StreamableHTTPSessionManager.run()` is single-entry |
| Every PRM's `authorization_servers[0]` and the facade's `issuer` are the identical string (Requirement 7.5) | `mcp_portal/discovery.py` (one environment string `MCP_AS_ISSUER`, import-time validation, string rendering — no `AnyHttpUrl`), proven by `test_mcp_discovery.py` and Property 17 | Kiro compares the two character for character and aborts on mismatch; owning both documents in one module makes the invariant structural |
| The MCP Lambda's environment stays under Lambda's 4 KB aggregate quota; the dispatch map is never an environment variable (D30) | `mcp-stack.ts` (`MCP_BACKEND_FUNCTIONS_PARAMETER` only; the map in the Advanced-tier SSM parameter) + `mcp-stack.test.ts` (asserts `MCP_BACKEND_FUNCTIONS` absent and the worst-case serialized environment < 3,000 bytes) | 43 ARNs ≈ 5.6 KB would be rejected by `UpdateFunctionConfiguration` and roll the deploy back; the test catches growth before CloudFormation does (review-4 H1) |
| No cross-stack reference to a resource that is replaced on update — in particular no imported `AWS::Lambda::LayerVersion` (D31) | `mcp-stack.ts` builds `McpSharedLayer` and `McpLayer` itself; `mcp-stack.test.ts` asserts the function's `Layers` contain no `Fn::ImportValue` and `ComputeStack` exposes no layer; `bin/app.ts` passes functions/tables/pool only | CloudFormation refuses to change an exported value another stack imports; a layer's ARN changes on every content change, so an imported layer would block every later `ComputeStack` shared-layer deploy (review-4 H2; the `NodeDesignerStack`/`BuildFleetStack`/`SyntheticDataStack` pattern) |
| A single `tools/call` causes at most one execution of the backing handler/route, and every outcome reaches the client as an MCP result (D32) | Portal: `dispatch.py` `LAMBDA` client with `retries={'total_max_attempts': 1}` + a unit test on `LAMBDA.meta.config` (the single re-invoke after `ResourceNotFoundException` follows an attempt that provably executed nothing); **the invoke budget is derived from the remaining invocation time** — `Invoke` (2 s + 20 s) is issued only while ≥ 24 s of the 29 s function remain, so the backing call ends inside the function's lifetime and a Lambda-level kill (bare gateway 5xx, no `isError`, no envelope) cannot occur after dispatch (review-5 M3); edge: `httpx2.HTTPTransport(retries=0)` in the child and `httpx.HTTPTransport(retries=0)` in the proxy, both asserted; every `M`/`M!` spec names a `status_tool` (Property 2) and the timeout message tells the model to observe before retrying | SDK retries after a client-side read timeout would re-run a mutating handler that is still executing (deployments, training, configuration pushes); the retry decision must be the model's, informed, not the transport's (review-4 M2) — and it can only be informed if the function lives long enough to answer |
| No `RO` tool changes Portal or device state (Requirements 5.1, 5.2, 10.1, 17.1; review-5 H1, review-7 H1) | `catalog.py`/`child/catalog.py` classification + one AST test per catalog: edge — no `subprocess`/`os.system`/`os.remove`/`os.unlink`/`os.replace`/`os.rename` call, no `shutil` call other than its reads, no file-creating `tempfile` call, no Pillow `Image.save` on a Pillow image value (Decision point 4's definition), no `os.makedirs`/`os.mkdir`/`os.rmdir`/`os.chmod` or `pathlib` twin of a listed call, and no write-mode `open`/`Path.open`/`Path.write_*` anywhere on an `RO` route's **transitive** call graph outside the spec's `accepted_effects` (revision 10, review-9 N1; revision 11, review-10 N7; revision 12, review-11 N3; fixed cases: `GET /snapshot` is not `RO`; `system.get_system_health`'s `shutil.disk_usage` is a read; the JP6 PNG staging is an entry), and no `RO` edge spec takes an `object`-typed argument, and every `RO` edge string argument has a `pattern` or `enum` or is in `RO_FREE_TEXT_ARGS` (Property 2; `test_child_catalog_arguments.py`); none of the listed ones is forwarded into a pipeline, a command or a persisted configuration (§7 step 5; `edge_image_source_preview` takes `image_source_id` alone — revision 11, review-10 M1; revision 12, review-11 N5); portal — no write call outside the spec's `accepted_writes` (matched on function, call, receiver and count) anywhere on an `RO` `handler_function`'s call graph (its module and the other `functions/*.py` modules, plus the derived `SHARED_LAYER_WRITERS` — review-9 N2), and no audit-result clause (fixed cases: `workflow_validation.validate_workflow` is not `RO`; the ten status-sync GETs are `RO` only with their listed writes; the eight audited reads are `RO`) | the classification is the role gate's input on the edge (D13 lets anonymous and Viewer principals call `RO` tools) and the Phase-1 gate on both servers (17.1), so it must be checked against what the route does, not what its name or HTTP verb says — the portal twin of review-5 H1 survived six reviews because only the edge had the test |
| No edge result carries a filesystem path, and no edge tool accepts one (Requirement 8.5; review-5 M1; review-6 M4; review-7 M1) | `child/shaping.py` path scrub on every 2xx body (`scrub_paths=True` on every spec) + Property 19; Property 2's rule that no edge `input_schema` property name matches `PATH_KEY_RE` (a `param_map` **target** may, only when the property's schema has a URL-scheme-anchored `pattern` — `stream_url → location`), and that bare-name arguments (`file_name`, `file_prefix`, `capture_prefix`) carry patterns without `/` | the scrub is the only layer that sees every body, so the "never returns a path" claim has to live there; the argument rule lives in the catalog test because a `captured_image_path` argument would otherwise be one row edit away |
| Every catalog argument is a parameter its route reads — or one of the enumerated `SERVER_CONSUMED_ARGS` — and every parameter the route requires is a required argument (Requirement 1.3, 8.3; review-6 M5; review-7 M2/M3) | Property 2's argument ↔ route clause (AST over the portal handler's `queryStringParameters`/body/`pathParameters` reads and `400 … required` branches / `validate_required_fields` lists; over the edge route's FastAPI signature, `Depends()` models and request model, or `ToolSpec.body_fields` for a free-`Dict` body) with the five review-6 rows and the two review-7 rows as fixed cases; the exemption list is a constant the tests read, not a convention; the reads are collected with constant folding and `getattr` delegation, path arguments are checked against the route template, and a route's own `usecase_id`, `limit` and `offset` reads must be forwarded (revision 10, review-9 M1/M2/N3/N4) | a row that omits a required parameter (`prefix`, `repository`, `object_type`) or offers one the route ignores (`offset` on a page-paginated route; `definition`, `component`, `question`) fails on every call or silently does nothing — a class of defect reading alone did not catch three times |

## Correctness Properties

*A property is a characteristic or behavior that should hold true across all valid executions of a system. Each property below is implemented as one Hypothesis (Python) or fast-check (TypeScript, where applicable) property test with at least 100 examples, tagged `Feature: mcp-servers, Property N: <text>`.*

### Property 1: Access-token acceptance

*For any* RS256 token signed by the test pool key with arbitrary `token_use ∈ {access, id, other}`, `client_id`, `scope` string, optional `aud` (string or list), `exp` around now, and issuer string, `CognitoAccessTokenVerifier.verify_token` returns an `AccessToken` if and only if the signature verifies, `iss` equals the configured issuer exactly, `exp` is in the future, `token_use == 'access'`, `client_id` is the MCP client id, the required scope is among the space-separated scopes, and `aud` is absent or intersects the allowed endpoint set; and `CURRENT_AUTH_FAILURE` is set with the token's `sub` exactly when the signature and issuer verified but a later check failed. **Validates: Requirements 2.1, 2.2, 2.4, 4.7**

### Property 2: Catalog well-formedness

*For any* `ToolSpec` in either catalog: the name matches `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,63}$` and is unique; `read_only ⇒ not destructive`; `destructive ⇒ exactly one of confirm_target / confirm_literal is set` and `not destructive ⇒ neither is set`; `confirm_target` set ⇒ a name names a property of `input_schema`, and a per-branch tuple has one entry per branch of the top-level `oneOf`, each naming a property that branch requires (`target_devices[0]`: the first item of an array), every array a per-branch `confirm_target` indexes has `minItems == maxItems == 1`, and every per-branch target string has a `pattern` that excludes `""` (revision 11, review-10 N5) — fixed cases (review-9 N7): `portal_devices_deployment_create.confirm_target == ('target_thing_group', 'target_devices[0]')`, and `confirm_literal` is set only on the four edge singleton tools of D9; `scope == 'usecase' ⇒ 'usecase_id' ∈ input_schema.required`; `scope == 'usecase_or_global' ⇒ 'usecase_id' ∈ input_schema.properties and ∉ required` (review-3 N3); `permissions` non-empty unless the tool is `portal_admin_whoami`/`edge_whoami` (the self-identity exception); every portal spec with a backing route (all but `portal_admin_whoami`, which is local) has its `(method, resource)` in `catalog_routes.json`; the input schema is a valid Draft 2020-12 schema with `additionalProperties: false`, no claim-named property (`sub`, `iss`, `aud`, `scope`, `client_id`, `token_use`, `username`, `email`, `custom:*` — the Input-validation set), a top-level `oneOf`/`anyOf` only when its branches differ in `required` alone, and the path-parameter identifier pattern (no `/`, not `.`/`..`) on every argument a `param_map` maps to a path parameter (revision 9); `handler_module`/`handler_function` name an existing module and the function that implements the route — never a module entry point that dispatches to route functions, never a cross-module shim (`portal_admin_whoami`, the one local spec, names `server.whoami` and is exempt from the `functions/` walks); `classification == 'mutating' ⇒ status_tool` names a tool in the same catalog whose `classification == 'read_only'`, and `classification == 'read_only' ⇒ status_tool is None` (D32, review-4 M2); **description (D35, review-5 M4):** `spec.description` is a non-empty sentence ending in `.` of ≤ 300 characters, and `render_description(spec)` contains `Touches: {resource_type}` and **ends with** exactly `"Read-only."` iff `read_only`, `"MUTATING."` iff mutating and not destructive, and `"MUTATING, DESTRUCTIVE — requires confirm=<confirm_target | repr(confirm_literal)>."` iff destructive (a per-branch tuple renders as `<a | b>`); `deprecated_alias` set ⇔ `alias_removed_in` set (a semver string), and no alias collides with any tool name or other alias; **`bind_usecase_path` (D36; review-9 N5)** is set only on a `read_only`, `scope == 'usecase'`, `result_shape == 'object'` portal spec and is a well-formed root-relative dotted path without `[]` — fixed cases: `portal_data_labeling_job_get.bind_usecase_path == 'job.usecase_id'`, and `portal_data_labeling_manifest_get` is absent from `CATALOG`; **edge shaping fields (D34):** `image_field`/`drop_fields` are set only on `RO` or `M` specs with `result_shape` consistent with their use (`image_field` ⇒ `'object'`), every `drop_fields` entry and `image_field` is a well-formed root-relative dotted path (`^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*(\[\])?(\.[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*(\[\])?)*$`), `result_constant` is set only together with `image_field`, and `scrub_paths` is `True` on every spec; **`limit` bounds (review-6 M1):** every list spec's `limit` schema has `minimum == 1`, `maximum == (limit_max or 200)`, `default == (limit_default or 50)` with `default ≤ maximum` — fixed case: `edge_workflow_images_list.input_schema['properties']['limit']['maximum'] == 2` and `['default'] == 2`; a spec has `page` iff its `param_map` maps `page`, and never both `page` and `offset`; **pagination forwarding (review-9 M2/N4):** when the route reads `limit` or `offset`, or the spec maps a cursor (`next_token`), `param_map` maps `limit` — and `offset` when the route reads it (portal: a `params.get('limit')`/`('offset')` read; edge: a route parameter named `limit`, the `PageParams` fields, or `maxResults`/`startingPoint`) — and a spec whose `limit` is not mapped (dispatcher-sliced) maps no cursor and has `offset` — fixed cases: `portal_admin_audit_logs_query`, `portal_workflows_builds_list`, `portal_workflows_build_logs_get` and `portal_data_captures_list` map `limit`, `portal_data_datasets_preview_get` maps `limit` and `offset`, `edge_registration_executions_list` maps `limit`, and `portal_admin_usecases_list`, `portal_devices_list` and `portal_devices_deployments_list` have `offset`; **no free-form `RO` arguments (revision 11, review-10 M1):** no `RO` edge `input_schema` has an `object`-typed property — fixed case: `edge_image_source_preview` offers no `image_source_configuration`, and a call carrying `{'image_source_configuration': {'processingPipeline': 'videoconvert ! filesink location=/tmp/x'}}` is `invalid_input` (`additionalProperties: false`); revision 9 added the last two); revision 10: at `c3bf88c1` the walk finds exactly five accepted effects on eight tools (Decision point 4; edge table) — six since revision 11's effect list, the sixth the JP6 Pillow staging on `_test_connection`/`_preview` (review-10 N7), and eight since revision 12's, the seventh and eighth `write_csi_config`'s `os.makedirs`/`os.chmod` on the same two tools (review-11 N3) — and the ingest-session lease/restart, the retention orphan sweep, the lazy Triton client and the shadow report stay outside its reach as fixed cases; a rerun on a skip-verification job, 3969–3995), the handler's set is the one it admits for the inputs the schema allows and the narrower check stays the handler's — fixed cases: `portal_workflows_validate` declares `workflow:edit`, not `workflow:read`; the four synthetic reads declare `manage_datasets`, not `view_datasets`; `portal_models_package`/`_publish` declare `promote_models`, not `manage_deployments`; `portal_data_labeling_review_get`/`_decide`/`_finalize` declare `update_usecases`; `portal_workflows_generation_job_get` declares `workflow:create` (review-8 M1; revision 9) — each with the former permission as the failing variant. **Validates: Requirements 1.2, 1.3, 3.1, 3.9, 5.1, 5.2, 5.6, 8.2, 8.3, 8.5, 10.1, 14.1, 14.2, 14.4, 17.1**

### Property 3: Tool list determinism and filtering

*For any* view, delivered-phase set, and mutating switch value, `tools_for_view` returns a list sorted by `(domain, name)` that contains a tool iff its phase is delivered and (it is read-only or the switch is on), and contains the alias entry of a spec with `deprecated_alias` iff it contains the spec itself — the alias sorted by its own name, carrying the spec's schema and annotations and a description starting `Deprecated alias of <name>; removed in <alias_removed_in>.` (D35, review-5 M4); calling it twice yields identical lists. **Validates: Requirements 1.7, 5.4, 10.7, 14.4, 17.3**

### Property 4: Portal authorization decision

*For any* tool other than the whoami tools, registry state (a `global` row and/or a Use_Case row with arbitrary roles and `status`, or none), `PORTAL_REGISTRY_ENFORCED` on/off, and Use_Case argument, the pre-dispatch decision allows the call **iff `shared_utils.rbac_manager.has_permission(sub, scope, p, user_info={'user_id': sub, 'username': …, 'email': …, 'role': 'Viewer'})` is True for some declared permission `p`**, where `scope` is the tool's resolved scope (`usecase_id`, `'global'`, or `usecase_id or 'global'` for `usecase_or_global`) — the real resolver under the configured mode is the oracle, so the property cannot drift from `_legacy_role`/`_lookup_identity` (review-3 M2); a denial writes exactly one `unauthorized_access` entry with `channel='mcp'` and `mcp_tool`; a registry read failure under enforcement yields `authorization_unavailable` and one `authorization_unavailable` entry (and under enforcement off the legacy resolver's swallow-to-Viewer behaviour is observed, never a crash); the decision never allows a call the web path would deny for the same registry state (never more permissive); Viewer is denied every mutating tool; under enforcement **on** with a `global` DataLabeler row, DataLabeler is allowed exactly the `labeling:tasks-self` tools and the whoami tools and denied everything else; under enforcement **off**, a non-PortalAdmin principal is denied every `global`-scoped tool whose permission Viewer lacks — including the labeler and build tools — which the test records as the documented D29 limitation. **Validates: Requirements 3.1–3.7, 4.7, 16.3**

### Property 5: Dispatch event faithfulness

*For any* tool and schema-valid argument set, the synthesized event has `httpMethod`/`resource`/`path`/`pathParameters`/`queryStringParameters`/`body` consistent with the spec's `param_map`, `requestContext.authorizer` equal to `{'claims': <the verified token payload object>}` (no added, removed or altered keys, no other authorizer keys), an `mcp` block with `channel='mcp'`, the tool name, server, client, request id, principal email and identity source; and the access token string appears nowhere in the serialized event. **Validates: Requirements 1.3, 2.7, 3.9, 4.2**

### Property 6: Marked audit entries per completion

*For any* tool, outcome (success, backend error with any status, denial, availability failure, pre-dispatch validation failure, and — for a `bind_usecase_path` spec — the D36 refusal of a 2xx body), `handler_audits`/`handler_audits_denials` flags, and a fake handler that writes `k ∈ {0, 1, 2}` entries through the real `log_audit_event` without `identity`/`event` inside a simulated MCP invocation: every entry written (handler's or envelope) carries the marker attributes and no denylisted detail key; on success with `handler_audits == (k ≥ 1)` the total is **exactly `max(k, 1)`** and the envelope is present iff `k = 0` (review-3 N4 — a handler writing two entries yields two marked entries and no envelope); on every pre-dispatch outcome the total is exactly one; on a handler-side failure the total is at least one and never contains an unmarked entry; on a D36 refusal the total is **`k + 1`**, the extra entry being the `denied` envelope with `details.reason='usecase_binding'`, whatever `handler_audits` says (revision 11, review-10 N4). Requirement 16.4's "exactly one Audit_Log entry with the MCP_Audit_Marker" is read as "exactly one entry added by the MCP layer when the handler adds none, otherwise the handler's own entries, every one marked". **Validates: Requirements 4.1, 4.2, 4.3, 4.5, 16.4**

### Property 7: Marker attribution round trip and context binding

*For any* synthesized event with or without an `mcp` block, `attribution_from` returns exactly the five Attribution_Fields when the block is absent (byte-identical to today's implementation) and the five fields plus the five marker fields (`channel='mcp'`, `'unknown'` for missing values) when it is present; and *for any* sequence of `get_user_from_event(e_i)` calls interleaved with `_X_AMZN_TRACE_ID` changes, `_attribution_attributes(None, None)` yields marked attributes iff an event carrying an `mcp` block was remembered under the current trace id (a later marker-less event under the same trace id does not replace it; one under a new trace id does), else the five `'unknown'` fields with `channel='api'`. **Validates: Requirements 4.1, 4.2, 4.3**

### Property 8: Backend status mapping and scrubbing

*For any* HTTP status and body (arbitrary JSON or text, possibly containing traceback markers or bearer strings), `map_backend_status` yields the category of the mapping table and a message containing neither `Traceback`, `File "`, nor the token; for a `bind_usecase_path` spec a 404 yields a `CallToolResult` equal to the D36 refusal's in `content` and `structured_content` except `request_id` (`not_found`, "no such object in the named Use_Case", `status: 404`), whatever the body (revision 11, review-10 N3; revision 12, review-11 N2). **Validates: Requirements 1.5, 8.6, 2.7, 9.7**

### Property 9: Result bounding

*For any* collection and `limit`/`offset`, the shaped result has at most `limit` items, is a contiguous slice starting at `offset`, serializes to ≤ `MAX_RESULT_BYTES`, and sets `truncated` iff items were dropped for size; and (edge, D34; review-5 M2) *for any* JSON body carrying a base64 string of arbitrary decoded size under the spec's `image_field`, the value **never counts toward `MAX_RESULT_BYTES`**: decoded size ≤ 2 MiB yields exactly one `ImageContent` whose `data` is the original string and whose `mime_type` matches the magic bytes, the field is absent from `result`, and the rest of the body is bounded as above; decoded size > 2 MiB yields `result_too_large` with `bytes` = the decoded size and `limit` = 2 MiB; every path named in `drop_fields` — root-relative, `[]` = every array element — resolves to nothing in `result` before the bound is evaluated and is listed in `dropped_fields` iff it resolved to something in the input (review-6 M2); **the oversized-image guard (D34(d))**: *for any* body in which an arbitrary string field (not the `image_field`, at any depth, including inside array items) holds a base64 image of ≥ 16 KiB, `result` contains `"<dropped: <n> bytes image>"` at that path with `n` the string's length, the path is in `dropped_fields`, no other field changed, and the result is never `result_too_large` on account of that field; strings < 16 KiB, non-image base64 of any size, and the `image_field` itself are untouched by the guard; the guard runs after `image_field` extraction and `drop_fields`, so a field they already handled is never double-counted. Fixed cases (review-6 M2 (a)–(f)): `{"name": "s", "logoImage": "data:image/png;base64,<400 KB>"}` through `edge_system_station_get` → `result == {"name": "s"}`, `dropped_fields == ["logoImage"]`; `{"images": [{"image": <300 KB>, "inferenceResult": {"mask_image": <200 KB>, "inference_result": "Anomaly"}}]}` through `edge_workflow_images_list` → both dropped, `dropped_fields == ["images[].image", "images[].inferenceResult.mask_image"]`; `{"total": 1, "page": 1, "size": 50, "results": [{"captureId": "c1", "maskImage": <300 KB>}]}` through `edge_results_list` → `dropped_fields == ["results[].maskImage"]`, `result["results"][0]["captureId"] == "c1"`; `{"captureId": "c1", "maskImage": <PNG base64>}` through `edge_result_get` → one `ImageContent(image/png)`, `result == {"captureId": "c1"}`; `{"captureId": "c1", "image": <600 KB JPEG base64>, "inferenceResult": {...}}` through `edge_workflow_run` → the guard replaces `image` (`dropped_fields == ["image"]`) — the body the route would send if `returnImageString` were ignored; `{"image": <600 KB JPEG base64>}` through `edge_image_source_capture` → one `ImageContent(image/jpeg)` and `result == {"captured": true}`; `{"registrationId": "r", "executions": [<3 000 × 300-byte rows>]}` through `edge_registration_get` → `result` has no `executions`, `dropped_fields == ["executions"]`, no `result_too_large`. **Validates: Requirements 1.6, 8.5**

### Property 10: Edge role-gate decision

*For any* principal (`source ∈ {local_session, existing_token_auth, anonymous}`, role ∈ the six Portal_Roles or null), tool classification, `confirm` presence, and mutating switch, `authz.decide` allows read-only tools for every principal, allows mutating tools iff the switch is on and `source == local_session` and role ∈ {Operator, UseCaseAdmin, PortalAdmin} and (`confirm` matches or the tool is not destructive), and otherwise returns the specific denial reason. **Validates: Requirements 10.2–10.6, 9.1**

### Property 11: Edge configuration parsing and transitions

*For any* sequence of component-configuration dicts (including read errors and missing keys) returned by an injected `read_component_config_via_ipc`, `McpConfig` is enabled only for explicit true representations, `LocalLoginConfig` observes the same `LocalLoginEnabled` values as before the refactor, and the supervisor's desired state after each poll equals `(server_enabled, mutating_enabled)` with a restart requested iff `mutating_enabled` changed while enabled. **Validates: Requirements 12.1, 12.2, 10.7**

### Property 12: Proxy exposure rule

*For any* peer address, listener kind (TLS/plaintext), presence of `Origin` (any value), allow-list (empty by default), and config state, the proxy decision is 403 `invalid_origin` when `Origin` is present and not in the allow-list (always, when the list is empty — review-5 N5); else 403 `mcp_loopback_only` when the peer is non-loopback and the listener is plaintext; else 503 when the server is disabled or not ready; else forward with an outbound header set that never contains `Origin`. **Validates: Requirements 12.4, 12.5**

### Property 13: Edge log record hygiene

*For any* tool completion record built by `child/logging.py` from arbitrary principal, headers, arguments, query strings and outcome, the serialized line contains the marker fields, principal, role, outcome, duration, and request id, and never contains the `Authorization` value, the `token` query value, any argument named like a credential (`password`, `token`, `secret`, `urlSecret`), or a body larger than 1 KiB. **Validates: Requirements 11.1, 11.2, 9.7**

### Property 14: Unchanged matrix after `AuthDecision`

*For any* combination of the decision-matrix inputs (Property 19 of portal-user-manager), the new `authorize_credential` raises exactly when the old one raised, returns `None` exactly in the open-access row, and otherwise returns an `AuthDecision` whose `source` names the admitting rule, whose `username`/`role` equal the validated account's for a Local_Session_Token, and whose `expires_at` equals the token payload's `exp`. **Validates: Requirements 9.1, 12.7, F8**

### Property 15: Naming and identifier convention

*For any* pair of tools (one per server) that share a concept argument (`workflow_id`, `registration_id`, `execution_id`, `device_id`, `usecase_id`), the argument names and schema types are identical. **Validates: Requirements 14.1, 14.3**

### Property 16: Edge credential forwarding

*For any* edge `ToolSpec` and credential (or none), `call_backend` places the credential in the `Authorization` header and never in the URL when `credential_in_query` is False, and in the `token` query parameter and never in a header when it is True; with no credential it sends neither; `X-Request-ID` is always forwarded. **Validates: Requirements 9.1, 9.7, 11.3**

### Property 17: Discovery document consistency

*For any* syntactically valid `https://` origin (host, optional port, no path) used as `MCP_AS_ISSUER`, canonical URL `<origin>/mcp`, view name, and scope string, `discovery.prm_document(url)["resource"] == url`, `prm_document(url)["authorization_servers"] == [origin]`, `authorization_server_document()["issuer"] == origin`, neither string ends with `/`, the facade's three Cognito endpoints start with `COGNITO_HOSTED_UI_ORIGIN` and `jwks_uri` with `COGNITO_ISSUER`, `code_challenge_methods_supported == ["S256"]`; and *for any* origin carrying a path, query, fragment or trailing slash, importing `discovery` with that value raises. **Validates: Requirements 2.4, 7.5**

### Property 18: Edge child transport security

*For any* request to the child's `/mcp` endpoint with an arbitrary `Origin` header value (any scheme/host/port, including `http://127.0.0.1:5000` and `http://localhost:5790`), an arbitrary `Host` header, and an arbitrary body size, the child started with no `--allowed-origins` (the default) answers 403 whenever `Origin` is present — and, started with a list, 403 iff the `Origin` is outside it (review-5 N5) — 421 whenever `Host` is neither `127.0.0.1:5790` nor `localhost:5790`, and 413 whenever the body exceeds 1 MiB — regardless of whether the request arrived through the proxy or directly over loopback — and otherwise processes the request exactly as a proxied one (review-3 M3). **Validates: Requirements 12.5, 9.1**

### Property 19: Edge result path hygiene

*For any* JSON body of arbitrary nesting (objects, arrays, scalars) whose keys are drawn from path-like names (`inferenceFilePath`, `imageDataFilePath`, `inputImageFilePath`, `archivePath`, `artifactPath`, `captureIdPath`, `outputDir`, `location`, `captureLocation`, `imageFileName`, arbitrary `*Path`/`*Dir`/`*Folder` keys) and ordinary names, and whose string values are drawn from absolute device paths (`/aws_dda/…`, `/tmp/…`, `/opt/…`, `file:///…`), relative paths (`snapshotfile/x.tar.gz`), bare file names, URLs with a scheme (`rtsp://host/stream`, `https://…`) and plain text, `child/shaping.scrub_paths(body)` returns a body in which **no path-like string value remains under a `PATH_KEY_RE` key** — path-like per D34(a): contains `/` and does not match `^(?!file:)[a-z][a-z0-9+.-]*://` (so a kept `rtsp://…/live` under `location` is consistent with the clause — review-7 N2), **no whitespace-free string value anywhere starts with `/aws_dda/`, `/tmp/`, `/opt/`, `/home/`, `/root/`, `/var/`, `/etc/`, `/usr/`, `/snapshot/` or `file://`** — the value rule is the single-token regex `^(file://)?/(aws_dda|tmp|opt|home|root|var|etc|usr|snapshot)/\S*$`, so a string that contains whitespace (a log line, an error message) is never a "path" even when it begins with one (review-6 N2), and strings under other keys that merely look like paths — e.g. `/mcp/status`'s `"endpoint": "/mcp"` — are left alone, which is why the value rule is anchored to device roots; every replaced value equals the file name of the original (`os.path.basename` after stripping `file://` and a trailing `/`), URL values with a non-`file:` scheme and all non-matching keys are byte-identical to the input, the `{"text": …}` wrapper of a non-JSON body is returned byte-identical, and `scrubbed_fields` lists exactly the key paths that changed; the walk is idempotent (`scrub(scrub(b)) == scrub(b)`). Fixed cases: `{"archivePath": "snapshotfile/snapshot-s-2026-10-19-08-15-00.tar.gz"}` → `"snapshot-s-2026-10-19-08-15-00.tar.gz"`; `{"images": [{"inferenceFilePath": "/aws_dda/inference-results/wf/0f3c.jsonl"}]}` → `"0f3c.jsonl"` with `scrubbed_fields == ["images[].inferenceFilePath"]`; `{"imageFileName": "img_001.jpg"}` unchanged with `scrubbed_fields == []` (a `PATH_KEY_RE` key whose value has no `/` — review-6 N6); `{"location": "rtsp://10.0.0.9:554/live"}` unchanged; `{"location": "/aws_dda/image-capture/folder-src"}` → `"folder-src"`; `{"text": "/aws_dda/x: started\nline 2"}` unchanged with `scrubbed_fields == []` (review-6 N2); `{"message": "/aws_dda/system/db.sqlite is locked"}` unchanged (whitespace → not a path token) while `{"message": "/aws_dda/system/db.sqlite"}` → `"db.sqlite"`. **Validates: Requirement 8.5**

## Testing Strategy

Both suites already use Hypothesis (`edge-cv-portal/backend/tests`, `test/backend-test`) and jest + fast-check (`edge-cv-portal/infrastructure/test`); the only new test-time dependencies are `mcp==2.3.0` (its `Client` is the protocol-level test harness) and `mangum==0.22.0` in the portal test requirements, and `mcp==2.3.0` in the edge host test requirements. **Interpreter (N4):** the build host's default `python3` is 3.9, which `mcp` does not support; `test/backend-test/mcp_edge/` and `edge-cv-portal/backend/tests/mcp/` are run with `uv run --python 3.11 -m pytest …` (or `~/.local/bin/python3.11 -m pytest`), and the modules that run inside the flask-app container (`build-custom.sh` list) are the backend-process modules only, which import no `mcp`. The edge child tests run on the host, not in the container, like the existing `test/backend-test/local_auth/` tests.

### Portal — `edge-cv-portal/backend/tests/mcp/`

| Requirement 16 criterion | Test module | Mechanism |
|---|---|---|
| 16.1 protocol level | `test_mcp_protocol.py` | `Client(server_object, raise_exceptions=True)` in memory for each view's `Server`: `server/discover` fields (name, version, instructions), `tools/list` (deterministic, `ttlMs`/`cacheScope` present, annotations match the catalog, **every description ends with the classification text matching its annotations and contains `Touches: <resource_type>`, and one spec given a test-only `deprecated_alias`/`alias_removed_in` is listed twice — the alias entry's description starts `Deprecated alias of …; removed in …` — and `call_tool(alias)` runs the target and returns a result carrying `deprecation` text**, D35 — review-5 M4), `call_tool` on one RO tool (`portal_devices_list` against a fake Lambda client returning a canned 200) and one M tool (`portal_devices_deployment_create` with `target_thing_group` and `confirm` equal to that thing-group name — review-9 N7 — and `MCP_MUTATING_TOOLS_ENABLED=true`), error shapes for `invalid_input`, `confirmation_required`, `mutating_tools_disabled`, unknown tool (`-32602`).
