`limit`'s `maximum` equals `min(200, N, limit_max)` where `N` is the handler's own cap when it has one (`min(int(params.get('limit', …)), N)` or a clamp constant). **Fixed regression cases:** `captures.list_captures` requires `usecase_id` and `prefix`, caps `limit` at 100 (→ `portal_data_captures_list.input_schema.required ⊇ {usecase_id, prefix}`, `limit.maximum == 30`, the spec's result-size bound); `datasets.count_images` and `get_image_preview` require `prefix`, `list_datasets` does not; `get_image_preview` caps `limit` at 50; **review-7:** `device_logs_analyzer.analyze_device_logs` reads exactly `usecase_id` and `hours` from the query string and no body (→ `portal_devices_logs_analyze` has `hours` and has neither `component` nor `question`); `workflow_validation.validate_workflow` reads `body.get('version')` only (→ `portal_workflows_validate` has `version`, no `definition`); `training.transform_manifest` requires `source_manifest_uri`; `compilation.start_compilation_job` reads `targets` and the schema has **no** `auto_triggered` property (asserted explicitly — Backlog 20); `greengrass_publish.publish_component` requires `component_name` and `component_version`; `data_management.create_folder` requires `folder_path` and `bucket` is defaulted (479–480; review-8 N1); `synthetic_data.get_prompt_template` requires `object_type` and `defect_type`; `build_jobs.list_build_branches` requires `repository`; `build_jobs.list_builds`/`get_build_logs` read `nextToken` (→ `param_map next_token → nextToken`); **review-8 M2 / revision 9:** `component_configuration.get_configuration_schema` requires `component_name` (239–240) and reads no `usecase_id`; `training.get_training_logs` reads `nextForwardToken` (1341), not `nextToken`; `deployments.get_deployment`/`cancel_deployment` and `device_logs` require query `usecase_id` (→ a `param_map` entry); `workflows.create_workflow` requires `name` and `definition` (366–369); `dda_labeling.submit_labeler_task` reads `annotation` (2792); the `setdefault`-scoped `labeling`/`dda_labeling` job, team and rerun specs have **no** `param_map['usecase_id']` (Backlog 27); **revision 10 (review-9 M1/M2/N3/N4):** the thirteen `usecase_id` rows with their reads — `devices.list_devices` 226 and `get_device` 370, `deployments.list_deployments` 925, `shared_components.list_shared_components` 990, `datasets.list_datasets` 111 and `get_image_preview` 368, `pre_labeled_datasets.list_datasets` 125, `workflow_validation.get_node_catalog` 294, `user_management.list_users` 72 (query); `datasets.count_images` 262, `pre_labeled_datasets.validate_manifest` 223, `workflow_generator.submit_generation` 657 (body); `usecases.delete_usecase` (path `id`, `usecases.handler` 1307–1308); the five route-paginated rows — `audit_logs.get_audit_logs` (`limit`, 89), `build_jobs.list_builds` (508), `build_jobs.get_build_logs` (698), `datasets.get_image_preview` (`offset`/`limit`, 370–371), `captures.list_captures` (123) — with `get_build_logs` as the failing variant (a spec that maps `next_token → nextToken` but leaves `limit` dispatcher-sliced fails: the route's default page is 200 events, the catalog default 50); the constant reads — `deployments.create_deployment`'s `body.get(deployment_preflight.ACKNOWLEDGEMENT_FIELD)` (1899–1900; constant at `deployment_preflight.py:101`), the tuning fields of `usecases.create_usecase`/`update_usecase` (`tuning_settings.SAMPLE_EXPORT_FIELD`/`SAMPLE_RETENTION_FIELD`, `tuning_settings.py:32/35`, iterated at 151–154 and `usecases.py:1538–1541`) and `update_usecase`'s local `updatable_fields` list (1958–1972, the only read of its `name`, `description`, `region`, `owner`, `default_device_group` and `data_s3_bucket`), `synthetic_data.patch_session`'s `PATCHABLE_FIELDS` (89–93, 543), `labeling.create_labeling_job`'s DDA arguments through `getattr(dda_labeling, 'create_dda_job')` (264–271 → `dda_labeling.py:1629`), and a path argument read only by the entry point (`portal_admin_usecase_get`, `usecases.handler` 1303–1304); and the three dispatcher-sliced rows `portal_admin_usecases_list`, `portal_devices_list`, `portal_devices_deployments_list` have `offset` |
| Catalog routes are registered Portal_API methods (review-3 M1) | `test_mcp_catalog_routes_export.py` (pytest) **and** `infrastructure/test/mcp-catalog-routes.test.ts` (jest) | Two halves pin the same file. **Python:** `catalog_routes()` equals the parsed `mcp_portal/catalog_routes.json` byte-for-byte (sorted, de-duplicated `{"method","resource","backend"}` triples) — a catalog change without `--export-routes` fails here; the same command writes the sibling `mcp_portal/catalog_tools.json` (`{"name","classification","destructive","phase","status_tool"}` per spec, sorted by name) and the test pins it the same way, so the `status_tool` mapping of D32 is reviewable as a checked-in file. **CDK:** synthesizes `ComputeStack` and reads the templates of its nested API stacks by construct id (`compute.node.findChild('ApiGateway'\|'CameraRegistryApi'\|'UserAdminApi'\|'QuickSetupApi'\|'DdaLabelingApi'\|'WorkflowManagerGapsApi'\|'WorkflowTuningApi') as cdk.NestedStack` — the ids at `compute-stack.ts:3807/3850/3867/3885/3907/3930/3959`), plus `NodeDesignerStack`'s `NodeDesignerApi` (`node-designer-stack.ts:1417`), `BuildFleetStack` and `SyntheticDataStack`; builds the set of `METHOD /template/path` strings with the walker of `camera-registry-infra.test.ts:283–305` generalized to a prefix table for imported parents (`PortalApi` root → ``, `DeviceResource` → `/devices/{id}`, `LabelingJobResource` → `/labeling/{id}`, `WorkflowGenerateResource` → `/workflows/generate`, `WorkflowResource` → `/workflows/{id}`; an imported parent absent from the table fails the test); asserts **every** `(method, resource)` in `catalog_routes.json` is in that set (exact template string, parameter names included, because `dda_labeling.py` routes on `event['resource']`), and — as the fixed regression case for M1 — that `POST /admin/users`, `POST /admin/users/{username}/disable`, `POST …/enable` and `DELETE /admin/users/{username}` are **absent** from both the route set and the catalog until Backlog 2 registers them. **Handler-dispatch half (review-8 M2; pytest, `test_mcp_catalog_routes_export.py`):** registration is not implementation, so for every spec with a backing route an AST walk from the backend module's entry point (`handler`/`lambda_handler`, followed through a forwarding shim — `camera_video_pin.handler` → `camera_registry.handler`, 45) must reach `handler_function` on a branch whose guard compares the request method with the spec's `method` literal and matches its `resource` (a `path`/`resource` comparison or a `(method, resource)` route-table key such as `synthetic_data.ROUTES`); where one `(method, resource)` reaches several functions through a query-key discriminator, the spec's required arguments must select its own (`deployments.handler`, 892–902). Fixed cases: `POST /components` and `POST /components/discover` are registered (`api-gateway-stack.ts:683`, 705) but **absent** from `components.lambda_handler`, which compares the method only with `'GET'` and `'DELETE'` (216–228; Backlog 21), and are asserted absent from the catalog; `portal_devices_deployment_for_target_get` is the discriminated case |

### Portal infrastructure — `edge-cv-portal/infrastructure/test/mcp-stack.test.ts`

`Template.fromStack(mcpStack)` assertions: the set of `AWS::ApiGatewayV2::Route` `RouteKey`s is **exactly** `{POST /mcp, GET /mcp, DELETE /mcp, POST /mcp/{view}, GET /mcp/{view}, DELETE /mcp/{view}, GET /.well-known/oauth-protected-resource, GET /.well-known/oauth-protected-resource/mcp, GET /.well-known/oauth-protected-resource/mcp/{view}, GET /.well-known/oauth-authorization-server}` (ten keys; the root PRM and the RFC 8414 facade are new in revision 3 — review-2 H1/M1) and no `$default` route exists; every route has `AuthorizationType: NONE` and the single Lambda integration (Requirement 16.6 — the enumerated exception set: the `/mcp*` routes are authenticated inside the Lambda by the SDK bearer middleware, proven by `test_mcp_http_transport.py`; the four `.well-known` GET shapes are unauthenticated by RFC 9728/8414); no `AWS::ApiGatewayV2::Authorizer` resource exists; the stage is `$default` with `DefaultRouteSettings.ThrottlingRateLimit/BurstLimit` = 20/40 and `RouteSettings` 10/20 for the six `/mcp*` keys; the Lambda environment contains `MCP_CLIENT_ID`, `MCP_REQUIRED_SCOPE`, `MCP_ALLOWED_AUDIENCES` (six URLs), `COGNITO_ISSUER`, `COGNITO_HOSTED_UI_ORIGIN`, `MCP_AS_ISSUER` (resolving to the `HttpApi` endpoint attribute with no trailing slash), `MCP_API_HOST`, and `MCP_BACKEND_FUNCTIONS_PARAMETER` resolving to the SSM parameter's name — **and does not contain `MCP_BACKEND_FUNCTIONS`** (review-4 H1); the serialized `Environment.Variables`, with every token placeholder expanded to a worst-case literal (50-character execute-api host, 64-character table names), is **below 3,000 bytes** (Lambda's quota is 4,096 bytes aggregate); an `AWS::SSM::Parameter` with `Name: /dda-portal/mcp/backend-functions`, `Type: String`, `Tier: Advanced` exists and its `Value` is an `Fn::Join` that references every `backendHandlers` ARN (43 `Fn::ImportValue` references — one per backend key: the 32 `ComputeStack` keys of the snapshot below plus the 11 `NodeDesignerStack`/`BuildFleetStack`/`SyntheticDataStack` handlers); the Lambda role has no `*` action or resource and exactly the **six** inline statement shapes (invoke; user-roles GetItem+Query; usecases Scan; audit PutItem; AdminGetUser; `ssm:GetParameter` on the parameter's ARN — no `ssm:DescribeParameters`) plus the basic-execution managed policy, and the serialized inline policy is below 9,000 characters (IAM's limit is 10,240); the stack contains exactly **two** `AWS::Lambda::LayerVersion` resources (`McpSharedLayer`, `McpLayer`) and the function's `Layers` property contains no `Fn::ImportValue` (review-4 H2); the function's `Timeout` is **29**, the environment contains `MCP_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` equal to `''` when no `mcpAllowedOrigins` context is given and to the joined list otherwise (an entry with a path or without a scheme fails synth), and the stack contains one `AWS::Logs::LogGroup` (`/dda-portal/mcp-server`, referenced by the function's `LoggingConfig.LogGroup`), one `AWS::Logs::MetricFilter` with pattern `"Task timed out"` on it and one `AWS::CloudWatch::Alarm` on its metric — and **no** `LogRetention` custom-resource function (`Custom::LogRetention`) (review-5 M3, N5); the user-pool client has `GenerateSecret: false`, `AllowedOAuthFlows: ['code']`, `AllowedOAuthFlowsUserPoolClient: true`, no `openid` scope, **and `template.hasResourceProperties('AWS::Cognito::UserPoolClient', { ExplicitAuthFlows: ['ALLOW_REFRESH_TOKEN_AUTH'] })` — the property must be present and equal to exactly that one-element list** (review-3 H1: with `authFlows: {}` CDK omits it and Cognito would default to SRP + custom + refresh; a second assertion `Match.not(Match.arrayWith(['ALLOW_USER_SRP_AUTH']))` names the failure mode in the test output); every `CallbackURLs` entry matches `^http://localhost(:\d+)?/` or `^https://` (review-3 N9); the resource server and client are resources **of the MCP stack** (not of `AuthStack`); `-c deployMcpServer=false` synthesizes no MCP resources; `-c mcpMutatingToolsEnabled=…` lands as `'true'`/`'false'` only (fast-check over arbitrary context strings, `context-helpers.property.test.ts` pattern). A snapshot test pins `ComputeStack.mcpBackendHandlers` (all 32 keys incl. `deviceLogsAnalyzer` and `componentConfiguration`) and `ApiGatewayStack.componentConfigurationHandler` so an accidental removal fails, and asserts that `ComputeStack` exposes **no** public layer member (`'sharedLayer' in computeStack` is false) so the cross-stack `LayerVersion` import of review-4 H2 cannot creep back; a companion assertion on `Template.fromStack(computeStack)` checks that every new `Output` with an `Export` resolves to a function ARN (`Fn::GetAtt … Arn`), never to a layer. **Opt-out guard (D33; review-4 M3) — `edge-cv-portal/tests/mcp_stack_optout.bats`** (the existing offline bats harness, `tests/test_helper.bash`, with its `aws` stub's `set_cfn_output`/`STUB_CFN_MISSING_STACKS` controls): sourcing `scripts/portal-mcp-stack.sh`, (1) `DEPLOY_MCP_SERVER=false` with `EdgeCVPortalMcpStack` present in the stub → the guard exits 1 and its stderr names `npx cdk destroy EdgeCVPortalMcpStack --force`; (2) `DEPLOY_MCP_SERVER=false` with the stack in `STUB_CFN_MISSING_STACKS` → exit 0 and `CDK_CONTEXT_ARGS` gains `-c deployMcpServer=false`; (3) unset, empty, `true`, `0`, `no` or `off` → exit 0 and no `deployMcpServer` context (only a value that trims and lower-cases to `false` is off — the `mcpServerEnabled` rule, review-5 N4), regardless of the stack's existence, while ` FALSE ` → treated as off; (4) a `describe-stacks` failure that is not "does not exist" (stub `AccessDenied`) → exit 1 (fail rather than guess, the `portal_registry_enforced_for_deploy` rule); (5) `deploy-infrastructure.sh` run end to end under the harness with the stubbed `npm`/`npx` and case (1) never reaches `cdk deploy`.

| 16.7 logging | `test_log_relay.py`, `test_child_logging.py` (Property 13) | JSON lines → structlog capture; malformed lines; relay exceptions |
| 16.1 protocol level | `test_child_protocol.py` | `Client(server_object)` in memory against the child's `Server` with a fake backend (`httpx2` `MockTransport`) and a fake `/local-auth/whoami`: list (descriptions and alias entries as the portal test, D35), one RO (`edge_registrations_list` — the fake root-array body's `artifactPath` comes back as its last segment with `scrubbed_fields == ["[].artifactPath"]`, revision 9), one M (`edge_registration_trigger` with `--mutating-enabled` and an Operator principal, **no body sent** — review-5 N3), one `credential_in_query` tool (`edge_execution_output_image_get`: asserts the fake backend saw `?token=` and no `Authorization` header — Property 16), **`edge_image_source_preview` against a fake body `{"image": <600 KB of base64 for a 450 KB JPEG>}` → exactly one `ImageContent(mime_type="image/jpeg")`, `result == {}`, and no `result_too_large`** (review-5 M2; the same body through a spec with `image_field=None` must produce `result_too_large`, as the control case), `edge_execution_overlay_get` with `{"maskImage": <PNG base64>, "maskBackground": {...}}` → one `ImageContent(image/png)` and `result == {"maskBackground": ...}`, `edge_workflow_images_list` with two items each carrying a 300 KB `image` → no `ImageContent`, `dropped_fields == ["images[].image"]`, both items returned, `inferenceFilePath` reduced to `<capture_id>.jsonl`, `edge_system_snapshot_create(confirm="snapshot")` (`--mutating-enabled`, Operator) → `result["archivePath"] == "snapshot-s-….tar.gz"` and `scrubbed_fields == ["archivePath"]`, without `confirm` → `confirmation_required` (revision 9), while the same call **without** `--mutating-enabled` is `mutating_tools_disabled` and for an `anonymous` principal `not_authorized` (review-5 H1), error shapes, `not_available_on_device` without `--features vllm`. **Review-6 cases:** `edge_workflow_images_list` called with no `limit` reaches the fake backend with `maxResults=2` and `startingPoint=0`, while `limit=3` is `invalid_input` naming `limit` **before** any backend call (M1); `edge_system_station_get` with a 400 KB `logoImage` data URL → `result` without `logoImage`, `dropped_fields == ["logoImage"]`, no `result_too_large`; `edge_results_list(page=2, limit=20, prediction="Anomaly")` reaches the backend as `?page=2&size=20&prediction=Anomaly` and a page of twelve items each carrying a 300 KB `maskImage` comes back with twelve items and `dropped_fields == ["results[].maskImage"]`; `edge_result_get` with a PNG `maskImage` → one `ImageContent(image/png)`; `edge_registration_get` with 3 000 fake executions → `dropped_fields == ["executions"]` and no `result_too_large`; `edge_workflow_run(workflow_id)` sends the body `{"returnImageString": false}` and `edge_workflow_run(workflow_id, capture_image_count=3, capture_time_interval=1)` sends `{"returnImageString": false, "captureImageCount": 3, "captureTimeInterval": 1}` with `resource_id == <captureTaskId>` from the fake response (M2); `edge_image_source_capture` against `{"image": <600 KB JPEG base64>}` → exactly one `ImageContent(image/jpeg)` and `result == {"captured": true}`, and `file_prefix="x/y"` is `invalid_input` (M3); `edge_static_image_pin(image_base64=<100 KB JPEG>, file_name="probe.jpg")` reaches the fake backend as `multipart/form-data` with one part named `file`, `filename="probe.jpg"`, bytes equal to the decoded argument; `image_base64` of 960 001 characters → `invalid_input`; no spec exposes a property matching `PATH_KEY_RE` (M4); `edge_results_summary_reset(workflow_id, confirm)` with no `reset_time` sends `{"resetTime": <int within 2 s of now>}` and with `reset_time=1700000000` sends exactly that (M5); a generic field holding a 20 KB JPEG under a spec with `image_field=None` → `"<dropped: 27308 bytes image>"` and the path in `dropped_fields` (the guard, M2); `edge_textgen_generate` is dispatched with `read=130.0` and every other spec with `read=60.0` (the patched transport records the per-request timeout — N4); `edge_execution_log_get` with a `text/plain` body whose first line is `/aws_dda/x: started` returns it unchanged with `scrubbed_fields == []` (N2). **Review-7 cases:** `edge_image_source_add(type="RTSP", name="cam", stream_url="rtsp://10.0.0.9:554/live")` reaches the fake backend with body key `location` equal to that URL and no `stream_url` key, `stream_url="/aws_dda/folder"` is `invalid_input` naming `stream_url`, and no spec exposes a property matching `PATH_KEY_RE` while `edge_image_source_add.param_map['stream_url'] == 'location'` (M1); `edge_textgen_generate(model_name, prompt="hi", max_tokens=8)` sends `{"prompt": "hi", "max_tokens": 8}` (M2); `edge_static_image_pin(image_base64="QUJD=A", file_name="x.jpg")` — schema-valid, not valid base64 — is `invalid_input` "image_base64 is not valid base64" and the fake backend receives no request (N3); `edge_results_update(workflow_id, inference_results=[{"capture_id": "c1", "human_classification": "Anomaly"}])` sends `{"inferenceResults": [{"captureId": "c1", "humanClassification": "Anomaly"}]}` and an item without `capture_id` is `invalid_input` (N4); `edge_execution_log_get` against a 900 000-character ASCII `text/plain` body returns `result["text"]` equal to the last 150 000 characters with `truncated is True` and `total_chars == 900_000` and no `result_too_large`, `tail_chars=2000` returns the last 2 000, and a 10 000-character body returns whole with no `truncated` key (N6); **review-8 N4:** bodies of 150 000 `"`+newline characters and of 150 000 U+FFFD characters each return a suffix whose `json.dumps({"text": …})` is ≤ `MAX_RESULT_BYTES − 1 000` bytes, with `truncated is True`, and neither is `result_too_large`; `edge_workflow_create` against a bare `"a1b2c3d4"` body → `resource_id == "a1b2c3d4"`; `edge_result_get(capture_id="../../../cameras/X/disconnect")` and `capture_id=".."` are `invalid_input` and the fake backend receives no request, and a value with `:` reaches it percent-encoded as one segment (revision 9); a fixed-order case for `result_constant` — a `{"image": <JPEG base64>, "note": "/aws_dda/x/y.txt"}` body through a spec whose `result_constant` is `{"captured": True, "where": "/aws_dda/system/a.txt"}` yields `result == {"captured": true, "where": "a.txt", "note": "y.txt"}`, proving the constant passes through the scrub after extraction (N1). **`test_credential_in_query_never_logged` (review-2 M4):** runs `configure_logging()` then the `credential_in_query` call with `caplog` at `DEBUG` on a *separate* capturing handler attached beneath the root level and with `capsys`; asserts the credential string appears in no emitted `LogRecord` (from any logger, including `httpx2`) and not on stderr; a sibling test deliberately calls `logging.basicConfig(level=logging.INFO, force=True)` **before** `configure_logging()` and asserts the same (the configuration must win) |
| 16.2 negatives | `test_proxy_route.py`, `test_child_principal.py` | proxy under the real `authorize_request` with each matrix row (no credential, expired token, bad signature, disabled account → 401; any `Origin` → 403 and never forwarded; non-loopback peer on plaintext → 403; credential in a query parameter at `/mcp` → 401 because `optional_oauth2_scheme` reads the header only); `test_proxy_route.py` also covers the buffered relay (review-3 N8): an upstream that sends headers and then stalls past the read timeout → 504 `mcp_timeout`; an upstream body > 4 MiB → 502 `mcp_bad_upstream` |
| 12.5 / child transport security (review-3 M3; Property 18) | `test_child_protocol.py::test_child_rejects_origin_and_foreign_host`, `test_property_child_transport_security.py` | drives the child's real Starlette app (`build_app(...)` → `streamable_http_app(...)` with the production `TransportSecuritySettings`) over `httpx2.ASGITransport`: `POST /mcp` with `Origin: http://127.0.0.1:5000` → 403 and `Origin: http://localhost:5790` → 403 (the SDK's loopback defaults would have accepted both — asserted by a control case that builds the app with `transport_security=None` and shows 200, so a future SDK change in either direction is noticed); `Host: 10.0.0.5:5790` → 421; `Host: 127.0.0.1:5790` and `Host: localhost:5790` → pass; body of 1 MiB + 1 byte → 413; a direct call with no `Authorization` on a local-login device → the fake `/local-auth/whoami` answers 401 and the tool result is `not_authenticated` (the child never acts on its own authority, D28) |
| 16.5 confirmation/switch | `test_child_guardrails.py` | as the portal |
| Edge classification honesty (review-5 H1) and result hygiene (review-5 M1/M2; Properties 9 and 19) | `test_child_catalog_classification.py`, `test_property_child_shaping.py` | **AST-based**, the `test_mcp_handler_audit_flags.py` mechanism: for each `RO` edge spec, parse the handler module (`src/backend/endpoints/<module>.py` or `workflow_engine/api.py`), locate `handler_function` and walk its call graph **transitively** over `src/backend` (revision 10, review-9 N1), resolving same-module calls, `from m import f` (absolute and relative), imported module objects including dotted chains (`snapshot.Snapshotter.take_snapshot`), `self.f`/`cls.f` in the class and its first-party bases, `C.f` and constructor calls `C(...)` → `C.__init__`, methods of **typed objects** — a local name, a `self.<attr>` member or a module global bound to `C(...)` or to a call of a function annotated `-> C`/`Optional[C]` (or, unannotated, returning such a value), and such a call used directly as a receiver (`get_stream_ingest_manager().capabilities(…)`, `streams.py:323`) — and **function references** used as values, i.e. callbacks and parameter defaults (`threading.Thread(target=self._run)`, `prober=run_probe`, `capabilities.py:325/353`); it does not enter `utils/auth.py` or `local_auth/` (authentication, not the route — the five `credential_in_query` routes call it inline) — and fail if a function on that graph calls `subprocess.*`, `os.system`, `os.remove`/`os.unlink`/`os.replace`/`os.rename`, a `shutil` function other than `disk_usage`/`which`/`get_terminal_size`, a file-creating `tempfile` function (not `gettempdir`), `open(..., mode)` with `w`/`a`/`x`/`+` in `mode`, `Path(...).write_*`, `os.makedirs`/`os.mkdir`/`os.rmdir`/`os.chmod`, a `pathlib` twin of a listed call on a `Path` value (`Path.unlink`/`rename`/`replace`/`touch`/`mkdir`/`rmdir`/`chmod`, `Path.open` with a write mode — a `.replace` on a `datetime` is no effect), or `.save(...)` on a Pillow image value as Decision point 4 defines it (a call of `Image.open`/`Image.new`/`Image.fromarray` or an `ImageOps` function, a method call on such a value, or a name bound from one, `with`-targets included), reported as `Image.save` (revision 11, review-10 N7; revision 12, review-11 N3 — fixed case: the chained `ImageOps.exif_transpose(Image.open(p)).save(q)` is an effect); every finding is reported as `'<module>.<function>:<effect-list name>'`, the key `accepted_effects` entries use; also fail if the route's HTTP method is not `GET` and it is not on the explicit read-only-POST list (`/image-sources/{id}/preview`, `…/test-connection` — accepted reading: ingest-session lease and possible restart, the HMI's own connection test, nothing persisted (review-6 N1) — and `/text-generation/{m}/generate`). **Fixed regression cases** (asserted independently of the catalog): `system.get_snapshot` → not `RO` (its directly-called imported helper `Snapshotter.take_snapshot` calls `subprocess.check_output`, `Snapshotter.py:70`); `inference_result.save_capture_ids_for_inference_results_download` → not `RO` (`open(capture_id_path, "w")`; the tool that wrapped it is gone — review-6 N3 — but the case stays as the guard against its return); `camera.connect_camera_endpoint`, `feature_config.start_feature_config`, `workflow.retry_dio_workflow` → not `RO` (GET-but-mutating rows); `image_source.preview_image`, `system.get_system_health`, `workflow.list_images` → `RO`; **`image_source.test_stream_connection`, `feature_config.list_feature_configs`, `feature_config.get_gpu_status`, `streams.get_stream_capabilities`, `api.list_registration_executions` → `RO`** (the last two added in revision 9: capability-probe subprocess and `/dev/shm` sweep; the retention index's orphan sweep on `notable=true`) — every effect the walk finds on them must be in the spec's `accepted_effects`, and an entry the walk no longer finds fails; **at `c3bf88c1` the walk finds exactly eight accepted effects on eight tools** (six until revision 12) (the `/dev/shm` sweep on `edge_workflows_list`, `edge_workflow_get`, `edge_image_sources_list`, `edge_image_source_get`, `_stream_health_get`, `_test_connection`, `_preview` and `edge_streams_capabilities_get`; `run_probe` on `_capabilities_get`; `run_command`, the empty-frame `delete_image`, `write_csi_config`'s `open` and — revision 12, review-11 N3 — its `os.makedirs`/`os.chmod` (`csi_capture.py:69/80`), and — revision 11, review-10 N7 — the JP6 staging `GstPipelineBuilder._stage_decoded_png:Image.save`, `pipeline_builder.py:187` (keyed by the effect-list name, not the receiver text `im.save`), a fixed case, on `_test_connection` and `_preview`; the stdlib's `tempfile.gettempdir`, `protocol.py:82`, is a lookup, not an effect) and nothing on the other 37 walked tools (`edge_whoami` and `edge_mcp_status_get` are new routes, walked once they exist); the accepted readings outside its reach stay asserted fixed cases with a comment — the ingest-session lease/restart, whose worker spawn (`stream_ingest/launch.py:84`) sits behind a manager passed in as an untyped parameter (`connection_test.py:55`); the lazy `TritonEdgeClient.get_instance` and `model_status_shadow.report` (no call of the effect list on their paths; review-6 N1); and the retention orphan sweep behind `notable=true` (an index injected through a constructor argument). **`test_child_catalog_arguments.py` (review-6 M5; review-7 M1/M2/N6)** is the edge half of the argument ↔ route check: for each spec, parse the route module, locate `handler_function`, collect its FastAPI signature (path/query parameters, the fields of `Depends()`-injected pydantic models such as `PageParams`, the fields of its request-model parameter) and assert every `param_map` target / pass-through argument / `body_constants` key **not in the edge `SERVER_CONSUMED_ARGS` (`confirm`, `limit`, `offset`, `tail_chars`)** is among them and every non-defaulted parameter or field is in `input_schema.required`; `limit`'s `maximum` must equal the route's own bound where the route has one (`MaxNumberOfResultsValidator` → 2, `PageParams.size` → 100, `EXECUTIONS_LIMIT_MAX` → 50); **when a spec sets `body_fields`** the route's body parameter must be annotated `Dict`/`Any` (asserted by AST — a spec may not set `body_fields` on a request-model route), the pass-through arguments and `param_map` targets are checked against `body_fields` instead, and every `body_fields` name must occur as a string literal in the route module; a `param_map` target matching `PATH_KEY_RE` is allowed only when the property's schema `pattern` is anchored on `^rtsps?://\|^rtmps?://`. Fixed cases: `edge_results_list` (`page`/`size`, no `offset`), `edge_registration_executions_list` (`limit → limit`, the route's own parameter, default 10, `api.py:200/210` — revision 10, the review-9 M2 rule), `edge_results_summary_reset` (`resetTime`), `edge_workflow_run` (`returnImageString` and the three capture fields), `edge_workflow_images_list` (`maxResults`/`startingPoint`, maximum 2), `edge_image_source_capture` (`filePrefix`), `edge_static_image_pin` (the one multipart spec — asserted as such, not through the signature); **review-7:** `edge_textgen_generate.body_fields == ('prompt', 'image', 'max_tokens')`, `generate_text`'s `body` parameter annotated `Dict[str, Any]` (`text_generation.py:562`), and the literals `"prompt"` (179), `"image"` (236) and `"max_tokens"` (193–194) present in the module — so a rename of a body key fails the test (M2); `edge_image_source_add`/`_update`: `param_map['stream_url'] == 'location'`, the property pattern is the URL pattern, and `location` is a field of `AddImageSourceRequest` (317) / `EditImageSourceRequest` (344) (M1); `edge_execution_log_get.text_tail_arg == 'tail_chars'` and `tail_chars` is in `SERVER_CONSUMED_ARGS`, not in the route's signature (N6); `edge_results_update` maps `inference_results → inferenceResults`, the only field of `UpdateInferenceResultsRequest` (150–151) (N4); **revision 9:** `edge_workflow_update` requires `name` (`WorkflowSchema.name`, `model/workflow.py:63`, is not filled from the stored row) and `imageSources` (`workflow_accessor.py:144–145`), `edge_execution_node_image_get` maps `node_id → nodeId` (`download_file.py:256`), `edge_image_source_update` offers neither `type` nor `cameraId` (`EditImageSourceRequest`, 340–350), and every property mapped to a path parameter carries the path-parameter identifier pattern; **revision 11 (review-10 M1):** no `RO` spec has an `object`-typed property, `edge_image_source_preview`'s schema is `{image_source_id}` alone, and a preview call carrying `image_source_configuration` with `processingPipeline` is `invalid_input`; **revision 12 (review-11 N5):** every `string` property of an `RO` spec has a `pattern` or an `enum` or its `(tool, argument)` pair is in `RO_FREE_TEXT_ARGS`, the constant equals the scan's four pairs, and a variant `RO` spec with `processing_pipeline?: string` fails. The Hypothesis module covers Property 19 (`scrub_paths` over generated nested bodies incl. the single-token value rule and the `{"text": …}` exemption, the fixed cases listed in the property) and the edge clauses of Property 9 (`image_field` extraction never counts toward `MAX_RESULT_BYTES`; dotted-path `drop_fields` removed before the bound; the ≥ 16 KiB oversized-image guard with the (a)–(f) fixed cases; decoded size > 2 MiB → `result_too_large`) |
| 16.9 legacy revision | `test_child_legacy_client.py` | `Client(streamable_http_client(url, http_client=httpx2.AsyncClient(transport=httpx2.ASGITransport(child_app))), mode="legacy")` |
| Import boundary | `test_child_import_boundary.py` | imports every `mcp_edge/child/*` module with `utils`, `local_auth`, `dao` stubbed to raise; and asserts `app.py`/supervisor never import `mcp` |
| Matrix preservation (review-2 H2) | `test/backend-test/api-endpoints/test_authorize_request.py` (**modified**: lines 424/430 assert `isinstance(result, AuthDecision)` with `source == 'local_session'` / `'existing_token_auth'`; line 420 and every raise assertion unchanged), `test/backend-test/utils/test_auth.py` (**unchanged** — `validate_token` still returns `None`; gate-listed and source-pinned by `test_preservation_secrets_auth_suite.py`), new `test/backend-test/mcp_edge/test_auth_decision.py` (Property 14 over the full matrix via the `configured(...)` context manager pattern of `test_authorize_request.py`) | `test_authorize_request.py` is the decision-matrix regression suite; it is **not** in the container gate list today (`build-custom.sh:262–267` lists only `test_auth.py` among the auth tests), so this design adds it (row below) to make the matrix a build gate; `test_auth.py` stays byte-identical so both existing gates stay green without edits |
| Container build gate (`build-custom.sh:262–267` pytest list) | gains `test/backend-test/mcp_edge/test_proxy_route.py`, `test_config.py`, `test_shared_ipc_reader.py`, `test_auth_decision.py` and `test/backend-test/api-endpoints/test_authorize_request.py` | backend-process modules only (no `mcp` import, so they run under the image's 3.10/3.11 interpreter); `test_auth.py` stays in the list unchanged; the preservation suite (`build-custom.sh:296–298`) keeps pinning `test_auth.py`'s source |

### Edge — on-hardware harness `test/on-hardware/harness/stages/test_50_mcp.py`

Capability `mcp` (new) in `devices.yaml`; the stage: (1) `GET /mcp/status` through the existing authenticated `harnesslib` client (credential reference `env:`/`file:` as today — no password is stored; login happens through `client.login()`); (2) connects the official `mcp` `Client` to `{base_url}/mcp` with `headers={'Authorization': f'Bearer {token}'}` taken from the harness session (never logged), lists tools, calls one RO tool per capability area present (`edge_system_health_get`, `edge_registrations_list`, `edge_cameras_list`, `edge_image_sources_list`, `edge_feature_configurations_list`, `edge_streams_capabilities_get`, `edge_static_image_pin_get`, `edge_execution_output_image_get` for the latest execution when one exists, `edge_image_source_preview` on the first camera or stream source when one exists — asserting exactly one `ImageContent` with an `image/*` type and no `result_too_large`, the on-hardware proof of the `image_field` path (review-5 M2) — `edge_workflow_images_list` on the harness workflow **with no `limit` argument** when that workflow is digital-input-triggered with its process running (the route serves no other workflow — `workflow.py:436–448`, `workflow_accessor.py:255–273`; skipped, not failed, otherwise; revision 9) — asserting the call succeeds (the default must be the route's maximum of 2, review-6 M1), at most two items come back, and no value under a `*Path` key contains `/` (Property 19 on real data) — `edge_results_list` on the harness workflow asserting `result["results"]` has at most `limit` items and no `maskImage` survives (review-6 M2), `edge_registration_get` on the first registration asserting `"executions" not in result` and `"executions" in dropped_fields`, and `edge_textgen_models_list` when `vllm`); (3) when the device profile declares `mcp_mutating: true`, calls one M tool per area with restoration: **`edge_static_image_pin`** with a bundled small JPEG (the `TEST_IMAGE_B64` asset pattern of `stages/test_25_vlm_image_generate.py:50–73`, well under 100 KB) and `file_name="harness-pin.jpg"`, then `edge_static_image_pin_get` asserting `pinned is True` and `metadata.fileName == "harness-pin.jpg"`, then `edge_static_image_unpin` — the step first calls `edge_static_image_pin_get` and is **skipped, not failed, when an image is already pinned** (the harness has no static-pin helper today — `grep -rn static-image-camera test/on-hardware/harness/` is empty, 2026-10-19 — and the original bytes cannot be re-pinned through MCP, so an operator's pin is never replaced; review-6 M4); `edge_results_summary_reset` on the harness workflow **with restoration (review-7 N5):** the stage first reads `lastResetTime` with `edge_results_summary_get`, calls `edge_results_summary_reset(workflow_id, confirm=workflow_id)` and asserts `edge_results_summary_get` now reports a non-null `lastResetTime` within the stage's window (the review-6 M5 defect would have left it null), then — when the original value was non-null — calls `edge_results_summary_reset(workflow_id, reset_time=<original>, confirm=workflow_id)` and asserts `lastResetTime` equals the original again (when the original was null there is nothing to restore; the stage records that it left the harness workflow's summary window at the reset time); `edge_execution_log_get` on the latest execution with `tail_chars=2000`, asserting `len(result["text"]) ≤ 2000` and that `truncated`/`total_chars` are present iff the log is longer (review-7 N6); `edge_cameras_rescan` — **idempotent in result, not in effect** (review-6 N6: it tears down and rebuilds the Aravis enumeration context), so the stage snapshots `edge_cameras_list` before and asserts the camera list is unchanged after; `edge_system_snapshot_create(confirm="snapshot")` — only when the device profile also declares `mcp_snapshot: true` (the script deletes every earlier archive, which the harness cannot restore, so it runs only on devices where no operator archive is kept; revision 9) — asserts the result's `archivePath` is a bare `snapshot-….tar.gz` file name containing no `/` and `scrubbed_fields == ["archivePath"]`, and that `/health` is still healthy afterwards (the script replaces the previous archive, so nothing to restore — review-5 H1). **`edge_image_source_capture` is deliberately not called by the harness** (review-6 M3): the route returns no identifier of the file it writes under the source's `imageCapturePath`, the harness has no `/captured-images` helper (`harnesslib/client.py` covers `/image-sources` CRUD, `preview`, `test-connection`, `stream-health` and `capture-task` only — verified 2026-10-19), and adding one would mean inferring "the newest file since the call" from a path-addressed route the design excludes; the image-source area's mutating coverage on hardware is `edge_cameras_rescan`, the `image_field` path is proven on hardware by `edge_image_source_preview` in step (2), and the capture tool's shaping is covered by `test_child_protocol.py` against the fake backend — honouring the harness's restoration guarantees; (4) asserts `/health` stays healthy and the container restart count is unchanged across the stage and for the sustained window `DDA_HARNESS_MCP_SOAK_S` (default 600 s); (5) negative: a request with a corrupted token → 401, and with `Origin` → 403 at the proxy; (6) when the harness runs **on the device** (`DDA_HARNESS_ON_DEVICE=true`, SSH-driven profile), a direct `POST http://127.0.0.1:5790/mcp` with `Origin: http://127.0.0.1:5000` → 403 and with no `Authorization` on a local-login device → an `isError` `not_authenticated` tool result (D28, review-3 M3) — skipped, not failed, when the harness runs remotely.

### Security preservation and build gate

`test/backend-test/security/preservation` runs unchanged; the implementation task rebaselines the Dockerfile masked baselines (`docker_baseline_backend_Dockerfile.jp5|jp6|jp7_masked.txt`) and the `src/backend/Dockerfile` entry in `docker_baseline_out_of_scope.json`, then re-runs the suite in the flask-app container before any component build (steering). `Dockerfile.x86_64_nvidia` and the recipe variants are **not** tracked by the suite today (no baseline exists), so no rebaseline step applies to them — confirm at implementation time (N2). `requirements.txt` and `docker-compose.yaml` are untouched, so their baselines do not move.

## Phasing

| Phase | Scope | Entry criteria |
|---|---|---|
| **1 — MVP (read-only)** | Both servers: every `RO` tool marked Phase 1 above (all Requirement 1.1 / 8.1 capability areas have at least their read tools), full authentication (Lambda-side verifier; root and path-suffixed PRM documents; RFC 8414 facade; SDK 401 challenge emitted but not relied upon — D27), authorization, audit marker incl. the request audit context, `channel` filter in the audit UI, Kiro configuration examples, documentation, all Phase-1 tests, CDK stack, edge venv + supervisor + proxy + `whoami` + `/mcp/status`, on-hardware stage (read-only part). `DELIVERED_PHASES = (1,)`; mutating switches default off | **Owner sign-off** on (a) the Requirement 3.9 interpretation (Security), (b) the H4 choice (no gateway authorizer; Lambda-side validation), (c) the **Requirement 2.4 header deviation** — the challenge reaches clients as `X-Amzn-Remapped-WWW-Authenticate` and discovery relies on the well-known URIs; declining this selects the CloudFront remedy (Backlog 10) before Phase 1 ships — (d) the **Requirement 2.4 "naming the User_Pool" reading** for the RFC 8414 facade (D27), and **(e) the `PORTAL_REGISTRY_ENFORCED=false` limitation (D29; review-3 M2): with enforcement off, the labeler self-service tools and the build tools (incl. the build-fleet reads, revision 9) are PortalAdmin-only through MCP, so Requirement 3.6 holds only under enforcement — accept as a documented limitation (recommended), or require `portalRegistryEnforced=true` for deployments that enable `deployMcpServer` (option B), or authorize the `AdminGetUser`-sourced `custom:role` augmentation (option C, Backlog 12, which reopens item (a))**, **(f) the Requirement 16.4 reading "exactly `max(k, 1)` marked entries per completion"** — the MCP layer adds exactly one `mcp_tool_call` envelope when the backing handler writes no entry of its own and none when it writes `k ≥ 1`, every one of which is marked (§2 `audit.py` "Count contract", Property 6; review-4 N3) — except a D36 refusal, which adds the `denied` envelope to the handler's `k` entries (`k + 1`; revision 11, review-10 N4), and **(g) `aud`-less MCP-client tokens accepted on `client_id` + scope** — a Cognito access token issued to the MCP app client that carries the `portal.tools` scope but no `aud` (the client did not send `resource`) is accepted, because only the MCP client can be granted that scope and the REST API accepts ID tokens only, so the token is usable nowhere else; this is a re-reading of Requirement 2.4's audience clause and the MCP specification's RFC 8707 MUST (Security "Token handling" item 1; review-4 N3), and **(h) the operation-level deferrals with no delivering phase (review-7 N8; revision 9):** video pin upload (`POST /static-video-camera/pin`, Backlog 17) and inference-results export (`POST /workflows/{id}/results/export`, Backlog 19) on the edge, and on the portal the static image/video pins (Backlog 22), component create/discover (Backlog 21), component configure (Backlog 23), bucket creation (Backlog 24) and, from revision 10, the labeling-manifest read `portal_data_labeling_manifest_get` (until Backlog 18 binds the job to its Use_Case; review-9 N5), are deferred to no phase of this spec because no MCP-compatible, implemented route exists for any of them (Decision point 4, Phasing note below), whereas Requirement 17.4's wording asks for "the phase that delivers them" — the owner either accepts this as a documented 17.4 deviation (the design's recommendation: the areas keep every other tool, and the routes do not exist to wrap), or adds the Backlog items' routes to Phase 3's entry criteria so Phase 3 delivers those tools, and **(i) the portal status-sync accepted reading (Decision point 4; review-8 H1):** ten Phase-1 GET tools stay `RO` although their handlers persist a status refresh, three of which are transitions the web UI's own poll also performs (the generation-job lazy reap, the compilation poll-fault latch, the detector-conversion finalize) — accept (recommended), or reclassify any of the three as `M` and move it to Phase 2, and **(j) the Use_Case binding of the Greengrass-backed reads (revision 10, review-9 N5, extended by its class scan):** these reads are scoped by account, not by Use_Case — `portal_devices_get`, `_logs_list`, `_logs_get`, `_logs_analyze` (Phase 2), `_deployment_get`, `_deployment_for_target_get` and `_components_get` read an object by name or id in the named Use_Case's account without binding it to that Use_Case (`devices.py:367–`, `device_logs.py:84–/265–`, `device_logs_analyzer.py:336–`, `deployments.py:1143–/1385–`, `components.py:560–`), as the collections list the whole account namespace (`devices.py:220–364`, `deployments.py:922–993`) — so in a **single-account** deployment, where Use_Cases share one Greengrass namespace, these reads are scoped by the account rather than by the named Use_Case (Backlog 26 records the binding); accept as REST parity (recommended: the objects carry no portal Use_Case the D36 check could compare — only portal-created deployments carry the `dda-portal:usecase-id` tag, `deployments.py:1868`, returned in the body's `tags`, which as a `bind_usecase_path` would also hide the untagged deployments the web UI shows), or defer the id-addressed tools until Backlog 26 binds them — an **open** owner decision, which revision 11 does not resolve; the **deployed-stack smoke check** recorded (observed challenge header name; the three discovery documents with identical issuer strings; `describe-user-pool-client` showing `ExplicitAuthFlows: ['ALLOW_REFRESH_TOKEN_AUTH']` — review-3 H1); Kiro IDE **and** Kiro CLI complete the OAuth flow against the deployed stack through the facade with the literal `redirectUri` `http://localhost:7899/oauth/callback`, exercising validate-first items (ii)–(viii) (incl. (viii): the access log shows no `Origin`-rejected request from Kiro, else `mcpAllowedOrigins` is set and the observation recorded — review-5 N5); the RBAC matrix test passes with enforcement on and off, including the usability guard and the tagged D29 denials; the catalog-route-registration tests (pytest + jest) pass; the two-invocations handler test passes; the `handler_audits` AST test passes with the review-2 M2 regression cases; `test_credential_in_query_never_logged` passes; `test_auth.py` and the preservation suite pass unmodified in the container gate; on-device verification on JP5 and JP6 (JP7/amd64 where available) with soak; preservation suite green with rebaselined Dockerfiles |
| **2 — low-consequence mutating** | Portal: data/labeling/synthetic authoring, model import/convert/stage, training and compilation creation, workflow authoring/**validation** (`portal_workflows_validate` — it persists `validation_status`, review-7 H1)/generation/test runs, node designer authoring/build/simulate/register, build submit/retry, camera registry add/update/refresh, static unpins, synthetic preview approval, labeler self-service writes, and two read-only tools: the long-fan-out read `portal_devices_logs_analyze` (no Bedrock call — its CloudWatch fan-out can outlast the 20 s read timeout; revision 9) and `portal_workflows_plugin_generate_status_get`, which polls a session that only the Phase-2 `portal_workflows_plugin_generate_start` creates (revision 10, review-9 N6). Edge: captures (`edge_image_source_capture` — frame as `ImageContent`, review-6 M3), static image pin and both unpins (video pin deferred — Backlog 17, review-6 M4), camera rescan/connect, results update/reset (export deferred — Backlog 19, review-6 N3), snapshot creation (`edge_system_snapshot_create`, review-5 H1). `DELIVERED_PHASES = (1, 2)` | Phase 1 shipped; owner sign-off on the Phase-2 destructive tools (the static unpins, and revision 9's snapshot create and plugin source set); `confirm` convention and mutating switches implemented and tested (Property 3, 10, 16.5); `handler_audits`/`handler_audits_denials` flags validated by the AST test; on-device verification of one M tool per edge area |
| **3 — high-consequence mutating** | Portal: deployments create/cancel, device delete/flags, component delete, shared-component provisioning, model/workflow/dataset/test-dataset/plugin/custom-node-type deletions, workflow package, model package/publish, plugin promote/demote/review, labeling stop/delete/finalize/teams, use-case create/update/delete, role assign/remove, account role change (account create/disable/enable/delete are **not** in any phase until Backlog 2 registers their routes — review-3 M1), edge-sync, build cancel and fleet administration, `portal_models_delete` (after the handler fix). Edge: workflow create/update/delete/run/retry, registration trigger/pause/resume, camera disconnect/features, image-source add/update/delete, result delete, model start/stop, DDA restart. `DELIVERED_PHASES = (1, 2, 3)` | Phase 2 shipped and in use; owner sign-off on the destructive list (incl. the revision-9 additions: deployment create, role remove, edge-sync device sync, team member remove, workflow update/retry); `models.py` `'Admin'` fix merged; a live check of which ARN `components.delete_component` accepts (Greengrass `DeleteComponent` documents a component-version ARN while the web UI passes the `_list` item's base `arn`, `Components.tsx:224`) before `portal_devices_component_delete` ships; the account-lifecycle routes (`POST /admin/users`, `…/disable`, `…/enable`, `DELETE /admin/users/{username}`) registered in `user-admin-api-stack.ts` **before** any account-lifecycle tool is added to the catalog (Backlog 2 — the route-registration test enforces the order); on-device verification of restart/pause/resume with restoration |

Deferred capability areas are none: every area has read tools in Phase 1; the mutating side of each area is listed with its phase above (17.4). Some **operations** inside covered areas are deferred to no phase because no MCP-compatible, implemented route exists for them today: on the edge video pin upload (`POST /static-video-camera/pin` — Backlog 17; review-6 M4) and results export (`POST /workflows/{id}/results/export` — Backlog 19; review-6 N3); on the portal (revision 9) the static image/video pins (Backlog 22), component create/discover (Backlog 21), component configure (Backlog 23) and bucket creation (Backlog 24); each becomes a Mutating_Tool in a follow-up spec once its route exists in an MCP-compatible form; and (revision 10) the labeling-manifest read, which returns as `portal_data_labeling_manifest_get` once Backlog 18 binds the job to its Use_Case. Because 17.4's wording is "the phase that delivers them", this is a **documented deviation carried as Phase-1 sign-off item (h)** (review-7 N8): the owner accepts it, or moves the Backlog items' routes into Phase 3's entry criteria. Documentation states the phase per tool (the catalog tables are the source).

## Documentation updates

| Document | Addition |
|---|---|
| `edge-cv-portal/ADMIN_GUIDE.md` | New section "MCP server (AI assistants)": enabling the stack (deployed by default by `deploy-infrastructure.sh`) and **disabling it in two steps — `npx cdk destroy EdgeCVPortalMcpStack --force` from `infrastructure/` (with the stack still in the app), then `DEPLOY_MCP_SERVER=false ./deploy-infrastructure.sh` (only the literal `false`, case-insensitively, is "off"); why the order matters (`ComputeStack` cannot drop exports the deployed MCP stack imports) and what the deploy script's guard message means (D33; review-4 M3, review-5 N4)**, the stack outputs, registering additional loopback redirect URIs (`mcpRedirectUris`), the mutating-tools switch (`mcpMutatingToolsEnabled`), the `mcpAllowedOrigins` fallback (empty by default; set only if the smoke check shows the client sending an `Origin` header — validate-first (viii), review-5 N5), wiring the `McpTimeoutAlarm` (a tool call the Lambda could not answer; review-5 M3) to the portal's notification topic, the registry-enforcement section — MCP resolves roles from the Portal_Identity_Registry; **with `PORTAL_REGISTRY_ENFORCED=false` every `global`-scoped tool resolves non-PortalAdmins to Viewer, so the labeler self-service tools (`portal_data_labeler_*`), and the build tools (`portal_workflows_build*`, incl. the build-fleet reads `portal_workflows_build_servers_list`/`_build_config_get`, revision 9) are PortalAdmin-only until enforcement is on and the account has a `global` registry row naming its role (the User Manager writes it; older accounts need the backfill); a PortalAdmin without a `global` row is Viewer through MCP** (D29; review-3 M2) — when the owner accepts sign-off item (j), the note that in a single-account deployment the Greengrass-backed device, log, deployment and component reads are scoped by account, as in the web UI (revision 10) — the note that the MCP app client allows only the hosted-UI code grant and the refresh grant (`ExplicitAuthFlows: ALLOW_REFRESH_TOKEN_AUTH`; no username/password sign-in against it), the note that MCP routes carry no gateway authorizer and are authenticated in the Lambda, Kiro IDE/CLI `mcp.json` example (incl. the `oauthScopes: []` fallback and the rule that `redirectUri` must be the literal registered `http://localhost:7899/oauth/callback` — Cognito registers plain-HTTP callbacks for `localhost` only, so Kiro's `127.0.0.1` example does not apply; use `mcpRedirectUris` for another port — review-3 N9), the discovery layout (root and path-suffixed PRM URLs, the RFC 8414 facade URL, why the facade exists, the observed challenge header name from the smoke check and why clients fall back to the well-known URIs), reading MCP audit entries (`channel` filter, `mcp_tool_call`, `authentication_failed`), supported protocol revisions, tested Kiro versions, `mcp` SDK version, dates (15.5); Troubleshooting: "OAuth issuer mismatch" (fetch the root PRM and the facade; compare `authorization_servers[0]` and `issuer` byte for byte), "`invalid_scope` from Cognito", "client reports no `WWW-Authenticate` header" (expected behind API Gateway; the client must use well-known discovery), and the documented alternative of advertising the Cognito issuer directly (not supported by Kiro's current documentation) |
| `README.md` | Pointer paragraph to both MCP documents |
| `docs/edge-mcp-server.md` (new) | Enabling per device (`McpServerEnabled`, `McpMutatingToolsEnabled` via deployment configuration merge), LAN (TLS, device certificate trust) vs tunnel connection (`docs/connect-to-device.md` cross-reference, port-forward to 5000/5443) **with the known limitation stated up front: "On a device with local login enabled but Existing_Token_Auth not configured, the Backend_API listens on plaintext 5000 only, so MCP is tunnel-only there; LAN access requires the TLS listener (authorization settings present)"** (review-4 N4), logging in with a synced account — `eval "$(scripts/dda-edge-mcp-login.sh https://<device>:5443)"`, the `read -s` password prompt, and the warning that pasting the `export` line by hand records the token in `~/.bash_history` (`HISTCONTROL=ignorespace` / `history -d`; review-4 N5) — Kiro `mcp.json` example with `${DDA_EDGE_TOKEN}` and the approved-env-var step, role gating table, residual risk of the open-access row, the **trust-boundary statement** ("the MCP child is bound to loopback, which under `network_mode: host` is reachable by any local process; it never acts on its own authority — every call carries the caller's credential to the Backend_API, where the existing authorizer decides — so a direct caller gains nothing it could not get from the Backend_API itself, but its `/mcp` request gets no proxy `api.access` record"; D28, review-3 M3), `/mcp/status`, tool list with phases, the note that the five image tools produce no `api.access` record (correlate via `mcp.access`) |
| `docs/connect-to-device.md` | Short "Forward the LocalServer port for MCP" subsection |
| LocalServer component configuration reference | Since no `.md` documents `LocalLoginEnabled` today, `docs/edge-mcp-server.md` carries a "Component configuration keys" table covering `LocalLoginEnabled`, `McpServerEnabled`, `McpMutatingToolsEnabled` (with recipe defaults) and the optional `McpAllowedOrigins` (CSV, absent ⇒ empty; validate-first (viii)), and the recipe `DefaultConfiguration` comment names the keys; the tool table states per tool its phase, classification text, `image_field`/`drop_fields` behaviour, its `limit` bound where narrower than 50/200 (`edge_workflow_images_list`: two per call — review-6 M1), that path-like fields come back as file names (`scrubbed_fields`), and that `edge_image_source_capture` returns the frame but no file identifier, `edge_static_image_pin` accepts ≈ 700 KiB at most (larger through the HMI), `edge_execution_log_get` returns the newest part of a Run_Log — at most `tail_chars` characters (default 150 000), fewer when their JSON encoding would exceed the result bound (`truncated`/`total_chars` tell when it cut — review-7 N6, review-8 N4), `edge_image_source_add/_update` take the stream address as `stream_url` (RTSP/RTMP only; folder sources are HMI-only — review-7 M1), video pin upload / results export are HMI-only (Backlog 17/19), and — beside the `mcp.json` example — that **text generation may take up to 130 s**: Kiro documents no per-server tool-call timeout (re-fetched 2026-10-19) and waits; a client with its own per-call timeout must raise it or bound the generation with `max_tokens` (review-7 N7) |
| `.kiro/settings/mcp.json` | Two `disabled: true` entries (`dda-portal`, `dda-edge-example`) with placeholder values |

## Design notes for the implementation phase (steering)

- **Security preservation baselines (C6, 13.1).** The change touches `src/backend/Dockerfile`, `Dockerfile.jp5`, `Dockerfile.jp6`, `Dockerfile.jp7`, `Dockerfile.x86_64_nvidia` (two additions each: the lock-file `COPY` + venv `RUN` at the per-file anchors of §6 — after `Dockerfile:44` / `Dockerfile.x86_64_nvidia:60` (`RUN ./edge_ml1_p_camera_management/prereqs_install.sh`) and after `Dockerfile.jp5:114` / `Dockerfile.jp6:163` / `Dockerfile.jp7:245` (`RUN pip install --no-cache-dir -r ./requirements.txt`) — and `COPY mcp_edge ./mcp_edge` after the `COPY user_accounts_sync` line) and all seven recipe variants. The edge change also modifies `test/backend-test/api-endpoints/test_authorize_request.py` (return-value assertions, review-2 H2) in the same commit as `src/backend/utils/auth.py`, and leaves `test/backend-test/utils/test_auth.py` untouched because the container gate's preservation suite pins its source. In the **same change**: recompute `test/backend-test/security/baselines/docker_baseline_backend_Dockerfile.jp5_masked.txt`, `…jp6_masked.txt`, `…jp7_masked.txt` and the `src/backend/Dockerfile` entry in `docker_baseline_out_of_scope.json`; `Dockerfile.x86_64_nvidia` and the recipes have no baseline today (confirm at implementation time; add none unless the suite has grown one by then); run the preservation suite in the flask-app container before the first component build; move `edge-cv-portal/infrastructure/cdk.out` aside (the MCP stack adds a template) or add its hashes to `cdk_out_baseline.json`/`secrets_cdk_out_baseline.json`. `src/backend/requirements.txt`, `src/docker-compose.yaml`, `station_install/setup_station.sh`, `layers/jwt/requirements.txt`, and `functions/requirements.txt` are deliberately untouched so their baselines stay valid. This run writes no code; the note binds the future tasks.
- **On-device verification before commit (C6, 13.3).** The edge change runs on the device (new routes, child process, venv, Dockerfiles, recipes, the shared IPC reader refactor). Before committing: build the affected variant one at a time (`run_jp_builds.sh`), deploy or hot-patch to a real JP5 **and** JP6 device (JP7/amd64 where available), exercise login → `tools/list` → one RO (incl. one image tool) and (Phase ≥ 2) one M tool per capability area through the documented transport, flip `McpServerEnabled`/`LocalLoginEnabled` at runtime to exercise the two pollers over the one IPC connection, and confirm `/health`, no crash loop, no container restart, no awscrt abort for a sustained period (the harness stage automates most of this). State the devices in the commit.
- **Build discipline.** One component variant at a time; no `deploy-portal.sh`/`deploy-infrastructure.sh` while a build runs (cdk.out drift guard).
- **Portal deploy order and cross-stack hygiene (review-4 H2/M3).** `EdgeCVPortalMcpStack` depends on `ComputeStack` (handler ARNs incl. the nested `ApiGatewayStack`'s component-configuration handler — **not** its layer), `AuthStack` (pool), `NodeDesignerStack`, `BuildFleetStack`, `SyntheticDataStack` (handlers); `cdk deploy --all` orders it last. The MCP stack carries its **own copy of the shared layer** (`McpSharedLayer`, D31) built from the same `backend/layers/shared` asset, so a shared-layer change such as D25 lands in `ComputeStack`, `NodeDesignerStack`, `BuildFleetStack`, `SyntheticDataStack` **and** the MCP stack on the same `cdk deploy --all` — no stack imports another's `LayerVersion`, so no `ComputeStack` update is ever blocked by an export in use. Every value the MCP stack imports is an ARN that is stable across updates (functions, tables, pool). The shared-layer change (D25) redeploys every function that uses a shared layer; it is additive and covered by the existing `test_portal_registry_units.py`/`test_property_audit_attribution.py` plus the new context tests. **Removing the MCP stack** is the two-step procedure of D33 (destroy first, then deploy with the flag off); the implementation task adds `scripts/portal-mcp-stack.sh` with the guard, sources it from `deploy-infrastructure.sh` directly before the `cdk deploy` line (after the trusted-accounts block), and adds the bats file. `deploy-portal.sh` needs no change: a `DEPLOY_MCP_SERVER` value set in the operator's environment is inherited by the `./deploy-infrastructure.sh` child it runs (`deploy-portal.sh:1296`), exactly as `PORTAL_REGISTRY_ENFORCED` is today (neither is in its `_export_passthrough_env` list, `deploy-portal.sh:152–160`, because inherited environment needs no re-export); the guard lives only in `deploy-infrastructure.sh`.

## Backlog (not in this spec; recorded for the owner)

1. Fix `models.py:522` `check_user_access(user_id, usecase_id, 'Admin')` (invalid role string denies all deletions) by checking `'DataScientist'` — the `DELETE_MODELS` holders (`shared_utils.py:894/934`), so `delete_models (U)` is exact — prerequisite for adding `portal_models_delete` to `CATALOG` (revision 9). Its "deployed" guard (526–530) reads `deployed_devices` from the TrainingJobs item, which nothing writes there (`greengrass_publish.py:1237` writes it on the Models-table item), so the fix should move the guard too.
2. Register the account-lifecycle routes `POST /admin/users`, `POST /admin/users/{username}/disable`, `POST /admin/users/{username}/enable` and `DELETE /admin/users/{username}` in `user-admin-api-stack.ts` (the handler branches exist in `user_admin.py` and the frontend already calls the create/disable/enable routes against the unregistered paths — a pre-existing portal defect) **before** any account-lifecycle tool (`portal_admin_account_create/_disable/_enable/_delete`) is added to the catalog; the catalog-route-registration test enforces the order (review-3 M1).
3. Per-principal MCP rate limiter (reuse `rate_limiter.evaluate` keyed by `sub`) if stage throttling proves insufficient.
4. A stdio bridge package for MCP clients without OAuth support.
5. Inventory `WorkflowTuningApiStack` routes for a `portal_workflows_tuning_*` family.
6. Portal-wide alignment of the edge dependency pins (the JP6 image already runs newer FastAPI/pydantic than `requirements.txt` states) — independent of MCP.
7. A header-accepting (`get_api_router()`) variant of the five `download_file.py` image routes, so the edge image tools can drop `credential_in_query` and gain `api.access` correlation.
8. Id-addressed captured-image listing/deletion routes (replacing the filesystem-path `GET|DELETE /captured-images` contract) so captured-image tools can be added.
9. Clean up `api-core-stack.ts`, `api-model-stack.ts`, `api-data-stack.ts` (instantiated by no stack in `bin/app.ts`): `api-core-stack.ts` and `api-data-stack.ts` are referenced by no `.ts` source and can be deleted with their compiled outputs; `api-model-stack.ts` is imported and synthesized by `infrastructure/test/detector-export-infra.test.ts:28/103/330` as a "duplicate route table", so deleting it requires retiring that test's duplicate-table assertions first (review-2 N3). Unrelated clean-up noticed during this design.
10. CloudFront distribution in front of the MCP HTTP API with a response-headers function that restores `WWW-Authenticate` from `X-Amzn-Remapped-WWW-Authenticate` (the field-report workaround) — becomes a Phase-1 prerequisite only if the owner declines the Requirement 2.4 header deviation (D27); otherwise optional hardening that also enables a custom domain (observed on the deployed HTTP API 2026-10-25: not remapped; item 10 is therefore unnecessary).
11. Bind the edge child to a Unix domain socket inside the container (`uvicorn --uds`; the backend's `httpx` 0.24.1 supports `HTTPTransport(uds=...)`) instead of `127.0.0.1:5790`, which would confine the child to the container's filesystem namespace under `network_mode: host` and make the proxy the only path; not adopted for the MVP because the readiness probe, the harness and the legacy-client test assume TCP and because the child already never acts on its own authority (D28; review-3 M3).
12. D29 option (C): read the principal's Cognito `custom:role` attribute through the `AdminGetUser` call `mcp_portal/principal.py` already makes for the email, and place it in the forwarded `requestContext.authorizer.claims` and in `user_info['role']`, so MCP reproduces the web UI's enforcement-off resolution (`_legacy_role` steps 1 and 4) exactly — only if the owner selects it at Phase 1 sign-off item (e); it changes the Requirement 3.9 "claims verbatim" interpretation (sign-off item (a)), Property 5 and `test_mcp_rbac_matrix.py`'s enforcement-off expectations (review-3 M2).
13. Authorize the internal shared-component provisioning call explicitly. `usecases.py:provision_shared_components_via_api` (175–300, called from `create_usecase` at 1761) SigV4-signs its POST to `/shared-components/provision` (`api-gateway-stack.ts:748–749`) with the caller's `user_id` in the body, which `shared_components.py:provision_components` uses for that internal call (784–790). Fix outside this spec: give the method `AuthorizationType.IAM` with `execute-api:Invoke` granted to the `UseCasesHandler` role, **or** replace the HTTP hop with a direct `lambda:InvokeFunction` from `usecases.py` and register the method behind the Cognito authorizer; in both cases drop the body-`user_id` branch or bind it to `requestContext.identity.userArn`. Found while verifying the catalog rows (review-4 N2). The MCP tool is unaffected: its schema has no `user_id` property (`additionalProperties: false`), dispatch is a Lambda invoke carrying verified claims, and the pre-dispatch `update_usecases` check runs regardless.
14. An id-addressed, header-authenticated snapshot route pair (`POST /snapshots` → `{snapshotId}`, `GET /snapshots/{snapshotId}` → the archive as a `FileResponse`) replacing the GET-that-creates `/snapshot` + file-name-addressed `/snapshotfile/{fileName}` contract, so a future `edge_system_snapshot_get` can return the archive as resource content within the 2 MiB bound or a size-exceeded error (Requirement 8.5) instead of only creating it (review-5 H1). Until then snapshot creation is the Phase-2 Mutating_Tool `edge_system_snapshot_create` and download stays HMI-only.
15. `list_images` items carry no `captureId` (`inference_results_utils.py:225` calls `read_image_from_jsonl` without one) although `save_image_object` supports it (160–161); adding the id to the items would let the MCP tool stop relying on the scrubbed `inferenceFilePath` file name as the capture id (D34). Independent of MCP.
16. `POST /image-sources/{id}/capture` returns the captured frame only (`CaptureImageResponse(image: str)`, `image_source.py:250–251`): `execute_image_source_pipeline`'s `captureLocation` is filtered out by the response model and no file name or id reaches the caller, so `edge_image_source_capture` can confirm the capture (`{"captured": true}` + `ImageContent`) but cannot name the file it wrote (review-6 M3). Returning the file's **name** (not its path) in the response — and, with Backlog 8's id-addressed captured-image routes, an id — would let the tool return a `resource_id`.
17. An upload-token or id-addressed **video pin** route (e.g. `POST /static-video-camera/pin` accepting `{"capturedImageId": …}` or a chunked/tokenised upload) so `edge_static_video_pin` can exist: today the route accepts only multipart up to 100 MiB (`utils/video_loop.py:66`) — far beyond the MCP proxy's 1 MiB request cap — or a device-path JSON body, which §7 step 5 forbids (review-6 M4). Until then video pin upload is HMI-only; `edge_static_video_pin_get`/`edge_static_video_unpin` are in the catalog.
18. Add the Use_Case membership check to `captures.py:list_captures` and to the three `datasets.py` routes (`list_datasets`, `count_images`, `get_image_preview`): `check_user_access(user['user_id'], usecase_id)` after `get_user_from_event(event)` — or `@rbac_check([VIEW_DATASETS], 'usecase_id')` — at the top of each route function (verified 2026-10-19). For MCP the pre-dispatch `view_datasets (U)` check already gates `portal_data_captures_list` and the three dataset tools, so the fix changes nothing for them (review-6 N5). **The same fix for `labeling.py` (review-8 N3; revision 9):** the routes `handler` (56–107) dispatches to — `list_labeling_jobs` (108), `get_labeling_job` (672), `get_manifest` (1125), `list_workteams` (1527) — and the `GroundTruth` branch of `create_labeling_job` (274–670), as the `DDA` delegation already does (`dda_labeling.py:1653–1655`); `get_labeling_job`/`get_manifest` also check that the job belongs to the authorized Use_Case (they address it by id, 675/1128). Until then MCP binds `portal_data_labeling_job_get` itself (D36) and keeps `portal_data_labeling_manifest_get`, whose body carries no Use_Case, out of `CATALOG` (revision 10, review-9 N5).
19. An **id-addressed inference-results export** (e.g. `POST /workflows/{id}/results/export` → `{exportId}` and a header-authenticated `GET /workflows/{id}/results/exports/{exportId}` returning the zip as a `FileResponse`) replacing the write-a-path-then-fetch-by-full-path contract of `inference_result.py:164–188` + `download_file.py:311–345`, so an `edge_results_export` tool can hand back something a client can use within the 2 MiB bound (or a size-exceeded error). Until then the POST is wrapped by no tool — its only output is a device path consumed solely by the excluded query-token route (review-6 N3) — and export stays HMI-only.
20. Derive the "auto-triggered" case of `compilation.py:start_compilation_job` (line 436, intended for the portal's own event-driven compilations) from the invocation source (`requestContext` absent / a SigV4 or Lambda-invoke identity) rather than from the request body, or drop the branch and grant the triggering role the permission; found by the review-7 M3 argument scan. The MCP tool is unaffected: `portal_models_compilation_create`'s schema has no `auto_triggered` property (`additionalProperties: false`), and the pre-dispatch `create_training_jobs` check runs regardless.
21. `POST /components` and `POST /components/discover` are registered (`api-gateway-stack.ts:683`, 705) but `components.lambda_handler` has no `POST` branch (216–228 — every POST answers 404) and the frontend never calls them: implement or deregister. `portal_devices_component_create`/`_components_discover` return once a handler branch exists; the handler-dispatch half of the route test keeps them out until then (review-8 M2).
22. An MCP-compatible input for the portal **static image/video pins**: `pin_static_image`/`pin_static_video` accept only a `stagingKey` issued by `POST …/static-image/upload-url` (`camera_registry.py:2035–2063`, a presigned S3 PUT the devices view excludes) plus `fileName` (2087–2096, 2454–2463), so no MCP-only call can succeed. An inline bounded image body or a pin by reference to an existing Portal object would let `portal_devices_static_image_pin`/`_video_pin` return (`staging_key → stagingKey`, `file_name → fileName` if the owner instead lifts the upload exclusion).
23. `component_configuration.configure_component` (261–348) validates the configuration and writes a `PENDING` Deployments-table item (299–322) but submits no Greengrass deployment, and nothing in the backend reads the record (no reader of `environment_variables`, no stream consumer), so the web UI's "configure" changes no device: implement the deployment or retire the route. The tool returns then as `portal_devices_component_configure` — `update_usecases (U)` (the handler checks `'UseCaseAdmin'`, 279), `M`, Phase 3, `usecase_id` (→ body), `component_name`, `configuration` (object), `target_devices[]` (all required, `validate_required_fields`, 267–270), `deployment_name?`, with a `status_tool` that can observe the deployment (review-8 M1/M2).
24. `POST /usecases/{id}/data/buckets` dispatches to `data_management.create_bucket` (322–341), a disabled stub that answers 400 on every call (bucket attachment goes through `PUT /usecases/{id}`): deregister the method or implement it; `portal_data_bucket_attach` returns only for an implemented route.
25. Audit-log Use_Case filtering is dead: `audit_logs.get_audit_logs` filters on a top-level `usecase_id` (123–137) that no audit entry carries (`log_audit_event`'s item, `shared_utils.py:355–368`, although `storage-stack.ts:161–171` defines a `usecase-actions-index` on it), so non-PortalAdmins always see zero entries. Fix outside this spec: write `usecase_id` as a top-level attribute, check `VIEW_AUDIT_LOGS` on it (80–111), and compare a non-admin's `user_id` filter with the stored `sub` (110–111). Then a `view_audit_logs (U)` form of `portal_admin_audit_logs_query` can return for UseCaseAdmins (Requirement 3.7); until then the tool is `system_admin (G)`.
26. REST-side follow-ups from the revision-9 sweep, each already covered on the MCP path by the pre-dispatch check (fix outside this spec): check the deployment-management permission in `deployments.create_deployment` (1506) and `cancel_deployment` (1974), and bind get/cancel/component-delete — like the device and device-log reads — to the authorized Use_Case (sign-off item (j)); check `create_usecases` in `usecases.create_usecase` (1427–1430) and give `POST /usecases/verify-role` its own branch (1301–1302); route account-level registry-row changes from `POST /users/assign-role` and `DELETE /users/{u}/roles/global` through the User Manager's status handling and last-PortalAdmin guard (the MCP schemas exclude `'global'`); authorize each Use_Case named in `usecase_ids` in `custom_node_types.register_node_type`/`update_node_type` (632–646); require `NODE_DESIGNER_READ` per Use_Case in `plugin_records.list_plugins` when no `usecase_id` is given (769–770); extract archives in `model_import.validate_model_artifact` with a member filter (1060–1061); write audit entries for role grants and edge-sync staging in the web path.
27. Make the record's Use_Case the `@rbac_check` scope unconditionally in `labeling._inject_job_usecase_scope` (991) and `dda_labeling._inject_job_usecase_scope` (524), `_inject_team_usecase_scope` (504) and `_inject_preview_run_usecase_scope` (5764) (`rbac_middleware.extract_usecase_id_from_event`, 45–46). Fix outside this spec: overwrite rather than `setdefault`, as `_inject_preview_usecase_scope` already does (5042). The MCP tools are unaffected while those specs keep `usecase_id` RBAC-only (no `param_map` entry — asserted by `test_mcp_catalog_arguments.py`).
28. Restrict the settings override of `POST /image-sources/{id}/preview` (revision 11, review-10 M1): drop `processingPipeline` from its free-dict `imageSourceConfiguration` (`endpoints/image_source.py:94`; `model/image_source_configuration.py:73`) or validate it against an element allowlist. `edge_image_source_preview` therefore takes no override. An MCP tuning preview, if wanted, is a separate Phase-2 `M` tool with the closed schema `{gain?: integer ≥ 0, exposure?: integer ≥ 1, imageCrop?: {top, bottom, left, right: integer ≥ 0}}` (`additionalProperties: false` at both levels), whose CSI write (`csi_capture.py:55–80`) is then a declared effect rather than an accepted `RO` reading — not added in this spec.
29. `portal_data_datasets_list` with its default `max_depth` 3 (`datasets.list_datasets`, `params.get('max_depth', '3')`, walked by `discover_datasets`) can exceed the Lambda tool timeout on a large data bucket: the first call of the task 18.2 owner check (2026-10-25) answered `timeout: the backing operation timed out` and the retry with `max_depth 1, limit 20` answered. Lower the default or page the walk (a continuation token per listed prefix); until then ADMIN_GUIDE tells callers to pass `max_depth` 1 and a `limit` on large buckets. Fix outside this spec; the tool's schema is unchanged.
30. The audit-log `channel` and `mcp_tool` filters of `audit_logs.get_audit_logs` run as a filtered table scan (`table.scan` with a `FilterExpression`, 200 raw rows per page, only the page sorted newest-first), so with a table dominated by `get_deployment` polling the MCP channel filter shows 0–1 MCP rows per page in arbitrary time order, and a tool call that is not on the current page cannot be told from one that was not logged (task 18.2 owner check, 2026-10-25). Add a `channel` or `user_id` + `timestamp` index so the MCP channel filter is a Query and the Audit Logs UI shows MCP entries in time order. Fix outside this spec.

## Response to the design review (revision 2)

Each finding of the first design review (the design reviews are kept outside the repository; verdict `CHANGES_REQUESTED`, 2026-10-18) and its disposition:

| Finding | Disposition | Where |
|---|---|---|
| **H1** Dockerfiles have no `COPY . .` | **Addressed.** `COPY mcp_edge ./mcp_edge` added after `COPY user_accounts_sync` in all five Dockerfiles (with line references), in addition to the lock-file COPY + venv RUN; rebaseline note updated | Key research findings (Edge Dockerfiles); Decision point 3 "Chosen"; §6 Dockerfile row; Design notes |
