Additional corrections made while addressing the findings (not raised by the review): the Cognito resource server and app client are created with `new cognito.UserPoolResourceServer(this, …)` / `new cognito.UserPoolClient(this, …)` in the MCP stack's scope rather than `userPool.addResourceServer()`, which would have placed the resource server in `AuthStack` and created a cross-stack cycle; `ATTRIBUTION_FIELDS` is left at five fields and marker fields are added only for MCP events, because `test_portal_registry_units.py` asserts exact five-key equality for ordinary events; the HTTP API stage is created explicitly (`createDefaultStage: false`) because the L2 default stage exposes no throttle option; `GET /workflows/{workflow_id}/results/export` (zip download on the query-token router) is listed explicitly as excluded.

## Response to the design review (revision 3)

Each finding of the second design review (verdict `CHANGES_REQUESTED`, 2026-10-18 20:41) and its disposition. Every factual claim below was re-verified against the filesystem, the installed wheels, and the cited web sources on 2026-10-18 before the text was changed.

| Finding | Disposition | Where |
|---|---|---|
| **H1** API Gateway remaps `WWW-Authenticate`; the ASGI test cannot detect it; the spec's rule is one-of | **Addressed — well-known-URI discovery is now the design (D27).** (1) Deployed-stack smoke check added to the Testing Strategy and Phase-1 entry criteria (record the header name the client receives and the three discovery documents). (2) Root `GET /.well-known/oauth-protected-resource` added beside the six path-suffixed PRMs, in the Starlette app (`discovery.py`), the HTTP API route set and the CDK assertion (ten route keys). (3) **Option (b) chosen:** the header is still emitted by the SDK but not relied upon; Requirement 2.4's header clause is recorded as a documented deviation with owner sign-off; the in-gateway `ResponseParameters` remedy is rejected (`x-amzn-*` reserved; remap ordering undocumented) and CloudFront is the designed remedy if the owner declines (Backlog 10). (4) Research-baseline sentence rewritten to the spec's one-of rule with the client fallback order; `test_mcp_http_transport.py` relabelled as proving SDK behaviour upstream of the gateway; the Overview, D2, Decision point 2, Error Handling, Security item 2 and the diagrams updated. Verified: AWS "important notes" page lists `WWW-Authenticate` as Remapped; HTTP API parameter-mapping page lists `x-amzn-*` as reserved; spec page text "MUST implement one of the following"; Kiro troubleshooting fetches the root PRM | Overview; Research baseline (MCP authorization; API Gateway header handling; Kiro); D2, D27; Decision point 2 "Discovery" + "Why no gateway authorizer"; §1 Routes/Outputs/environment; §2 `discovery.py`; Data Models PRM; Security item 2; Error Handling; Testing Strategy (`test_mcp_http_transport.py`, smoke check, CDK); Phasing; Documentation; Backlog 10 |
| **H2** Existing tests pin `validate_token -> None` and `authorize_credential(valid) -> None`; `test_auth.py` is a build gate | **Addressed, with a different fix than suggested.** Verified `test_auth.py:60–62` (`assertIsNone(result)`), `test_authorize_request.py:420/424/430`, and `build-custom.sh:247–252`. Also found that `test/backend-test/security/preservation/test_preservation_secrets_auth_suite.py` (in the same gate, `build-custom.sh:281–283`) pins `test_auth.py`'s **source text** — including the literal `self.assertIsNone(result)` — so the review's suggested edit of `test_auth.py:61` would fail that suite. The design therefore keeps `validate_token`'s `None` contract (its body moves into a new private `_introspect_token -> dict` that `authorize_credential` calls) so `test_auth.py` is **not edited**; `test_authorize_request.py:424/430` are modified in the same commit as `utils/auth.py` (420 unchanged); `test_authorize_request.py` is cited as the decision-matrix regression suite; the incorrect sentences were corrected; grep confirmed no other test asserts on these return values | Key research findings (Tests); §6 `utils/auth.py` row; Testing Strategy "Matrix preservation"; Security F8; Design notes; revision-2 response table (M3 row annotated) |
| **M1** PRM → Cognito issuer → OIDC discovery contradicted by Kiro docs and the PKCE rule; facade should be primary | **Addressed.** The RFC 8414 facade is the design (D27): `GET /.well-known/oauth-authorization-server` served by the MCP Lambda with `issuer = MCP_AS_ISSUER` (the HTTP API origin, no trailing slash), hosted-UI endpoints, Cognito `jwks_uri`, `code_challenge_methods_supported: ["S256"]`, `token_endpoint_auth_methods_supported: ["none"]`, etc.; every PRM's `authorization_servers = [MCP_AS_ISSUER]`; token validation unchanged (`iss` stays Cognito's). Added: the `AnyHttpUrl` trailing-slash trap (verified with pydantic 2.14.1) as the reason `discovery.py` renders strings itself; `userPoolDomain` prop and `COGNITO_HOSTED_UI_ORIGIN`; the Requirement 2.4 "naming the User_Pool" reading as a sign-off item; direct Cognito discovery kept only as a documented alternative. Verified: Kiro page text ("must serve authorization-server metadata at the RFC 8414 well-known URL"); live pool: both RFC 8414 locations 400, no `code_challenge_methods_supported`; spec security-considerations text ("MUST refuse to proceed") | D27; Decision point 2 "Token acquisition" + "Why a facade"; §1 props/routes/environment; §2 `discovery.py`; Data Models facade; Security item 2; Testing (`test_mcp_discovery.py`); Phasing; Documentation |
| **M2** `handler_audits` AST test counts denial-only calls | **Addressed.** `handler_audits` redefined: ≥ 1 call whose `result` is literal `'success'`/`'pending'`, absent, or non-literal; `'denied'`/`'failure'`/`'failed'`/`'rejected'` ignored (literal distribution surveyed: success 121, denied 31, failure 32, rejected 7, failed 1, pending 1, absent 7, non-literal 11). `handler_audits_denials` broadened to routes whose body or directly-called helper writes a `'denied'` entry (`camera_registry.authorize`, `workflows`/`workflow_packaging.forbidden_response`, `deployments.get_camera_binding_context`). AST test evaluates the 5th positional / `result=` argument; fixed regression cases added (`camera_registry.get_cameras`/`get_conflicts` → False/True, `deployments.list_deployments` → False/False, `devices.list_devices` → True, `get_camera_binding_context` → False/True, `_fail_conversion` → False). | **N3** `ToolSpec.scope` cannot express "U when `usecase_id` given, else G" | Addressed: `scope = 'usecase_or_global'` added with the `rbac_check(allow_global=True)` rule; `authz.py` resolves it; the two rows marked `U\|G`; Property 2 requires `usecase_id` optional (not required) for it | §2 `catalog.py`, `authz.py`; Tool catalogs intro; admin/data rows; Property 2 |
| **N4** "exactly one entry on success" false for `k = 2` | Addressed: `max(k, 1)` contract in `audit.py`, C5, Property 6 and a new Invariants row, with the Requirement 16.4 reading stated | §2 `audit.py`; Security C5; Property 6; Invariants |
| **N5** `channel=api` filter hides pre-change entries | Addressed: `(attribute_not_exists(#channel) OR #channel = :channel)` for `api`, `#channel = :channel` for `mcp`, `#mcp_tool = :mcp_tool`; appended to the existing `filter_expressions` list (`audit_logs.py:122–170`) | §3 `get_audit_logs` |
| **N6** 7.3 "require the header" vs absent-header legacy path | Addressed: D17 states the absent-header → 2025-03-26 rule as the specification's backward-compatibility behaviour (`DEFAULT_NEGOTIATED_VERSION`, verified) and as the declared 7.2 legacy path; Input validation row reworded; Research baseline records the SDK's era routing | Research baseline (SDK API surface); D17; Input validation |
| **N7** `set_principal` mechanism unspecified | Addressed: test-only `tests/mcp/_principal.py:set_principal` sets `auth_context_var` to `AuthenticatedUser(AccessToken(...))` before the in-memory `Client` is entered and resets on exit; lives under `tests/`, not in the shipped package; `on_call_tool` has no other principal source | Research baseline (SDK API surface); Testing Strategy (`test_mcp_protocol.py`) |
| **N8** 504 `mcp_timeout` unreachable after the status line | Addressed: non-`text/event-stream` upstream bodies (the only kind the child emits) are **buffered** before the response is constructed, so a read timeout anywhere maps to 504; a 4 MiB upstream cap → 502 `mcp_bad_upstream`; the streaming branch is kept only for a `text/event-stream` upstream and its truncation limitation is stated; `test_proxy_route.py` covers both | Decision point 3 "Transport"; §5 `proxy.py`; Error Handling (edge); Testing Strategy |
| **N9** Kiro `redirectUri` literal match not in the validate-first list | Addressed: validate-first item (v) added; **and** the Cognito rule that plain-HTTP callbacks are accepted for `localhost` only (cited) means Kiro's `127.0.0.1` example cannot be registered — the default stays `http://localhost:7899/oauth/callback`, `mcp.json` must carry it literally, `mcpRedirectUris` is the knob for another port, and the CDK validator now rejects any plain-HTTP host other than `localhost` (Input validation row corrected from `localhost\|127.0.0.1`); documented in the ADMIN_GUIDE row and the Client-configuration text | Research baseline (Cognito); Decision point 2 "Cognito mapping" + validate-first (v); §1 `McpUserPoolClient`; Client configuration; Input validation; Testing Strategy (CDK `CallbackURLs` assertion); Documentation |

Additional corrections made while addressing the findings (not raised by the review): the child now also enforces its own 1 MiB request-body cap (`max_request_body_size`), since a direct loopback call does not pass through the proxy's cap; the matrix test's usability guard exempts PortalAdmin-only permissions by construction, because the review's wording ("empty for every non-PortalAdmin role in both modes") would have failed the intentionally admin-only `manage_users`/`system_admin`/`create_usecases` tools; the edge diagram gained the direct-loopback-caller edge; the Phase-1 entry criteria now include the catalog-route tests and the `describe-user-pool-client` evidence.

## Response to the design review (revision 6)

Each finding of the fifth design review (verdict `CHANGES_REQUESTED`, 2026-10-19; 1 HIGH, 4 MEDIUM, 8 NIT) and its disposition. Every factual claim below was re-verified on 2026-10-19 against the filesystem on branch `remediation` (HEAD `a1615269`, the commit the review checked, and again at `c3bf88c1` after the merge that landed mid-revision — Key research findings note): `src/backend/endpoints/system.py`, `snapshot/Snapshotter.py`, `snapshot/snapshot.sh`, `utils/constants.py`, `endpoints/workflow.py`, `utils/inference_results_utils.py`, `utils/utils.py`, `endpoints/image_source.py`, `gstreamer/gst_pipeline_executor.py`, `gstreamer/pipeline_builder.py`, `workflow_engine/api.py`, `workflow_engine/run_artifacts.py`, `endpoints/inference_result.py`, `endpoints/download_file.py`, `endpoints/camera.py`, `endpoints/feature_config.py`, `edge-cv-portal/infrastructure/lib/compute-stack.ts`, `synthetic-data-stack.ts`, `api-gateway-stack.ts`, `context-helpers.ts`, `edge-cv-portal/deploy-infrastructure.sh`, `scripts/portal-registry-enforcement.sh`, the 2026-09-29 nested `ApiGateway` template (345 resources), the installed `mangum` 0.22.0 (`scope["aws.context"]` set in `handlers/api_gateway.py`, `alb.py`, `lambda_at_edge.py`) and `mcp` 2.3.0 wheels; and the two AWS pricing pages were fetched the same day. No finding is dropped; one NIT (N6) is half-disputed on evidence, below.

| Finding | Disposition | Where |
|---|---|---|
| **H1** `edge_system_snapshot_get` wraps a state-changing route (`subprocess` → `/snapshot/snapshot.sh`, archive under `/aws_dda/system/`) and returns a path, yet is `RO`/Phase 1 | **Addressed — reclassified, as recommended.** The tool is now `edge_system_snapshot_create`: `classification='mutating'`, `destructive=False`, `idempotent=False`, `role_policy='mutate'`, **Phase 2**, `status_tool='edge_system_health_get'`, no arguments; D13 therefore keeps anonymous and Viewer principals away from it. Two premises corrected on evidence while agreeing with the conclusion: the body is `{"archivePath": "snapshotfile/<file>.tar.gz"}` (the download-route path, `Snapshotter.py:71`, `SNAPSHOT_FILE_PATTERN` at `constants.py:110`), not the absolute `/aws_dda/system/...` path, and `snapshot.sh` **removes** the previous `snapshot-*.tar*` before writing the new archive, so repeated calls replace rather than accumulate — still a shell-script run and a disk write per call, so still mutating. The result is shaped by the D34 scrub to the bare file name (`scrubbed_fields: ["archivePath"]`; the "basename" option). Regression coverage: `test_child_catalog_classification.py` fixes `system.get_snapshot` as not `RO` and runs the AST rule (no `subprocess`/`os.system`/`os.remove`/`shutil`/write-mode `open` in any `RO` route or its directly-called helpers, imported modules included) over every `RO` edge spec; `test_child_protocol.py` covers the tool under `--mutating-enabled`, with the switch off, and for `anonymous`; the harness calls it in the mutating stage. The capability-coverage line now says snapshot **creation** is Phase 2 and **download** is excluded; the GET-but-mutating list in Decision point 4 names it; no `deprecated_alias` is kept because the old name never shipped (14.4 applies to released names); Backlog 14 records the id-addressed route pair that would allow a future read tool | Key research findings (Edge route effects); Decision point 4; Tool catalogs intro (`status_tool` convention), edge row, coverage line; Phasing (Phase 2); Invariants (new row); Property 2 (edge classification clause); Testing Strategy (`test_child_catalog_classification.py`, `test_child_protocol.py`, harness); Backlog 14 |
| **M1** `RO` tools return device paths (`inferenceFilePath`, `imageFileName`, `archivePath`); "never returns a filesystem path" has no enforcing rule | **Addressed — scrub rule and Property 19, as recommended, with one refinement.** New **D34** and `child/shaping.py`: `ToolSpec.scrub_paths: bool = True`; a recursive walk over every 2xx JSON body replaces path-like values under keys matching `PATH_KEY_RE = ^(.*[Pp]ath\|.*[Dd]ir(ectory)?\|.*[Ll]ocation\|.*[Ff]ile[Nn]ame\|.*[Ff]older)$`, and any string under a device root (`/aws_dda/`, `/tmp/`, `/opt/`, `/home/`, `/root/`, `/var/`, `/etc/`, `/usr/`, `/snapshot/`, with or without `file://`), and records the key paths in `structured_content.scrubbed_fields`. **Refinement (file name, not drop):** the value is reduced to `os.path.basename(...)` rather than deleted, because reading the bodies showed the file name is load-bearing — `list_images` items have no `captureId` (`inference_results_utils.py:225`), so `inferenceFilePath`'s file name `<capture_id>.jsonl` is the only key into the id-addressed capture image tools, and the snapshot file name is what an operator acts on; a bare file name shows nothing of the directory layout and cannot be used by any MCP tool (the path-taking routes stay excluded), so Requirement 8.5 is met. Path-like is "contains `/` and is not a non-`file:` URL", so a stream source's `rtsp://…` `location` survives while a folder source's directory is reduced. Reading the bodies also found three more path fields the review did not list, now covered: `artifactPath` (`registration_to_dict`), `outputDir` (`execution_to_dict`), `captureIdPath` (`edge_results_export`). §7 step 5 reworded exactly as proposed ("Never accepts a filesystem path as an argument … and returns none under the scrubbed keys (Property 19)"); Property 19 added with the review's fixed cases plus `artifactPath`, `location` (both kinds) and idempotence; the edge Input-validation table gained the rule; a Data Models example shows the shaped bodies | D34; §5 `child/catalog.py`, `child/shaping.py`; §7 step 5; edge catalog rows (`edge_workflow_images_list`, `edge_registrations_*`, `edge_registration_executions_list`, `edge_execution_get`, `edge_results_export`, `edge_image_sources_*`, exclusion paragraph); Data Models (shaped result); Error Handling (edge, scrub row); Input validation; Invariants; Property 19; Testing Strategy (`test_property_child_shaping.py`, harness) |
| **M2** `edge_image_source_preview` / `_test_connection` return base64 inside JSON; `ImageContent` never fires and ordinary frames hit `result_too_large` | **Addressed, as recommended, plus one more tool and one companion field.** `ToolSpec.image_field: str \| None` (`'image'` on both named tools); shaping rule inserted before the size bound exactly as proposed (decode, sniff magic bytes with `image/jpeg` default, ≤ 2 MiB → `ImageContent(data=<original base64>)` and the field deleted from `result`, larger → `result_too_large {bytes, limit}`, undecodable → `backend_failure`; `MAX_RESULT_BYTES` applies to the remainder); Property 9 gained "an `image_field` value never counts toward `MAX_RESULT_BYTES`"; `test_child_protocol.py` gained the 600 KB base64 case asserting one `ImageContent` and no `result_too_large` (with a control case through `image_field=None`). Found while verifying: **`edge_execution_overlay_get`** also carries its frame as base64 (`get_workflow_execution_overlay` → `read_mask_overlay` → `{maskImage: <base64 \| None>, maskBackground}`, `api.py:465–481`) and now has `image_field='maskImage'`; and **`edge_workflow_images_list` items each embed a base64 frame (`image`)** that would exceed the bound on every page — a new `drop_fields: tuple[str, ...]` (`('image',)` on that tool, recorded as `dropped_fields`) removes it and the row points at the id-addressed `edge_capture_output_image_get`. The on-hardware stage now calls `edge_image_source_preview` on a real source and asserts the `ImageContent`, closing the observability gap the review named. `download_file.py`'s `image/jpg` media type is normalized to `image/jpeg` | D23, D34; Key research findings (Edge route effects); §5 `child/catalog.py`, `child/shaping.py`; §7 step 5; edge catalog rows (`edge_image_source_preview`, `_test_connection`, `edge_execution_*_overlay_get`, `edge_workflow_images_list`); Data Models (shaped result); Error Handling (edge image rows); Property 9; Testing Strategy (`test_child_protocol.py`, `test_property_child_shaping.py`, harness) |
| **M3** Invoke budget (2 s + 25 s) does not fit inside the MCP Lambda's 28 s after cold start; a Lambda-killed call yields a bare 5xx with no `isError`/envelope and invites a retry | **Addressed, as recommended, with the "raise to 29 s" option taken.** `dispatch.py`: `Config(connect_timeout=2, read_timeout=20, retries={'total_max_attempts': 1})` and a pre-dispatch guard `LAMBDA_CONTEXT.get_remaining_time_in_millis() < MIN_DISPATCH_BUDGET_MS` → `ToolFailure('timeout', 'insufficient time budget to start the backing operation; retry')` with the plain message (nothing ran) and no `Invoke`; `handler()` stores the Lambda `context` in `dispatch.LAMBDA_CONTEXT` per invocation (sound for the same one-invocation-per-container reason as D25; Mangum also exposes it as `scope["aws.context"]` — verified in `mangum/handlers/api_gateway.py` (both payload formats), `alb.py` and `lambda_at_edge.py` of the installed 0.22.0). **Parameters:** function timeout **29 s** (the review's alternative; under the 30 s integration limit) and threshold **24 000 ms** rather than 23 000 — same 5 s pre-dispatch allowance (29 − 24) as the review's 28 − 23, but 2 s instead of 1 s left after a maxed-out 22 s invoke for the envelope `PutItem`, the telemetry line and the response; the arithmetic is written into D32. The review proposed normalising with the `portalRegistryEnforced` **truthy** set ("a value that normalizes to off appends `false`"); that would make `0`/`no`/`off` append `-c deployMcpServer=false` while the CDK helper `mcpServerEnabled` (the `groundedSamWorkerEnabled` default-ON semantics the design already chose, `context-helpers.ts:11–20`: only an explicit `false` disables) would treat those same values as **on** — two rules again. ## Response to the design review (revision 7)

Each finding of the sixth design review (verdict `CHANGES_REQUESTED`, 2026-10-19; 0 HIGH, 5 MEDIUM, 6 NIT) and its disposition. Every factual claim below was re-verified on 2026-10-19 against the filesystem on branch `remediation` at HEAD `c3bf88c1` (unchanged since the review): `src/backend/endpoints/workflow.py`, `inference_result.py`, `image_source.py`, `static_image_camera.py`, `static_video_camera.py`, `system.py`, `feature_config.py`, `camera.py`, `text_generation.py`, `download_file.py`, `workflow_engine/api.py`, `workflow_engine/run_retention.py`, `utils/utils.py`, `utils/constants.py`, `utils/inference_results_utils.py`, `utils/static_image_camera.py`, `utils/video_loop.py`, `gstreamer/gst_pipeline_executor.py`, `gstreamer/pipeline_builder.py`, `stream_ingest/connection_test.py`, `dda_triton/triton_edge_client.py`, `edge_ml1_p_camera_management/aravis_functions.py`, `data_models/common.py`, `dao/sqlite_db/models.py`, `dao/sqlite_db/workflow_metadata_dao.py`, `dao/sqlite_db/inference_result_dao.py`, `resources/pagination/base_paginator.py`, `resources/accessors/inference_result_accessor.py`, `src/frontend/src/api/InferenceResultAPI.ts`, `edge-cv-portal/backend/functions/captures.py`, `datasets.py`, and `test/on-hardware/harness/harnesslib/client.py` + `stages/test_25_vlm_image_generate.py`. No finding is dropped; none is disputed — every review premise held on re-reading, and two review line citations were tightened (`list_images` spans 423–432, the validator call being at 432; the executor's return is `gst_pipeline_executor.py:94`, line 93 being the preceding `raise`). Where the review offered a choice, the choice and its reason are stated in the row.

Applied the same field to the other narrow routes found while re-reading: `edge_registration_executions_list` (`limit_max=50`, the route clamps to 1..50), `edge_results_list` (100, `PageParams.size`), `portal_data_captures_list` (100/20), `portal_data_datasets_preview_get` (50/8); D23 and the Tool-catalogs intro state the rule | Key research findings (new bullet); D23; §2 `catalog.py`; §5 `child/catalog.py`; Tool catalogs intro; edge row; Error Handling (edge, new "Child argument schema" row); Input validation; Property 2; Testing Strategy (`test_child_protocol.py`, `test_child_catalog_arguments.py`, harness) |

Rows: `edge_system_station_get.drop_fields=('logoImage',)` — **the `drop_fields` option chosen over `image_field`**: the logo is a branding asset with no operational value, and a Phase-1 poll-style tool should not carry a few hundred KB of image into the model's context on every call (`dropped_fields` still tells the caller a logo exists); `edge_workflow_images_list.drop_fields=('images[].image', 'images[].inferenceResult.mask_image')`; `edge_results_list.drop_fields=('results[].maskImage',)` pointing at `edge_result_get`; `edge_result_get.image_field='maskImage'`; `edge_workflow_run` split from `_retry` and given `body_constants={"returnImageString": False}` (new `ToolSpec.body_constants`, merged beneath the caller's arguments), the optional `capture_image_count?`/`capture_time_interval?`/`capture_prefix?` (→ the three `RunWorkflowRequest` fields, with the route's 1–100 / 1–120 bounds) **and** `return_partial_results_early?` (the fourth field the row omitted), `drop_fields=('inferenceResult.mask_image',)`, `resource_id` = `captureId` or `captureTaskId` (the route returns the latter for the capture-task branch, `workflow.py:345`), `status_tool='edge_results_list'`; `edge_registration_get.drop_fields=('executions',)` — **drop chosen over `collection_key='executions'`** because a bounded, filterable view of the executions already exists (`edge_registration_executions_list`, `limit` ≤ 50, `notable`) and slicing an embedded list inside an `object`-shaped result would need a second pagination mechanism for one row; `triggerHealth` is kept. The dataset row split in three: `_list` (`usecase_id`, `prefix?`, `max_depth?`), `_preview_get` (`usecase_id`, `prefix`, `offset?`, `limit ≤ 50`, `extensions?`), `_count` (`usecase_id`, `prefix`), each with its handler function and the line of its `required` check. `edge_results_list`: `page?` (≥ 1) and `limit ≤ 100` with `param_map {page → page, limit → size}`, `collection_key='results'`, the six optional filters with the route's literal values, and **no `offset`** — the row states why (the route paginates by page; an `offset` the dispatcher applied to an already-cut page would silently return nothing past page 1). `edge_results_summary_reset`: `reset_time?` (epoch seconds → `resetTime`) and `body_constants={"resetTime": NOW_EPOCH_S}` — the dispatcher substitutes `int(time.time())`, as the HMI does (`InferenceResultAPI.ts:101–106`) — with the row recording the `None` → `summaryStartTime` → `>= NULL` → zero chain. **Mechanical clause:** Property 2's argument ↔ route consistency rule (portal: AST over `params.get`/`body.get`/`pathParameters` reads and `400 … required` branches; edge: the route's FastAPI signature, `Depends()` models and request model; every required parameter must be a required argument; `body_constants` keys must be request-model fields), implemented as `test_mcp_catalog_arguments.py` and `test_child_catalog_arguments.py` with the five rows as fixed cases; Decision point 4 and 7 and a new Invariants row name the rule | Key research findings; D23; Decision points 4 and 7; §2 `catalog.py`; §5 `child/catalog.py`, `child/dispatch.py`; Tool catalogs intro; portal `data` rows; edge rows (`edge_results_summary_get`/`_list`, `edge_result_get`, `edge_results_summary_reset`); Input validation; Invariants; Property 2; Testing Strategy (two new test modules, `test_child_protocol.py`, harness) |
| **N1** `test_connection`, `list_feature_configs`, `get_gpu_status` have runtime side effects the catalog calls none | Addressed as proposed: one "accepted reading" sentence per row — `run_connection_test` takes an ingest-session lease (starting a session when none runs) and may restart a backing-off one, released in `finally`, the HMI's own connection test; `list_feature_configs`/`get_gpu_status` lazily construct the Triton client (`TritonEdgeClient.get_instance` → `create_server`, only when the model repository is non-empty — the empty-repo guard at `feature_config.py:93–106`/`134`) and publish a model-status shadow report, the HMI's own polling effects — "persists nothing the tool could be said to have changed, so `RO` under the persisted-state reading"; the three functions are fixed `RO` cases in `test_child_catalog_classification.py` with the readings in the comment, and `…/test-connection` on the read-only-POST list carries the note; Property 2's classification clause lists them. HEAD did not move during this revision (`c3bf88c1` before and after).

## Response to the design review (revision 9)

Each finding of the eighth design review (verdict `CHANGES_REQUESTED`, 2026-10-19; 1 HIGH, 2 MEDIUM, 4 NIT) and its disposition, followed by the results of a row-by-row check of **all 164** revision-8 catalog rows against their routes on five axes — route and dispatch, classification, permission and authorization, arguments, shaping — that was run before this revision so that it could correct every wrong row at once rather than only the rows a finding names. Every changed cell's file:line was re-read on 2026-10-20 at HEAD `c3bf88c1` on branch `remediation` (unchanged since the review; no tracked file modified), and the revised portal classification rule (Decision point 4) was run as a throwaway AST script over all 89 portal `RO` tools that have a backing route: it reports exactly the twelve `accepted_writes` entries the rows declare and nothing else. No finding is dropped. One review option is rejected (N4's lower-default alternative), and four review statements are corrected on evidence: H1's reap call site is `workflow_generator.py:1309–1312` (1275–1279 is docstring); H1 fix 1's rationale ("`record_audit_event_strict` is caught by its `put_item`") does not hold for a walk that never enters the shared layer, where that `put_item` sits (`shared_utils.py:470`); N1's default is at `data_management.py:479–480` (476 is `get_usecase`); and the review's §3 claim that the edge `RO` routes call no `shutil` function is false for `system.get_system_health` (`shutil.disk_usage`, 213). `requirements.md` is unchanged: no requirements defect was found (the narrowed audit-log tool and the deferrals stay within 3.7 and 17.4 with sign-off item (h)).

## Response to the design review (revision 10)

Each finding of the ninth design review (verdict `CHANGES_REQUESTED`, 2026-10-20; 0 HIGH, 2 MEDIUM, 7 NIT) and its disposition. Because every earlier review found more instances of a defect class in rows its revision had not checked, each finding was applied to its **whole class**, and each has a second row with the class scan: what was checked, which rows changed, which were confirmed. The scans read the source at HEAD `c3bf88c1` on branch `remediation` (the working tree; HEAD equal to that commit, no tracked file modified) with throwaway static scripts (`ast` and `grep`; nothing imported or run). No finding is dropped; three of N1's proposed seeds and the scope of N5's sign-off item are changed on evidence (rows N1 and N5). `requirements.md` is unchanged: no requirements defect was found. Arithmetic: **161 rows** (37 changed in place — 30 portal, 7 edge — and 124 unchanged; none added or removed); tools: portal 185 − 1 (`portal_data_labeling_manifest_get`) = **184** (87 / 53 / 44 by phase; 89 `RO`, 66 `M`, 29 `M!`), edge **73** unchanged (47 / 9 / 17; 47 `RO`, 12 `M`, 14 `M!`).

## Response to the design review (revision 11)

Each finding of the tenth design review (verdict `CHANGES_REQUESTED`, 2026-10-21; 0 HIGH, 1 MEDIUM, 7 NIT) and its disposition. As in revision 10, each finding was applied to its **whole class**, and each has a second row with the class scan: rows checked, rows changed, rows confirmed. The scans read the source at HEAD `c3bf88c1` on branch `remediation` (the working tree; HEAD equal to that commit, no tracked file modified) with throwaway static scripts (`ast` and `grep`; nothing imported or run). The two owner-confirmed choices are applied as recorded for this pass: M1 takes the review's option (b), and N7 puts Pillow writes on the effect list. Sign-off item (j) stays an open owner decision. No finding is dropped or disputed, and `requirements.md` is unchanged (no requirements defect found). Arithmetic: **161 rows** (27 changed in place — 23 portal, 4 edge — and 134 unchanged; none added or removed); tools unchanged, because M1 removes an argument, not a tool, and no class or phase cell changed: portal **184** (87 / 53 / 44 by phase; 89 `RO`, 66 `M`, 29 `M!`), edge **73** (47 / 9 / 17; 47 `RO`, 12 `M`, 14 `M!`).

| **M1** `edge_image_source_preview` (`RO`) forwards a free-dict settings override | **Addressed, option (b).** The row offers `image_source_id` only. The route's `imageSourceConfiguration` override (`endpoints/image_source.py:94`) is not offered, so nothing the caller supplies reaches the pipeline or the CSI host service's config (`csi_capture.py:55–80`). Without the override the CSI write stores the source's own saved settings, so `ACCEPTED_EFFECT_NOTE` ("nothing the caller supplied is persisted") holds unchanged. Option (a) was not taken: its closed schema still persists caller tuning for CSI sources, which needs a new sign-off item or dropping those keys, and it leaves the CAMERA-source gain/exposure path unexamined. Previewing unsaved settings stays possible through the Phase-3 `edge_image_source_update` followed by the preview. A tuning-preview tool is not added; Backlog 28 records it with the REST-side override restriction for `POST /image-sources/{id}/preview`. ## Response to the design review (revision 12)

Each finding of the eleventh design review (verdict `APPROVED`, 2026-10-22; 0 HIGH, 0 MEDIUM, 6 NIT) and its disposition. The review allowed applying the six NITs without another review loop, so they were applied as the Phase-0 tasks 1.14 and 1.19–1.23 before any code, by targeted edits, and checked structurally: headings, the six catalog tables, cell counts and the recount. The three choices the tasks sync recorded are applied as recorded: review-11 N2 pins `status=404` on both outcomes, N3 takes the optional `pathlib`/`os` extension, and N5 takes option (b), `RO_FREE_TEXT_ARGS`. The owner gave the go to implement on 2026-10-22; formal acceptance of the design text and sign-off item (j) stay open owner decisions. No finding is dropped or disputed, and `requirements.md` is unchanged. Arithmetic: **161 rows** (8 changed in place for these findings, 2 portal and 6 edge, and 2 more portal rows, `portal_admin_role_assign` and `portal_models_compilation_create`, reworded without a change of meaning; none added or removed); tools unchanged, because no class or phase cell changed: portal **184** (87 / 53 / 44 by phase; 89 `RO`, 66 `M`, 29 `M!`), edge **73** (47 / 9 / 17; 47 `RO`, 12 `M`, 14 `M!`).

|---|---|---|
| **N1** (task 1.19) `model_name` was no longer mapped to its path parameter on three edge rows | **Addressed as specified.** `edge_model_start` and `edge_model_stop` write `model_name` (→ path `modelName`), and `edge_textgen_generate` writes `model_name` (→ path `model_name`). The conventions keep "(a name ending in `_id`)" for the bare-identifier rule and the collision raise, and add that any other argument filling a path parameter is written with its `(→ path …)` entry, so every path parameter of every row has a `param_map` entry. Property 2 gains the fixed case `edge_model_start.param_map['model_name'] == 'modelName'` and the clause that every `{name}` segment of a spec's `resource` is a `param_map` target. §5 `child/dispatch.py` now says where the entries come from. A path-parameter scan of the 161 rows (text rules: an explicit `(→ path …)`, a matching `_id` name or camelCase form, or the one remaining bare `_id` name) finds every path parameter filled; before this revision it found these three rows as the only non-`_id` fillers without an entry | Tool-catalogs conventions; edge rows `edge_model_start`, `edge_model_stop`, `edge_textgen_generate`; Property 2; §5 `child/dispatch.py`; revision-11 N2 class-scan row (annotated) |
| **N2** (task 1.14) the not-found result was pinned on category and message only | **Addressed as specified.** On a `bind_usecase_path` spec both outcomes, the D36 refusal and a backend 404, return `ToolFailure('not_found', 'no such object in the named Use_Case', status=404)`. The real backend status (the 2xx, or the 404) is kept only in the `mcp_tool` log line and the envelope's `details.status`. Property 8 and the 16.4 audit test compare the whole `CallToolResult` (`content` and `structured_content`) except `request_id`. Checked and aligned: D21 (`status?` names the exception), §2 `errors.py` (`status` is the backend status where there is one), §2 `shaping.py`, §4 step 9, the Data Models log line and the Error Handling backend-status and Use_Case-binding rows. The Tool error result example (an edge 403) is a plain backend status and needed no change | D21; D36; §2 `errors.py`, `shaping.py`; §4 step 9; Data Models log line; Error Handling (backend status, Use_Case binding); Property 8; Testing Strategy (16.4) |
| **N3** (task 1.20) the Pillow effect was defined two ways, and its entry key broke the `<call>` convention | **Addressed as specified, with the optional extension.** Decision point 4 defines a Pillow image value once: a call of `Image.open`, `Image.new`, `Image.fromarray` or an `ImageOps` function, a method call on such a value, or a name bound from one (assignment or `with` target); `.save(...)` on such a value is the effect `Image.save`. Property 2, the Invariants row and the Testing Strategy cite that definition, and the Testing Strategy adds the chained `ImageOps.exif_transpose(Image.open(p)).save(q)` as a fixed case. The conventions state that the `<call>` of an `accepted_effects` entry is the effect-list name the walk reports, not the receiver text, so the staging site `im.save` (`pipeline_builder.py:187`) is the entry `…:Image.save`. **Extension:** the effect list adds `os.makedirs`/`os.mkdir`/`os.rmdir`/`os.chmod` and the `pathlib` twins of the listed calls on a `Path` value (`Path.unlink`/`rename`/`replace`/`touch`/`mkdir`/`rmdir`/`chmod`, `Path.open` with a write mode; the value rule keeps a `datetime.replace` out). On an `RO` path at `c3bf88c1` they reach only `write_csi_config`'s `os.makedirs` (`csi_capture.py:69`) and `os.chmod` (80), re-read for this revision; this agrees with the review-11 walk with the extended list. So `edge_image_source_test_connection` and `edge_image_source_preview` gain `'workflow_engine.csi_capture.write_csi_config:os.makedirs'` and `':os.chmod'`, the accepted-effect count becomes eight effects on eight tools (six before), and no classification changes | Decision point 4; Tool-catalogs conventions (entry format); edge rows `edge_image_source_stream_health_get` / `edge_image_source_test_connection` and `edge_image_source_preview`; Property 2 (edge clause, effect count); Invariants; Testing Strategy `test_child_catalog_classification.py` |

The edge row `edge_static_image_pin` and Backlog 17 write `edge_static_video_pin_get`/`edge_static_video_unpin`.
